\textstyle{H(Z_{1},\mathsf{w}|_{Z_{1}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H(Z_{1},i_{1}^{!}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}|_{Z}}\omega_{Z})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H(Z_{1},\tilde{i}_{1}^{!}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\omega_{X}).}

We have shown in the above that
H(Z_{1},\mathsf{w}|_{Z_{1}})\cong H(Z_{1},\tilde{i}_{1}^{!}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\omega_{X})
.
As
Z_{1}
is the attracting set of a maximal component of
Z^{\mathsf{A}}
, we can replace
(Z,X)
in (
2.14
) by
(Z_{1},Z)
and obtain

H(Z_{1},\mathsf{w}|_{Z_{1}})\cong H(Z_{1},i_{1}^{!}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}|_{Z}}\omega_{Z}).

It follows that the vetical map in (
2.15
)
is an isomorphism. This concludes the proof.
∎

Remark 2.5
.

As long as
Z
is the form of
\bigcup_{F}\operatorname{Attr}(F)
for a collection of fixed components
\{F\}\subseteq\operatorname{Fix}_{\mathsf{A}}(X)
, the above proof works and the map
H(Z,\mathsf{w}|_{Z})\to H(X,\mathsf{w})_{Z}
is an isomorphism for such
Z
.

Definition 2.6
.

We say that a class in
H(X,\mathsf{w})
is
supported
on a closed subvariety
Z
, if its image under

H(X,\mathsf{w})\to H(U,\mathsf{w}|_{U})

vanishes.

2.2.5.
Torus equivariance

Let
\mathsf{T}
be a torus acting on
(X,\mathsf{w})
as in Setting
2.1
,
all constructions above can be generalized to this setting.
For a subtorus
\mathsf{A}\subseteq\mathsf{T}
, denote
i_{\mathsf{A}}\colon X^{\mathsf{A}}\to X
to be the closed embedding of torus fixed locus.
We have proper pushforward and Gysin pullback

(2.16)

i_{\mathsf{A},*}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w}),\quad i_{\mathsf{A}}^{*}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}}).

By (
2.11
), the composition
i_{\mathsf{A}}^{*}i_{\mathsf{A},*}
is the multiplication by the Euler class
e^{\mathsf{T}}(N_{X^{\mathsf{A}}/X})
.
Therefore
i_{\mathsf{A},*}
is
injective
. And
i_{\mathsf{A}}^{*}
is
surjective
after localization, i.e.

i_{\mathsf{A}}^{*}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})_{\mathrm{loc}}\twoheadrightarrow H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})_{\mathrm{loc}},

where
(-)_{\mathrm{loc}}:=(-)\otimes_{H^{*}_{\mathsf{T}}(\operatorname{pt})}\operatorname{Frac}(H^{*}_{\mathsf{T}}(\operatorname{pt}))
denotes the localized theory.

Remark 2.7
.

(1) Proposition
2.4
holds equivariantly as long as the extra torus preserves the potential
\mathsf{w}
and commutes with
\mathsf{A}
action.
(2) If the extra torus in (1) contains
\mathsf{A}
, i.e. as the
\mathsf{T}
in Setting
2.1
,
one can show the equivariant version of the map in Proposition
2.4
is injective.

2.3.
Critical
K
-theory

2.3.1.
Definition

Let
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T})
be as in Setting
2.1
.
Consider the dg-category
\mathrm{Fact}_{\mathrm{coh}}(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T})
of coherent factorizations
of
\mathsf{w}

[
BFK
, Def. 3.1]
, whose objects are pairs

\mathcal{E}_{-1}\xrightarrow{d_{0}}\mathcal{E}_{0},\quad\mathcal{E}_{0}\xrightarrow{d_{-1}}\mathcal{E}_{-1}

of morphisms in
\mathrm{Coh}_{\mathsf{T}}(X)

such that

d_{-1}\circ d_{0}=d_{0}\circ d_{-1}=\mathsf{w}.

Let
\mathrm{HFact}_{\mathrm{coh}}(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T})
be the homotopy category of
\mathrm{Fact}_{\mathrm{coh}}(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T})
, which is a triangulated
category, and
\mathrm{Acy}_{\mathrm{coh}}
denote the minimal thick triangulated subcategory containing totalizations of short exact sequences
of coherent factorizations. Define the
triangulated category of coherent factorizations
to be the following Verdier quotient
[
Orl
]
,
[
BFK
, Def. 3.9]

\mathrm{MF}_{\mathrm{coh}}(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T}):=\mathrm{HFact}_{\mathrm{coh}}(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T})/\mathrm{Acy}_{\mathrm{coh}}.

Without causing confusions, we also denote it to be
\mathrm{MF}(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T})
, or sometimes write it as
\mathrm{MF}([X/\mathsf{T}],\mathsf{w})
.

Definition 2.8
.

The
critical
K
-theory
of
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T})
is the Grothendieck group of
\mathrm{MF}_{\mathrm{}}(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T})
:

K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w}):=K_{0}^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w}):=K_{0}(\mathrm{MF}_{\mathrm{}}(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T})).

2.3.2.
Canonical map

Let
i\colon Z(\mathsf{w})\subseteq X
be the inclusion of the zero locus of the function
\mathsf{w}
.
There is an equivalence of categories (
[
BFK
, Rmk. 3.65]
,
[
Hir
, Thm. 3.6]
) from the
\mathsf{T}
-equivariant singularity category of
Z(\mathsf{w})
:

\operatorname{D}^{b}\operatorname{Coh}_{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{w}))/\operatorname{Perf}_{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{w}))\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathrm{MF}_{\mathrm{}}(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T}),

which induces an exact sequence of the Grothendieck groups (e.g.
[
PS
, Lem. 1.10]
):

(2.17)

\to K(\operatorname{Perf}_{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{w})))\to K(\operatorname{D}^{b}\operatorname{Coh}_{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{w})))\xrightarrow{\mathrm{can}}K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})\to 0.

More generally, let
i\colon Z\hookrightarrow X
be the embedding of a
\mathsf{T}
-invariant closed subset, there exists a
matrix factorization category

\mathrm{MF}_{\mathrm{}}(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T})_{Z}

with support
on
Z
, whose Grothendieck group is denoted by

K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})_{Z}.

One has a surjective
canonical map
(e.g.
[
VV1
, Prop. 2.6]
):

(2.18)

\operatorname{can}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z\cap Z(\mathsf{w}))\twoheadrightarrow K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})_{Z},

where
K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z\cap Z(\mathsf{w})):=K_{0}(\operatorname{D}^{b}\operatorname{Coh}_{\mathsf{T}}(Z\cap Z(\mathsf{w})))
.

2.3.3.
Euler class operator

Let
\pi\colon E\to X
be a vector bundle of rank
r
and
\mathsf{w}
be a regular function on
X
.
We define the
K-theoretic Euler class operator
on critical
K
-theory:

(2.19)

e_{K}(E)\cdot(-)\colon K(X,\mathsf{w})\to K(X,\mathsf{w}),

[(\mathcal{E}_{-1},\mathcal{E}_{0},d_{-1},d_{0})]\mapsto[\left(\wedge^{*}E^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{E}_{-1},\wedge^{*}E^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{E}_{0},d_{-1},d_{0}\right)].

It is straightforward to check that the Euler class operator commutes with the canonical map (
2.18
).

2.3.4.
Functorial properties

Matrix factorization categories have functorial properties, e.g.
pullbacks
by
flat
morphisms and
pushforwards

by
proper morphisms
(
[
BFK
]
,
[
VV1
, §2.2.2]
), critical
K
-groups also have such properties. For regular embeddings, one
can also define
Gysin pullbacks
for critical
K
-groups.

In particular, for the inclusion
i_{\mathsf{A}}\colon X^{\mathsf{A}}\hookrightarrow X
of
\mathsf{A}
-fixed locus,
there are proper pushforward and Gysin pullback:

(2.20)

i_{\mathsf{A},*}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w}),\quad i_{\mathsf{A}}^{*}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}}).

By
[
VV1
, Prop. 2.8]
3
3

3

When working with torus
invariant
functions, the flatness condition on functions imposed in
[
VV1
, Prop. 2.8]
(which uses
[
VV1
, Lem. 2.4]
) can be dropped off by following the argument of
[
Toda3
, Lem. 2.4.7]
.
, we have

(2.21)

i_{\mathsf{A}}^{*}\circ i_{\mathsf{A},*}(-)=e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}(N_{X^{\mathsf{A}}/X})\cdot(-),

where
e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}(-)\cdot
is the equivariant version of the
K
-theoretic Euler class operator (
2.18
).

This moreover induces an isomorphism

(2.22)

K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})_{\mathrm{loc}}\xrightarrow{\cong}K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})_{\mathrm{loc}}

of localized critical
K
-theories,
where
(-)_{\mathrm{loc}}:=(-)\otimes_{K_{\mathsf{T}}(\operatorname{pt})}\operatorname{Frac}(K_{\mathsf{T}}(\operatorname{pt}))
.

Given a
\mathsf{T}
-equivariant vector bundle
\pi\colon\widetilde{X}\to X
, it is straightforward to show the pullback map

(2.23)

\pi^{*}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(\widetilde{X},\mathsf{w}\circ\pi)

is an isomorphism.

2.3.5.
Excisions

Let
j\colon U\hookrightarrow X
be the inclusion of an open subset, and
i\colon Z\hookrightarrow X
be the closed embedding of its complement.
There is an excision sequence

(2.24)

\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z,\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{*}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j^{*}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(U,\mathsf{w}).}

It is obvious that
j^{*}\circ i_{*}=0
. The map
j^{*}
is surjective because it fits into a commutative diagram
(below the canonical map is given as (
2.17
))

\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathsf{w}^{-1}(0))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j^{*}\hskip 8.19447pt}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{can}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(U\cap\mathsf{w}^{-1}(0))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{can}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j^{*}\hskip 8.19447pt}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(U,\mathsf{w}).}

The following is a useful result showing when
\ker(j^{*})=\operatorname{im}(i_{*})
holds.

Proposition 2.9
.

Let
F
be a connected component of
X^{\mathsf{A}}
and
Z=\operatorname{Attr}^{f}(F)
with immersion
i\colon Z\to X
.
Then
\ker(j^{*})=\operatorname{im}(i_{*})
in the sequence (
2.24
).

Proof.

The case when
F
is maximal, i.e.
\operatorname{Attr}^{f}(F)=\operatorname{Attr}(F)
is essentially proven in
[
P
, Thm. 2.5]
. Strictly speaking,
[
P
, Thm. 2.5]
assumes
X
to be affine, but the argument works for quasi-projective
X
. For the convenience of readers, we explain the details here. Let
p\colon Z=\operatorname{Attr}(F)\to F
be the attraction morphism. By
[
Hal
, Thm. 3.35, Cor. 3.28]
, there is a semi-orthogonal decomposition (SOD) for the derived category of coherent sheaves:

\displaystyle\left\langle\cdots,i_{*}\operatorname{D}^{b}([Z/\mathsf{T}])_{-2},i_{*}\operatorname{D}^{b}([Z/\mathsf{T}])_{-1},\mathbb{G},i_{*}\operatorname{D}^{b}([Z/\mathsf{T}])_{0},i_{*}\operatorname{D}^{b}([Z/\mathsf{T}])_{1},\cdots\right\rangle=\operatorname{D}^{b}([X/\mathsf{T}]),

such that

\left\langle\cdots,\operatorname{D}^{b}([Z/\mathsf{T}])_{-2},\operatorname{D}^{b}([Z/\mathsf{T}])_{-1},\operatorname{D}^{b}([Z/\mathsf{T}])_{0},\operatorname{D}^{b}([Z/\mathsf{T}])_{1},\cdots\right\rangle

is an SOD of
\operatorname{D}^{b}([Z/\mathsf{T}])
,
where the weight is with respect to a cocharacter in the chamber
\mathfrak{C}
.
Moreover, under the restriction to the open locus
\operatorname{D}^{b}([X/\mathsf{T}])\twoheadrightarrow\operatorname{D}^{b}([(X\setminus Z)/\mathsf{T}])
, we have an equivalence of categories

\displaystyle\mathbb{G}\cong\operatorname{D}^{b}([(X\setminus Z)/\mathsf{T}]).

This induces an SOD for the matrix factorization category (e.g.
[
P
, Prop. 2.1]
):

\displaystyle\left\langle\cdots,i_{*}\mathrm{MF}([Z/\mathsf{T}],\mathsf{w})_{-2},i_{*}\mathrm{MF}([Z/\mathsf{T}],\mathsf{w})_{-1},\mathbb{W},i_{*}\mathrm{MF}([Z/\mathsf{T}],\mathsf{w})_{0},i_{*}\mathrm{MF}([Z/\mathsf{T}],\mathsf{w})_{1},\cdots\right\rangle=\mathrm{MF}([X/G],\mathsf{w}),

and an equivalence of categories:

\mathbb{W}\cong\mathrm{MF}([(X\setminus Z)/\mathsf{T}],\mathsf{w}).

By passing to the Grothendieck group, there is a decomposition

\displaystyle K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z,\mathsf{w})\oplus K^{\mathsf{T}}(X\setminus Z,\mathsf{w})=K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w}),

where the map
K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z,\mathsf{w})\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})
is
i_{*}
. In particular we have
\ker(j^{*})=\operatorname{im}(i_{*})
.

For general
F
,
Z=\operatorname{Attr}^{f}(F)
admits a filtration

\emptyset=Z_{0}\subseteq Z_{1}\subseteq Z_{2}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq Z_{n}=Z

by closed subvarieties
Z_{i}
, such that
Z_{i}\setminus Z_{i-1}=\operatorname{Attr}(F_{i})
for some union of fixed components
F_{i}
and
F_{i}
is maximal in
X\setminus Z_{i-1}
. We prove for general
Z
by induction on
n
. Let
i_{1}\colon Z_{1}\hookrightarrow Z
and
j_{1}\colon Z\setminus Z_{1}\hookrightarrow X\setminus Z_{1}
be the embeddings, and denote
\tilde{i}_{1}:=i\circ i_{1}
. By the induction hypothesis, the lemma holds when replacing
(X,Z)
by
(X\setminus Z_{1},Z\setminus Z_{1})
. Suppose that
\alpha\in\ker(j^{*})
, then
\alpha|_{X\setminus Z_{1}}\in\operatorname{im}(j_{1*})
, i.e.
\exists\,\beta\in K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z\setminus Z_{1},\mathsf{w})
such that
\alpha|_{X\setminus Z_{1}}=j_{1*}\beta
. Take
\tilde{\beta}\in K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z,\mathsf{w})
such that
\tilde{\beta}|_{Z\setminus Z_{1}}=\beta
, then we have
(\alpha-i_{*}\tilde{\beta})|_{X\setminus Z_{1}}=0
. Using the
n=1
case, we can find
\gamma\in K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z_{1},\mathsf{w})
such that
\alpha-i_{*}\tilde{\beta}=\tilde{i}_{1*}\gamma
, and this implies that
\alpha=i_{*}(\tilde{\beta}+i_{1*}\gamma)
, in particular
\alpha\in\operatorname{im}(i_{*})
. This shows that
\ker(j^{*})=\operatorname{im}(i_{*})
.
∎

3.
Stable envelopes

In this section, we define stable envelopes on critical loci and prove their uniqueness in both cohomology and
K
-theory.
We show that they can be constructed from stable envelope correspondences
4
4

4

Introducing stable envelope correspondences is essential for constructing stable envelopes for critical loci, see Remark
3.27
.
in §
3.3
through a convolution construction §
3.5
. We provide existence results for stable envelope (correspondences), one for any critical loci and with respect to one dimensional torus
\mathsf{A}
(Proposition
3.23
), the other for critical loci of functions on symmetric GIT quotients without constrain on the dimension of the torus. The cohomological case is shown in
Theorem
3.25
and Section
4
, while the
K
-theoretic case makes use of nonabelian stable envelopes and Hall
operations, see §
3.6
, §
5
.
We prove the triangle lemma in §
3.9
.

In this section, we often further impose the following condition.

Setting 3.1
.

We assume that there exists a proper morphism
\pi\colon X\to X_{0}
to an affine variety
X_{0}
.

Remark 3.2
.

The assumption in Setting
3.1
is equivalent to that
\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})
is a finite generated
\mathbb{C}
-algebra and the natural morphism

X\to\operatorname{Spec}\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})
is proper. In particular, we can take
X_{0}=\operatorname{Spec}\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})
. If we further assume that there is an algebraic group
G
action on
X
, then
G
naturally acts on
\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})
linearly and
X\to\operatorname{Spec}\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})
is
G
-equivariant.

Remark 3.3
.

The assumption in Setting
3.1
implies that
\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F)
is a closed subset in
X
for all
F\in\operatorname{Fix}_{\mathsf{A}}(X)
by
[
MO
, Lem. 3.2.7]
, and
\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}
is proper over
X
along the projection map
X\times X^{\mathsf{A}}\to X
by
[
MO
, §3.5]
.

3.1.
Cohomological stable envelopes

In this section, we use the following shorthand to denote
\mathsf{T}
-equivariant critical cohomology of
(X,\mathsf{w})
and
(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}|_{X^{\mathsf{A}}})
:

H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w}):=H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\omega_{X}),\quad H^{\mathsf{T}}\left(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}}\right):=H^{\mathsf{T}}\left(X^{\mathsf{A}},\varphi_{\mathsf{w}|_{X^{\mathsf{A}}}}\omega_{X^{\mathsf{A}}}\right).

3.1.1.
Definitions

Definition 3.4
.

Fix
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A})
as in Setting
2.1
, a choice of a chamber
\mathfrak{C}
as Definition
2.2
. The
cohomological stable envelope
for
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C})
is a map of
H^{*}_{\mathsf{T}}(\operatorname{pt})
-modules:

\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})

such that for any
\gamma\in H^{\mathsf{T}/\mathsf{A}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})
(and we identify
\gamma
with
\gamma\otimes 1\in H^{\mathsf{T}/\mathsf{A}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\otimes\mathbb{Q}[\operatorname{Lie}(\mathsf{A})]\cong H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})
) supported on a connected component
F\subseteq X^{\mathsf{A}}
,
\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\gamma)
satisfies the following axioms:

(i)

\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\gamma)
is supported on
\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F)
;

(ii)

\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\gamma)|_{F}=e^{\mathsf{T}}(N_{F/X}^{-})\cdot\gamma
;

(iii)

For any
F^{\prime}\neq F
, the inequality
\deg_{\mathsf{A}}\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\gamma)\big|_{F^{\prime}}<\deg_{\mathsf{A}}e^{\mathsf{T}}(N_{F^{\prime}/X}^{-})
holds.

Here
(-)|_{F}
means the Gysin pullback
\left(F\hookrightarrow X\right)^{*}(-)
,

N_{F/X}^{\pm}
is the sub-bundle of
N_{F/X}
which are positive/negative with respect to the chamber
\mathfrak{C}
, and
\deg_{\mathsf{A}}
of a class in
H^{\mathsf{T}}(F^{\prime},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\cong H^{\mathsf{T}/\mathsf{A}}(F^{\prime},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\otimes\mathbb{Q}[\operatorname{Lie}(\mathsf{A})]
is the
\mathbb{Q}[\operatorname{Lie}(\mathsf{A})]
-polynomial degree, where we define the
\mathsf{A}
-degree of
0
to be
-1
.

Definition 3.5
.

Fix
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A})
as in Setting
2.1
, a cohomological
normalizer
is an element
\epsilon\in\{\pm 1\}^{\operatorname{Fix}_{\mathsf{A}}(X)}
, i.e. a sign
\epsilon_{F}\in\{\pm 1\}
for each fixed component
F
. If
\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}
is a cohomological stable envelope for
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C})
, then we define

\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C},\epsilon}:=\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}\cdot\>\epsilon\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w}),

i.e. for any
\gamma\in H^{\mathsf{T}}(F,\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})
,
\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C},\epsilon}(\gamma)=\epsilon_{F}\cdot\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\gamma)
.

3.1.2.
Uniqueness

Proposition 3.6
.

Let
\gamma\in H^{\mathsf{T}/\mathsf{A}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})
be supported on a connected component
F\subseteq X^{\mathsf{A}}
, then
\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\gamma)
which satisfies the axioms (i)–(iii) in Definition
3.4
is unique if it exists.

Proof.

Let
\beta\in H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})
be supported on
\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F)
and satisfies
\deg_{\mathsf{A}}\beta|_{F^{\prime}}<\deg_{\mathsf{A}}e^{\mathsf{T}}(N^{-}_{F^{\prime}/X})
for any embedding
i\colon F^{\prime}\hookrightarrow X
of a fixed component such that
F\preceq F^{\prime}
. We claim that
\beta=0
.

Pick a total ordering on the set of fixed component refining
\preceq
, and let
Z
be the minimal fixed component such that
\operatorname{Supp}\beta\subseteq\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}(Z)
and
\operatorname{Supp}\beta\nsubseteq\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}(Z)\setminus\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(Z)
. Factor
i\colon Z\hookrightarrow X
as the following composition

\displaystyle i\colon Z\overset{f_{1}}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(Z)\overset{f_{2}}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}(Z)\overset{f_{3}}{\hookrightarrow}X.

Here
f_{1}
is regular and
f_{2}
is open. By Proposition
2.4
,
\operatorname{Supp}\beta\subseteq\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}(Z)
implies that
\exists\,\alpha\in H^{\mathsf{T}}(\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}(Z),\mathsf{w}|_{\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}(Z)})
such that
\beta=f_{3,*}\alpha
. The maps
f_{2}
and
f_{3}
fit into the following Cartesian diagram

\textstyle{\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{2}}
\scriptstyle{g}
\scriptstyle{\Box}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}(Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{3}}
\textstyle{X\setminus(\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}(Z)\setminus\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(Z))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pth}
\textstyle{X,}

where
g
is a regular closed embedding and
h
is an open embedding. Therefore we have

\displaystyle i^{*}\beta=f_{1}^{*}g^{*}h^{*}f_{3*}\alpha=f_{1}^{*}g^{*}g_{*}f_{2}^{*}\alpha=f_{1}^{*}e^{\mathsf{T}}(N_{\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(Z)/X})\cdot f_{2}^{*}\alpha=e^{\mathsf{T}}(N^{-}_{Z/X})\cdot f_{1}^{*}f_{2}^{*}\alpha,

where the third equality uses (
2.11
) and the last one uses the definition of attracting set. The multiplication by
e^{\mathsf{T}}(N^{-}_{Z/X})
is injective on
H^{\mathsf{T}}(Z,\mathsf{w}|_{Z})
, so if
f_{1}^{*}f_{2}^{*}\alpha\neq 0
then

\deg_{\mathsf{A}}e^{\mathsf{T}}(N^{-}_{Z/X})\cdot f_{1}^{*}f_{2}^{*}\alpha\geqslant\deg_{\mathsf{A}}e^{\mathsf{T}}(N^{-}_{Z/X}),

which contradicts with the condition that
\deg_{\mathsf{A}}i^{*}\beta<\deg_{\mathsf{A}}e^{\mathsf{T}}(N^{-}_{Z/X})
; therefore
f_{1}^{*}f_{2}^{*}\alpha=0
. We note that

\mathsf{w}|_{\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(Z)}=\pi^{*}(\mathsf{w}|_{Z}),

where
\pi:\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(Z)\to Z
is the projection map which is an affine fibration.
By (
2.12
), the pullback

\pi^{*}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(Z,\mathsf{w}|_{Z})\to H^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(Z),\mathsf{w}|_{\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(Z)}\right)

is an isomorphism, which implies that
f_{1}^{*}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(Z),\mathsf{w}|_{\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(Z)}\right)\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(Z,\mathsf{w}|_{Z})
is an isomorphism because
\pi\circ f_{1}=\operatorname{Id}
. This forces
f_{2}^{*}\alpha=0
, i.e.
\operatorname{Supp}\alpha\subseteq\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}(Z)\setminus\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(Z)
, contradicting with the choice of
Z
; hence
\beta=0
.

Now if
\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\in H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})
are two classes that satisfy (i)–(iii) in Definition
3.4
, then
\Gamma_{1}-\Gamma_{2}
satisfies the hypothesis above; hence
\Gamma_{1}=\Gamma_{2}
.
∎

Remark 3.7
.

Let
\leq
be a partial order on
\operatorname{Fix}_{\mathsf{A}}(X)
that refines
\preceq
in (
2.1
), and define

\displaystyle\operatorname{Attr}^{\leq}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F):=\bigcup_{F\leq F^{\prime}}\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F^{\prime}).

Suppose that
\operatorname{Stab}^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\gamma)\in H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})
is a class which satisfies the axioms (ii) and (iii) in Definition
3.4
and the following modifications of (i):

(i’)

\operatorname{Stab}^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\gamma)
is supported on
\operatorname{Attr}^{\leq}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F_{i})
.

Suppose that
\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}
exists, then
\operatorname{Stab}^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\gamma)=\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\gamma)
. This is because the same argument in the proof of Proposition
3.6
implies that the cohomology class that satisfies (i’) as above and (ii) and (iii) as in the Definition
3.4
is unique.

Lemma 3.8
.

Let
\leq
be a partial order on
\operatorname{Fix}_{\mathsf{A}}(X)
that refines
\preceq
in (
2.1
), and
Z\subseteq X
be an
\mathsf{A}
-invariant closed subvariety. Then

(3.1)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Attr}^{\leq}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F)_{X}\cap Z=\bigcup_{F\leq F^{\prime}}\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}\left(F^{\prime}\cap Z\right)_{Z},

where the subscripts
X
and
Z
mean taking attracting sets in
X
and in
Z
respectively.

Proof.

Let us choose a
\sigma\in\mathfrak{C}
, then

\displaystyle\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F)_{X}\cap Z=\left\{x\in Z\>|\>\lim_{t\to 0}\sigma(t)\cdot x\in F\cap Z\right\}=\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F\cap Z)_{Z}.

It follows that

\operatorname{Attr}^{\leq}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F)_{X}\cap Z=\left(\bigcup_{F\leq F^{\prime}}\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F^{\prime})_{X}\right)\cap Z=\bigcup_{F\leq F^{\prime}}\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F^{\prime}\cap Z)_{Z}.\qed

Remark 3.9

(The ample partial order)
.

A useful refinement of
\preceq
is induced by an ample
\mathsf{A}
-equivariant line bundle
\mathcal{L}
on
X

[
MO
, §3.2.4]
. Namely we define

(3.2)

\displaystyle F<F^{\prime}\Longleftrightarrow\left(\mathrm{weight}\>\mathcal{L}|_{F}-\mathrm{weight}\>\mathcal{L}|_{F^{\prime}}\right)\big|_{\mathfrak{C}}>0.

The ample partial order has the nice feature that it is compatible with the restriction to closed subvarieties, that is, for an
\mathsf{A}
-invariant closed subvariety
Z
, we have

\displaystyle F<F^{\prime}\Longrightarrow(F\cap Z)<(F^{\prime}\cap Z),

in particular,

\displaystyle\operatorname{Attr}^{\leq}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F)_{X}\cap Z=\operatorname{Attr}^{\leq}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F\cap Z)_{Z}.

3.2.
K
-theoretic stable envelopes

3.2.1.
Definition

Definition 3.10
.

Fix
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A})
as in Setting
2.1
, a choice of chamber
\mathfrak{C}
, and an
\mathsf{s}\in\operatorname{Pic}_{\mathsf{A}}(X)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{R}
which will be called a
slope
. The
K-theoretic stable envelope
for
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C},\mathsf{s})
is a map of
K_{\mathsf{T}}(\operatorname{pt})
-modules:

\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})

such that for any
\gamma\in K^{\mathsf{T}/\mathsf{A}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})
(and we identify
\gamma
with
\gamma\otimes 1\in K^{\mathsf{T}/\mathsf{A}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\otimes\mathbb{Q}[\mathsf{A}]\cong K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})
) supported on a connected component
F\in\operatorname{Fix}_{\mathsf{A}}(X)
,
\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\gamma)
satisfies the following axioms:

(i)

\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\gamma)
is supported on
\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F)
;

(ii)

\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\gamma)|_{F}=e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}(N_{F/X}^{-})\cdot\gamma
;

(iii)

For any
F^{\prime}\neq F
, there is a strict inclusion of polytopes:

\deg_{\mathsf{A}}\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\gamma)\big|_{F^{\prime}}\subsetneq\deg_{\mathsf{A}}e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}(N_{F^{\prime}/X}^{-})+\mathrm{shift}_{F^{\prime}}-\mathrm{shift}_{F},

where

\mathrm{shift}_{F}:=\mathrm{weight}_{\mathsf{A}}\left(\det(N_{F/X}^{-})^{1/2}\otimes\mathsf{s}|_{F}\right),

\deg_{\mathsf{A}}
of a Laurent polynomial is the Newton polytope:

\deg_{\mathsf{A}}\sum_{\mu}f_{\mu}z^{\mu}:=\text{Convex hull}\left(\{\mu:f_{\mu}\neq 0\}\right)\subseteq\mathrm{Char}(\mathsf{A})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{R},

and we define the
\mathsf{A}
-degree of
0
to be the empty set.

Definition 3.11
.

We say a slope
\mathsf{s}\in\operatorname{Pic}_{\mathsf{A}}(X)\otimes\mathbb{R}
is generic if for any
F\prec F^{\prime}
, the
\mathsf{A}
-weight difference

\displaystyle\mathrm{weight}_{\mathsf{A}}\left(\det(N_{F^{\prime}/X}^{-})^{1/2}\otimes\mathsf{s}|_{F^{\prime}}\right)-\mathrm{weight}_{\mathsf{A}}\left(\det(N_{F/X}^{-})^{1/2}\otimes\mathsf{s}|_{F}\right)

is nonintegral.

Remark 3.12
.

Suppose that the slope
\mathsf{s}
is generic, then any inclusion

\deg_{\mathsf{A}}\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\gamma)\big|_{F^{\prime}}\subseteq\deg_{\mathsf{A}}e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}(N_{F^{\prime}/X}^{-})+\mathrm{shift}_{F^{\prime}}-\mathrm{shift}_{F}

must be strict.

Remark 3.13
.

Since
X
is smooth and
\operatorname{Pic}(\mathsf{A})=0
, every line bundle on
X
admits a (not necessarily unique)
\mathsf{A}
-equivariant structure by
[
CG
, Thm. 5.1.9]

5
5

5

The positive integer
n
in
[
CG
, Thm. 5.1.9]
is determined by
[
CG
, Prop. 5.1.17]
which can be taken to be
1
for the case
G=\mathsf{A}
since
\operatorname{Pic}(\mathsf{A})=0
.
. Assume
\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}^{*})=\mathbb{C}^{*}
, then the set of
\mathsf{A}
-equivariant structures on a line bundle
\mathcal{L}\in\operatorname{Pic}(X)
form a
\mathrm{Char}(\mathsf{A})
-torsor, in this case we have a fiber sequence:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Char}(\mathsf{A})\to\operatorname{Pic}_{\mathsf{A}}(X)\to\operatorname{Pic}(X).

Note that for any
\chi\in\mathrm{Char}(\mathsf{A})
,
\mathsf{s}
and
\mathsf{s}\otimes\chi
induce the same shifts in Definition
3.10
(iii), so we can take
\mathsf{s}\in\operatorname{Pic}(X)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{R}
if
\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}^{*})=\mathbb{C}^{*}
.

Definition 3.14
.

Fix
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A})
as in Setting
2.1
, a
K
-theoretic
normalizer
is an element
\mathcal{E}\in\pm\operatorname{Pic}_{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}})
, i.e. a
K
-theory class
\mathcal{E}\in K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}})
such that for every
F\in\operatorname{Fix}_{\mathsf{A}}(X)
, either
\mathcal{E}|_{F}
or
-\mathcal{E}|_{F}
is a line bundle. If
\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}
is a
K
-theoretic stable envelope for
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C},\mathsf{s})
, then we define

\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C},\mathcal{E}}:=\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}\cdot\>\mathcal{E}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w}),

i.e. for any
\gamma\in K^{\mathsf{T}}(F,\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})
,
\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C},\mathcal{E}}(\gamma)=\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\mathcal{E}|_{F}\cdot\gamma)
.

Remark 3.15
.

The normalization condition and degree condition in Definition
3.10
is slightly different from the ordinary definition of
K
-theoretic stable envelope in the case
\mathsf{w}=0
assuming the existence of polarization
T^{1/2}_{X}

[
Oko1
, §9.1]
. In fact, the original definition can be restored by the following.

\displaystyle\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}\text{ in \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Oko}{}{}, \lx@sectionsign 9.1]}}=\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{\mathfrak{C},\mathcal{E}}\text{ in this paper, where }

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}=(-1)^{\operatorname{rk}N^{1/2,+}_{F/X}}\left(\frac{\det N^{-}_{F/X}}{\det N^{1/2}_{F/X}}\right)^{1/2},\text{ and }\mathsf{s}^{\prime}=\mathsf{s}\otimes\left(\det T^{1/2}_{X}\right)^{-1/2}.

3.2.2.
Uniqueness

Proposition 3.16
.

Let
\mathsf{s}\in\operatorname{Pic}_{\mathsf{A}}(X)\otimes\mathbb{R}
be a generic slope and
\gamma\in K^{\mathsf{T}/\mathsf{A}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})
be supported on a connected component
F\subseteq X^{\mathsf{A}}
, then
\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\gamma)
which satisfies the axioms (i)–(iii) in Definition
3.10
is unique if it exists.

Proof.

The argument is similar to the proof of Proposition
3.6
, and we omit the details.
∎

Remark 3.17
.

Let
\leq
be a partial order on
\operatorname{Fix}_{\mathsf{A}}(X)
that refines
\preceq
, and define
\operatorname{Attr}^{\leq}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F)
as in the Remark
3.7
. Suppose that
\operatorname{Stab}^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\gamma)\in K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})
is a class which satisfies the axioms (ii) and (iii) in Definition
3.4
and the following modifications of (i):

(i’)

\operatorname{Stab}^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\gamma)
is supported on
\operatorname{Attr}^{\leq}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F_{i})
.

Suppose that
\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}
exists, then
\operatorname{Stab}^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\gamma)=\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\gamma)
. This is because the same argument in the proof of Proposition
3.16
implies that the class that satisfies (i’) as above and (ii) and (iii) as in the Definition
3.10
is unique.

Remark 3.18
.

Let
\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}
be a
K
-theoretic stable envelope for
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C},\mathsf{s})
as in the Definition
3.10
. Let
\mathcal{L}
be a
\mathsf{T}
-equivariant line bundle on
X
, and denote
\mathcal{L}^{\mathsf{A}}:=\mathcal{L}|_{X^{\mathsf{A}}}
, then we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\circ\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ(\mathcal{L}^{\mathsf{A}})^{-1}=\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}\otimes\mathcal{L}}_{\mathfrak{C}}.

3.3.
Stable envelope correspondences

Stable envelopes defined in the previous section are most effectively constructed through convolutions, which requires
stable envelope correspondences introduced in this section.

3.3.1.
Definitions and uniqueness

Definition 3.19
.

Let
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A})
be in the Setting
2.1
, and fix a chamber
\mathfrak{C}
. A
cohomological stable envelope correspondence
for
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C})
is a critical cohomology class with support (
2.7
) on
\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}
:

[\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}]\in H^{\mathsf{T}}(X\times X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}\boxminus\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})_{\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}}

which satisfies the following two axioms:

(i)

For any fixed component
F\in\operatorname{Fix}_{\mathsf{A}}(X)
,
[\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}]|_{F\times F}=e^{\mathsf{T}}(N_{F/X}^{-})\cdot[\Delta_{F}]
for diagonal

\Delta_{F}\subseteq F\times F
;

(ii)

For any
F^{\prime}\neq F
, the inequality
\deg_{\mathsf{A}}[\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}]\big|_{F^{\prime}\times F}<\deg_{\mathsf{A}}e^{\mathsf{T}}(N_{F^{\prime}/X}^{-})
holds.

Here

[\Delta_{F}]\in H^{\mathsf{T}}(F\times F,\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}}\boxminus\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})_{\Delta_{F}}

is the image of
[\Delta_{F}]\in H^{\mathsf{T}}(\Delta_{F})
under the canonical map (
2.8
). Note also that
\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}\cap\,(F\times F)=\Delta_{F}
.

Definition 3.20
.

Let
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A})
be in the Setting
2.1
, and fix a chamber
\mathfrak{C}
and an
\mathsf{s}\in\operatorname{Pic}_{\mathsf{A}}(X)
. A
K
-theoretic stable envelope correspondence
for
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C},\mathsf{s})
is a class

[\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}]\in K^{\mathsf{T}}(X\times X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}\boxminus\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})_{\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}}

which satisfies the following two axioms:

(i)

For any fixed component
F\in\operatorname{Fix}_{\mathsf{A}}(X)
,
[\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}]\big|_{F\times F}=e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}(N_{F/X}^{-})\cdot[\Delta_{F}]
;

(ii)

For any
F^{\prime}\neq F
, there is a strict inclusion of polytopes
\deg_{\mathsf{A}}[\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}]\big|_{F^{\prime}\times F}\subsetneq\deg_{\mathsf{A}}e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}(N_{F^{\prime}/X}^{-})+\mathrm{shift}_{F^{\prime}}-\mathrm{shift}_{F}
.

Here

[\Delta_{F}]\in K^{\mathsf{T}}(F\times F,\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}}\boxminus\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})_{\Delta_{F}}

is the image of
[\mathcal{O}_{\Delta_{F}}]\in K^{\mathsf{T}}(\Delta_{F})
under the canonical map defined in (
2.17
).

Proposition 3.21
.

Suppose that
\mathsf{w}=0
, then the class
[\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}]
(resp.
[\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}]
) in Definition
3.19
(resp. Definition
3.20
) is unique if exists.

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of Proposition
3.6
and Proposition
3.16
respectively, and we omit it.
∎

3.3.2.
Existence

We notice that
\mathsf{w}\boxminus\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}}
vanishes on
\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}
for arbitrary
\mathsf{A}
-invariant function
\mathsf{w}
. So the canonical maps (
2.8
), (
2.18
) in this setting are:

\displaystyle\operatorname{can}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(X\times X^{\mathsf{A}})_{\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}}\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(X\times X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}\boxminus\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})_{\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}},\quad\operatorname{can}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(X\times X^{\mathsf{A}})_{\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}}\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(X\times X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}\boxminus\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})_{\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}}.

Here by definition (
2.7
),
H^{\mathsf{T}}(X\times X^{\mathsf{A}})_{\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}}=H^{\mathsf{T}}(\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}})
, and

K^{\mathsf{T}}(X\times X^{\mathsf{A}})_{\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}}\cong K^{\mathsf{T}}(\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}})
by dévissage.

Lemma 3.22
.

Let
[\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}]
be a cohomological stable envelope correspondence for
(X,0,\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C})
, then the image
\operatorname{can}([\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}])
under canonical map is a cohomological stable envelope correspondence for
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C})
.

Similarly, let
[\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}]
be a
K
-theoretic stable envelope correspondence for
(X,0,\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C},\mathsf{s})
, then
\operatorname{can}([\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}])
is a
K
-theoretic stable envelope correspondence for
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C},\mathsf{s})
.

Proof.

The axioms (i) and (ii) in Definition
3.19
for nontrivial potential
\mathsf{w}
follows from the corresponding axioms for trivial potential
\mathsf{w}=0
because canonical map commutes with Gysin pullback and preserves
\deg_{\mathsf{A}}
. Namely, we have

\displaystyle\left(\operatorname{can}([\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}])\right)|_{F\times F}=\operatorname{can}\left([\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}]|_{F\times F}\right)=\operatorname{can}\left(e^{\mathsf{T}}(N_{F/X}^{-})\cdot[\Delta_{F}]\right)=e^{\mathsf{T}}(N_{F/X}^{-})\cdot[\Delta_{F}],

and

\displaystyle\deg_{\mathsf{A}}\left(\operatorname{can}([\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}])\right)|_{F^{\prime}\times F}=\deg_{\mathsf{A}}\operatorname{can}\left([\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}]|_{F^{\prime}\times F}\right)<\deg_{\mathsf{A}}e^{\mathsf{T}}(N_{F^{\prime}/X}^{-}).

The
K
-theoretic counterpart is proven similarly.
∎

In the case when
\dim\mathsf{A}=1
, there are two choices of chambers
\{+,-\}
. In this case, the stable envelope correspondence always exists, by the next proposition
(as motivated by
[
Oko1
, §9.2.3]
).

Proposition 3.23
.

Let
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A})
be in the Setting
2.1
and suppose that
\mathsf{A}\cong\mathbb{C}^{*}
.

•

Then there exists a cohomological stable envelope correspondence
[\operatorname{Stab}_{+}]
for
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathbb{C}^{*},+)
.

•

If we fix
\mathsf{s}\in\operatorname{Pic}_{\mathsf{A}}(X)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{R}
, then there exists a
K
-theoretic stable envelope correspondence
[\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{+}]
for
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathbb{C}^{*},+,\mathsf{s})
.

Proof.

We first prove the cohomology version. According to Lemma
3.22
, it suffices to construct
[\operatorname{Stab}_{+}]
for
(X,0,\mathsf{T},\mathbb{C}^{*},+)
.

\operatorname{Attr}^{f}:=\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{+}
admits a filtration
6
6

6

To prove the existence of such filtration, let us define
Z_{i}
inductively by setting
Z_{1}=\operatorname{Attr}(\Delta)
and

Z_{i}:=Z_{i-1}\cup\operatorname{Attr}(\overline{Z}_{i-1}\setminus Z_{i-1})
.
Note that
\overline{Z}_{i-1}\setminus Z_{i-1}
are unions of torus fixed locus, which are distinct for different
i
. As the number of torus fixed components is finite,
the induction process terminates at some
Z_{n}
(and
Z_{n}
is closed in
X\times X^{\mathsf{A}}
). Then
Z_{n}
contains
\operatorname{Attr}^{f}
by the definition of
\operatorname{Attr}^{f}
.
\operatorname{Attr}^{f}
contains
Z_{1}
by definition; thus it contains
\overline{Z}_{1}
since it is closed, then it follows that
\operatorname{Attr}^{f}
contains
Z_{2}
, and by induction
\operatorname{Attr}^{f}
contains
Z_{n}
. Thus
\operatorname{Attr}^{f}=Z_{n}
.

\emptyset=Z_{0}\subseteq Z_{1}\subseteq Z_{2}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq Z_{n}=\operatorname{Attr}^{f}

by open subvarieties
Z_{i}
, such that
Z_{1}=\operatorname{Attr}(\Delta)
where
\Delta\subseteq X^{\sigma}\times X^{\sigma}
is the diagonal, and

Z_{i}=Z_{i-1}\cup\operatorname{Attr}(\overline{Z}_{i-1}\setminus Z_{i-1}),

where
\operatorname{Attr}(-)
is the attracting set in
X\times X^{\sigma}
.
We write

\displaystyle\overline{Z}_{i-1}\setminus Z_{i-1}=\bigcup_{F_{j}\in\operatorname{Fix}_{\sigma}(X)}(G_{j_{i}}\times F_{j})\cap\operatorname{Attr}^{f},

where
G_{j_{i}}
is a union of torus fixed components, and
\bigcup_{F_{j}\in\operatorname{Fix}_{\sigma}(X)}(G_{j_{i}}\times F_{j})
is the union of
F^{\prime}\times F
for all
F^{\prime}\succ F
such that
(F^{\prime}\times F)\cap(\overline{Z}_{i-1}\setminus Z_{i-1})\neq\emptyset
.
Then by construction

Z_{i}\setminus Z_{i-1}=\bigcup_{F_{j}\in\operatorname{Fix}_{\sigma}(X)}\operatorname{Attr}\left((G_{j_{i}}\times F_{j})\cap\operatorname{Attr}^{f}\right).

We claim that there exists
[\operatorname{Stab}]_{i}
in equivariant Chow homology
A^{\mathsf{T}}(Z_{i})
which satisfies the following two axioms:

(i)

For any fixed component
F\in\operatorname{Fix}_{\mathsf{A}}(X)
,
[\operatorname{Stab}]_{i}\big|_{F\times F}=e^{\mathsf{T}}(N_{F/X}^{-})\cdot[\Delta_{F}]
;

(ii)

For any
F^{\prime}\succ F
such that
(F^{\prime}\times F)\cap Z_{i}\neq\emptyset
,
\deg_{\sigma}[\operatorname{Stab}]_{i}\big|_{F^{\prime}\times F}<\deg_{\sigma}e^{\mathsf{T}}(N_{F^{\prime}/X}^{-})
.

Then
[\operatorname{Stab}_{+}]
is the image of
[\operatorname{Stab}]_{n}
under the cycle map

A^{\mathsf{T}}(\operatorname{Attr}^{f})\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(\operatorname{Attr}^{f})

sending algebraic cycle to Borel-Moore homology class.
Define

[\operatorname{Stab}]_{1}:=\sum_{F\in\operatorname{Fix}_{\sigma}(X)}[\operatorname{Attr}(F\times F)],

then
[\operatorname{Stab}]_{1}
obviously satisfies axiom (i). For
i\geqslant 2
and assume that we have constructed
[\operatorname{Stab}]_{i-1}\in A^{\mathsf{T}}(Z_{i-1})
, then we use the surjective map
A^{\mathsf{T}}(Z_{i})\twoheadrightarrow A^{\mathsf{T}}(Z_{i-1})
to find a preimage of
[\operatorname{Stab}]_{i-1}
in
A^{\mathsf{T}}(Z_{i})
, denoted by
[\operatorname{Stab}]^{\sim}_{i-1}
. Write
Z_{i}\setminus Z_{i-1}=\operatorname{Attr}(M_{i}\cap\operatorname{Attr}^{f})
,
where

M_{i}=\bigcup_{F_{j}\in\operatorname{Fix}_{\sigma}(X)}G_{j_{i}}\times F_{j},

then define

\beta:=[\operatorname{Stab}]^{\sim}_{i-1}\big|_{M_{i}}\in A^{\mathsf{T}}\left(M_{i}\cap\operatorname{Attr}^{f}\right).

Let
\alpha
be the unique class in
A^{\mathsf{T}}\left(M_{i}\cap\operatorname{Attr}^{f}\right)
such that

\deg_{\sigma}\left(\beta-e^{\mathsf{T}}(N_{X^{\sigma}/X}^{-})\cdot\alpha\right)<\deg_{\sigma}e^{\mathsf{T}}(N_{X^{\sigma}/X}^{-}).

Here the existence uses the fact that
\mathsf{A}
is one dimensional, so one can use Euclidean algorithm to find
\alpha
.
The uniqueness follows from the fact that given another
\alpha^{\prime}
satisfying above inequality, then the difference of the LHS also
has degree less than the RHS, i.e.

\deg_{\sigma}\left((\alpha^{\prime}-\alpha)\cdot e^{\mathsf{T}}(N_{X^{\sigma}/X}^{-})\right)<\deg_{\sigma}e^{\mathsf{T}}(N_{X^{\sigma}/X}^{-}),

which implies that
\alpha^{\prime}=\alpha
.

Let
p_{i}\colon Z_{i}\setminus Z_{i-1}=\operatorname{Attr}(M_{i}\cap\operatorname{Attr}^{f})\to M_{i}\cap\operatorname{Attr}^{f}
be the projection,
j_{i}\colon Z_{i}\setminus Z_{i-1}\hookrightarrow Z_{i}
be the closed embedding, and define

[\operatorname{Stab}]_{i}:=[\operatorname{Stab}]^{\sim}_{i-1}-j_{i*}p_{i}^{*}(\alpha).

Then
[\operatorname{Stab}]_{i}
satisfies axiom (i) since
F\times F\subseteq Z_{i-1}
.
Note that we have

\operatorname{Attr}(M_{i}\cap\operatorname{Attr}^{f})=\operatorname{Attr}(M_{i}\cap Z_{i})=\operatorname{Attr}(M_{i})\cap Z_{i},

and following Cartesian diagrams

\textstyle{M_{i}\cap Z_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Box}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Attr}(M_{i})\cap Z_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447ptj_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\Box}
\textstyle{Z_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Attr}(M_{i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X\times X^{\sigma},}

therefore

\displaystyle(M_{i}\hookrightarrow X\times X^{\sigma})^{*}j_{i*}p_{i}^{*}(\alpha)

\displaystyle=(M_{i}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Attr}(M_{i}))^{*}(\operatorname{Attr}(M_{i})\hookrightarrow X\times X^{\sigma})^{*}j_{i*}p_{i}^{*}(\alpha)

\displaystyle=e^{\mathsf{T}}(N_{X^{\sigma}/X}^{-})\cdot(M_{i}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Attr}(M_{i}))^{*}p_{i}^{*}(\alpha)

\displaystyle=e^{\mathsf{T}}(N_{X^{\sigma}/X}^{-})\cdot\alpha,

thus

\displaystyle\deg_{\sigma}[\operatorname{Stab}]_{i}\big|_{M_{i}}=\deg_{\sigma}\left(\beta-e^{\mathsf{T}}(N_{X^{\sigma}/X}^{-})\cdot\alpha\right)<\deg_{\sigma}e^{\mathsf{T}}(N_{X^{\sigma}/X}^{-}),

i.e.
[\operatorname{Stab}]_{i}
satisfies axiom (ii).
This proves the cohomology version.

For the
K
-theory version, we claim that there exists
[\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}]_{i}
in equivariant
K
-theory
K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z_{i})
which satisfies the following two axioms:

(i’)

For any fixed component
F\in\operatorname{Fix}_{\mathsf{A}}(X)
,
[\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}]_{i}|_{F\times F}=e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}(N_{F/X}^{-})\cdot[\Delta_{F}]
;

(ii’)

For any
F^{\prime}\succ F
such that
(F^{\prime}\times F)\cap Z_{i}\neq\emptyset
,
\deg_{\sigma}[\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}]_{i}\big|_{F^{\prime}\times F}\subseteq\deg_{\sigma}e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}(N_{F^{\prime}/X}^{-})+\mathrm{shift}_{F^{\prime}}-\mathrm{shift}_{F}
.

Then
[\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{+}]
is
[\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}]_{n}
. Define

[\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}]_{1}:=[\mathcal{O}_{Z_{1}}],

then
[\operatorname{Stab}]_{1}
obviously satisfies axiom (i). For
i\geqslant 2
and assume that we have constructed
[\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}]_{i-1}\in K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z_{i-1})
, then we use the surjective map
K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z_{i})\twoheadrightarrow K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z_{i-1})
to find a preimage of
[\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}]_{i-1}
in
K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z_{i})
, denoted by
[\operatorname{Stab}]^{\sim}_{i-1}
. Similar to the cohomological counterpart, we define

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\alpha,\beta\in K^{\mathsf{T}}\left(M_{i}\cap\operatorname{Attr}^{f}\right),\text{ such that }\beta:=[\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}]^{\sim}_{i-1}\big|_{M_{i}},\text{ and }

\displaystyle\deg_{\sigma}\left((\beta-e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}(N_{X^{\sigma}/X}^{-})\cdot\alpha)|_{F^{\prime}\times F}\right)\subsetneq\deg_{\sigma}e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}(N_{F^{\prime}/X}^{-})+\mathrm{shift}_{F^{\prime}}-\mathrm{shift}_{F}.

Define

[\operatorname{Stab}]_{i}:=[\operatorname{Stab}]^{\sim}_{i-1}-j_{i*}p_{i}^{*}(\alpha).

Then
[\operatorname{Stab}]_{i}
satisfies axiom (i) since
F\times F\subseteq Z_{i-1}
;
[\operatorname{Stab}]_{i}
satisfies axiom (ii) as

\displaystyle(M_{i}\hookrightarrow X\times X^{\sigma})^{*}j_{i*}p_{i}^{*}(\alpha)

\displaystyle=(M_{i}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Attr}(M_{i}))^{*}(\operatorname{Attr}(M_{i})\hookrightarrow X\times X^{\sigma})^{*}j_{i*}p_{i}^{*}(\alpha)

\displaystyle=e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}(N_{X^{\sigma}/X}^{-})\cdot(M_{i}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Attr}(M_{i}))^{*}p_{i}^{*}(\alpha)

\displaystyle=e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}(N_{X^{\sigma}/X}^{-})\cdot\alpha,

thus

\displaystyle\deg_{\sigma}[\operatorname{Stab}]_{i}\big|_{F^{\prime}\times F}=\deg_{\sigma}\left((\beta-e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}(N_{X^{\sigma}/X}^{-})\cdot\alpha)|_{F^{\prime}\times F}\right)\subsetneq\deg_{\sigma}e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}(N_{F^{\prime}/X}^{-})+\mathrm{shift}_{F^{\prime}}-\mathrm{shift}_{F}.

This proves the
K
-theory version.
∎

When ambient spaces are symplectic, we have the following existence result.

Theorem 3.24

(
[
MO
, Prop. 3.5.1]
)
.

Suppose that
X
is a smooth symplectic variety with symplectic form
\Omega
such that
\mathsf{A}
fixes
\Omega
and
\mathsf{T}
scales
\Omega
. Fix a chamber
\mathfrak{C}
,
and a
\mathsf{T}
-invariant function
\mathsf{w}\colon X\to\mathbb{C}
.
Then cohomological stable envelope correspondence exists.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.22
, we are left construct cohomological stable envelope correspondence for
(X,0,\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C})
, which
is done in
[
MO
, Prop. 3.5.1]
.
∎

When ambient spaces are
symmetric GIT quotients
, we have the following existence result.

Theorem 3.25
.

Suppose that
X
is a symmetric GIT quotient with an action of tori
\mathsf{A}\subseteq\mathsf{T}
as Definition
4.1
. Fix a chamber
\mathfrak{C}
,
and a
\mathsf{T}
-invariant function
\mathsf{w}\colon X\to\mathbb{C}
.
Then cohomological stable envelope correspondence exists.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.22
, we are left to construct cohomological stable envelope correspondence for
(X,0,\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C})
, which
is constructed in Theorem
4.3
.
∎

Remark 3.26
.

Things might go wrong without the self-duality assumption on the
\mathsf{A}
-action. For example, take
X=\mathrm{Tot}\left(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-1)^{\oplus 2}\right)
with zero potential and let
\mathsf{T}=\mathsf{A}=\mathbb{C}^{*}_{a}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{b}
act on
X
, where
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{a}
is the natural torus symmetry on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
and extends to act on
X
by setting one of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-1)
to have zero weight at
0
and another one to have zero weight at
\infty
, and
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{b}
acts on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
trivially and scales the fibers of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-1)^{\oplus 2}
with weight
1
. Then cohomological stable envelope does not exist in the chamber
\{0<b<a\}
.
We refer to Proposition
9.26
for a
K
-theoretic example.

Remark 3.27
.

Elliptic stable envelopes have been constructed for symmetric quiver varieties with zero potential in
[
IMZ
]
, and it is shown in
loc.

cit.
that
K
-theoretic and cohomological limits of elliptic stable envelopes give rise to classes
[\mathsf{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}]\in K^{\mathsf{T}}(X\times X^{\mathsf{A}})
and
[\mathsf{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}]\in H^{\mathsf{T}}(X\times X^{\mathsf{A}})
that satisfy the axioms (i) (ii) in Definition
3.20
and
3.19
respectively. Moreover one can show that
[\mathsf{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}]
and
[\mathsf{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}]
are supported on
\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}
, in particular they can be lifted to classes in
K^{\mathsf{T}}(\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}})
and
H^{\mathsf{T}}(\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}})
respectively.

However, the lifts are
not necessarily unique
because neither
K^{\mathsf{T}}(\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}})\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(X\times X^{\mathsf{A}})
nor
H^{\mathsf{T}}(\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}})\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(X\times X^{\mathsf{A}})
is injective in general. It is not clear whether we can choose a lift to make it a stable envelope correspondence for the zero potential in the sense of Definition
3.20
or
3.19
respectively. Therefore introducing stable envelope correspondences is necessary.

3.4.
Transpose of stable envelope correspondences

3.4.1.
Convolutions

Let
(X_{i},\mathsf{w}_{i})
(
i=1,2,3
) be as in Setting
2.1
and
p_{ij}\colon X_{1}\times X_{2}\times X_{3}\to X_{i}\times X_{j}
the projection.
Let
Z_{12}\subseteq X_{1}\times X_{2}
,
Z_{23}\subseteq X_{2}\times X_{3}
be
\mathsf{T}
-invariant closed subschemes such that

p_{13}\colon p_{12}^{-1}(Z_{12})\cap p_{23}^{-1}(Z_{23})\to X_{1}\times X_{3}

is a proper map and denote the image by

Z_{12}\circ Z_{23}:=p_{13}(p_{12}^{-1}(Z_{12})\cap p_{23}^{-1}(Z_{23})).

We define the
convolution
on critical cohomology (e.g.
[
CG
, pp. 112]
,
[
VV1
, §2.4]
):

(3.3)

\circ\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(X_{1}\times X_{2},\mathsf{w}_{1}\boxminus\mathsf{w}_{2})_{Z_{12}}\otimes H^{\mathsf{T}}(X_{2}\times X_{3},\mathsf{w}_{2}\boxminus\mathsf{w}_{3})_{Z_{23}}\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(X_{1}\times X_{3},\mathsf{w}_{1}\boxminus\mathsf{w}_{3})_{Z_{12}\circ Z_{23}},

(\beta,\alpha)\mapsto\beta\circ\alpha:=p_{13*}(p_{12}^{*}\beta\otimes p_{23}^{*}\alpha).

Here the tensor product
\otimes
is defined generally as follows:
for a smooth
Y
, closed subschemes
Z,Z^{\prime}\subseteq Y
and

a\in H^{\mathsf{T}}(Y,\mathsf{w})_{Z},\quad b\in H^{\mathsf{T}}(Y,\mathsf{w}^{\prime})_{Z^{\prime}},

the Thom-Sebastiani isomorphism implies

a\boxtimes b\in H^{\mathsf{T}}(Y\times Y,\mathsf{w}\boxplus\mathsf{w}^{\prime})_{Z\times Z^{\prime}},

then the diagonal pullback gives

a\otimes b:=\Delta^{*}(a\boxtimes b)\in H^{\mathsf{T}}(Y,\mathsf{w}+\mathsf{w}^{\prime})_{Z\cap Z^{\prime}}.

Similarly we define the
convolution
on critical
K
-theory (e.g.
[
CG
, pp. 251]
,
[
VV1
, §2.3.2]
):

(3.4)

\circ\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(X_{1}\times X_{2},\mathsf{w}_{1}\boxminus\mathsf{w}_{2})_{Z_{12}}\otimes K^{\mathsf{T}}(X_{2}\times X_{3},\mathsf{w}_{2}\boxminus\mathsf{w}_{3})_{Z_{23}}\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(X_{1}\times X_{3},\mathsf{w}_{1}\boxminus\mathsf{w}_{3})_{Z_{12}\circ Z_{23}},

(\beta,\alpha)\mapsto\beta\circ\alpha:=p_{13*}(p_{12}^{*}\beta\otimes p_{23}^{*}\alpha).

Here the derived tensor product
\otimes
is defined generally as follows: for a smooth
Y
, closed subschemes
Z,Z^{\prime}\subseteq Y
and

a\in K^{\mathsf{T}}(Y,\mathsf{w})_{Z},\quad b\in K^{\mathsf{T}}(Y,\mathsf{w}^{\prime})_{Z^{\prime}},

derived (exterior) tensor product gives

a\boxtimes b\in K^{\mathsf{T}}(Y\times Y,\mathsf{w}\boxplus\mathsf{w}^{\prime})_{Z\times Z^{\prime}},

then the diagonal pullback gives

a\otimes b:=\Delta^{*}(a\boxtimes b)\in K^{\mathsf{T}}(Y,\mathsf{w}+\mathsf{w}^{\prime})_{Z\cap Z^{\prime}}.

Remark 3.28
.

For the convolution on critical cohomology (
3.3
) to be well-defined, it is enough to assume that
p_{12}^{-1}(Z_{12})\cap p_{23}^{-1}(Z_{23})\cap\left(\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}_{1})\times\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}_{2})\times\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}_{3})\right)
is proper over
X_{1}\times X_{3}
. This is because the vanishing cycle sheaf
\varphi_{\mathsf{w}_{i}}\omega_{X_{i}}
is supported on
\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}_{i})
,
i\in\{1,2,3\}
.

3.4.2.
Transpose correspondences

Given a class
[\alpha]\in H^{\mathsf{T}}(X\times Y,\mathsf{w}_{1}\boxminus\mathsf{w}_{2})_{Z}
, we define its
transpose

(3.5)

[\alpha]^{\mathrm{t}}\in H^{\mathsf{T}}(Y\times X,\mathsf{w}_{2}\boxminus\mathsf{w}_{1})_{Z}

to be the image of
[\alpha]
under the natural isomorphism
X\times Y\cong Y\times X
followed by the automorphism
\mathbb{A}^{1}\xrightarrow{-1}\mathbb{A}^{1}
on the target of the potential function. The
K
-theory version is defined similarly.

Lemma 3.29
.

Under Setting
3.1
, let
[\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}]
be a cohomological stable envelope correspondence for
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C})
, then
the convolution

[\operatorname{Stab}_{-\mathfrak{C}}]^{\mathrm{t}}\circ[\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}]\in H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}}\times X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}}\boxminus\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})_{(\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{-\mathfrak{C}})^{\mathrm{t}}\circ\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}}

equals to
the diagonal class
[\Delta]
.

Let
\mathsf{s}
be a generic slope, and
[\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}]
be a
K
-theoretic stable envelope correspondence for
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C},\mathsf{s})
, then the convolution

[\operatorname{Stab}^{-\mathsf{s}}_{-\mathfrak{C}}]^{\mathrm{t}}\circ[\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}+\frac{\mathcal{K}}{2}}_{\mathfrak{C}}]\in K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}}\times X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}}\boxminus\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})_{(\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{-\mathfrak{C}})^{\mathrm{t}}\circ\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}}

equals to
[\mathcal{O}_{\Delta}]
. Here
\mathcal{K}=\det\Omega^{1}_{X}
is the canonical bundle of
X
and
+
denotes the group operation of
\operatorname{Pic}_{\mathsf{A}}(X)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{R}
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to
[
MO
, Thm. 4.4.1]
for the cohomology version and
[
OS
, Prop. 1]
for the
K
-theory version. We give details for the
K
-theory version. To simplify notation, let us write

\displaystyle\mathsf{s}_{1}=\mathsf{s}+\frac{\mathcal{K}}{2}\>.

The diagonal map
X\hookrightarrow X\times X
induces an embedding

\Delta_{23}\colon X^{\mathsf{A}}\times X\times X^{\mathsf{A}}\hookrightarrow X^{\mathsf{A}}\times X\times X\times X^{\mathsf{A}}.

Denote
C=\Delta_{23}^{-1}\left((\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{-\mathfrak{C}})^{\mathrm{t}}\times\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}\right)
. By the proof of
[
MO
, Thm. 4.4.1]
,
C
is proper over
X^{\mathsf{A}}\times X^{\mathsf{A}}
; therefore

(3.6)

\displaystyle[\operatorname{Stab}^{-\mathsf{s}}_{-\mathfrak{C}}]^{\mathrm{t}}\circ[\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}_{1}}_{\mathfrak{C}}]:=p_{*}\Delta_{23}^{*}\left([\operatorname{Stab}^{-\mathsf{s}}_{-\mathfrak{C}}]^{\mathrm{t}}\boxtimes[\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}_{1}}_{\mathfrak{C}}]\right)\in K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}}\times X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}}\boxminus\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})_{p(C)}

is well-defined. Here
p\colon X^{\mathsf{A}}\times X\times X^{\mathsf{A}}\to X^{\mathsf{A}}\times X^{\mathsf{A}}
is the projection, and we denote
(\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{-\mathfrak{C}})^{\mathrm{t}}\circ\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}:=p(C)
.

We compute (
3.6
) using
\mathsf{A}
-localization: its restriction to the component
F_{1}\times F_{2}
equals to

(3.7)

\displaystyle\sum_{F\in\operatorname{Fix}_{\mathsf{A}}(X)}\frac{\left([\operatorname{Stab}^{-\mathsf{s}}_{-\mathfrak{C}}]|_{F\times F_{1}}\right)^{\mathrm{t}}\circ\left([\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}_{1}}_{\mathfrak{C}}]|_{F\times F_{2}}\right)}{e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}(N_{F/X})}.

According to axiom (ii) in Definition
3.20
, we have an inclusion

\deg_{\mathsf{A}}\left([\operatorname{Stab}^{-\mathsf{s}}_{-\mathfrak{C}}]\big|_{F\times F_{1}}\right)^{\mathrm{t}}\circ\left([\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}_{1}}_{\mathfrak{C}}]\big|_{F\times F_{2}}\right)\subseteq\deg_{\mathsf{A}}e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}(N_{F/X})\\
+\operatorname{weight}_{\mathsf{A}}\left(\det(N^{+}_{F_{1}/X})^{-1/2}\otimes\mathsf{s}|_{F_{1}}\right)-\operatorname{weight}_{\mathsf{A}}\left(\det(N^{-}_{F_{2}/X})^{1/2}\otimes\mathsf{s}_{1}|_{F_{2}}\right).

In the above, we have used
\operatorname{weight}_{\mathsf{A}}\det(N^{-}_{F/X})\otimes\det(N^{+}_{F/X})\otimes\mathcal{K}|_{F}=0
. Therefore the function

(3.8)

\displaystyle[\operatorname{Stab}^{-\mathsf{s}}_{-\mathfrak{C}}]^{\mathrm{t}}\circ[\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}_{1}}_{\mathfrak{C}}]\cdot\left(\det(N^{-}_{F_{1}/X})^{-1/2}\otimes(\mathsf{s}|_{F_{1}})^{-1}\boxtimes\det(N^{-}_{F_{2}/X})^{1/2}\otimes\mathsf{s}_{1}|_{F_{2}}\right)

in the variable
a\in\mathsf{A}
is bounded in any direction of taking limit
a\to\infty
.

Since
[\operatorname{Stab}^{-\mathsf{s}}_{-\mathfrak{C}}]^{\mathrm{t}}\circ[\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}_{1}}_{\mathfrak{C}}]
is defined in integral
K
-theory, then (
3.8
) is a constant function in
a
.
In particular,

\displaystyle\deg_{\mathsf{A}}([\operatorname{Stab}^{-\mathsf{s}}_{-\mathfrak{C}}]^{\mathrm{t}}\circ[\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}_{1}}_{\mathfrak{C}}])\big|_{F_{1}\times F_{2}}=\operatorname{weight}_{\mathsf{A}}\left(\det(N^{-}_{F_{1}/X})^{1/2}\otimes\mathsf{s}|_{F_{1}}\right)-\operatorname{weight}_{\mathsf{A}}\left(\det(N^{-}_{F_{2}/X})^{1/2}\otimes\mathsf{s}_{1}|_{F_{2}}\right).

Since
[\operatorname{Stab}^{-\mathsf{s}}_{-\mathfrak{C}}]^{\mathrm{t}}\circ[\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}_{1}}_{\mathfrak{C}}]
is supported on
(\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{-\mathfrak{C}})^{\mathrm{t}}\circ\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}
,
([\operatorname{Stab}^{-\mathsf{s}}_{-\mathfrak{C}}]^{\mathrm{t}}\circ[\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}_{1}}_{\mathfrak{C}}])\big|_{F_{1}\times F_{2}}
vanishes if
F_{2}\npreceq F_{1}
. If
F_{2}\prec F_{1}
, then
\operatorname{weight}_{\mathsf{A}}\left(\det(N^{-}_{F_{1}/X})^{1/2}\otimes\mathsf{s}|_{F_{1}}\right)-\operatorname{weight}_{\mathsf{A}}\left(\det(N^{-}_{F_{2}/X})^{1/2}\otimes\mathsf{s}_{1}|_{F_{2}}\right)
is nonintegral since
\mathsf{s}
is generic, and this implies that
([\operatorname{Stab}^{-\mathsf{s}}_{-\mathfrak{C}}]^{\mathrm{t}}\circ[\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}_{1}}_{\mathfrak{C}}])\big|_{F_{1}\times F_{2}}
vanishes. When
F_{1}=F_{2}
, the only nonzero summand in (
3.8
) is
F=F_{1}
, which gives
[\mathcal{O}_{\Delta}]
. It follows that
[\operatorname{Stab}^{-\mathsf{s}}_{-\mathfrak{C}}]^{\mathrm{t}}\circ[\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}_{1}}_{\mathfrak{C}}]=[\mathcal{O}_{\Delta}]
.
∎

3.5.
Existence of stable envelopes via convolutions

We work under Setting
3.1
.
According to Remark
3.3
,
\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}
is proper over
X
; therefore any class

[\alpha]\in H^{\mathsf{T}}(X\times X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}\boxminus\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})_{\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}}

induces a map (by convolution (
3.3
)):

(3.9)

\alpha\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})_{Z}\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})_{\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ Z},\quad\gamma\mapsto[\alpha]\circ\gamma.

Similarly, any
K
-theory class

[\beta]\in K^{\mathsf{T}}(X\times X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}\boxminus\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})_{\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}}

induces a map (by convolution (
3.4
)):

(3.10)

\beta\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})_{Z}\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})_{\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ Z},\quad\gamma\mapsto[\beta]\circ\gamma.

One can check that convolutions (
3.3
), (
3.4
) are associative, so compositions of convolutions induce
compositions of maps induced.
Convolutions are also compatible with canonical maps as shown below.

Lemma 3.30
.

Let
[\beta]\in K^{\mathsf{T}}(X\times X^{\mathsf{A}})_{\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
and
\operatorname{can}([\beta])\in K^{\mathsf{T}}(X\times X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}\boxminus\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})_{\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
be its image under canonical map (
2.18
). Then we have a commutative diagram

(3.11)

\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}})_{Z(\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{\mathsf{A}}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{can}}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{can}(\beta)}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X)}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces K^{\mathsf{T}}(X)_{Z(\mathsf{w})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{can}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w}),}

where
i\colon Z(\mathsf{w})\hookrightarrow X
and
i^{\mathsf{A}}\colon Z(\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\hookrightarrow X^{\mathsf{A}}
are embeddings.

Similarly, let
[\alpha]\in H^{\mathsf{T}}(X\times X^{\mathsf{A}})_{\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
and
\operatorname{can}([\alpha])\in H^{\mathsf{T}}(X\times X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}\boxminus\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})_{\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
be its image under canonical map (
2.5
). Then we have a commutative diagram

(3.12)

\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}})_{Z(\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{\mathsf{A}}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{can}}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{can}(\alpha)}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X)}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces H^{\mathsf{T}}(X)_{Z(\mathsf{w})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{can}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w}).}

Proof.

The canonical maps are well-defined as
\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}\subseteq Z\left(\mathsf{w}\boxminus\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}}\right)
.
Note also that

\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}\cap\,(X\times Z(\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}}))\subseteq Z(\mathsf{w})\times Z(\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}}),

therefore the convolutions
\beta
in the middle vertical arrow of (
3.11
) and
\alpha
in the middle vertical arrow of (
3.12
) are well-defined. The left square of (
3.11
) commutes as proper pushforward commutes with flat pullbacks and Gysin pullbacks, similarly the left square of (
3.12
) commutes for the same reason. The right square of (
3.11
) commutes as canonical map (
2.18
) commutes with flat pullbacks, proper pushforwards and Gysin pullbacks
(e.g.
[
VV1
, Lem. 2.4]
7
7

7

When working with torus
invariant
functions, the flatness condition on functions imposed in
[
VV1
, Lem. 2.4]
can be dropped off by following the argument of
[
Toda3
, Lem. 2.4.7]
.
). Similarly the right square of (
3.12
) commutes as canonical map (
2.5
) commutes with smooth pullbacks, proper pushforwards and Gysin pullbacks.
∎

By applying convolutions to stable envelope correspondences (Definitions
3.19
,
3.20
), we obtain:

Proposition 3.31
.

Let
[\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}]
be a cohomological stable envelope correspondence for
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C})
, then

\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})

is a cohomological stable envelope.

Let
[\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}]
be a
K
-theoretic stable envelope correspondence for
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C},\mathsf{s})
, then

\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})

is a
K
-theoretic stable envelope.

Proof.

We check the axioms in Definition
3.4
for the cohomological case, the
K
-theoretic counterpart is similar.
We work with a torus fixed component
F
of
X^{\mathsf{A}}
.
Denote projections
\pi_{1}\colon X\times F\to X
,
\pi_{2}\colon X\times F\to F
.
The axiom (i) is obvious as we have

\pi_{1}\left(\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}\,\bigcap\,(X\times F)\right)\subseteq\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F).

The axiom (ii) holds because

\displaystyle(F\hookrightarrow X)^{*}\pi_{1*}\left([\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}]\otimes\pi_{2}^{*}\gamma\right)

\displaystyle=\pi_{1*}\left((F\times F\hookrightarrow X\times F)^{*}[\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}]\otimes(F\times F\to F)^{*}\gamma\right)

\displaystyle=e^{\mathsf{T}}(N^{-}_{F/X})\cdot\gamma.

The axiom (iii) holds because

\displaystyle(F^{\prime}\hookrightarrow X)^{*}\pi_{1*}\left([\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}]\otimes\pi_{2}^{*}\gamma\right)=\pi_{1*}\left((F^{\prime}\times F\hookrightarrow X\times F)^{*}[\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}]\otimes(F^{\prime}\times F\to F)^{*}\gamma\right),

and
\deg_{\mathsf{A}}[\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}]\big|_{F^{\prime}\times F}<\deg_{\mathsf{A}}e^{\mathsf{T}}(N_{F^{\prime}/X}^{-})
.
∎

Combining Proposition
3.31
with Proposition
3.23
and Theorems
3.24
,
3.25
, we obtain:

Theorem 3.32
.

Let
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A})
be in Setting
2.1
and Setting
3.1
,
\mathfrak{C}
be a chamber and
\mathsf{s}
be a slope.

Its cohomological stable envelope exists when either

\,\,(1)\,\,\mathsf{A}\cong\mathbb{C}^{*},\,\mathrm{or}\,\,\,(2)\,\,X\mathrm{\,\,is\,\,a\,\,smooth\,\,symplectic\,\,variety},\mathrm{\,such\,\,that\,\,\mathsf{A}\,\,fixes\,\,the\,\,symplectic\,\,form\,\,and\,\,\mathsf{T}\,\,scales\,\,it},\,\mathrm{or}

(3)\,\,X\mathrm{\,\,is\,\,a\,\,symmetric\,\,GIT\,\,quotient},\,\mathrm{with\,\,an\,\,action\,\,by\,\,tori\,\,\mathsf{A}\subseteq\mathsf{T}\,\,as\,\,Definition\,\,\ref{def of sym var}}.

In the first case, the
K
-theoretic stable envelope also exists.

Definition 3.33
.

Let
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T})
be in Settings
2.1
,
3.1
. Fix a nontrivial
\sigma:\mathbb{C}^{*}\to\mathsf{T}
, we take
\mathsf{A}
to be the image of
\sigma
.

•

The cohomological stable envelope in Theorem
3.32
(1) is denoted by
\operatorname{Stab}_{\sigma}
.

•

Fix
\mathsf{s}\in\operatorname{Pic}_{\sigma}(X)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{R}
, the
K
-theoretic stable envelope in Theorem
3.32
(1) is denoted
by
\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\sigma}
.

The above cohomological stable envelope
\operatorname{Stab}_{\sigma}
has the following nice property, which will be used only in the proof of
Proposition
8.8
.

Proposition 3.34
.

Let
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A})
be in the Setting
2.1
and Setting
3.1
and
\mathfrak{C}
a chamber. Then the cohomological stable envelope
\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}
exists if and only if
\operatorname{Stab}_{\sigma}=\operatorname{Stab}_{\sigma^{\prime}}
for all
(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})\in\mathfrak{C}\times\mathfrak{C}
.

Proof.

Suppose that
\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}
exists, then
\operatorname{Stab}_{\sigma}=\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}
for all
\sigma\in\mathfrak{C}
. Conversely, if
\operatorname{Stab}_{\sigma}=\operatorname{Stab}_{\sigma^{\prime}}
for all
(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})\in\mathfrak{C}\times\mathfrak{C}
, then for arbitrary
\sigma\in\mathfrak{C}
,
\operatorname{Stab}_{\sigma}
satisfies all three axioms in Definition
3.4
. Axioms (i) and (ii) hold automatically, and the axiom (iii) holds since
\deg_{\mathsf{A}}f<\deg_{\mathsf{A}}g
if and only if
\deg_{\sigma}f<\deg_{\sigma}g
for a set of
\sigma
that spans
\operatorname{Lie}\mathsf{A}
.
∎

3.6.
Existence of stable envelopes via Hall operations

For any symmetric GIT quotient
X
with an action of tori
\mathsf{A}\subseteq\mathsf{T}
as Definition
4.1
, and any
\mathsf{T}
-invariant function
\mathsf{w}\colon X\to\mathbb{C}
,
its
K
-theoretic
stable envelope
\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}
(for a generic slope
\mathsf{s}
) can be obtained by commutative diagram

(3.13)

\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{X}^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bigoplus_{\phi/\sim}K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{X}^{\mathsf{A},\phi},\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447pt\,\,\bigoplus_{\phi/\sim}\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{X},\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{res}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bigoplus_{\phi/\sim}K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A},\phi},\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\bigoplus_{\phi/\sim}\mathbf{\Psi}_{K}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447pt\,\,\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w}).}

Here the direct sum is taken over all homomorphisms
\phi:\mathsf{A}\to G
modulo certain equivalence
\sim
(
4.12
),

\mathbf{\Psi}_{K}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}
are nonabelian stable envelopes, defined using window subcategories,
\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}
are Hall operations on the stack quotients,
and
\mathrm{res}
is the restriction map to the open substack.
This diagram will be proven in Theorem
5.5
. A similar diagram also holds for the cohomological case (Theorem
4.22
).
We leave the details to §
5
.

We remark that
\mathbf{\Psi}_{K}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}
has a categorical lift to a functor. Combining with the categorical Hall product (e.g.
[
P
]
),
we may define the composed functor
\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}
as a “categorical stable envelope”.

3.7.
Integrality of the inverse of stable envelopes

The inverse of stable envelopes can be computed using the transpose of stable envelope correspondences by Lemma
3.29
, this has the following application
on integrality.

Proposition 3.35
.

Under Setting
3.1
, and assume that the
\mathsf{A}
weights in
\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})
are nonpositive, and that stable envelope correspondences exist. Then
\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}^{-1}
is defined for the integral cohomology class, i.e. it maps
H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})
to
H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})
; similarly
(\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}})^{-1}
is defined for the integral
K
-theory class, i.e. it maps
K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})
to
K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})
.

Proof.

Let
X_{0}=\operatorname{Spec}\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})
, then by the assumption the induced
\mathsf{A}
-action on
X_{0}
is attracting with respect to
\mathfrak{C}
, i.e.
X_{0}=\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(X_{0}^{\mathsf{A}})
. Equivalently,
X_{0}^{\mathsf{A}}=\operatorname{Attr}_{-\mathfrak{C}}(X_{0}^{\mathsf{A}})
. It follows that
\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{-\mathfrak{C}}\subseteq X\times_{X_{0}}X^{\mathsf{A}}
. Then
\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{-\mathfrak{C}}
is proper over
X^{\mathsf{A}}
since
X
is proper over
X_{0}
by Setting
3.1
. Lemma
3.29
implies that
\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}^{-1}
is induced by the correspondence
[\operatorname{Stab}_{-\mathfrak{C}}]^{\mathrm{t}}
which is then defined over integral cohomology class. Similarly,
(\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}})^{-1}
is induced by the correspondence
[\operatorname{Stab}^{\frac{\mathcal{K}}{2}-\mathsf{s}}_{-\mathfrak{C}}]^{\mathrm{t}}
which is then defined over integral
K
-theory class.
∎

3.8.
Stable envelopes with coefficient in a subtorus

Occasionally, we also consider the equivariant critical cohomology/
K
-theory with respect to a subtorus
\mathsf{T}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathsf{T}
which does
not
necessarily contain
\mathsf{A}
. Then we can regard stable envelope correspondences (assuming existence) in the
\mathsf{T}^{\prime}
-equivariant critical cohomology/
K
-theory, and induce convolution maps:

\displaystyle\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}^{\prime}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\to H^{\mathsf{T}^{\prime}}(X,\mathsf{w}),\qquad\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}^{\prime}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\to K^{\mathsf{T}^{\prime}}(X,\mathsf{w}).

The following injectivity result will be used in
[
COZZ1
]
.

Proposition 3.36
.

Under Setting
3.1
, and assume that stable envelope correspondences exist. Let
\mathsf{T}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathsf{T}
be the subtorus as above.

•

If
\mathsf{A}
-action on
\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w})^{\mathsf{T}^{\prime}}
is attracting with respect to
\mathfrak{C}

8
8

8

This means for arbitrary point
x\in\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w})^{\mathsf{T}^{\prime}}
,
\lim_{t\to 0}\sigma(t)\cdot x
exists for all cocharacter
\sigma
in
\mathfrak{C}
.
, then
\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}^{\prime}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\to H^{\mathsf{T}^{\prime}}(X,\mathsf{w})
is injective after
\mathsf{T}^{\prime}
-localization.

•

If
\mathsf{A}
-action on
X^{\mathsf{T}^{\prime}}
is attracting with respect to
\mathfrak{C}
, then
\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}^{\prime}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\to K^{\mathsf{T}^{\prime}}(X,\mathsf{w})
is injective after
\mathsf{T}^{\prime}
-localization.

Proof.

Let us first prove the cohomology case. In view of Lemma
3.29
, it is enough to show that the transpose correspondence
[\operatorname{Stab}_{-\mathfrak{C}}]^{\mathrm{t}}
induces a well-defined convolution map

H^{\mathsf{T}^{\prime}}(X,\mathsf{w})_{\mathrm{loc}}\to H^{\mathsf{T}^{\prime}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})_{\mathrm{loc}}.

By Remark
3.28
, we only need to check that the
\mathsf{T}^{\prime}
-fixed locus in
\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{-\mathfrak{C}}\cap\left(\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\times\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w})\right)
is proper over
X^{\mathsf{A}}
. Let
\pi\colon X\to X_{0}
be a
\mathsf{T}
-equivariant morphism with affine target
X_{0}
as in the Setting
3.1
. Then by the assumption, we have
\pi(\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w})^{\mathsf{T}^{\prime}})\subseteq\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(X_{0}^{\mathsf{A}})
. Since
\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(X_{0}^{\mathsf{A}})\cap\operatorname{Attr}_{-\mathfrak{C}}(X_{0}^{\mathsf{A}})=X_{0}^{\mathsf{A}}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\left(\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{-\mathfrak{C}}\cap\left(\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\times\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w})\right)\right)^{\mathsf{T}^{\prime}}\subseteq X\times_{X_{0}}X^{\mathsf{A}}

and the latter is proper over
X^{\mathsf{A}}
because
X\to X_{0}
is proper. This proves the cohomology case.

For the
K
-theory case, the argument is word-by-word the same as above except that we do not have an analog of Remark
3.28
for
K
-theory so we need to replace the critical locus by the entire
X
.
∎

3.9.
Triangle lemma

Let
\mathfrak{C}
be a chamber and let
\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}
be a face of some dimension. Consider

\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}=\operatorname{Span}\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathfrak{a}=\operatorname{Lie}(\mathsf{A})

with associated subtorus
\mathsf{A}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathsf{A}
. The cone
\mathfrak{C}
projects to a cone in
\mathfrak{a}/\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}
that we denote by
\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}
.

We show that triangle lemma always holds for
K
-theoretic stable envelopes with generic slopes.

Lemma 3.37
.

Let
\mathsf{s}\in\operatorname{Pic}_{\mathsf{A}}(X)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{R}
be a generic slope. Suppose that
K
-theoretic stable envelopes
\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}
,
\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}
, and
\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}
exist, where

\mathsf{s}^{\prime}=\mathsf{s}|_{X^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}}\otimes\det\left(N^{-}_{X^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}/X}\right)^{1/2}\in\operatorname{Pic}_{\mathsf{A}}(X^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{R}.

Then the following diagram

\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}}

is commutative.

Proof.

The proof is essentially the same as
[
Oko1
, Prop. 9.2.8]
. We choose generic
\xi^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}
,
\xi\in\mathfrak{C}
such that

|\!|\delta\xi|\!|\ll|\!|\xi^{\prime}|\!|,

where
\delta\xi:=\xi-\xi^{\prime}
. Then for arbitrary
F\in\operatorname{Fix}_{\mathsf{A}}(X)
(denote
F^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Fix}_{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}(X)
such that
F\subseteq F^{\prime}
) and arbitrary
\gamma\in K^{\mathsf{T}/\mathsf{A}}(F,\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})
,
\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\xi^{\prime}}\circ\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{\delta\xi}(\gamma)
is supported on
\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F)
and its Gysin pullback to
F
equals to

\displaystyle\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\xi^{\prime}}\circ\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{\delta\xi}(\gamma)|_{F}=e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}(N^{-}_{F^{\prime}/X})\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{\delta\xi}(\gamma)|_{F}=e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}(N^{-}_{F^{\prime}/X})e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}(N^{-}_{F/F^{\prime}})\gamma=e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}(N^{-}_{F/X})\gamma.

Moreover, we claim that
\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\xi^{\prime}}\circ\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{\delta\xi}(\gamma)
satisfies the axiom (iii) in the Definition
3.10
. Let
