\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{X}^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime},\phi},\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A},\phi},\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}\hskip 16.38895pt\hskip 16.38895pt}

\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{\Psi}^{\phi}_{H}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime},\phi},\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\,\,\,\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{\Psi}^{\phi}_{H}}

By Theorem
4.22
, the right, the backward, and the left squares are commutative. The upper triangle is commutative because Hall operations are associative. These imply the commutativity of the lower triangle.

4.7.
Proof of Theorem
4.19

The equality
\mathrm{res}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{H}=\operatorname{id}
is obvious from the definition of (
4.10
).

4.7.1.
Degree bound

To show that
\mathbf{\Psi}_{H}
satisfies the degree bound condition (
4.11
), it is enough to show:

(4.15)

\displaystyle\deg_{\sigma}(\mathbf{j}_{\sigma}\times\operatorname{id})^{!}[\overline{\Delta_{X}}]<\frac{1}{2}(\dim R^{\sigma\emph{-}\text{moving}}-\dim\operatorname{Lie}(G)^{\sigma\emph{-}\text{moving}}),

where
(\mathbf{j}_{\sigma}\times\operatorname{id})^{!}[\overline{\Delta_{X}}]
is the pullback of the fundamental class of
\overline{\Delta_{X}}
along the map
\mathbf{j}_{\sigma}\times\operatorname{id}:\mathfrak{X}^{\sigma}\times X\to\mathfrak{X}\times X
, which is an element in
H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{X}^{\sigma}\times_{\mathfrak{X}}\overline{\Delta_{X}})
.

We first prove a similar statement for the abelian quotient. Define
\widehat{\mathfrak{X}}:=[R/H]
and consider the diagonal

\Delta_{\widehat{X}}\subseteq\widehat{X}\times\widehat{X}.

Take its closure
\overline{\Delta_{\widehat{X}}}
in
\widehat{\mathfrak{X}}\times\widehat{X}
. Without loss of generality, we assume that image of
\sigma
is contained in
H
, and let

\widehat{\mathbf{j}}_{\sigma}:\widehat{\mathfrak{X}}^{\sigma}:=[R^{\sigma}/H]\to\widehat{\mathfrak{X}}

be the natural map.

Lemma 4.23
.

We have

(4.16)

\displaystyle\deg_{\sigma}(\widehat{\mathbf{j}}_{\sigma}\times\operatorname{id})^{!}[\overline{\Delta_{\widehat{X}}}]<\frac{1}{2}\dim R^{\sigma\emph{-}\text{moving}},

where
(\widehat{\mathbf{j}}_{\sigma}\times\operatorname{id})^{!}[\overline{\Delta_{\widehat{X}}}]
is the pullback of the fundamental class of
\overline{\Delta_{\widehat{X}}}
along the map
\widehat{\mathbf{j}}_{\sigma}\times\operatorname{id}:\widehat{\mathfrak{X}}^{\sigma}\times\widehat{X}\to\widehat{\mathfrak{X}}\times\widehat{X}
, which is an element in

H^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\widehat{\mathfrak{X}}^{\sigma}\times_{\widehat{\mathfrak{X}}}\overline{\Delta_{\widehat{X}}}\right)
.

Proof.

Consider the graph of the quotient map
\widehat{q}\colon R^{\theta\emph{-}s}\to\widehat{X}
:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Graph}(\widehat{q})\subset\widehat{X}\times R^{\theta\emph{-}s},

and take the closure of
\mathrm{Graph}(\widehat{q})
in
\widehat{X}\times R
, denoted by
\overline{\mathrm{Graph}(\widehat{q})}
. Then
\overline{\Delta_{\widehat{X}}}=[\overline{\mathrm{Graph}(\widehat{q})}/H]
, and

\displaystyle H^{\mathsf{T}}(\widehat{\mathfrak{X}}^{\sigma}\times_{\widehat{\mathfrak{X}}}\overline{\Delta_{\widehat{X}}})=H^{\mathsf{T}\times H}\left((\widehat{X}\times R^{\sigma})\>\bigcap\>\overline{\mathrm{Graph}(\widehat{q})}\right).

To prove (
4.16
), it suffices to show

\dim(\widehat{X}\times R^{\sigma})\>\bigcap\>\overline{\mathrm{Graph}(\widehat{q})}-\frac{1}{2}\dim R^{\sigma\emph{-}\text{moving}}<\dim\overline{\mathrm{Graph}(\widehat{q})}+\dim\widehat{X}\times R^{\sigma}-\dim\widehat{X}\times R,

where the right-hand-side is the homological degree of
(\widehat{X}\times R^{\sigma}\hookrightarrow\widehat{X}\times R)^{!}[\overline{\mathrm{Graph}(\widehat{q})}]
. It is elementary to see that the above inequality is equivalent to

(4.17)

\displaystyle\dim(\widehat{X}\times R^{\sigma})\>\bigcap\>\overline{\mathrm{Graph}(\widehat{q})}<\frac{1}{2}(\dim R+\dim R^{\sigma}).

Since
R\cong R^{\vee}
as
H
-representations, we have an isomorphism of
H
-representations:

R\cong T^{*}N\oplus R_{0},

where
N
is a direct sum of nontrivial weight spaces and
R_{0}
is a trivial
H
-representation. Define

\nu\colon R=T^{*}N\times R_{0}\xrightarrow{\mu\times\operatorname{id}}\mathfrak{h}^{*}\times R_{0},

where
\mu\colon T^{*}N\to\mathfrak{h}^{*}
is the moment map, then the restriction of
\nu
to
R^{{\theta}\emph{-}s}
is smooth. By passing to the quotient, we obtain a smooth map

\bar{\nu}\colon\widehat{X}\to\mathfrak{h}^{*}\times R_{0},

and for any
(x,y)\in\mathfrak{h}^{*}\times R_{0}
,
\bar{\nu}^{-1}(x,y)
is a smooth symplectic variety. Let
\mathrm{pr}\colon R\to R_{0}
be the projection, then
\mathrm{Graph}(\widehat{q})
is contained in
(\bar{\nu}\times\mathrm{pr})^{-1}(\mathfrak{h}^{*}\times\Delta_{R_{0}})\subset\widehat{X}\times R
.

For any
(x,y,y)\in\mathfrak{h}^{*}\times\Delta_{R_{0}}
, we have a Lagrangian:

(\bar{\nu}\times\mathrm{pr})^{-1}(x,y,y)\>\bigcap\>\mathrm{Graph}(\widehat{q})\subseteq\bar{\nu}^{-1}(x,y)\times T^{*}N.

After taking the closure, we get a Lagrangian

(\bar{\nu}\times\mathrm{pr})^{-1}(x,y,y)\>\bigcap\>\overline{\mathrm{Graph}(\widehat{q})}\subseteq\bar{\nu}^{-1}(x,y)\times T^{*}N.

Let
\mathrm{pr}^{\sigma}\colon R^{\sigma}=T^{*}N^{\sigma}\times R_{0}\to R_{0}
be the projection. Using the same argument as Lemma
4.11
,

(\bar{\nu}\times\mathrm{pr}^{\sigma})^{-1}(x,y,y)\>\bigcap\>\overline{\mathrm{Graph}(\widehat{q})}\subseteq\bar{\nu}^{-1}(x,y)\times T^{*}N^{\sigma}

is isotropic, and we have

\displaystyle\dim\>(\bar{\nu}\times\mathrm{pr}^{\sigma})^{-1}(x,y,y)\>\bigcap\>\overline{\mathrm{Graph}(\widehat{q})}\leqslant\frac{1}{2}\dim\>\bar{\nu}^{-1}(x,y)\times T^{*}N^{\sigma}=\frac{1}{2}(\dim R+\dim R^{\sigma})-\dim\mathfrak{h}^{*}\times R_{0}.

There exists a dense open subset
U\subset\mathfrak{h}^{*}
such that
\mu^{-1}(U)\subseteq(T^{*}N)^{\bar{\theta}\emph{-}s}
. It follows that
{\nu}^{-1}(U\times R_{0})\subseteq R^{\bar{\theta}\emph{-}s}
. We note that
\mathrm{Graph}(\widehat{q})
is contained in the closed subset
\widehat{X}\times_{\mathfrak{h}^{*}\times R_{0}}R
, so
\overline{\mathrm{Graph}(\widehat{q})}\subseteq\widehat{X}\times_{\mathfrak{h}^{*}\times R_{0}}R
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\left(\bar{\nu}^{-1}(U\times R_{0})\times R\right)\>\bigcap\>\overline{\mathrm{Graph}(\widehat{q})}\subseteq\left(\bar{\nu}^{-1}(U\times R_{0})\times R\right)\>\bigcap\>\left(\widehat{X}\times_{\mathfrak{h}^{*}\times R_{0}}R\right)\subseteq\bar{\nu}^{-1}(U\times R_{0})\times R^{\bar{\theta}\emph{-}s}.

Since
R^{\sigma}\cap R^{\bar{\theta}\emph{-}s}=\emptyset
, we see that

\displaystyle(\bar{\nu}\times\mathrm{pr}^{\sigma})^{-1}(x,y,y)\>\bigcap\>\overline{\mathrm{Graph}(\widehat{q})}=\emptyset

for all
(x,y,y)\in U\times\Delta_{R_{0}}
. As a result, we have

\displaystyle\dim(\widehat{X}\times R^{\sigma})\>\bigcap\>\overline{\mathrm{Graph}(\widehat{q})}\leqslant\dim\mathfrak{h}^{*}\times\Delta_{R_{0}}-1+\frac{1}{2}\dim\>\bar{\nu}^{-1}(x,y)\times T^{*}N^{\sigma}=\frac{1}{2}(\dim R+\dim R^{\sigma})-1.

This proves (
4.17
).
∎

Next, we deduce (
4.15
) from (
4.16
). An argument similar to Lemma
4.15
shows that

(4.18)

\displaystyle\eta_{\widehat{\mathfrak{X}}\times\widehat{X}\to\mathfrak{X}\times X}([\overline{\Delta_{\widehat{X}}}])=|W|\cdot(-1)^{\sharp}\cdot[\overline{\Delta_{X}}].

Here
\eta_{\widehat{\mathfrak{X}}\times\widehat{X}\to\mathfrak{X}\times X}
is defined for the
G/H\times G/H
-bundle
\widehat{\mathfrak{X}}\times\widehat{X}\to\mathfrak{X}\times X
by
\eta_{\widehat{\mathfrak{X}}\times\widehat{X}\to\mathfrak{X}\times X}=(p_{2}\times p_{2})_{*}\circ\left((p_{1}\times p_{1})^{!}\right)^{-1}
, where

\displaystyle p\colon\widehat{X}\xrightarrow{p_{1}}\overline{X}:=R^{\theta\emph{-}s}\times_{G}(G/B)\xrightarrow{p_{2}}X,\quad p\colon\widehat{\mathfrak{X}}\xrightarrow{p_{1}}\overline{\mathfrak{X}}:=[R/B]\xrightarrow{p_{2}}\mathfrak{X}

are the natural projections.

Define
\widetilde{\mathbf{j}}_{\sigma}:\widetilde{\mathfrak{X}}^{\sigma}:=\mathfrak{X}^{\sigma}\times_{\mathfrak{X}}\widehat{\mathfrak{X}}\to\widehat{\mathfrak{X}}
the pullback of
\mathbf{j}_{\sigma}
to
\widehat{\mathfrak{X}}
, then by base change we have

(4.19)

\displaystyle({\mathbf{j}}_{\sigma}\times\operatorname{id})^{!}\eta_{\widehat{\mathfrak{X}}\times\widehat{X}\to\mathfrak{X}\times X}([\overline{\Delta_{\widehat{X}}}])=\eta_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{X}}^{\sigma}\times\widehat{X}\to{\mathfrak{X}}^{\sigma}\times X}\left((\widetilde{\mathbf{j}}_{\sigma}\times\operatorname{id})^{!}[\overline{\Delta_{\widehat{X}}}]\right).

We compute
\eta_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{X}}^{\sigma}\times\widehat{X}\to{\mathfrak{X}}^{\sigma}\times X}
as follows. Note that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathfrak{X}}^{\sigma}\cong[R^{\sigma}\times_{G^{\sigma}}G/H].

Here
R^{\sigma}\times_{G^{\sigma}}G
is the quotient of
R^{\sigma}\times G
by the diagonal action of
G^{\sigma}
, where
G^{\sigma}
acts on
R^{\sigma}
naturally and acts on
G
by left multiplication.
R^{\sigma}\times_{G^{\sigma}}G
has a right
H
-action on
G
, and
[R^{\sigma}\times_{G^{\sigma}}G/H]
is the quotient stack. We notice that for every
w\in W^{\sigma}\backslash W
(
W
and
W^{\sigma}
are Weyl groups of
G
and
G^{\sigma}
respectively),
\widehat{\mathfrak{X}}^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}
is naturally a closed substack of
\widetilde{\mathfrak{X}}^{\sigma}
via the embedding

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathfrak{X}}^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}=[R^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}/H]\cong[R^{\sigma}\times_{G^{\sigma}}G^{\sigma}\cdot w/H]\hookrightarrow[R^{\sigma}\times_{G^{\sigma}}G/H]\cong\widetilde{\mathfrak{X}}^{\sigma}.

Therefore, after
H_{\mathbb{C}^{*}}(\operatorname{pt})
-localization, we have

(4.20)

\displaystyle\eta_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{X}}^{\sigma}\times\widehat{X}\to{\mathfrak{X}}^{\sigma}\times X}(\cdots)=\sum_{w\in W^{\sigma}\backslash W}\eta_{\widehat{\mathfrak{X}}^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}\times\widehat{X}\to{\mathfrak{X}}^{\sigma}\times X}\left(\frac{(\widehat{\mathfrak{X}}^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}\times\widehat{X}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\mathfrak{X}}^{\sigma}\times\widehat{X})^{!}(\cdots)}{e^{\mathsf{T}\times G^{\sigma}}(N_{w})}\right).

Here
N_{w}
is the normal bundle of
[R^{\sigma}\times_{G^{\sigma}}P_{\sigma}\cdot w/B]
in
[R^{\sigma}\times_{G^{\sigma}}G/B]
, where
P_{\sigma}=\operatorname{Attr}_{+}(G^{\sigma})
is a parabolic subgroup of
G
. Note that
\sigma
fixes
R^{\sigma}\times G^{\sigma}\cdot w/H
, and
N_{w}
is
\sigma
-moving with

\operatorname{rk}N_{w}=\dim G/P_{\sigma}=\frac{1}{2}\dim\operatorname{Lie}(G)^{\sigma\emph{-}\text{moving}}.

Thus
\eta_{\widehat{\mathfrak{X}}^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}\times\widehat{X}\to{\mathfrak{X}}^{\sigma}\times X}
does not change the
\sigma
-degree and
e^{\mathsf{T}\times G^{\sigma}}(N_{w})^{-1}
decreases the
\sigma
-degree by
\frac{1}{2}\dim\operatorname{Lie}(G)^{\sigma\emph{-}\text{moving}}
. Combining (
4.18
), (
4.19
) and (
4.20
), we get

\displaystyle\deg_{\sigma}(\mathbf{j}_{\sigma}\times\operatorname{id})^{!}[\overline{\Delta_{X}}]

\displaystyle=\deg_{\sigma}\eta_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{X}}^{\sigma}\times\widehat{X}\to{\mathfrak{X}}^{\sigma}\times X}\left((\widetilde{\mathbf{j}}_{\sigma}\times\operatorname{id})^{!}[\overline{\Delta_{\widehat{X}}}]\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\max_{w\in W^{\sigma}\backslash W}\left\{\deg_{w^{-1}(\sigma)}(\widehat{\mathbf{j}}_{w^{-1}(\sigma)}\times\operatorname{id})^{!}[\overline{\Delta_{\widehat{X}}}]-\frac{1}{2}\dim\operatorname{Lie}(G)^{\sigma\emph{-}\text{moving}}\right\}

by (
4.16
)

\displaystyle<\frac{1}{2}(\dim R^{\sigma\emph{-}\text{moving}}-\dim\operatorname{Lie}(G)^{\sigma\emph{-}\text{moving}}).

This proves (
4.15
).

4.7.2.
Uniqueness

Next we show that
\mathbf{\Psi}_{H}
is the unique
H_{\mathsf{T}}(\operatorname{pt})
-linear map from
H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})
to
H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{X},\mathsf{w})
that satisfies the conditions in Theorem
4.19
. The idea is similar to that of uniqueness of stable envelopes.

It is known that the unstable locus

R^{\theta\emph{-}u}:=R\setminus R^{\theta\emph{-}ss}

admits equivariant Kempf-Ness (KN) stratification by connected locally-closed subvarieties
[
Kir
,
DH
]
.
The KN stratification is constructed iteratively by selecting a pair
(\sigma_{i}\in\operatorname{cochar}(G),Z_{i}:=R^{\sigma_{i}})
which maximizes the numerical invariant

(4.21)

\displaystyle\mu(\sigma):=\frac{(\sigma,\theta)}{|\sigma|}

among those
(\sigma,Z)
for which
Z
is not contained in the union of the previously defined strata. Here
|\cdot|
is a fixed conjugation-invariant norm on the cocharacters of
G
.

One defines the open subvariety
Z_{i}^{*}\subseteq Z_{i}
to consist of those points not lying on previously defined strata, and

Y_{i}:=\operatorname{Attr}_{\sigma_{i}}(Z_{i}^{*}).

Then define the new strata

S_{i}=G\cdot Y_{i}.

Note that
Y_{i}
is invariant under the parabolic subgroup
P_{i}:=\operatorname{Attr}_{\sigma_{i}}(G^{\sigma_{i}})
, and it is known that the natural map

(4.22)

G\times^{P_{i}}Y_{i}\to S_{i}

is an isomorphism.

Remark 4.24
.

For a KN stratum
S_{i}=G\cdot\operatorname{Attr}_{\sigma}(Z^{*}_{i})
associated with a cocharacter
\sigma
, we have natural isomorphisms

\displaystyle H^{G}(S_{i},\mathsf{w})\cong H^{P_{i}}(\operatorname{Attr}_{\sigma_{i}}(Z^{*}_{i}),\mathsf{w})\cong H^{G^{\sigma_{i}}}(\operatorname{Attr}_{\sigma_{i}}(Z^{*}_{i}),\mathsf{w})\cong H^{G^{\sigma_{i}}}(Z^{*}_{i},\mathsf{w}).

The first isomorphism is induced by (
4.22
), which equals
(\operatorname{Attr}_{\sigma_{i}}(Z^{*}_{i})\hookrightarrow S_{i})^{*}\circ\mathrm{res}^{G}_{P_{i}}
; the second isomorphism follows from general fact that

H^{P_{i}}(-)\cong H^{G^{\sigma_{i}}}(-),

since
G^{\sigma_{i}}
is a Levi subgroup in
P_{i}
; the third isomorphism is induced by Gysin pullback
(Z^{*}_{i}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Attr}_{\sigma_{i}}(Z^{*}_{i}))^{*}

[
Dav1
, Eqn. (37)]
. The composition
H^{G}(S_{i},\mathsf{w})\cong H^{G^{\sigma_{i}}}(Z^{*}_{i},\mathsf{w})
is given by
(Z^{*}_{i}\hookrightarrow S_{i})^{*}\circ\mathrm{res}^{G}_{G^{\sigma_{i}}}
, or equivalently
([Z^{*}_{i}/G^{\sigma_{i}}]\to[S_{i}/G])^{*}
.

We denote the following natural map between quotient stacks:

(4.23)

\mathbf{i}_{\sigma_{i}}:[Z^{*}_{i}/G^{\sigma_{i}}]\to\mathfrak{X},

which factors as an open immersion
[Z^{*}_{i}/G^{\sigma_{i}}]\hookrightarrow[Z_{i}/G^{\sigma_{i}}]
followed by
\mathbf{j}_{\sigma_{i}}:[R^{\sigma_{i}}/G^{\sigma_{i}}]\to[R/G]
. In particular, we have well-defined Gysin pullbacks:

\mathbf{i}_{\sigma_{i}}^{*}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{X},\mathsf{w})\to H^{\mathsf{T}}([Z^{*}_{i}/G^{\sigma_{i}}],\mathsf{w}).

And according to what we have established,

\displaystyle\deg_{\sigma_{i}}\mathbf{i}_{\sigma_{i}}^{*}\mathbf{\Psi}_{H}(\gamma)<\frac{1}{2}(\dim\mathfrak{X}-\dim\mathfrak{X}^{\sigma_{i}})=\operatorname{rk}N_{S_{i}/R}

holds for all
\gamma\in H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})
. By Remark
4.24
,
\mathbf{i}_{\sigma_{i}}^{*}
can be identified with the Gysin pullback
(S_{i}\hookrightarrow R)^{*}
followed by the isomorphism
H^{G}(S_{i},\mathsf{w})\cong H^{G^{\sigma_{i}}}(Z^{*}_{i},\mathsf{w})
.

Suppose that there exists a nonzero class
\alpha\in H^{\mathsf{T}\times G}(R,\mathsf{w})
supported on
R^{\theta\emph{-}u}
with the property that

\deg_{\sigma_{j}}(S_{j}\hookrightarrow R)^{*}\alpha<\operatorname{rk}N_{S_{j}/R}

for all KN strata
S_{j}
. Let
S
be a union of KN strata with a maximal strata
S_{i}
(open in
S
), such that
\alpha
is supported on
S
and
(S_{i}\hookrightarrow R)^{*}\alpha\neq 0
. Then

\displaystyle\alpha=(S\hookrightarrow R)_{*}\widetilde{\alpha}

for some
\widetilde{\alpha}\in H^{\mathsf{T}\times G}(S,\mathsf{w})
, and we have

\displaystyle\deg_{\sigma_{i}}(S_{i}\hookrightarrow R)^{*}\alpha=\deg_{\sigma_{i}}e^{G}(N_{S_{i}/R})\cdot(\widetilde{\alpha}|_{S_{i}})\geqslant\operatorname{rk}N_{S_{i}/R}.

We get a contradiction, and therefore we must have
\alpha=0
. This proves the uniqueness.

4.8.
Proof of Theorem
4.22

We claim that
\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{H}^{\phi}
is induced by the correspondence

[Z]\in H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{X}\times X^{\mathsf{A},\phi},\mathsf{w}\boxminus\mathsf{w})_{Z},

where
Z=\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}\left(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A},\phi}}\right)
is the closure of
\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}\left(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A},\phi}}\right)
inside the stack
\mathfrak{X}\times X^{\mathsf{A},\phi}
(here attracting set is taken inside the stable locus
X\times X^{\mathsf{A},\phi}
), and
[Z]
is the image of the fundamental class of
Z
under the canonical map (
2.8
).

By construction,
\mathbf{\Psi}_{H}^{\phi}
is induced by the correspondence
[\overline{\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A},\phi}}}]
. Then the map
\mathfrak{q}^{*}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{H}^{\phi}
is induced by the pullback correspondence
[(\mathfrak{q}\times\operatorname{id})^{-1}(\overline{\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A},\phi}}})]
along the morphism

(\mathfrak{q}\times\operatorname{id}):\mathfrak{L}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\times X^{\mathsf{A},\phi}\to\mathfrak{X}^{\mathsf{A},\phi}\times X^{\mathsf{A},\phi}.

Since
\mathfrak{q}
is smooth with connected fibers and
\overline{\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A},\phi}}}
is an irreducible stack,
(\mathfrak{q}\times\operatorname{id})^{-1}(\overline{\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A},\phi}}})
is an irreducible closed substack of
\mathfrak{L}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\times X^{\mathsf{A},\phi}
. Note that
(\mathfrak{q}\times\operatorname{id})^{-1}(\overline{\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A},\phi}}})
contains an open substack
(\mathfrak{q}\times\operatorname{id})^{-1}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A},\phi}})
and the latter is isomorphic to
\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}\left(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A},\phi}}\right)
. So the map
\mathfrak{p}_{*}\circ\mathfrak{q}^{*}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{H}^{\phi}
is induced by the correspondence
\left[(\mathfrak{p}\times\operatorname{id})\left((\mathfrak{q}\times\operatorname{id})^{-1}(\overline{\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A},\phi}}})\right)\right]
. Note that
(\mathfrak{p}\times\operatorname{id})
maps
(\mathfrak{q}\times\operatorname{id})^{-1}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A},\phi}})
isomorphically onto
\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}\left(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A},\phi}}\right)
. Then by the irreducibility of
(\mathfrak{q}\times\operatorname{id})^{-1}(\overline{\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A},\phi}}})
, we have

(\mathfrak{p}\times\operatorname{id})\left((\mathfrak{q}\times\operatorname{id})^{-1}(\overline{\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A},\phi}}})\right)=Z.

This proves the claim that
\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{H}^{\phi}
is induced by the correspondence
[Z]
.

Since the restriction of
Z
to the open substack
X\times X^{\mathsf{A},\phi}
is the closure of
\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}\left(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A},\phi}}\right)
inside
X\times X^{\mathsf{A},\phi}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{res}\circ\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{H}^{\phi}=\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}.

Since
\mathrm{res}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{H}=\operatorname{id}
, it remains to show that the image of
\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{H}^{\phi}
lies in the image of
\mathbf{\Psi}_{H}
. By Theorem
4.19
, this amounts to showing that for all nonzero cocharacter
\sigma:\mathbb{C}^{*}\to G
(by conjugation we can assume that the image of
\sigma
is in
G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}
), the inequality

\displaystyle\deg_{\sigma}\mathbf{j}_{\sigma}^{*}\circ\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{H}^{\phi}(\gamma)<\frac{1}{2}(\dim\mathfrak{X}-\dim\mathfrak{X}^{\sigma})

holds for all
\gamma\in H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A},\phi},\mathsf{w})
. Equivalently, the limit

(4.24)

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\frac{{\mathbf{j}}_{\sigma}^{*}\circ\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{H}^{\phi}(\gamma)}{\sqrt{e^{\mathsf{T}\times G^{\sigma}}\left((R-\operatorname{Lie}(G))^{\sigma\text{-moving}}\right)}}=0\,\,\text{ for all cocharacters $\sigma:\mathbb{C}^{*}\to G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}$},

where
t
is the equivariant parameter in
H_{\mathbb{C}^{*}}(\operatorname{pt})=\mathbb{Q}[t]
and

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathbf{j}}_{\sigma}^{*}\circ\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{H}^{\phi}(\gamma)}{\sqrt{e^{\mathsf{T}\times G^{\sigma}}\left((R-\operatorname{Lie}(G))^{\sigma\text{-moving}}\right)}}\in H^{\mathsf{T}\times G^{\sigma}/\sigma(\mathbb{C}^{*})}(R^{\sigma},\mathsf{w})(t^{1/2}).

Consider the morphism
\widetilde{p}:\widetilde{L}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\to R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})
defined in Remark
4.21
. The
\sigma
-fixed locus of
\widetilde{L}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}
decomposes into connected components

\displaystyle\left(\widetilde{L}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\right)^{\sigma}=\bigsqcup_{w\in W^{\sigma}\backslash W/W^{\phi}}\left(\widetilde{L}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\right)^{\sigma}_{w},\qquad\left(\widetilde{L}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\right)^{\sigma}_{w}=G^{\sigma}\times^{P^{\phi,\sigma}_{\mathfrak{C},w}}\left(L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\right)^{w^{-1}(\sigma)},

where
W^{\sigma},W,W^{\phi}
are Weyl groups of
G^{\sigma},G,G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}
respectively,
w^{-1}(\sigma):=w^{-1}\cdot\sigma\cdot w
is a cocharacter of
G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}
, and

P^{\phi,\sigma}_{\mathfrak{C},w}=G^{\sigma}\bigcap wP^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}w^{-1},

which acts on
\left(L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\right)^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}
via
g\mapsto w^{-1}gw\curvearrowright\left(L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\right)^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}
.

Denote the induced maps between
\sigma
-fixed loci:

\displaystyle\widetilde{p}^{\sigma}_{w}:\left(\widetilde{L}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\right)^{\sigma}_{w}\to R^{\sigma},\quad q^{\sigma}_{w}\colon\left(L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\right)^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}\to\left(R^{\mathsf{A},\phi}\right)^{w^{-1}(\sigma)},

and the induced maps between quotient stacks:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{q}^{\sigma}_{w}\colon\left[\frac{\left(L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\right)^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}}{P^{\phi,\sigma}_{\mathfrak{C},w}}\right]\longrightarrow\left[\frac{\left(R^{\mathsf{A},\phi}\right)^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}}{(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}}\right],\quad\mathbf{j}^{\phi}_{w^{-1}(\sigma)}\colon\left[\frac{\left(R^{\mathsf{A},\phi}\right)^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}}{(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}}\right]\longrightarrow\left[\frac{R^{\mathsf{A},\phi}}{G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}}\right].

Applying equivariant localization to the morphism
\widetilde{p}:\widetilde{L}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\to R
with respect to equivariant parameter
t
:

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathbf{j}}_{\sigma}^{*}\circ\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{H}^{\phi}(\gamma)}{e^{\mathsf{T}\times G^{\sigma}}\left(N_{R^{\sigma}/R}\right)}=\sum_{w\in W^{\sigma}\backslash W/W^{\phi}}\widetilde{p}^{\sigma}_{w*}\left(\frac{\mathrm{ind}^{G^{\sigma}}_{P^{\phi,\sigma}_{\mathfrak{C},w}}\circ\mathfrak{q}^{\sigma*}_{w}\circ\mathbf{j}^{\phi*}_{w^{-1}(\sigma)}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{H}^{\phi}(\gamma)}{e^{\mathsf{T}\times G^{\sigma}}\left(N_{\left(\widetilde{L}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\right)^{\sigma}_{w}/\widetilde{L}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{w\in W^{\sigma}\backslash W/W^{\phi}}\widetilde{p}^{\sigma}_{w*}\circ\mathrm{ind}^{G^{\sigma}}_{P^{\phi,\sigma}_{\mathfrak{C},w}}\left(\frac{\mathfrak{q}^{\sigma*}_{w}\circ\mathbf{j}^{\phi*}_{w^{-1}(\sigma)}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{H}^{\phi}(\gamma)}{e^{\mathsf{T}\times P^{\phi,\sigma}_{\mathfrak{C},w}}\left(N_{\left(\widetilde{L}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\right)^{\sigma}_{w}/\widetilde{L}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}}\bigg|_{\left(L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\right)^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{w\in W^{\sigma}\backslash W/W^{\phi}}\widetilde{p}^{\sigma}_{w*}\circ\mathrm{ind}^{G^{\sigma}}_{P^{\phi,\sigma}_{\mathfrak{C},w}}\circ\mathfrak{q}^{\sigma*}_{w}\left(\frac{\mathbf{j}^{\phi*}_{w^{-1}(\sigma)}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{H}^{\phi}(\gamma)}{e^{\mathsf{T}\times(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}}\left((L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}-\operatorname{Lie}(P^{\phi,\sigma}_{\mathfrak{C},w})+\operatorname{Lie}(G))^{w^{-1}(\sigma)\text{-moving}}\right)}\right).

Then to prove (
4.24
) for a given cocharacter
\sigma
, it is enough to show that

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\frac{\sqrt{e^{(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}}\left((N_{R^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}/R}+\operatorname{Lie}(G))^{w^{-1}(\sigma)\text{-moving}}\right)}\cdot\mathbf{j}^{\phi*}_{w^{-1}(\sigma)}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{H}^{\phi}(\gamma)}{e^{(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}}\left((L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}-\operatorname{Lie}(P^{\phi,\sigma}_{\mathfrak{C},w})+\operatorname{Lie}(G))^{w^{-1}(\sigma)\text{-moving}}\right)}=0\,\,\text{ for all $w\in W^{\sigma}\backslash W/W^{\phi}$}.

Here we have used the following facts:

\displaystyle\deg_{w^{-1}(\sigma)}e^{(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}}\left((N_{R^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}/R}+\operatorname{Lie}(G))^{w^{-1}(\sigma)\text{-moving}}\right)=\deg_{\sigma}e^{(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{\sigma}}\left((N_{R^{\sigma}/R}+\operatorname{Lie}(G))^{\sigma\text{-moving}}\right).

Since

\displaystyle\deg_{w^{-1}(\sigma)}e^{(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}}\left((N_{R^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}/R}+\operatorname{Lie}(G))^{w^{-1}(\sigma)\text{-moving}}\right)=\deg_{w^{-1}(\sigma)}e^{(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}}\left((R+\operatorname{Lie}(G))^{w^{-1}(\sigma)\text{-moving}}\right),

and

\displaystyle\quad\,\deg_{w^{-1}(\sigma)}e^{(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}}\left((L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}-\operatorname{Lie}(P^{\phi,\sigma}_{\mathfrak{C},w})+\operatorname{Lie}(G))^{w^{-1}(\sigma)\text{-moving}}\right)=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\deg_{w^{-1}(\sigma)}e^{(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}}\left((R+\operatorname{Lie}(G))^{w^{-1}(\sigma)\text{-moving}}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\deg_{w^{-1}(\sigma)}e^{(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}}\left((R^{\mathsf{A},\phi}-\operatorname{Lie}(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}))^{w^{-1}(\sigma)\text{-moving}}\right),

it remains to show that

(4.25)

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\frac{\mathbf{j}^{\phi*}_{w^{-1}(\sigma)}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{H}^{\phi}(\gamma)}{\sqrt{e^{(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}}\left((R^{\mathsf{A},\phi}-\operatorname{Lie}(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}))^{w^{-1}(\sigma)\text{-moving}}\right)}}=0\,\,\text{ for all $w\in W^{\sigma}\backslash W/W^{\phi}$}.

By Theorem
4.19
, we have

\displaystyle\deg_{w^{-1}(\sigma)}\mathbf{j}^{\phi*}_{w^{-1}(\sigma)}\mathbf{\Psi}^{\phi}(\gamma)<\frac{1}{2}\deg_{w^{-1}(\sigma)}e^{(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}}\left((R^{\mathsf{A},\phi}-\operatorname{Lie}(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}))^{w^{-1}(\sigma)\text{-moving}}\right),

then (
4.25
) follows from the above degree bound.

4.9.
Explicit formulas of cohomological stable envelopes

Theorem
4.22
provides a tool to produce explicit formulas of cohomological stable envelopes for symmetric GIT quotient.
As critical cohomology lacks a fundamental class in general, in the following corollary, we restrict to the case when
\mathsf{w}=0
, which reduces to the ordinary BM homology. Results in this section will be used to calculate examples in
[
COZZ1
]
.

Corollary 4.25
.

Assume
\mathsf{w}=0
, then we have

(4.26)

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Psi}_{H}\circ\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}([X^{\mathsf{A},\phi}])=\sum_{w\in W/W^{\phi}}w\left(e^{\mathsf{T}\times G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}}\left(T\mathfrak{X}^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-repl}}\right)\right)\cdot[\mathfrak{X}]\>,

in particular,

\displaystyle\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}([X^{\mathsf{A},\phi}])=\sum_{w\in W/W^{\phi}}w\left(e^{\mathsf{T}\times G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}}\left(T\mathfrak{X}^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-repl}}\right)\right)\cdot[X]\>.

Here
W
,
W^{\phi}
are Weyl groups of
G
,
G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}
respectively,

T\mathfrak{X}=R-\operatorname{Lie}(G)
and
(-)^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-repl}}
is the repelling part with respect to homomorphism
\phi\colon\mathsf{A}\to G
, and
w
acts on a weight
\mu
of
G
by
w(\mu)(g):=\mu(w^{-1}\cdot g\cdot w)
.

Proof.

By definition of nonabelian stable envelope, we have

\mathbf{\Psi}^{\phi}_{H}([X^{\mathsf{A},\phi}])=[\mathfrak{X}^{\mathsf{A},\phi}].

Then (
4.26
) follow from Theorem
4.22
and explicit formulas of cohomological Hall operations in
[
KS
, Thm. 2]
.
∎

5.
K
-theoretic stable envelopes on symmetric GIT quotients

In this section, we show that
K
-theoretic stable envelopes exist for symmetric GIT quotients with any
\mathsf{T}
-invariant potential functions (Theorem
5.5
).
The proof makes use of nonabelian stable envelopes (see Definition
5.2
, and Theorem
5.3
for a characterization property)
and Hall operations, similarly as in the previous section.
As a side application, we derive an explicit formula of
K
-theoretic stable envelopes when potentials are zero (Corollary
5.6
).

5.1.
Window category and nonabelian stable envelope

We use the same notations as in Definition
4.1
.

Definition 5.1
.

Let
\mathfrak{X}=[R/G]
,
\mathsf{s}\in\mathrm{Char}(G)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{R}
be a
generic slope
, that is, the projection of
\mathsf{s}
to
\mathrm{Char}(H)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{R}
is not integral for any subgroup
H\subseteq G
. The
window subcategory
of
\operatorname{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathfrak{X})
with slope
\mathsf{s}
is the full triangulated dg-subcategory
\mathbb{M}^{\mathsf{s}}
in
\operatorname{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathfrak{X})
generated by
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}}\otimes U
, where
U
is an irreducible
G
-module whose characters lie in the polytope

\frac{1}{2}\deg_{G}e^{G}_{K}(R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})-\operatorname{Lie}(G))+\operatorname{weight}_{G}\mathsf{s}.

Let
\mathsf{w}\colon\mathfrak{X}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
be a function, the
window subcategory
of
\mathrm{MF}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathfrak{X},\mathsf{w})
with slope
\mathsf{s}
is the full subcategory
\mathrm{MF}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathbb{M}^{\mathsf{s}},\mathsf{w})
in
\mathrm{MF}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathfrak{X},\mathsf{w})
generated by matrix factorizations
(\mathcal{E}_{\bullet},d_{\bullet})
with
\mathcal{E}_{0},\mathcal{E}_{1}\in\mathbb{M}^{\mathsf{s}}
.

It is shown in
[
HS
, Thm. 1.2]
that the restriction functor

\mathbb{M}^{\mathsf{s}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}\operatorname{D}^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}(X)

is an equivalence of triangulated dg-categories. Then the restriction functor

\mathrm{MF}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathbb{M}^{\mathsf{s}},\mathsf{w})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}\mathrm{MF}_{\mathrm{coh}}(X,\mathsf{w})

is an equivalence of triangulated dg-categories. Passing to Grothendieck’s group, we get an isomorphism

K_{0}(\mathrm{MF}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathbb{M}^{\mathsf{s}},\mathsf{w}))\cong K(X,\mathsf{w}).

Note that
[
HS
, Thm. 1.2]
holds in the
\mathsf{T}
-equivariant case (mentioned in the proof
[
HS
, Cor. 5.2]
), so the above statements still hold in the
\mathsf{T}
-equivariant version.

Definition 5.2
.

The inverse of the above isomorphism followed by the natural map
K_{0}(\mathrm{MF}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathbb{M}^{\mathsf{s}},\mathsf{w}))\to K(\mathfrak{X},\mathsf{w})
induces a map

(5.1)

\mathbf{\Psi}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{X},\mathsf{w}),

which we call the (
K
-theoretic)
nonabelian stable envelope
.

By construction we have
\mathrm{res}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}=\operatorname{id}
, where

\mathrm{res}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{X},\mathsf{w})\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})

is the restriction to the stable locus. Moreover, we have the following analog of Theorem
4.19
.

Theorem 5.3
.

\mathbf{\Psi}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}
is the unique
K_{\mathsf{T}}(\operatorname{pt})
-linear map from
K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})
to
K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{X},\mathsf{w})
that satisfies the following:

•

\mathrm{res}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}=\operatorname{id}
,

•

for all nonzero cocharacter
\sigma:\mathbb{C}^{*}\to G
and all
\gamma\in K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})
, there is a strict inclusion of polytopes

\displaystyle\deg_{\sigma}\mathbf{j}^{*}_{\sigma}\mathbf{\Psi}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}(\gamma)\subsetneq\frac{1}{2}\deg_{\sigma}e^{G}_{K}(R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})-\operatorname{Lie}(G))+\operatorname{weight}_{\sigma}\mathsf{s},

where
\mathbf{j}_{\sigma}:[R^{\sigma}/G^{\sigma}]\to\mathfrak{X}
is the natural morphism between quotient stacks.

Proof.

By the construction of window categories,
\mathbf{\Psi}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}
satisfies the two conditions. The uniqueness is proven similarly as Theorem
4.19
and we omit the details.
∎

5.2.
Hall operations

Definition 5.4
.

Consider the diagram (
4.13
), and we define the
K
-theoretic
Hall operations

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}:=\mathfrak{p}_{*}\circ\mathfrak{q}^{*}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{X}^{\mathsf{A},\phi},\mathsf{w})\longrightarrow K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{X},\mathsf{w}).

Theorem 5.5
.

Let
\mathsf{s}\in\mathrm{Char}(G)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{R}
be a generic slope. Then the composition

\displaystyle\mathrm{res}\circ\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{K}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A},\phi},\mathsf{w})\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})

is a
K
-theoretic stable envelope with slope
\mathsf{s}
, where
\mathbf{\Psi}_{K}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}
is the
K
-theoretic nonabelian stable envelope for the stack
\mathfrak{X}^{\mathsf{A},\phi}
with the slope

\displaystyle\mathsf{s}^{\prime}=\mathsf{s}\otimes\det\left(T\mathfrak{X}^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-repl}}\right)^{1/2}\in\mathrm{Char}(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{R}.

In the above,
T\mathfrak{X}^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-repl}}
is the
\mathfrak{C}
-repelling part of
T\mathfrak{X}=R-\operatorname{Lie}(G)
with respect to
\mathsf{A}
-action via
\mathsf{A}\xrightarrow{(\phi,\operatorname{id})}G\times\mathsf{A}
.
Moreover, the following diagram is commutative

(5.2)

\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{X}^{\mathsf{A},\phi},\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{X},\mathsf{w})}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A},\phi},\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{\Psi}_{K}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w}).\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{\Psi}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}}

Proof.

We claim that the image of
\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{K}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}
is contained in the image of
\mathbf{\Psi}_{K}^{\mathsf{s}}
. By Theorem
5.3
, it is enough to show:

•

for all nonzero cocharacter
\sigma:\mathbb{C}^{*}\to G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}
and all
\gamma\in K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})
, there is a strict inclusion of polytopes

\displaystyle\deg_{\sigma}\mathbf{j}^{*}_{\sigma}\circ\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{K}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}(\gamma)\subsetneq\frac{1}{2}\deg_{\sigma}e^{G}_{K}(R-\operatorname{Lie}(G))+\operatorname{weight}_{\sigma}\mathsf{s}.

For a vector bundle
V
, denote

(5.3)

\displaystyle\hat{e}^{G}_{K}(V):=\det(V)^{1/2}\cdot e^{G}_{K}(V),

and extend the definition to
K
-theory class by setting

\hat{e}^{G}_{K}(V_{1}-V_{2})=\hat{e}^{G}_{K}(V_{1})/\hat{e}^{G}_{K}(V_{2})

for vector bundles
V_{1},V_{2}
. Then the degree condition can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\deg_{\sigma}\mathbf{j}^{*}_{\sigma}\circ\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{K}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}(\gamma)\subsetneq\frac{1}{2}\deg_{\sigma}\hat{e}^{G}_{K}(N_{R^{\sigma}/R}-\operatorname{Lie}(G))+\operatorname{weight}_{\sigma}\mathsf{s},

equivalently the limits

(5.4)

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to 0,\infty}\frac{{\mathbf{j}}_{\sigma}^{*}\circ\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{K}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}(\gamma)}{\sqrt{\hat{e}^{G^{\sigma}}_{K}\left(N_{R^{\sigma}/R}-\operatorname{Lie}(G)\right)}\cdot\sigma^{*}(\mathsf{s})}\,\,\text{ exist for all cocharacters $\sigma:\mathbb{C}^{*}\to G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}$},

where
t
is the equivariant parameter in
K_{\mathbb{C}^{*}}(\operatorname{pt})=\mathbb{Q}[t^{\pm}]
and

\frac{{\mathbf{j}}_{\sigma}^{*}\circ\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{K}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}(\gamma)}{\sqrt{\hat{e}^{G^{\sigma}}_{K}\left(N_{R^{\sigma}/R}-\operatorname{Lie}(G)\right)}\cdot\sigma^{*}(\mathsf{s})}\in K^{\mathsf{T}\times G^{\sigma}/\sigma(\mathbb{C}^{*})}(R^{\sigma},\mathsf{w})(t^{1/2}).

Consider the morphism
\widetilde{p}:\widetilde{L}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\to R
in Remark
4.21
. The
\sigma
-fixed locus of
\widetilde{L}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}
decomposes into connected components

\displaystyle\left(\widetilde{L}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\right)^{\sigma}=\bigsqcup_{w\in W^{\sigma}\backslash W/W^{\phi}}\left(\widetilde{L}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\right)^{\sigma}_{w},\qquad\left(\widetilde{L}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\right)^{\sigma}_{w}=G^{\sigma}\times^{P^{\phi,\sigma}_{\mathfrak{C},w}}\left(L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\right)^{w^{-1}(\sigma)},

where
W^{\sigma},W,W^{\phi}
are Weyl groups of
G^{\sigma},G,G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}
respectively,
w^{-1}(\sigma):=w^{-1}\cdot\sigma\cdot w
is a cocharacter of
G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}
, and

P^{\phi,\sigma}_{\mathfrak{C},w}=G^{\sigma}\bigcap wP^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}w^{-1},

which acts on
\left(L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\right)^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}
via
g\mapsto w^{-1}gw\curvearrowright\left(L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\right)^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}
.

Denote the induced maps between
\sigma
-fixed loci:

\displaystyle\widetilde{p}^{\sigma}_{w}\colon\left(\widetilde{L}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\right)^{\sigma}_{w}\to R^{\sigma},\quad q^{\sigma}_{w}\colon\left(L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\right)^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}\to\left(R^{\mathsf{A},\phi}\right)^{w^{-1}(\sigma)},

and the induced maps between quotient stacks:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{q}^{\sigma}_{w}\colon\left[\frac{\left(L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\right)^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}}{P^{\phi,\sigma}_{\mathfrak{C},w}}\right]\longrightarrow\left[\frac{\left(R^{\mathsf{A},\phi}\right)^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}}{(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}}\right],\quad\mathbf{j}^{\phi}_{w^{-1}(\sigma)}\colon\left[\frac{\left(R^{\mathsf{A},\phi}\right)^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}}{(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}}\right]\longrightarrow\left[\frac{R^{\mathsf{A},\phi}}{G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}}\right].

Applying equivariant localization to the morphism
\widetilde{p}:\widetilde{L}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\to R
with respect to equivariant parameter
t
:

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathbf{j}}_{\sigma}^{*}\circ\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{K}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}(\gamma)}{e^{\mathsf{T}\times G^{\sigma}}_{K}\left(N_{R^{\sigma}/R}\right)}=\sum_{w\in W^{\sigma}\backslash W/W^{\phi}}\widetilde{p}^{\sigma}_{w*}\left(\frac{\mathrm{ind}^{G^{\sigma}}_{P^{\phi,\sigma}_{\mathfrak{C},w}}\circ\mathfrak{q}^{\sigma*}_{w}\circ\mathbf{j}^{\phi*}_{w^{-1}(\sigma)}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{K}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}(\gamma)}{e^{\mathsf{T}\times G^{\sigma}}_{K}\left(N_{\left(\widetilde{L}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\right)^{\sigma}_{w}/\widetilde{L}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{w\in W^{\sigma}\backslash W/W^{\phi}}\widetilde{p}^{\sigma}_{w*}\circ\mathrm{ind}^{G^{\sigma}}_{P^{\phi,\sigma}_{\mathfrak{C},w}}\left(\frac{\mathfrak{q}^{\sigma*}_{w}\circ\mathbf{j}^{\phi*}_{w^{-1}(\sigma)}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{K}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}(\gamma)}{e^{\mathsf{T}\times P^{\phi,\sigma}_{\mathfrak{C},w}}_{K}\left(N_{\left(\widetilde{L}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\right)^{\sigma}_{w}/\widetilde{L}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}}\bigg|_{\left(L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\right)^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{w\in W^{\sigma}\backslash W/W^{\phi}}\widetilde{p}^{\sigma}_{w*}\circ\mathrm{ind}^{G^{\sigma}}_{P^{\phi,\sigma}_{\mathfrak{C},w}}\circ\mathfrak{q}^{\sigma*}_{w}\left(\frac{\mathbf{j}^{\phi*}_{w^{-1}(\sigma)}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{K}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}(\gamma)}{e^{\mathsf{T}\times(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}}_{K}\left((L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}-\operatorname{Lie}(P^{\phi,\sigma}_{\mathfrak{C},w})+\operatorname{Lie}(G))^{w^{-1}(\sigma)\text{-moving}}\right)}\right).

Note that by self-duality, we have

e^{\mathsf{T}\times G^{\sigma}}_{K}\left(N_{R^{\sigma}/R}\right)=\hat{e}^{\mathsf{T}\times G^{\sigma}}_{K}\left(N_{R^{\sigma}/R}\right)\cdot(\text{some $\mathsf{T}/\mathsf{A}$-character}).

Then to prove (
5.4
) for a given cocharacter
\sigma
, it is enough to show that

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to 0,\infty}\frac{\sqrt{\hat{e}^{(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}}_{K}\left(N_{R^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}/R}+\operatorname{Lie}(G)\right)}\cdot\mathbf{j}^{\phi*}_{w^{-1}(\sigma)}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{K}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}(\gamma)}{e^{(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}}_{K}\left((L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}-\operatorname{Lie}(P^{\phi,\sigma}_{\mathfrak{C},w})+\operatorname{Lie}(G))^{w^{-1}(\sigma)\text{-moving}}\right)\cdot w^{-1}(\sigma)^{*}(\mathsf{s})}\,\,\text{ exist for all $w\in W^{\sigma}\backslash W/W^{\phi}$}.

Here we have used the following facts:
w^{-1}(\sigma)^{*}(\mathsf{s})=\sigma^{*}(\mathsf{s})
since
\mathsf{s}
is Weyl-invariant, and

\displaystyle\deg_{w^{-1}(\sigma)}\hat{e}^{(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}}_{K}\left(N_{R^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}/R}+\operatorname{Lie}(G)\right)=\deg_{\sigma}\hat{e}^{(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{\sigma}}_{K}\left(N_{R^{\sigma}/R}+\operatorname{Lie}(G)\right).

According to the definition of
\mathsf{s}^{\prime}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{s}^{\prime}=\mathsf{s}\otimes\det\left(L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}-\operatorname{Lie}(P^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}})\right)^{-1/2},

and

\displaystyle\quad\,\,e^{(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}}_{K}\left((L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}-\operatorname{Lie}(P^{\phi,\sigma}_{\mathfrak{C},w})+\operatorname{Lie}(G))^{w^{-1}(\sigma)\text{-moving}}\right)\cdot w^{-1}(\sigma)^{*}(\mathsf{s})

\displaystyle=\hat{e}^{(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}}_{K}\left((L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}-\operatorname{Lie}(P^{\phi,\sigma}_{\mathfrak{C},w})+\operatorname{Lie}(G))^{w^{-1}(\sigma)\text{-moving}}\right)\cdot w^{-1}(\sigma)^{*}(\mathsf{s}^{\prime}).

Since

\displaystyle\deg_{w^{-1}(\sigma)}\hat{e}^{(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}}_{K}\left(N_{R^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}/R}+\operatorname{Lie}(G)\right)=\deg_{w^{-1}(\sigma)}\hat{e}^{(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}}_{K}\left(R+\operatorname{Lie}(G)\right),

and

\displaystyle\quad\,\deg_{w^{-1}(\sigma)}\hat{e}^{(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}}_{K}\left((L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}-\operatorname{Lie}(P^{\phi,\sigma}_{\mathfrak{C},w})+\operatorname{Lie}(G))^{w^{-1}(\sigma)\text{-moving}}\right)

\displaystyle=\deg_{w^{-1}(\sigma)}\hat{e}^{(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}}_{K}\left(L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}-\operatorname{Lie}(P^{\phi,\sigma}_{\mathfrak{C},w})+\operatorname{Lie}(G)\right),

it remains to show that

(5.5)

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to 0,\infty}\frac{\mathbf{j}^{\phi*}_{w^{-1}(\sigma)}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{K}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}(\gamma)}{\sqrt{\hat{e}^{(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}}_{K}\left(R^{\mathsf{A},\phi}-\operatorname{Lie}(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})\right)}\cdot w^{-1}(\sigma)^{*}(\mathsf{s}^{\prime})}\,\,\text{ exist for all $w\in W^{\sigma}\backslash W/W^{\phi}$}.

By Theorem
5.3
, we have

\displaystyle\deg_{w^{-1}(\sigma)}\mathbf{j}^{\phi*}_{w^{-1}(\sigma)}\mathbf{\Psi}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{K}(\gamma)\subseteq\frac{1}{2}\deg_{w^{-1}(\sigma)}e^{G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}}_{K}(R^{\mathsf{A},\phi}-\operatorname{Lie}(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}))+\operatorname{weight}_{w^{-1}(\sigma)}\mathsf{s}^{\prime},

then (
5.5
) follows from the above degree bound. This proves our claim.

In the remainder of the proof, we need to show that
\mathrm{res}\circ\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{K}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}
satisfies the axioms of
K
-theoretic stable envelopes.

The image of
\mathfrak{p}:\mathfrak{L}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\to\mathfrak{X}
is a closed substack of
\mathfrak{X}
and it contains

\mathfrak{p}(\mathfrak{q}^{-1}(X^{\mathsf{A},\phi}))=\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(X^{\mathsf{A},\phi})

as dense substack. Then
\mathrm{res}\circ\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{K}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}
is supported on
\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(X^{\mathsf{A},\phi})\subseteq\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}(X^{\mathsf{A},\phi})
. This verifies the support axiom (Definition
3.10
(i)).
Moreover, we have

\mathfrak{p}^{-1}(\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(X^{\mathsf{A},\phi}))=\mathfrak{q}^{-1}(X^{\mathsf{A},\phi}),

where
\mathfrak{p}
induces an isomorphism
\mathfrak{q}^{-1}(X^{\mathsf{A},\phi})\cong\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(X^{\mathsf{A},\phi})
, and
\mathfrak{q}:\mathfrak{q}^{-1}(X^{\mathsf{A},\phi})\to X^{\mathsf{A},\phi}
is given by the attraction map. In particular, we have the following Cartesian diagram

\textstyle{X^{\mathsf{A},\phi}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{k}^{\phi}}
\scriptstyle{\Box}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(X^{\mathsf{A},\phi})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a}
\scriptstyle{i}
\scriptstyle{\Box}
\textstyle{U\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{k}\circ u}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{X}^{\mathsf{A},\phi}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathfrak{L}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{q}}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{p}}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{X},}

where
\mathbf{k}\colon X\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{X}
and
\mathbf{k}^{\phi}\colon X^{\mathsf{A},\phi}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{X}^{\mathsf{A},\phi}
are the open immersions of the stable loci,
a
is the attraction map,

\displaystyle u\colon U=X\setminus\left(\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}(X^{\mathsf{A},\phi})\setminus\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(X^{\mathsf{A},\phi})\right)\hookrightarrow X

is the natural open immersion, and
i
is the natural closed immersion. It follows that

\displaystyle u^{*}\circ\mathrm{res}\circ\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{K}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}=i_{*}\circ a^{*}\circ\mathbf{k}^{\phi*}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{K}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}=i_{*}\circ a^{*}.

Thus

\mathrm{res}\circ\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{K}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}(\gamma)\big|_{X^{\mathsf{A},\phi}}=i_{*}\circ a^{*}(\gamma)\big|_{X^{\mathsf{A},\phi}}=e(N^{-}_{X^{\mathsf{A},\phi}/X})\cdot\gamma

for all
\gamma\in K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A},\phi},\mathsf{w})
. This verifies the normalization axiom (Definition
3.10
(ii)).

Finally, we verify the degree axiom (Definition
3.10
(iii)) as follows. Let
\phi^{\prime}:\mathsf{A}\to G
be a homomorphism that is not equivalent to
\phi
. We assume that
X^{\mathsf{A},\phi^{\prime}}
is nonempty. Since
X^{\mathsf{A},\phi^{\prime}}=X^{\mathsf{A},g\phi^{\prime}g^{-1}}
for any
g\in G
, we can assume that
\phi^{\prime}(\mathsf{A})
lies in
G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}
without loss of generality. The degree axiom can be restated as a strict inclusion of polytopes

\deg_{\mathsf{A}}\mathrm{res}\circ\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{K}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}(\gamma)\big|_{X^{\mathsf{A},\phi^{\prime}}}\subsetneq\deg_{\mathsf{A}}\hat{e}^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}\left(N_{X^{\mathsf{A},\phi^{\prime}}/X}^{-}\right)+\operatorname{weight}_{\mathsf{A}}\phi^{\prime*}(\mathsf{s})-\operatorname{weight}_{\mathsf{A}}\phi^{*}(\mathsf{s})-\operatorname{weight}_{\mathsf{A}}\det\left(N_{X^{\mathsf{A},\phi}/X}^{-}\right)^{1/2},

for all
\gamma\in K^{\mathsf{T}/\mathsf{A}}(X^{\mathsf{A},\phi},\mathsf{w})
.

Consider the natural morphism
\mathbf{j}^{\phi^{\prime}}:[R^{\mathsf{A},\phi^{\prime}}/G^{\phi^{\prime}(\mathsf{A})}]\to\mathfrak{X}
, which induces an isomorphism between the stable locus of
[R^{\mathsf{A},\phi^{\prime}}/G^{\phi^{\prime}(\mathsf{A})}]
and
X^{\mathsf{A},\phi^{\prime}}
. Note that

\displaystyle\deg_{\mathsf{A}}\hat{e}^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}\left(N_{X^{\mathsf{A},\phi^{\prime}}/X}^{-}\right)=\frac{1}{2}\deg_{\mathsf{A}}\hat{e}^{\mathsf{T}\times G^{\phi^{\prime}(\mathsf{A})}}_{K}\left(N_{R^{\mathsf{A},\phi^{\prime}}/R}-\operatorname{Lie}(G)\right),

where
\mathsf{A}
acts on
N_{R^{\mathsf{A},\phi^{\prime}}/R}-\operatorname{Lie}(G)
via the homomorphism
\mathsf{A}\xrightarrow{(\phi^{\prime},\operatorname{id})}G^{\phi^{\prime}(\mathsf{A})}\times\mathsf{A}
and the latter
\mathsf{A}
-action factors through the flavour group
\mathsf{T}
. We also note that

\displaystyle\operatorname{weight}_{\mathsf{A}}\phi^{*}(\mathsf{s})+\operatorname{weight}_{\mathsf{A}}\det(N_{X^{\mathsf{A},\phi}/X}^{-})^{1/2}=\operatorname{weight}_{\mathsf{A}}\phi^{*}(\mathsf{s}^{\prime})+\operatorname{weight}_{\mathsf{A}}\det\left(L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}-\operatorname{Lie}(P^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}})\right)^{-1/2},

where
\mathsf{A}
action on
\det\left(L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}-\operatorname{Lie}(P^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}})\right)^{-1/2}
factors through the flavour group
\mathsf{T}
. In the following discussions we denote this character by
\det_{\mathrm{flav}}\left(L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}-\operatorname{Lie}(P^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}})\right)^{-1/2}
. Then the degree axiom follows from the following condition:

(5.6)

\begin{split}\lim\frac{\phi^{*}(\mathsf{s}^{\prime})\cdot\det_{\mathrm{flav}}\left(L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}-\operatorname{Lie}(P^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}})\right)^{-1/2}\cdot\mathbf{j}^{\phi^{\prime}*}\circ\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{K}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}(\gamma)}{\phi^{\prime*}(\mathsf{s})\cdot\sqrt{\hat{e}^{\mathsf{T}\times G^{\phi^{\prime}(\mathsf{A})}}_{K}\left(N_{R^{\mathsf{A},\phi^{\prime}}/R}-\operatorname{Lie}(G)\right)}}\\
\text{exist in all directions of taking equivariant parameter $\mathsf{a}\to\infty$.}\end{split}

Consider the morphism
\widetilde{p}:\widetilde{L}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\to R
defined in Remark
4.21
. Let
\mathsf{A}
act on
\widetilde{L}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}
and
R
by the homomorphism
\mathsf{A}\xrightarrow{(\phi^{\prime},\operatorname{id})}G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}\times\mathsf{A}
. The
\mathsf{A}
-fixed locus, denoted by
\left(\widetilde{L}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\right)^{\phi^{\prime}}
, decomposes into connected components

\displaystyle\left(\widetilde{L}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\right)^{\phi^{\prime}}=\bigsqcup_{w\in W^{\phi^{\prime}}\backslash W/W^{\phi}}\left(\widetilde{L}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\right)^{\phi^{\prime}}_{w},\qquad\left(\widetilde{L}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\right)^{\phi^{\prime}}_{w}=G^{\phi^{\prime}(\mathsf{A})}\times^{P^{\phi,\phi^{\prime}}_{\mathfrak{C},w}}\left(L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\right)^{\mathsf{A},w^{-1}(\phi^{\prime})},

where
W^{\phi^{\prime}},W,W^{\phi}
are Weyl groups of
G^{\phi^{\prime}(\mathsf{A})},G,G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}
respectively,
w^{-1}(\phi^{\prime}):=w^{-1}\cdot\phi^{\prime}\cdot w
is a homomorphism from
\mathsf{A}
to
G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}
, and

P^{\phi,\phi^{\prime}}_{\mathfrak{C},w}=G^{\phi^{\prime}(\mathsf{A})}\bigcap wP^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}w^{-1},

which acts on
\left(L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\right)^{\mathsf{A},w^{-1}(\phi^{\prime})}
via
g\mapsto w^{-1}gw\curvearrowright\left(L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\right)^{\mathsf{A},w^{-1}(\phi^{\prime})}
.

Denote the induced maps between
\mathsf{A}
-fixed loci:

\displaystyle\widetilde{p}^{\phi^{\prime}}_{w}:\left(\widetilde{L}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\right)^{\phi^{\prime}}_{w}\to R^{\phi^{\prime}},\quad q^{\phi^{\prime}}_{w}\colon\left(L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\right)^{\mathsf{A},w^{-1}(\phi^{\prime})}\to\left(R^{\mathsf{A},\phi}\right)^{\mathsf{A},w^{-1}(\phi^{\prime})},

and the induced map between quotient stacks:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{q}^{\phi^{\prime}}_{w}\colon\left[\frac{\left(L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\right)^{\mathsf{A},w^{-1}(\phi^{\prime})}}{P^{\phi,{\phi^{\prime}}}_{\mathfrak{C},w}}\right]\longrightarrow\left[\frac{\left(R^{\mathsf{A},\phi}\right)^{\mathsf{A},w^{-1}(\phi^{\prime})}}{(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{\mathsf{A},w^{-1}(\phi^{\prime})}}\right],\quad\mathbf{j}^{\phi}_{\mathsf{A},w^{-1}(\phi^{\prime})}\colon\left[\frac{\left(R^{\mathsf{A},\phi}\right)^{\mathsf{A},w^{-1}(\phi^{\prime})}}{(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\phi^{\prime}(\mathsf{A}))}}\right]\longrightarrow\left[\frac{R^{\mathsf{A},\phi}}{G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}}\right].

Applying equivariant localization to the morphism
\widetilde{p}:\widetilde{L}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\to R
with respect to the
\mathsf{A}
action via
\mathsf{A}\xrightarrow{(\phi^{\prime},\operatorname{id})}G^{\phi^{\prime}(\mathsf{A})}\times\mathsf{A}
, we have

\displaystyle\quad\,\,\frac{{\mathbf{j}}^{\phi^{\prime}*}\circ\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{K}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}(\gamma)}{e^{\mathsf{T}\times G^{\phi^{\prime}(\mathsf{A})}}_{K}\left(N_{R^{\mathsf{A},\phi^{\prime}}/R}\right)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{w\in W^{\phi^{\prime}}\backslash W/W^{\phi}}\widetilde{p}^{\phi^{\prime}}_{w*}\circ\mathrm{ind}^{G^{\phi^{\prime}(\mathsf{A})}}_{P^{\phi,{\phi^{\prime}}}_{\mathfrak{C},w}}\circ\mathfrak{q}^{{\phi^{\prime}}*}_{w}\left(\frac{\mathbf{j}^{\phi*}_{\mathsf{A},w^{-1}({\phi^{\prime}})}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{K}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}(\gamma)}{e^{\mathsf{T}\times(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\phi^{\prime}(\mathsf{A}))}}_{K}\left((L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}-\operatorname{Lie}(P^{\phi,{\phi^{\prime}}}_{\mathfrak{C},w})+\operatorname{Lie}(G))^{\mathsf{A},w^{-1}(\phi^{\prime})\text{-moving}}\right)}\right).

Then to prove (
5.6
), it is enough to show that the limit of

\textstyle{\frac{\phi^{*}(\mathsf{s}^{\prime})\cdot\det_{\mathrm{flav}}\left(L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}-\operatorname{Lie}(P^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}})\right)^{-1/2}\cdot\sqrt{\hat{e}^{\mathsf{T}\times(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\phi^{\prime}(\mathsf{A}))}}_{K}\left(N_{R^{\mathsf{A},w^{-1}({\phi^{\prime}})}/R}+\operatorname{Lie}(G)\right)}}{w^{-1}({\phi^{\prime}})^{*}(\mathsf{s})\cdot e^{\mathsf{T}\times(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\phi^{\prime}(\mathsf{A}))}}_{K}\left((L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}-\operatorname{Lie}(P^{\phi,{\phi^{\prime}}}_{\mathfrak{C},w})+\operatorname{Lie}(G))^{\mathsf{A},w^{-1}({\phi^{\prime}})\text{-moving}}\right)}\times\mathbf{j}^{\phi*}_{\mathsf{A},w^{-1}({\phi^{\prime}})}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{K}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}(\gamma)}

exist for all
w\in W^{\phi^{\prime}}\backslash W/W^{\phi}
and all directions of
\mathsf{a}\to\infty
. We note that

\deg_{\mathsf{A}}e^{\mathsf{T}\times(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\phi^{\prime}(\mathsf{A}))}}_{K}\left((L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}-\operatorname{Lie}(P^{\phi,{\phi^{\prime}}}_{\mathfrak{C},w}))^{\mathsf{A},w^{-1}({\phi^{\prime}})\text{-moving}}\right)=\operatorname{weight}_{\mathsf{A}}{\det}_{(w^{-1}(\phi^{\prime}),\operatorname{id})}\left(L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}-\operatorname{Lie}(P^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}})\right)^{-1/2}\\
+\deg_{\mathsf{A}}\hat{e}^{\mathsf{T}\times(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\phi^{\prime}(\mathsf{A}))}}_{K}\left((L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}-\operatorname{Lie}(P^{\phi,{\phi^{\prime}}}_{\mathfrak{C},w}))^{\mathsf{A},w^{-1}({\phi^{\prime}})\text{-moving}}\right),

where
{\det}_{(w^{-1}(\phi^{\prime}),\operatorname{id})}\left(L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}-\operatorname{Lie}(P^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}})\right)^{-1/2}
is the
\mathsf{A}
-character induced by

\displaystyle\mathsf{A}\xrightarrow{(\phi^{\prime},\operatorname{id})}G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}\times\mathsf{A}\curvearrowright{\det}\left(L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}-\operatorname{Lie}(P^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}})\right)^{-1/2}.

We have equation between
\mathsf{A}
-characters

{\det}_{(w^{-1}(\phi^{\prime}),\operatorname{id})}\left(L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}-\operatorname{Lie}(P^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}})\right)^{-1/2}={\det}_{w^{-1}(\phi^{\prime})}\left(L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}-\operatorname{Lie}(P^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}})\right)^{-1/2}\cdot{\det}_{\mathrm{flav}}\left(L^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}-\operatorname{Lie}(P^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}})\right)^{-1/2}.

Therefore it remains to show that

(5.7)

\begin{split}\lim\frac{\phi^{*}(\mathsf{s}^{\prime})\cdot\mathbf{j}^{\phi*}_{\mathsf{A},w^{-1}({\phi^{\prime}})}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{K}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}(\gamma)}{w^{-1}({\phi^{\prime}})^{*}(\mathsf{s}^{\prime})\cdot\sqrt{\hat{e}^{\mathsf{T}\times(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\phi^{\prime}(\mathsf{A}))}}_{K}\left(R^{\mathsf{A},\phi}-\operatorname{Lie}(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})\right)}}\\
\text{exist for all $w\in W^{\phi^{\prime}}\backslash W/W^{\phi}$ and all directions of $\mathsf{a}\to\infty$.}\end{split}

Note that the action

\mathsf{A}\xrightarrow{(w^{-1}(\phi^{\prime}),\operatorname{id})}G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}\times\mathsf{A}\curvearrowright R^{\mathsf{A},\phi}-\operatorname{Lie}(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})

factors through
\mathsf{A}\xrightarrow{w^{-1}(\phi^{\prime})/\phi}G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}
, where
w^{-1}(\phi^{\prime})/\phi
is the multiplicative way of writing the difference between two homomorphisms
w^{-1}(\phi^{\prime})
and
\phi
.
By Theorem
5.3
, we have

\displaystyle\deg_{\mathsf{A}}\mathbf{j}^{\phi*}_{\mathsf{A},w^{-1}(\phi^{\prime})}\mathbf{\Psi}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{K}(\gamma)\subsetneq\frac{1}{2}\deg_{\mathsf{A}}e^{\mathsf{T}\times G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}}_{K}(R^{\mathsf{A},\phi}-\operatorname{Lie}(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}))+\operatorname{weight}_{\mathsf{A}}(w^{-1}(\phi^{\prime})/\phi)^{*}(\mathsf{s}^{\prime}).

Then (
5.7
) follows from the above degree bound. This finishes the proof.
∎

5.3.
Explicit formulas of
K
-theoretic stable envelopes

Corollary 5.6
.

Let
\mathsf{s}\in\mathrm{char}(G)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{R}
be a generic slope and
\chi\in\mathrm{char}(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})
such that
\mathsf{s}\otimes\det\left(T\mathfrak{X}^{\mathsf{A},w(\phi)\text{-repl}}\right)^{1/2}
is in a sufficiently small neighbourhood of
\chi
. Assume
\mathsf{w}=0
, then

(5.8)

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Psi}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}\circ\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}([\mathcal{L}_{\chi}])=\sum_{w\in W/W^{\phi}}w\left(\chi\cdot e^{\mathsf{T}\times G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}}_{K}\left(T\mathfrak{X}^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-repl}}\right)\right)\cdot[\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}}]\>,

in particular,

\displaystyle\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}([\mathcal{L}_{\chi}])=\sum_{w\in W/W^{\phi}}w\left(\chi\cdot e^{\mathsf{T}\times G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}}_{K}\left(T\mathfrak{X}^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-repl}}\right)\right)\cdot[\mathcal{O}_{X}]\>.

Here
\mathcal{L}_{\chi}\in\operatorname{Pic}(X^{\mathsf{A},\phi})
is the descent of the character
\chi
,
W
,
W^{\phi}
are Weyl groups of
G
,
G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}
respectively,

T\mathfrak{X}=R-\operatorname{Lie}(G)
and
(-)^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-repl}}
is the repelling part with respect to homomorphism
\phi\colon\mathsf{A}\to G
, and
w
acts on a weight
\mu
of
G
by
w(\mu)(g):=\mu(w^{-1}\cdot g\cdot w)
.

Proof.

Using Theorem
5.5
, we see that

\mathbf{\Psi}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{K}([\mathcal{L}_{\chi}])=\chi\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}^{\mathsf{A},\phi}},

for the above choice of
\mathsf{s}^{\prime}
. Then (
5.8
) follows from Theorem
5.5
and explicit formula of
K
-theoretic Hall operations in
[
P
, Prop. 3.4]
.
∎

Remark 5.7
.

(1) When
X^{\mathsf{A},\phi}
is an affine space,
[X^{\mathsf{A},\phi}]
generates
H^{\mathsf{T}}([X^{\mathsf{A},\phi}])
over
H_{\mathsf{T}}(\operatorname{pt})
and
[\mathcal{L}_{\chi}]
generates
K^{\mathsf{T}}([X^{\mathsf{A},\phi}])
over
K_{\mathsf{T}}(\operatorname{pt})
. In this case, Corollaries
4.25
,
5.6
completely determine the formulas of
\mathbf{\Psi}_{H}\circ\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}
and
\mathbf{\Psi}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}\circ\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}
respectively. (2) We also expect an explicit formula in the setting of elliptic cohomology.

6.
Deformed dimensional reductions and stable envelopes

In this section, we introduce dimensional reduction data and their compatibility (Definition
6.2
, Proposition
6.4
).
Compatible dimensional reduction data have natural interpolation maps between their critical cohomology and
K
-theory, which are always isomorphisms
in the cohomological case (Proposition
6.9
), and are isomorphisms in the
K
-theoretic case when deformed dimensional reduction holds
(Proposition
6.8
).

We show that stable envelopes are compatible with the interpolation maps (Theorems
6.10
,
6.11
).
This provides flexible tools to compare critical theories and corresponding stable envelopes for different quiver models of the same critical loci.

We provide examples of compatible dimensional reduction data in §
6.3
. In particular, this implies that
(the critical) stable envelopes on tripled quivers with canonical cubic potentials reproduce stable envelopes of Nakajima quiver varieties.
More examples will be discussed in
[
COZZ1
]
.

6.1.
Dimensional reduction data

Consider the following situation. Let
Y
be a smooth quasi-projective
\mathsf{T}
-variety with a
\mathsf{T}
-equivariant vector bundle
E
, and let
X:=\mathrm{Tot}_{Y}(E)
with natural projection
\pi\colon X\to Y
. Assume that
\mathsf{w}\colon X\to\mathbb{C}
is a function of the form

\mathsf{w}=\langle e,s\rangle+\pi^{*}(\phi),

where
e
is the fiber coordinate of
E
,
s\in\Gamma(Y,E^{\vee})
is an

\mathsf{T}
-invariant section, and
\phi\colon Y\to\mathbb{C}
is a
\mathsf{T}
-invariant map. Note that the pairing
\langle e,s\rangle
is
\mathsf{T}
-invariant. We call the tuple
(X,Y,s,\phi)
a
dimensional reduction datum
. Let
Z(s)
be the classical vanishing locus of
s
in
Y
, and
Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s)
be the derived vanishing locus of
s
in
Y
. They fit into the following diagram

\textstyle{\pi^{-1}(Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt\,\,\mathbf{i}^{\mathrm{der}}}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\scriptstyle{\Box}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\scriptstyle{\Box}
\textstyle{\pi^{-1}(Z(s))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{i}\hskip 8.19447pt\,\,}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

where

) is the closed embedding of

Definition 6.1
.

Let
(X,Y,s,\phi)
be a dimensional reduction datum. We say that
cohomological deformed dimensional reduction
holds for the tuple
(X,Y,s,\phi)
if the composition

(6.1)

\displaystyle\mathbf{i}_{*}\circ\pi^{*}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(s),\phi)\cong H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})

is an isomorphism. We say that the
K
-
theoretic deformed dimensional reduction
holds for the tuple
(X,Y,s,\phi)
if the composition

(6.2)

\displaystyle\mathbf{i}^{\mathrm{der}}_{*}\circ\pi^{*}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s),\phi)\cong K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})

is an isomorphism.

Definition 6.2
.

We say that dimensional reduction data
(X,Y,s,\phi)
and
(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime},s^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})
are
compatible
if there exists

(1)

a
\mathsf{T}
-equivariant closed embedding
j\colon Y^{\prime}\hookrightarrow Y
,

(2)

a
\mathsf{T}
-equivariant surjective vector bundle map
\operatorname{pr}\colon E|_{Y^{\prime}}\twoheadrightarrow E^{\prime}
,

as in the following commutative diagram with
X^{\prime\prime}:=Y^{\prime}\times_{Y}X=\mathrm{Tot}_{Y^{\prime}}(E|_{Y^{\prime}})
:

(6.3)

\textstyle{X^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{\prime}}
\textstyle{X^{\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{pr}}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{j}}
\scriptstyle{\Box}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{Y^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j}
\textstyle{Y}

such that

(i)

\phi|_{Y^{\prime}}=\phi^{\prime}
,
s|_{Y^{\prime}}=\operatorname{pr}^{\vee}\circ s^{\prime}
.
11
11

11

This is equivalent to
\operatorname{pr}^{*}(\mathsf{w}^{\prime})=\mathsf{w}|_{X^{\prime\prime}}
.

(ii)

the natural map
Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s^{\prime})\to Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s)
induced by
\operatorname{pr}:j^{*}E\twoheadrightarrow E^{\prime}
is an isomorphism.

Here we explain how the map in (ii) is induced. It is known that

Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s)\cong\textbf{Spec}_{Y}\mathrm{Kos}(E,s),

for Koszul complex

\mathrm{Kos}(E,s)=\left(\bigwedge^{\operatorname{rk}E}E\to\cdots\to\bigwedge^{2}E\to E\overset{s}{\to}\mathcal{O}_{Y}\right),

which is a dg-algebra over
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
. The map
Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s^{\prime})\to Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s)
is given by the dg-algebra map

\mathrm{Kos}(E,s)\to j_{*}\mathrm{Kos}(E^{\prime},s^{\prime}),

which is induced by
\operatorname{pr}:j^{*}E\twoheadrightarrow E^{\prime}
.

We explain how to re-state condition (ii) in Definition
6.2

without
involving derived algebraic geometry.

Lemma 6.3
.

Suppose that
(X,Y,s,\phi)
and
(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime},s^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})
are two sets of dimensional reduction data, and assume that there exists maps (1) and (2) as in the Definition
6.2
which satisfy condition (i), and assume moreover that
Z(s^{\prime})
is not empty, then there exists a natural vector bundle map on
Z(s^{\prime})
:

(6.4)

\rho:\ker(\operatorname{pr})|_{Z(s^{\prime})}\to N^{\vee}_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{Z(s^{\prime})}.

Proof.

In fact, denote
\mathcal{K}:=\ker(\operatorname{pr})
. For arbitrary
y\in Z(s^{\prime})
, there exists an open neighbourhood
y\in U\subseteq Y
such that
\mathcal{K}|_{U\cap Y^{\prime}}
extends to a sub-bundle
\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{U}\subseteq E|_{U}
. Note that
s|_{U}
maps
\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{U}
to
I_{Y^{\prime}}|_{U}
, then we get

\displaystyle\rho_{U\cap Z(s^{\prime})}:\mathcal{K}|_{U\cap Z(s^{\prime})}=\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{U}/I_{Z(s^{\prime})}|_{U}\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{U}\longrightarrow(I_{Y^{\prime}}/I_{Z(s^{\prime})}\cdot I_{Y^{\prime}})|_{U\cap Z(s^{\prime})}=N^{\vee}_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{U\cap Z(s^{\prime})}.

The map
\rho_{U\cap Z(s^{\prime})}
does not depend on the choice of extension
\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{U}
because any other choice is different from a given one by a map
\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{U}\to I_{Y^{\prime}}|_{U}\cdot E|_{U}
and after restricting to
Z(s^{\prime})
, the image of this map is inside
I_{Z(s^{\prime})}\cdot I_{Y^{\prime}}|_{U\cap Z(s^{\prime})}
. Consequently, for a pair of open subset
U
and
V
,
\rho_{U\cap Z(s^{\prime})}|_{U\cap V\cap Z(s^{\prime})}=\rho_{V\cap Z(s^{\prime})}|_{U\cap V\cap Z(s^{\prime})}
. Therefore the family of maps
\{\rho_{U\cap Z(s^{\prime})}\}_{U}
glues to a map
(
6.4
).

An equivalent construction of (
6.4
) is given as follows. The map
\operatorname{pr}:j^{*}E\twoheadrightarrow E^{\prime}
induces two dg-algebra maps:

f:\mathrm{Kos}(E,s)\to j_{*}\mathrm{Kos}(E^{\prime},s^{\prime}),\quad g:\mathbf{L}j^{*}\mathrm{Kos}(E,s)\to\mathrm{Kos}(E^{\prime},s^{\prime}).

Applying
\mathbf{L}j^{*}
to the first map, and we get the following commutative diagram of dg-algebras on
Y^{\prime}
:

(6.5)

\textstyle{\mathbf{L}j^{*}\mathrm{Kos}(E,s)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{L}j^{*}(f)}
\scriptstyle{\,\,\,g}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Kos}(E^{\prime},s^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{L}j^{*}j_{*}\mathrm{Kos}(E^{\prime},s^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447pta}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Kos}(E^{\prime},s^{\prime}),}

where
a
is the adjunction map
\mathbf{L}j^{*}j_{*}\to\mathrm{id}
. Then we have induced map on the mapping cones

\mathrm{Cone}(g)\to\mathrm{Cone}(a).

Let
i^{\prime}\colon Z(s^{\prime})\hookrightarrow Y^{\prime}
be the natural embedding, then

H^{-1}(\mathbf{L}i^{\prime*}(\mathrm{Cone}(g)))\cong i^{\prime*}\mathcal{K},\quad H^{-1}(\mathbf{L}i^{\prime*}(\mathrm{Cone}(a)))\cong i^{\prime*}N^{\vee}_{Y^{\prime}/Y},

and the induced map
i^{\prime*}\mathcal{K}\to i^{\prime*}N^{\vee}_{Y^{\prime}/Y}
equals to
\rho
in (
6.4
).
∎

Proposition 6.4
.

Suppose that
(X,Y,s,\phi)
and
(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime},s^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})
are two sets of dimensional reduction data, and assume that there exists maps (1) and (2) as in the Definition
6.2
which satisfy condition (i). Then the condition (ii) is equivalent to

(ii’)

Z(s)
is set-theoretically contained in
Y^{\prime}
, and
\rho:\ker(\operatorname{pr})|_{Z(s^{\prime})}\to N^{\vee}_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{Z(s^{\prime})}
(
6.4
) is an isomorphism.

Proof.

(ii)
\Longrightarrow
(ii’). Since
Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s^{\prime})\cong Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s)
,
Z(s)
is set-theoretically contained in
Y^{\prime}
. Moreover

\mathrm{Kos}(E,s)\to j_{*}\mathrm{Kos}(E^{\prime},s^{\prime})

being a quasi-isomorphism implies that the map

\mathrm{Cone}(g)\to\mathrm{Cone}(a)

induced from the commutative diagram (
6.5
) is a quasi-isomorphism. In particular,

\rho:i^{\prime*}\ker(\operatorname{pr})\cong H^{-1}(\mathbf{L}i^{\prime*}(\mathrm{Cone}(g)))\to H^{-1}(\mathbf{L}i^{\prime*}(\mathrm{Cone}(a)))\cong i^{\prime*}N^{\vee}_{Y^{\prime}/Y}

is an isomorphism.

(ii’)
\Longrightarrow
(ii). We only need to show
\mathrm{Kos}(E,s)\to j_{*}\mathrm{Kos}(E^{\prime},s^{\prime})
is a quasi-isomorphism. Since
Z(s)\subseteq Y^{\prime}
and

s|_{Y^{\prime}}=\operatorname{pr}^{\vee}\circ s^{\prime}
, we have
Z(s)=Z(s^{\prime})
as sets. Assume
Z(s^{\prime})\neq\emptyset
(otherwise (ii) trivially holds). To show that
\mathrm{Kos}(E,s)\to j_{*}\mathrm{Kos}(E^{\prime},s^{\prime})
is a quasi-isomorphism, it is enough to check it locally in a Zariski neighbourhood of any point in
Y^{\prime}
. To this end, we can assume that there is a decomposition of vector bundle

E\cong\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}\oplus\widetilde{E}^{\prime},\,\,\,\,\mathrm{such}\,\,\mathrm{that}\,\,\,\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}|_{Y^{\prime}}\cong\ker(\operatorname{pr}),\,\,\,\widetilde{E}^{\prime}|_{Y^{\prime}}\cong E^{\prime},

and
\operatorname{pr}
equals to the natural projection
(\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}\oplus\widetilde{E}^{\prime})|_{Y^{\prime}}\to\widetilde{E}^{\prime}|_{Y^{\prime}}
. Let us write

s=(s_{1},s_{2}):\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}\oplus\widetilde{E}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{O}_{Y},

then the image of
s_{1}
is contained in
I_{Y^{\prime}}
(the ideal that defines
Y^{\prime}
). Then we consider the vector bundle map on
Y^{\prime}
:

\displaystyle\tau\colon\ker(\operatorname{pr})=\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}/I_{Y^{\prime}}\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}\longrightarrow I_{Y^{\prime}}/I^{2}_{Y^{\prime}}=N^{\vee}_{Y^{\prime}/Y},

which is defined as the image of
s_{1}
. By construction,
\tau|_{Z(s^{\prime})}=\rho
which is an isomorphism by assumption, so there exists an open neighbourhood
V
of
Z(s^{\prime})
in
Y
such that
\tau|_{V\cap Y^{\prime}}
is an isomorphism. Then it suffices to show that
\mathrm{Kos}(E,s)|_{V}\to j_{*}\mathrm{Kos}(E^{\prime},s^{\prime})|_{V}
is a quasi-isomorphism.
We have decomposition

\mathrm{Kos}(E,s)|_{V}\cong\mathrm{Kos}(\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{V},s_{1}|_{V})\otimes\mathrm{Kos}(\widetilde{E}^{\prime}_{V},s_{2}|_{V}).

Since
\tau|_{V\cap Y^{\prime}}
is an isomorphism, (locally)
s_{1}
maps a basis of
\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{V}
to a regular sequence that generates
I_{Y^{\prime}}|_{V}
; thus
\mathrm{Kos}(\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}_{V},s_{1}|_{V})
is quasi-isomorphic to
j_{*}\mathcal{O}_{V\cap Y^{\prime}}
as a dg
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-algebra. It follows that

\mathrm{Kos}(E,s)|_{V}\cong j_{*}\mathbf{L}j^{*}\mathrm{Kos}(\widetilde{E}^{\prime}_{V},s_{2}|_{V})\cong j_{*}\mathrm{Kos}(E^{\prime},s^{\prime})|_{V}.\qed

Note that compatible dimensional reduction data gives a further consequence.

Proposition 6.5
.

Suppose that
(X,Y,s,\phi)
and
(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime},s^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})
are compatible dimensional reduction data, then
\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w})
is a subscheme of
X^{\prime\prime}
, and
\operatorname{pr}\colon X^{\prime\prime}\to X^{\prime}
maps
\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w})
isomorphically onto
\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}^{\prime})
.
12
12

12

In fact, the derived critical loci are also isomorphic. This can be proven using the
(-1)
-shifted version of
[
KP
, Cor. 5.3]
, and the fact that the derived zero locus of
s
and
s^{\prime}
are isomorphic and the restriction of
\phi
and
\phi^{\prime}
on them coincide. We thank Tasuki Kinjo to pointing out this.

Proof.

We first notice that

\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w})\subseteq\pi^{-1}(Z(s)).

Then by Definition
6.2
(i), we have embedding

\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w})\subseteq\pi^{-1}(Z(s^{\prime}))\subseteq X^{\prime\prime}.

Note that for any smooth closed subvariety
Z\subseteq X
, there is an inclusion

\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w})\cap Z\subseteq\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}|_{Z}),

and taking
Z=X^{\prime\prime}
leads to

\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w})\subseteq\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}|_{X^{\prime\prime}}).

By the condition (1) in Definition
6.2
, we have

\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}|_{X^{\prime\prime}})=\operatorname{pr}^{-1}(\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}^{\prime})),

thus
\operatorname{pr}\colon X^{\prime\prime}\to X^{\prime}
restricts to give
\operatorname{pr}:\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w})\to\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}^{\prime})
. It remains to show the map is surjective and any fiber contains exactly one point. Since we can check this locally, we replace
Y
by an open subset and without loss of generality we assume
E\cong\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}\oplus\widetilde{E}^{\prime}
such that
\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}|_{Y^{\prime}}\cong\ker(\operatorname{pr})
and
\widetilde{E}^{\prime}|_{Y^{\prime}}\cong E^{\prime}
and
\operatorname{pr}
equals to the natural projection
(\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}\oplus\widetilde{E}^{\prime})|_{Y^{\prime}}\to\widetilde{E}^{\prime}|_{Y^{\prime}}
.
Shrink
Y
if necessary, we assume

\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}^{\vee}\cong\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{O}_{Y}\cdot k_{i},\quad\widetilde{E}^{\prime\vee}\cong\bigoplus_{j=1}^{m}\mathcal{O}_{Y}\cdot e_{j},

such that the morphism given by
\{t_{i}=s(k_{i}^{\vee})\}_{i=1}^{n}\colon Y\to\mathbb{A}^{n}
is smooth,
where
k_{i}^{\vee}
is dual basis of
k_{i}
.
We have

\mathsf{w}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}\cdot t_{i}+\sum_{j=1}^{m}e_{j}\cdot s(e_{j}^{\vee})+\phi,\quad\mathsf{w}^{\prime}=\sum_{j=1}^{m}e_{j}\cdot s(e_{j}^{\vee})|_{Y^{\prime}}+\phi|_{Y^{\prime}},

then it follows that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w})=\operatorname{pr}^{-1}(\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}^{\prime}))\bigcap\left\{k_{i}+\sum_{j=1}^{m}e_{j}\cdot\frac{\partial s(e_{j}^{\vee})}{\partial t_{i}}+\frac{\partial\phi}{\partial t_{i}}=0\>\bigg|\>1\leqslant i\leqslant n\right\}.

Since
k_{i}=-\sum_{j=1}^{m}e_{j}\cdot\frac{\partial s(e_{j}^{\vee})}{\partial t_{i}}-\frac{\partial\phi}{\partial t_{i}}

(1\leqslant i\leqslant n)
gives a section of
\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}
on
\mathrm{Tot}(\widetilde{E}^{\prime})
,
\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w})
is section of the projection
\operatorname{pr}^{-1}(\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}^{\prime}))\to\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}^{\prime})
; therefore
\operatorname{pr}
maps
\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w})
isomorphically onto
\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}^{\prime})
.
∎

We note that for a dimensional reduction datum
(X,Y,s,\phi)
, its
\mathsf{A}
-fixed part
(X^{\mathsf{A}},Y^{\mathsf{A}},s^{\mathsf{A}},\phi^{\mathsf{A}})
is also a dimensional reduction datum.

Lemma 6.6
.

Suppose that
(X,Y,s,\phi)
and
(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime},s^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})
are compatible dimensional reduction data, then the fixed locus data
(X^{\mathsf{A}},Y^{\mathsf{A}},s^{\mathsf{A}},\phi^{\mathsf{A}})
and
(X^{\prime\mathsf{A}},Y^{\prime\mathsf{A}},s^{\prime\mathsf{A}},\phi^{\prime\mathsf{A}})
are also compatible.

Proof.

Taking
\mathsf{A}
-invariance, we get
\mathsf{T}
-equivariant closed embedding
j^{\mathsf{A}}\colon Y^{\prime\mathsf{A}}\hookrightarrow Y^{\mathsf{A}}
and
\mathsf{T}
-equivariant surjective vector bundle map

\operatorname{pr}^{\mathsf{A}}\colon E|_{Y^{\prime\mathsf{A}}}^{\mathrm{fix}}\twoheadrightarrow E^{\prime}|_{Y^{\prime\mathsf{A}}}^{\mathrm{fix}}.

Then the condition (i) in Definition
6.2
is obviously satisfied. By Proposition
6.4
, it is enough to verify condition (ii’). Since
s\in\Gamma(Y,E^{\vee})^{\mathsf{A}}
, we have
Z(s^{\mathsf{A}})=Z(s)^{\mathsf{A}}
, so
Z(s^{\mathsf{A}})
is set-theoretically contained in
Y^{\prime\mathsf{A}}
. The natural map
\rho^{\mathsf{A}}:\ker(\operatorname{pr}^{\mathsf{A}})|_{Z(s^{\prime\mathsf{A}})}\to N^{\vee}_{Y^{\prime\mathsf{A}}/Y^{\mathsf{A}}}|_{Z(s^{\prime\mathsf{A}})}
is the
\mathsf{A}
-fixed part of the pullback of
\rho:\ker(\operatorname{pr})|_{Z(s^{\prime})}\cong N^{\vee}_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{Z(s^{\prime})}
to
Z(s^{\prime\mathsf{A}})
, so
\rho^{\mathsf{A}}
is an isomorphism.
∎

Proposition 6.7
.

Suppose that
(X,Y,s,\phi)
and
(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime},s^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})
are compatible dimensional reduction data. Let
\delta_{H}
and
\delta^{\mathsf{A}}_{H}
be the natural maps

(6.6)

\displaystyle\delta_{H}:=\tilde{j}_{*}\circ\operatorname{pr}^{*}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\prime},\mathsf{w}^{\prime})\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w}),\quad\delta^{\mathsf{A}}_{H}:=\tilde{j}^{\mathsf{A}}_{*}\circ\operatorname{pr}^{\mathsf{A}*}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\prime\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\prime\mathsf{A}})\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}}),

where we refer to diagram (
6.3
) for those maps.
Then we have commutative diagram

\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces,}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447pti^{*}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\prime\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\prime\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta^{\mathsf{A}}_{H}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\prime},\mathsf{w}^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces,}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{H}}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447pte^{\mathsf{T}}\left(N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{X^{\prime\mathsf{A}}}^{\mathrm{mov}}\right)\cdot\,i^{\prime*}}

where
i^{\prime}\colon X^{\prime\mathsf{A}}\hookrightarrow X^{\prime}
and
i\colon X^{\mathsf{A}}\hookrightarrow X
are closed embeddings of fixed points loci.

Similarly let
\delta_{K}
and
\delta^{\mathsf{A}}_{K}
be the natural maps

(6.7)

\displaystyle\delta_{K}:=\tilde{j}_{*}\circ\operatorname{pr}^{*}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\prime},\mathsf{w}^{\prime})\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w}),\quad\delta^{\mathsf{A}}_{K}:=\tilde{j}^{\mathsf{A}}_{*}\circ\operatorname{pr}^{\mathsf{A}*}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\prime\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\prime\mathsf{A}})\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}}),

then we have commutative diagram

\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447pti^{*}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\prime\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\prime\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta^{\mathsf{A}}_{K}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\prime},\mathsf{w}^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{K}}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447pte^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}\left(N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{X^{\prime\mathsf{A}}}^{\mathrm{mov}}\right)\cdot\,i^{\prime*}}

Proof.
