Consider the following Cartesian diagram of smooth varieties:

\textstyle{X^{\prime\prime\mathsf{A}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{j}^{\mathsf{A}}}
\scriptstyle{i^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\Box}
\textstyle{X^{\mathsf{A}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\textstyle{X^{\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{j}}
\textstyle{X}

According to Remark
B.2
, we have

\displaystyle i^{*}\circ\tilde{j}_{*}=\tilde{j}^{\mathsf{A}}_{*}\circ e^{\mathsf{T}}(N_{X^{\prime\prime}/X}|_{X^{\prime\prime\mathsf{A}}}^{\mathrm{mov}})\cdot i^{\prime\prime*}.

We note that
\operatorname{pr}\circ i^{\prime\prime}=i^{\prime}\circ\operatorname{pr}^{\mathsf{A}}
. Then we have

i^{*}\circ\delta_{H}=i^{*}\circ\tilde{j}_{*}\circ\operatorname{pr}^{*}=\tilde{j}^{\mathsf{A}}_{*}\circ e^{\mathsf{T}}(N_{X^{\prime\prime}/X}|_{X^{\prime\prime\mathsf{A}}}^{\mathrm{mov}})\cdot i^{\prime\prime*}\circ\operatorname{pr}^{*}=\tilde{j}^{\mathsf{A}}_{*}\circ e^{\mathsf{T}}(N_{X^{\prime\prime}/X}|_{X^{\prime\prime\mathsf{A}}}^{\mathrm{mov}})\cdot\operatorname{pr}^{\mathsf{A}*}\circ\,i^{\prime*}\\
=\tilde{j}^{\mathsf{A}}_{*}\circ\operatorname{pr}^{\mathsf{A}*}\circ\,e^{\mathsf{T}}(N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{X^{\prime\mathsf{A}}}^{\mathrm{mov}})\cdot i^{\prime*}=\delta^{\mathsf{A}}_{H}\circ e^{\mathsf{T}}(N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{X^{\prime\mathsf{A}}}^{\mathrm{mov}})\cdot i^{\prime*}.

This proves the statement for cohomology. For the
K
-theory counterpart, we use Remark
B.5
to get

i^{*}\circ\tilde{j}_{*}=\tilde{j}^{\mathsf{A}}_{*}\circ e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}(N_{X^{\prime\prime}/X}|_{X^{\prime\prime\mathsf{A}}}^{\mathrm{mov}})\cdot i^{\prime\prime*},

and the rest of computation is the same as above.
∎

Proposition 6.8
.

Suppose that
(X,Y,s,\phi)
and
(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime},s^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})
are compatible dimensional reduction data.
If
K
-theoretic deformed dimensional reduction holds for both
(X,Y,s,\phi)
and
(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime},s^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})
, then the natural map
\delta_{K}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\prime},\mathsf{w}^{\prime})\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})
defined in Proposition
6.7
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Using
Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s)\cong Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s^{\prime})
, we have Cartesian diagram:

\textstyle{\pi^{-1}(Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{pr}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{i}^{\prime\prime\mathrm{der}}}
\scriptstyle{\Box}
\textstyle{\pi^{\prime-1}(Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s^{\prime}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{i}^{\prime\mathrm{der}}}
\textstyle{X^{\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{pr}}
\textstyle{X^{\prime}.}

Since
\operatorname{pr}
is smooth, we have

\mathbf{i}^{\prime\prime\mathrm{der}}_{*}\circ\operatorname{pr}^{*}=\operatorname{pr}^{*}\circ\mathbf{i}^{\prime\mathrm{der}}_{*}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\delta_{K}\circ(\mathbf{i}^{\prime\mathrm{der}}_{*}\circ\pi^{\prime*})=\tilde{j}_{*}\circ\operatorname{pr}^{*}\circ\mathbf{i}^{\prime\mathrm{der}}_{*}\circ\pi^{\prime*}=\tilde{j}_{*}\circ\mathbf{i}^{\prime\prime\mathrm{der}}_{*}\circ\operatorname{pr}^{*}\circ\pi^{\prime*}=\mathbf{i}^{\mathrm{der}}_{*}\circ\pi^{*},

where in the last equation we use
\tilde{j}\circ\mathbf{i}^{\prime\prime\mathrm{der}}=\mathbf{i}^{\mathrm{der}}
and
\pi^{\prime}\circ\operatorname{pr}=\pi
. By assumption, both
\mathbf{i}^{\prime\mathrm{der}}_{*}\circ\pi^{\prime*}
and
\mathbf{i}^{\mathrm{der}}_{*}\circ\pi^{*}
are isomorphisms; thus
\delta_{K}
is an isomorphism.
∎

Proposition
6.8
holds for cohomology in more general setting,
where deformed dimensional reductions for
(X,Y,s,\phi)
and
(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime},s^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})
are not needed as assumptions.

Proposition 6.9
.

Suppose that
(X,Y,s,\phi)
and
(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime},s^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})
are compatible dimensional reduction data. Then the map
\delta_{H}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\prime},\mathsf{w}^{\prime})\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})
defined in Proposition
6.7
is an isomorphism.
13
13

13

In view of the previous footnote, this isomorphism can also be proven from the gluing of DT perverse sheaves, as the condition in Definition
6.2
implies the orientation data of the derived critical locus of
\mathsf{w}
and
\mathsf{w}^{\prime}
are equivalent.

Proof.

The map
\delta_{H}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\prime},\mathsf{w}^{\prime})\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})
is induced by the following chain of maps

\displaystyle H^{\mathsf{T}}_{*-2\operatorname{rk}\ker(\operatorname{pr})}(X^{\prime},\varphi_{\mathsf{w}^{\prime}}\omega_{X^{\prime}})\xrightarrow{\cong}H^{\mathsf{T}}_{*}(X^{\prime\prime},\varphi_{\mathsf{w}|_{X^{\prime\prime}}}\tilde{j}^{!}\omega_{X})\xrightarrow{\cong}H^{\mathsf{T}}_{*}(X,\tilde{j}_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}|_{X^{\prime\prime}}}\tilde{j}^{!}\omega_{X})\longrightarrow H^{\mathsf{T}}_{*}(X,\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\omega_{X}),

where the first two isomorphisms are obvious, the last map is induced by the natural morphism in
\operatorname{D}^{b}_{c}(X)
:

(6.8)

\displaystyle\tilde{j}_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}|_{X^{\prime\prime}}}\tilde{j}^{!}\omega_{X}\longrightarrow\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\omega_{X}.

To show that
\delta_{H}
is an isomorphism, it remains to show the pushforward of (
6.8
) to
Y
:

(6.9)

\pi_{*}\tilde{j}_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}|_{X^{\prime\prime}}}\tilde{j}^{!}\omega_{X}\longrightarrow\pi_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\omega_{X}

is an isomorphism in
\operatorname{D}^{b}_{c}(Y)
. In the proof of Proposition
6.5
, we know that étale locally on
Y
, we have a decomposition
and a basis:

E\cong\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}\oplus\widetilde{E}^{\prime},\quad\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}^{\vee}\cong\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{O}_{Y}\cdot k_{i},\quad\widetilde{E}^{\prime\vee}\cong\bigoplus_{j=1}^{m}\mathcal{O}_{Y}\cdot e_{j},

such that the morphism given by
\{t_{i}=s(k_{i}^{\vee})\}_{i=1}^{n}\colon Y\to\mathbb{A}^{n}
is smooth, where
k_{i}^{\vee}
is dual basis of
k_{i}
. In this coordinate,

\mathsf{w}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}\cdot t_{i}+\sum_{j=1}^{m}e_{j}\cdot s(e_{j}^{\vee})+\phi.

Let
\pi_{1}\colon\mathrm{Tot}(E)\to\mathrm{Tot}(\widetilde{E}^{\prime})
be the projection whose fibers contribute to the function
\mathsf{w}
by

\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{i}\cdot t_{i}
, where
\bigcap_{i=1}^{n}\{t_{i}=0\}
cuts out
Y^{\prime}\subseteq Y
in an étale neighbourhood.
Then the deformed dimensional reduction for regular sections (Theorem
C.1
) implies that the natural map

\displaystyle\pi_{1*}\tilde{j}_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}|_{X^{\prime\prime}}}\tilde{j}^{!}\omega_{X}\longrightarrow\pi_{1*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\omega_{X}

is an isomorphism. Pushing forward to
Y
, we obtain (
6.9
).
∎

6.2.
Compatibility with stable envelopes

Suppose that
(X,Y,s,\phi)
and
(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime},s^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})
are compatible dimensional reduction data as in the Definition
6.2
.
As
X\to Y
and
X^{\prime}\to Y^{\prime}
are vector bundles projections equivariant under
\mathsf{A}
,
there are one-to-one correspondences

\mathrm{Fix}_{\mathsf{A}}(X)\cong\mathrm{Fix}_{\mathsf{A}}(Y),\quad\mathrm{Fix}_{\mathsf{A}}(X^{\prime})\cong\mathrm{Fix}_{\mathsf{A}}(Y^{\prime}),

with isomorphisms given by projections and
\mathsf{A}
-fixed parts of inverse maps of projections.

The embedding
Y^{\prime}\hookrightarrow Y
induces a map

\mathfrak{r}:\mathrm{Fix}_{\mathsf{A}}(X^{\prime})\cong\mathrm{Fix}_{\mathsf{A}}(Y^{\prime})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Fix}_{\mathsf{A}}(Y)\cong\mathrm{Fix}_{\mathsf{A}}(X),

which is neither injective nor surjective in general. The map
\mathfrak{r}
respects the ample partial order
\leq
defined in the Remark
3.9
. Namely,
\forall\,F,F^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Fix}_{\mathsf{A}}(X^{\prime})
,

(6.10)

\displaystyle F<F^{\prime}\Longleftrightarrow\mathfrak{r}(F)<\mathfrak{r}(F^{\prime}).

Theorem 6.10
.

Suppose that
(X,Y,s,\phi)
and
(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime},s^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})
are compatible dimensional reduction data as in the Definition
6.2
. Fix a normalizer

\epsilon\in\{\pm 1\}^{\operatorname{Fix}_{\mathsf{A}}(X)}
, and define
\epsilon^{\prime}\in\{\pm 1\}^{\operatorname{Fix}_{\mathsf{A}}(X^{\prime})}
by

\epsilon^{\prime}_{F}:=\epsilon_{\mathfrak{r}(F)}\cdot(-1)^{\operatorname{rk}N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{F}^{+}}.

Fix a chamber
\mathfrak{C}\subseteq\operatorname{Lie}(\mathsf{A})_{\mathbb{R}}
, and we assume that cohomological stable envelopes exist for
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C})
and
(X^{\prime},\mathsf{w}^{\prime},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C})
. Then we have the following commutative diagram:

(6.11)

\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C},\epsilon}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\prime\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\prime\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C},\epsilon^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\delta^{\mathsf{A}}_{H}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\prime},\mathsf{w}^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{H}}

Proof.

Define

\mathcal{S}=\delta_{H}\circ\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C},\epsilon^{\prime}}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\prime\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\prime\mathsf{A}})\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w}).

For an arbitrary
F\in\operatorname{Fix}_{\mathsf{A}}(X^{\prime})
, and take an arbitrary
\gamma\in H^{\mathsf{T}/\mathsf{A}}(F,\mathsf{w}^{\prime\mathsf{A}})
, we need to show that
\mathcal{S}(\gamma)
satisfies the axioms (ii) and (iii) in Definition
3.4
and axiom (i’) in Remark
3.7
, namely

(1)

\mathcal{S}(\gamma)
is supported on
\operatorname{Attr}^{\leq}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\mathfrak{r}(F))
;

(2)

\mathcal{S}(\gamma)\big|_{\mathfrak{r}(F)}=\epsilon_{\mathfrak{r}(F)}\cdot e^{\mathsf{T}}(N_{\mathfrak{r}(F)/X}^{-})\cdot\delta^{\mathsf{A}}_{H}(\gamma)
;

(3)

For any
F^{\prime}\neq\mathfrak{r}(F)
, the inequality
\deg_{\mathsf{A}}\mathcal{S}(\gamma)\big|_{F^{\prime}}<\deg_{\mathsf{A}}e^{\mathsf{T}}(N_{F^{\prime}/X}^{-})
holds.

Here we choose the ample partial order
\leq
as in Remark
3.9
.

Denote
\beta=\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C},\epsilon^{\prime}}(\gamma)
, then
\beta
is supported on
\operatorname{Attr}^{\leq}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F)
. It follows that
\delta_{H}(\beta)=\tilde{j}_{*}\circ\operatorname{pr}^{*}(\beta)
is supported on
\operatorname{pr}^{-1}(\operatorname{Attr}^{\leq}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F))
. Since the complex
\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\omega_{X}
is supported on
\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w})
,
\delta_{H}(\beta)
is supported on
\operatorname{pr}^{-1}(\operatorname{Attr}^{\leq}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F))\cap\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w})
. According to Proposition
6.5
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{pr}^{-1}(\operatorname{Attr}^{\leq}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F))\cap\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w})=(\operatorname{pr}|_{\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w})})^{-1}(\operatorname{Attr}^{\leq}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F)\cap\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}^{\prime})).

By Lemma
3.8
, we have

\operatorname{Attr}^{\leq}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F)\cap\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}^{\prime})=\bigcup_{F\leq F^{\prime}}\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F^{\prime}\cap\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}^{\prime}))_{\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}^{\prime})},

where the subscript means taking attracting set inside
\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}^{\prime})
. Therefore
\delta_{H}(\beta)
is supported on

\displaystyle\bigcup_{F\leq F^{\prime}}\left(\operatorname{pr}|_{\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w})})^{-1}(\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F^{\prime}\cap\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}^{\prime}))_{\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}^{\prime})}\right)

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{F\leq F^{\prime}}\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\mathfrak{r}(F^{\prime})\cap\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}))_{\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w})}

by (
6.10
)

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\mathfrak{r}(F)\leq\mathfrak{r}(F^{\prime})}\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\mathfrak{r}(F^{\prime})\cap\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}))_{\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w})}

since
F^{\prime\prime}\cap\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w})\neq\emptyset\Longleftrightarrow F^{\prime\prime}=\mathfrak{r}(F^{\prime})
for some
F^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\mathfrak{r}(F)\leq F^{\prime\prime}}\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F^{\prime\prime}\cap\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}))_{\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w})}

by Lemma
3.8

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Attr}^{\leq}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\mathfrak{r}(F))\cap\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}).

In particular,
\delta_{H}(\beta)
is supported on
\operatorname{Attr}^{\leq}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\mathfrak{r}(F))
. This proves (1).

To show (2) and (3), we notice that

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}(\gamma)\big|_{X^{\mathsf{A}}}=i^{*}\circ\delta_{H}(\beta)=\delta^{\mathsf{A}}_{H}\circ e^{\mathsf{T}}\left(N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{X^{\prime\mathsf{A}}}^{\mathrm{mov}}\right)\cdot i^{\prime*}(\beta)=\delta^{\mathsf{A}}_{H}\left(e^{\mathsf{T}}\left(N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{X^{\prime\mathsf{A}}}^{\mathrm{mov}}\right)\cdot\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C},\epsilon^{\prime}}(\gamma)|_{X^{\prime\mathsf{A}}}\right),

where the second equality follows from Proposition
6.7
. Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}(\gamma)\big|_{\mathfrak{r}(F)}=\delta^{\mathsf{A}}_{H}\left(e^{\mathsf{T}}\left(N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{F}^{\mathrm{mov}}\right)\cdot\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C},\epsilon^{\prime}}(\gamma)|_{F}\right)=\delta^{\mathsf{A}}_{H}\left(\epsilon^{\prime}_{F}\cdot e^{\mathsf{T}}\left(N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{F}^{\mathrm{mov}}\right)\cdot e^{\mathsf{T}}(N_{F/X^{\prime}}^{-})\cdot\gamma\right).

According to Proposition
6.4
, we have

N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{Z(s^{\prime})^{\mathsf{A}}}^{+}=\left(\ker(\operatorname{pr})|_{Z(s^{\prime})^{\mathsf{A}}}^{-}\right)^{\vee}\in K^{\mathsf{T}}\left(Z(s^{\prime})^{\mathsf{A}}\right).

Via pullback along
\pi^{\prime\mathsf{A}}\colon X^{\prime\mathsf{A}}\to Y^{\prime\mathsf{A}}
, we have

(6.12)

e^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\pi^{\prime\mathsf{A}*}N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{Z(s^{\prime})^{\mathsf{A}}}^{+}\right)=(-1)^{\operatorname{rk}N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{X^{\prime\mathsf{A}}}^{+}}e^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\pi^{\prime\mathsf{A}*}\ker(\operatorname{pr})|_{Z(s^{\prime})^{\mathsf{A}}}^{-}\right)\in K^{\mathsf{T}}\left((\pi^{\prime\mathsf{A}})^{-1}(Z(s^{\prime})^{\mathsf{A}})\right).

As we have inclusion

\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}^{\prime\mathsf{A}})\subseteq(\pi^{\prime\mathsf{A}})^{-1}(Z(s^{\prime})^{\mathsf{A}}),

we can restrict (
6.12
) to
\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}^{\prime\mathsf{A}})
and the restriction

(6.13)

e^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\pi^{\prime\mathsf{A}*}N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{Z(s^{\prime})^{\mathsf{A}}}^{+}\right)\Big|_{\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}^{\prime\mathsf{A}})}=(-1)^{\operatorname{rk}N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{X^{\prime\mathsf{A}}}^{+}}e^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\pi^{\prime\mathsf{A}*}\ker(\operatorname{pr})|_{Z(s^{\prime})^{\mathsf{A}}}^{-}\right)\Big|_{\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}^{\prime\mathsf{A}})}

acts on
H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\prime\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\prime\mathsf{A}})
.
Note that

(6.14)

\left(\pi^{\prime\mathsf{A}*}N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{Z(s^{\prime})^{\mathsf{A}}}^{+}\right)\Big|_{\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}^{\prime\mathsf{A}})}\cong\left(\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}^{\prime\mathsf{A}})\hookrightarrow X^{\prime\mathsf{A}}\right)^{*}\left(\pi^{\prime\mathsf{A}*}N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{Y^{\prime\mathsf{A}}}^{+}\right),

(6.15)

\left(\pi^{\prime\mathsf{A}*}\ker(\operatorname{pr})|_{Z(s^{\prime})^{\mathsf{A}}}^{-}\right)\Big|_{\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}^{\prime\mathsf{A}})}\cong\left(\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}^{\prime\mathsf{A}})\hookrightarrow X^{\prime\mathsf{A}}\right)^{*}\left(\pi^{\prime\mathsf{A}*}\ker(\operatorname{pr})|_{Z(s^{\prime})^{\mathsf{A}}}^{-}\right).

For simplicity, we write

N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{X^{\prime\mathsf{A}}}^{+}=\left(\pi^{\prime\mathsf{A}*}N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{Y^{\prime\mathsf{A}}}^{+}\right),\quad\ker(\operatorname{pr})|_{X^{\prime\mathsf{A}}}^{-}=\left(\pi^{\prime\mathsf{A}*}\ker(\operatorname{pr})|_{Z(s^{\prime})^{\mathsf{A}}}^{-}\right)

and omit the pullback along
\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}^{\prime\mathsf{A}})\hookrightarrow X^{\prime\mathsf{A}}
in (
6.14
), (
6.15
). Then (
6.13
) becomes

(6.16)

e^{\mathsf{T}}\left(N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{X^{\prime\mathsf{A}}}^{+}\right)=(-1)^{\operatorname{rk}N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{X^{\prime\mathsf{A}}}^{+}}e^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\ker(\operatorname{pr})|_{X^{\prime\mathsf{A}}}^{-}\right),

which acts on
H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\prime\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\prime\mathsf{A}})
. Consequently, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}(\gamma)\big|_{\mathfrak{r}(F)}

\displaystyle=\delta^{\mathsf{A}}_{H}\left((-1)^{\operatorname{rk}N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{F}^{+}}\cdot\epsilon^{\prime}_{F}\cdot e^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\ker(\operatorname{pr})|_{F}^{-}+N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{F}^{-}\right)\cdot e^{\mathsf{T}}(N_{F/X^{\prime}}^{-})\cdot\gamma\right)

\displaystyle=\epsilon_{\mathfrak{r}(F)}\cdot e^{\mathsf{T}}(N_{\mathfrak{r}(F)/X}^{-})\cdot\delta^{\mathsf{A}}_{H}(\gamma).

This proves (2).

Finally, for any
F^{\prime}\neq\mathfrak{r}(F)
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}(\gamma)\big|_{F^{\prime}}=\delta^{\mathsf{A}}_{H}\left(e^{\mathsf{T}}\left(N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{\mathfrak{r}^{-1}(F^{\prime})}^{\mathrm{mov}}\right)\cdot\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C},\epsilon^{\prime}}(\gamma)|_{\mathfrak{r}^{-1}(F^{\prime})}\right).

Suppose that
\mathfrak{r}^{-1}(F^{\prime})=\{F_{1},\cdots,F_{n}\}
, then
F_{i}\neq F
for all
i
. The same argument as above shows that

\displaystyle e^{\mathsf{T}}\left(N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{F_{i}}^{\mathrm{mov}}\right)\cdot\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C},\epsilon^{\prime}}(\gamma)|_{F_{i}}=\pm e^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\ker(\operatorname{pr})|_{F_{i}}^{-}+N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{F_{i}}^{-}\right)\cdot\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C},\epsilon^{\prime}}(\gamma)|_{F_{i}}

Since
\delta^{\mathsf{A}}_{H}
preserves
\deg_{\mathsf{A}}
, we have

\displaystyle\deg_{\mathsf{A}}\delta^{\mathsf{A}}_{H}\left(e^{\mathsf{T}}\left(N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{F_{i}}^{\mathrm{mov}}\right)\cdot\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C},\epsilon^{\prime}}(\gamma)|_{F_{i}}\right)

\displaystyle=\deg_{\mathsf{A}}e^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\ker(\operatorname{pr})|_{F_{i}}^{-}+N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{F_{i}}^{-}\right)+\deg_{\mathsf{A}}\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C},\epsilon^{\prime}}(\gamma)|_{F_{i}}

\displaystyle<\deg_{\mathsf{A}}e^{\mathsf{T}}\left(\ker(\operatorname{pr})|_{F_{i}}^{-}+N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{F_{i}}^{-}\right)+\deg_{\mathsf{A}}e^{\mathsf{T}}(N_{F_{i}/X^{\prime}}^{-})

\displaystyle=\deg_{\mathsf{A}}e^{\mathsf{T}}(N_{F^{\prime}/X}^{-}),

where the second inequality follows from the axiom (iii). Therefore

\displaystyle\deg_{\mathsf{A}}\mathcal{S}(\gamma)\big|_{F^{\prime}}\leqslant\max_{1\leqslant i\leqslant n}\deg_{\mathsf{A}}\delta^{\mathsf{A}}_{H}\left(e^{\mathsf{T}}\left(N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{F_{i}}^{\mathrm{mov}}\right)\cdot\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C},\epsilon^{\prime}}(\gamma)|_{F_{i}}\right)<\deg_{\mathsf{A}}e^{\mathsf{T}}(N_{F^{\prime}/X}^{-}).

This proves (3).
∎

Theorem 6.11
.

Suppose that
(X,Y,s,\phi)
and
(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime},s^{\prime},\phi^{\prime})
are compatible dimensional reduction data as in the Definition
6.2
. Fix a normalizer
\mathcal{E}\in\pm\operatorname{Pic}_{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}})
, and define
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\in\pm\operatorname{Pic}_{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\prime\mathsf{A}})
by

\mathcal{E}^{\prime}|_{F}:=\mathcal{E}|_{F}\otimes(-1)^{\operatorname{rk}N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{F}^{+}}\det(N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{F}^{+}).

Fix a generic slope
\mathsf{s}\in\operatorname{Pic}_{\mathsf{A}}(Y)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{R}
, and define
\mathsf{s}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Pic}_{\mathsf{A}}(Y^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{R}
by

\mathsf{s}^{\prime}:=\mathsf{s}|_{Y^{\prime}}\otimes\det(N_{Y^{\prime}/Y})^{1/2}.

Fix a chamber
\mathfrak{C}\subseteq\operatorname{Lie}(\mathsf{A})_{\mathbb{R}}
, and assume that
K
-theoretic stable envelopes exist for
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C},\mathsf{s})
and
(X^{\prime},\mathsf{w}^{\prime},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C},\mathsf{s}^{\prime})
.

Assume moreover that
\ker(\operatorname{pr})=N^{\vee}_{Y^{\prime}/Y}
in
K^{\mathsf{T}}(Y^{\prime})
.
Then we have the following commutative diagram:

(6.17)

\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C},\mathcal{E}}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\prime\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\prime\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{\mathfrak{C},\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\delta^{\mathsf{A}}_{K}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\prime},\mathsf{w}^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{K}}

Proof.

Define

\mathcal{S}=\delta_{K}\circ\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{\mathfrak{C},\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\prime\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\prime\mathsf{A}})\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w}).

For an arbitrary
F\in\operatorname{Fix}_{\mathsf{A}}(X^{\prime})
, and take an arbitrary
\gamma\in K^{\mathsf{T}/\mathsf{A}}(F,\mathsf{w}^{\prime\mathsf{A}})
, we need to show that
\mathcal{S}(\gamma)
satisfies the axioms (ii) and (iii) in Definition
3.10
and axiom (i’) in Remark
3.17
, namely

(1)

\mathcal{S}(\gamma)
is supported on
\operatorname{Attr}^{\leq}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\mathfrak{r}(F))
;

(2)

\mathcal{S}(\gamma)\big|_{\mathfrak{r}(F)}=\mathcal{E}|_{\mathfrak{r}(F)}\cdot\ e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}(N_{\mathfrak{r}(F)/X}^{-})\cdot\delta^{\mathsf{A}}_{K}(\gamma)
;

(3)

For any
F^{\prime}\neq\mathfrak{r}(F)
, there is a strict inclusion of polytopes

\displaystyle\deg_{\mathsf{A}}\mathcal{S}(\gamma)\big|_{F^{\prime}}\subsetneq\deg_{\mathsf{A}}e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}(N_{F^{\prime}/X}^{-})

\displaystyle+\mathrm{weight}_{\mathsf{A}}\left(\det(N_{F^{\prime}/X}^{-})^{1/2}\otimes\mathsf{s}|_{F^{\prime}}\right)-\mathrm{weight}_{\mathsf{A}}\left(\det(N_{\mathfrak{r}(F)/X}^{-})^{1/2}\otimes\mathsf{s}|_{\mathfrak{r}(F)}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathrm{weight}_{\mathsf{A}}\mathcal{E}|_{\mathfrak{r}(F)}.

Here we choose the ample partial order
\leq
as in Remark
3.9
.

Denote
\beta=\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{\mathfrak{C},\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}(\gamma)
, then
\beta
is supported on
\operatorname{Attr}^{\leq}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F)
. It follows that
\delta_{K}(\beta)=\tilde{j}_{*}\circ\operatorname{pr}^{*}(\beta)
is supported on
\operatorname{pr}^{-1}(\operatorname{Attr}^{\leq}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F))
. Since the critical
K
-theory is supported on
\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w})
,
\delta_{K}(\beta)
is supported on
\operatorname{pr}^{-1}(\operatorname{Attr}^{\leq}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F))\cap\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w})
. The same argument as in the proof of Theorem
6.10
shows that

\displaystyle\operatorname{pr}^{-1}(\operatorname{Attr}^{\leq}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F))\cap\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w})=\operatorname{Attr}^{\leq}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\mathfrak{r}(F))\cap\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}).

Thus
\delta_{K}(\beta)
is supported on
\operatorname{Attr}^{\leq}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\mathfrak{r}(F))
. This proves (1).

To show (2) and (3), we notice that

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}(\gamma)\big|_{X^{\mathsf{A}}}=i^{*}\circ\delta_{K}(\beta)=\delta^{\mathsf{A}}_{K}\circ e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}\left(N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{X^{\prime\mathsf{A}}}^{\mathrm{mov}}\right)\cdot i^{\prime*}(\beta)=\delta^{\mathsf{A}}_{K}\left(e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}\left(N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{X^{\prime\mathsf{A}}}^{\mathrm{mov}}\right)\cdot\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{\mathfrak{C},\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}(\gamma)|_{X^{\prime\mathsf{A}}}\right),

where the second equality follows from Proposition
6.7
. Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}(\gamma)\big|_{\mathfrak{r}(F)}

\displaystyle=\delta^{\mathsf{A}}_{K}\left(e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}\left(N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{F}^{\mathrm{mov}}\right)\cdot\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{\mathfrak{C},\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}(\gamma)|_{F}\right)

\displaystyle=\delta^{\mathsf{A}}_{K}\left(\mathcal{E}^{\prime}|_{F}\cdot e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}\left(N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{F}^{\mathrm{mov}}\right)\cdot e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}(N_{F/X^{\prime}}^{-})\cdot\gamma\right).

Similarly as (
6.16
), we have

e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}(N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{X^{\prime\mathsf{A}}}^{+})=(-1)^{\operatorname{rk}N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{X^{\prime\mathsf{A}}}^{+}}\det(N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{X^{\prime\mathsf{A}}}^{+})^{-1}e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}(\ker(\operatorname{pr})|_{X^{\prime\mathsf{A}}}^{-}).

Therefore

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}(\gamma)\big|_{\mathfrak{r}(F)}

\displaystyle=\delta^{\mathsf{A}}_{K}\left((-1)^{\operatorname{rk}N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{F}^{+}}\det(N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{F}^{+})^{-1}\mathcal{E}^{\prime}|_{F}\cdot e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}\left(\ker(\operatorname{pr})|_{F}^{-}+N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{F}^{-}\right)\cdot e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}(N_{F/X^{\prime}}^{-})\cdot\gamma\right)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{E}|_{\mathfrak{r}(F)}\cdot e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}(N_{\mathfrak{r}(F)/X}^{-})\cdot\delta^{\mathsf{A}}_{K}(\gamma).

This proves (2).

Finally, for any
F^{\prime}\neq\mathfrak{r}(F)
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}(\gamma)\big|_{F^{\prime}}=\delta^{\mathsf{A}}_{K}\left(e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}\left(N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{\mathfrak{r}^{-1}(F^{\prime})}^{\mathrm{mov}}\right)\cdot\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{\mathfrak{C},\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}(\gamma)|_{\mathfrak{r}^{-1}(F^{\prime})}\right).

Suppose that
\mathfrak{r}^{-1}(F^{\prime})=\{F_{1},\cdots,F_{n}\}
, then
F_{i}\neq F
for all
i
. The same argument as above shows that

\displaystyle e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}\left(N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{F_{i}}^{\mathrm{mov}}\right)\cdot\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{\mathfrak{C},\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}(\gamma)|_{F_{i}}=\pm\det(N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{F_{i}}^{+})^{-1}e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}\left(\ker(\operatorname{pr})|_{F_{i}}^{-}+N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{F_{i}}^{-}\right)\cdot\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{\mathfrak{C},\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}(\gamma)|_{F_{i}}.

Since
\delta^{\mathsf{A}}_{K}
preserves
\deg_{\mathsf{A}}
, we have

\displaystyle\deg_{\mathsf{A}}\delta^{\mathsf{A}}_{K}

\displaystyle\left(e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}\left(N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{F_{i}}^{\mathrm{mov}}\right)\cdot\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{\mathfrak{C},\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}(\gamma)|_{F_{i}}\right)

\displaystyle\subseteq\text{Convex hull}\left[\deg_{\mathsf{A}}\left(\det(N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{F_{i}}^{+})^{-1}e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}\left(\ker(\operatorname{pr})|_{F_{i}}^{-}+N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{F_{i}}^{-}\right)\right)+\deg_{\mathsf{A}}e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}(N^{-}_{F_{i}/X^{\prime}})\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\operatorname{weight}_{\mathsf{A}}(\det(N_{F_{i}/X^{\prime}}^{-})^{1/2}\otimes\mathsf{s}^{\prime}|_{F_{i}})-\operatorname{weight}_{\mathsf{A}}(\det(N_{F/X^{\prime}}^{-})^{1/2}\otimes\mathsf{s}^{\prime}|_{F})+\operatorname{weight}_{\mathsf{A}}\mathcal{E}^{\prime}|_{F}

\displaystyle=\deg_{\mathsf{A}}e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}(N_{F^{\prime}/X}^{-})+\mathrm{weight}_{\mathsf{A}}\left(\det(N_{F^{\prime}/X}^{-})^{1/2}\otimes\mathsf{s}|_{F^{\prime}}\right)-\mathrm{weight}_{\mathsf{A}}\left(\det(N_{\mathfrak{r}(F)/X}^{-})^{1/2}\otimes\mathsf{s}|_{\mathfrak{r}(F)}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathrm{weight}_{\mathsf{A}}\mathcal{E}|_{\mathfrak{r}(F)}.

Therefore

\displaystyle\deg_{\mathsf{A}}\mathcal{S}(\gamma)\big|_{F^{\prime}}\subseteq\text{Convex hull}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\deg_{\mathsf{A}}\delta^{\mathsf{A}}_{K}\left(e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}\left(N_{Y^{\prime}/Y}|_{F_{i}}^{\mathrm{mov}}\right)\cdot\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{\mathfrak{C},\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}(\gamma)|_{F_{i}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\subseteq\deg_{\mathsf{A}}e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}(N_{F^{\prime}/X}^{-})+\mathrm{weight}_{\mathsf{A}}\left(\det(N_{F^{\prime}/X}^{-})^{1/2}\otimes\mathsf{s}|_{F^{\prime}}\right)-\mathrm{weight}_{\mathsf{A}}\left(\det(N_{\mathfrak{r}(F)/X}^{-})^{1/2}\otimes\mathsf{s}|_{\mathfrak{r}(F)}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathrm{weight}_{\mathsf{A}}\mathcal{E}|_{\mathfrak{r}(F)}.

Since
\mathsf{s}
is generic, the above inclusion must be strict by Remark
3.12
. This proves (3).
∎

6.3.
Examples of compatible dimensional reduction data

Example 6.12
.

Suppose that
(X,Y,s,\phi)
is a dimensional reduction datum such that
s
is regular.
Then
Z(s)
is a smooth subvariety in
Y
with codimension equals to
\operatorname{rk}E
, and
Z(s)\cong Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s)
. It is easy to see that
(X,Y,s,\phi)
and

\left(Z(s),Z(s),0,\phi|_{Z(s)}\right)
are compatible dimensional reduction data (Definition
6.2
).

In particular, starting with a quiver, the associated tripled quivers with canonical cubic potentials and Nakajima varieties of the double quiver
provide such examples.

Example 6.13
.

Given a quiver
Q=(Q_{0},Q_{1})
, we define the
doubled quiver
to be
\overline{Q}:=(Q_{0},Q_{1}\sqcup Q_{1}^{*})
, where
Q_{1}^{*}
is the same as
Q_{1}
but with reversed arrow direction. We define the
tripled quiver

\widetilde{Q}:=(Q_{0},Q_{1}\sqcup Q_{1}^{*}\sqcup Q_{0})
, which adds one edge loop to each node on top of
\overline{Q}
.

Fix
\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}\in\mathbb{N}^{Q_{0}}
, we consider two
symmetric quiver varieties
(see Definition
4.5
for details of construction):

(1)

\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\overline{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})
associated with the doubled quiver
\overline{Q}
;

(2)

\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\widetilde{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})
associated with the tripled quiver
\widetilde{Q}
.

Assume
\theta
is generic, so that
\theta
-semistable loci coincide with
\theta
-stable loci:

R(\overline{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{ss}=R(\overline{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{s},\quad R(\widetilde{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{ss}=R(\widetilde{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{s}.

Let
G=\prod_{i\in Q_{0}}\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v}_{i})
be the
gauge group
, then the quotient map

R(\overline{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{s}\to\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\overline{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})=R(\overline{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{s}/G

is a principal
G
-bundle.
Denote the associated adjoint bundle to be
\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\overline{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})
. Then the
moment map
for
G\curvearrowright R(\overline{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})
descends to a section
\mu\in\Gamma\left(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\overline{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\mathcal{G}^{\vee}\right)
. The total space
\mathrm{Tot}(\mathcal{G})
is naturally identified with an open subscheme
\overset{\bullet}{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\widetilde{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})\subseteq{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\widetilde{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})
consisting of
G
-equivalence classes in
R(\widetilde{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})
, which are
\theta
-stable when restricted to
\overline{Q}
.
The zero locus of
\mu
:

\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(\overline{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}):=Z(\mu)

is the
Nakajima quiver variety

[
Nak1
,
Nak2
]
(which we assume to be nonempty).
Note that
\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(\overline{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})
is a smooth subvariety in
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\overline{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})
with codimension equals to
\mathcal{G}
, and
Z(\mu)=Z^{\mathrm{der}}(\mu)
.

Denote
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
the torus that acts on
R(\overline{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})
by assigning weights
R(\overline{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})=M\oplus\hbar^{-1}M^{\vee}
, where

\displaystyle M=\bigoplus_{a\in Q_{1}}\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{v}_{t(a)}},\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{v}_{t(a)}})\oplus\bigoplus_{i\in Q_{0}}\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{d}_{i}},\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{v}_{i}}).

We choose torus
\mathsf{T}
which contains
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
such that
\mathsf{T}=(\mathsf{T}/\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar})\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
, and let
\mathsf{A}\subseteq\mathsf{T}/\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
be a subtorus. Choose a function
\phi:\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\overline{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})\to\mathbb{C}
and a section
\mu\in\Gamma(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\overline{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\mathcal{\hbar}^{-1}\mathcal{G}^{\vee})
and appropriate
\mathsf{T}
action such that
\phi
and
\mu
are
\mathsf{T}
-invariant. Let us define the
\mathsf{T}
action on edge loops by scaling with weight
\hbar
, then
\mathsf{T}
fixes the pairing
\langle e,\mu\rangle
. Then according to Example
6.12
, the dimensional reduction data

\left(\overset{\bullet}{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\widetilde{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\overline{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\mu,\phi\right),\quad\left(\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(\overline{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(\overline{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),0,\phi|_{\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(\overline{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}\right)

are compatible.

Remark 6.14
.

In the above example, if we take
\phi=0
, then dimensional reduction for critical cohomology
[
Dav1
, Thm. A.1]
and respectively for critical
K
-theory
[
Isi
, Thm. 3.6]
implies that

\displaystyle\delta_{H}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(\overline{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}))

\displaystyle\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(\overset{\bullet}{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\widetilde{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\widetilde{\mathsf{w}})

\displaystyle\delta_{K}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(\overline{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}))

\displaystyle\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(\overset{\bullet}{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\widetilde{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\widetilde{\mathsf{w}})

are isomorphisms. Here
\delta_{H}
and
\delta_{K}
are the maps defined in Proposition
6.7
and

(6.18)

\widetilde{\mathsf{w}}=\sum_{i\in Q_{0}}\operatorname{tr}(\varepsilon_{i}\mu_{i}),

is the
cubic potential
, where
\varepsilon_{i}
is the edge loop on the node
i
, and
\mu_{i}
is the
i
-th component of moment map.

It is known that
[
Dav2
, Lem. 6.3]
:

\mathrm{Crit}(\widetilde{\mathsf{w}})\subseteq\overset{\bullet}{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\widetilde{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),

therefore the restriction from
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\widetilde{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})
to
\overset{\bullet}{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\widetilde{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})
gives isomorphisms:

\displaystyle H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\widetilde{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\widetilde{\mathsf{w}})\cong H^{\mathsf{T}}(\overset{\bullet}{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\widetilde{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\widetilde{\mathsf{w}}),\quad K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\widetilde{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\widetilde{\mathsf{w}})\cong K^{\mathsf{T}}(\overset{\bullet}{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\widetilde{Q},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\widetilde{\mathsf{w}}).

More examples on compatible dimensional reduction data are discussed in
[
COZZ1
]
, which are used to study shifted Yangians of
\mathfrak{gl}_{2}
,
shifted affine Yangians of
\mathfrak{gl}_{1}
and their representations.

7.
Deformations of potentials and stable envelopes

In this section, we discuss deformations of potentials and how they interplay with stable envelopes. This provides powerful tools
in the computations of stable envelopes, relating different modules of quantum groups, see
[
COZZ1
]
.

Setting 7.1
.

Fix
(X,\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A})
in Setting
2.1
. Suppose there is a torus action
\mathbb{G}_{m}\curvearrowright X
which commutes with
\mathsf{T}
-action.

•

Assume that
\mathsf{f}\colon X\to\mathbb{C}
is a
(\mathsf{T}\times\mathbb{G}_{m})
-invariant function, and
\mathsf{g}\colon X\to\mathbb{C}
is a
\mathsf{T}
-invariant function together with a decomposition

\mathsf{g}=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathsf{g}_{i},

such that
\mathbb{G}_{m}
scales
\mathsf{g}_{i}
with weight
-\mathsf{n}_{i}<0
, i.e.
(u\cdot\mathsf{g}_{i})(x):=\mathsf{g}_{i}(u^{-1}\cdot x)=u^{-n_{i}}\cdot\mathsf{g}_{i}(x)
.

•

Let
\widetilde{X}:=X\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
with
(\mathsf{T}\times\mathbb{G}_{m})
-action given by the aforementioned action on
X
and the action

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{pr}}\mathbb{G}_{m}\curvearrowright\mathbb{A}^{1}

on
\mathbb{A}^{1}
, where
\mathbb{G}_{m}\curvearrowright\mathbb{A}^{1}
is of weight
-1
. Let
t
be the coordinate on
\mathbb{A}^{1}
, with
(\mathsf{T}\times\mathbb{G}_{m})
-invariant function:

\displaystyle\mathsf{w}:=\mathsf{f}+\sum_{i=1}^{m}t^{\mathsf{n}_{i}}\mathsf{g}_{i}\colon\widetilde{X}\to\mathbb{C},\quad(x,t)\mapsto f(x)+\sum_{i=1}^{m}t^{\mathsf{n}_{i}}\mathsf{g}_{i}(x).

When we discuss the relation with stable envelopes, we work in the following setting.

Setting 7.2
.

Let
(X,\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathsf{f},\mathsf{g})
be as in Setting
7.1
. Fix
a chamber
\mathfrak{C}\subseteq\operatorname{Lie}(\mathsf{A})_{\mathbb{R}}
and a slope
\mathsf{s}\in\operatorname{Pic}_{\mathsf{A}}(X)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{R}
. Assume
cohomological and
K
-theoretic stable envelope correspondences exist for
(X,0,\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C})
and
(X,0,\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C},\mathsf{s})
respectively.

Remark 7.3
.

Since canonical maps take stable envelope correspondences for zero potential to stable envelope correspondences for any potential, then according to Proposition
3.31
, cohomological and
K
-theoretic stable envelopes exist for
(X,\mathsf{f},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C}),(X,\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C})
and
(X,\mathsf{f},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C},\mathsf{s}),(X,\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C},\mathsf{s})
respectively in Setting
7.2
.

7.1.
Specialization for Borel-Moore homology and
K
-theory of zero loci

Lemma 7.4
.

Under Setting
7.1
, let
Z(\mathsf{w})\subseteq\widetilde{X}
denote the zero locus of
\mathsf{w}
and
Z(\mathsf{w})^{*}
its open subset fitting into commutative diagrams

\textstyle{Z(\mathsf{w})^{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Box}
\textstyle{Z(\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\curvearrowright}
\textstyle{\widetilde{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{pr}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{C}^{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{A}^{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{A}^{1},}

where
\operatorname{pr}
is the projection and the left square is Cartesian. Then the left vertical map is a trivial fibration such that

Z(\mathsf{w})^{*}\cong Z(\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g})\times\mathbb{C}^{*}.

Proof.

There is an automorphism

(7.1)

\mathbf{a}\colon\widetilde{X}^{*}:=X\times\mathbb{C}^{*}\to\widetilde{X}^{*},\quad(x,t)\mapsto(t^{-1}\cdot x,t),

where
t\cdot x
means identifying
\mathbb{C}^{*}
with
\mathbb{G}_{m}
and
t
acts on
x
via the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
action. Then

\mathbf{a}^{*}(\mathsf{w})=\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g},

therefore
\mathbf{a}^{-1}(Z(\mathsf{w})^{*})=Z(\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g})\times\mathbb{C}^{*}
.
∎

It follows that the projection
Z(\mathsf{w})\to\mathbb{C}
fits in the setting of
[
CG
, §2.6.30]
.

Definition 7.5
.

Under Setting
7.1
,
we define a
specialization map
:

(7.2)

\displaystyle\mathsf{sp}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g}))\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f})),

as the composition of the following maps

\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}_{i}(Z(\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{pr}^{*}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}_{i+2}(Z(\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g})\times\mathbb{C}^{*})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{a}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}_{i+2}(Z(\mathsf{w})^{*})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\underset{t\to 0}{\lim}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}_{i}(Z(\mathsf{w})\cap\{t=0\})=H_{i}^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f})),}

where
\underset{t\to 0}{\lim}
is the map defined in
[
CG
, §2.6.30]
.

Remark 7.6
.

According to the construction in
[
CG
, §2.6.30]
, we have an equality

\displaystyle\underset{t\to 0}{\lim}(-)=\delta\circ(-\cap\alpha).

Here
\alpha\in H_{1}(\mathbb{C}^{*})\cong\mathbb{Q}
is the Borel-Moore homology class given by pushforward of the fundamental class
[\mathbb{R}]\in H_{1}(\mathbb{R})
under the exponential map

\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{C}^{*},\quad s\mapsto e^{s},

and for any
\theta\in[0,2\pi)
, the maps
s\mapsto e^{s+i\theta}
and
s\mapsto e^{s}
induce the same map
H_{1}(\mathbb{R})\to H_{1}(\mathbb{C}^{*})
on homologies.

(-)\cap\alpha\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}_{j}(Z(\mathsf{w})^{*})\to H^{\mathsf{T}}_{j-1}(Z(\mathsf{w})^{*})
is the intersection pairing with
\alpha
.
\delta
is the boundary map in the excision long exact sequence

\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}_{i+1}(Z(\mathsf{w})^{*})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}_{i}(Z(\mathsf{w})\cap\{t=0\})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}_{i}(Z(\mathsf{w}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}_{i}(Z(\mathsf{w})^{*})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta}
.

Then it follows that
\mathsf{sp}
(
7.2
) equals to the composition of the following maps

\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}_{i}(Z(\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{id}\otimes\alpha}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}_{i+1}(Z(\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g})\times\mathbb{C}^{*})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{a}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}_{i+1}(Z(\mathsf{w})^{*})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}_{i}(Z(\mathsf{w})\cap\{t=0\})=H_{i}^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f})).}

Similarly, there is a
K
-theoretic

specialization map
(ref.
[
CG
, §5.3]
).

Definition 7.7
.

Under Setting
7.1
, we define

(7.3)

\displaystyle\mathsf{sp}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g}))\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f})),

as the composition of the following maps

\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{pr}^{*}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g})\times\mathbb{C}^{*})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{a}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{w})^{*})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\underset{t\to 0}{\lim}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{w})\cap\{t=0\})=K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f})).}

Here
\underset{t\to 0}{\lim}
is defined by an extension to a class on
Z(\mathsf{w})
followed by
refined Gysin pullback
i^{!}_{0}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{w}))\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{w})\cap\{t=0\})
. This is well-defined as we have the excision exact sequence:

\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{w})\cap\{t=0\})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{0*}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{w}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{w})^{*})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0,}

and
i^{!}_{0}\circ i_{0*}(-)=0\in K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{w})\cap\{t=0\})
.

Remark 7.8
.

If
\mathsf{g}=0
, then the specialization maps (
7.2
) and (
7.3
) are identity maps. This can be seen as follows. In this case,
Z(\mathsf{w})=Z(\mathsf{f})\times\mathbb{C}
and
\mathbf{a}
maps
Z(\mathsf{w})^{*}
to itself. Then
\mathbf{a}_{*}
is the identity map because the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
action on
H^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f}))
is trivial. In particular
\mathbf{a}_{*}\circ\operatorname{pr}^{*}(\gamma)=\gamma\otimes[\mathbb{C}^{*}]
for any
\gamma\in H^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f}))
. It follows that

\displaystyle\mathsf{sp}(\gamma)=\underset{t\to 0}{\lim}(\gamma\otimes[\mathbb{C}^{*}])=\gamma\otimes\underset{t\to 0}{\lim}([\mathbb{C}^{*}])=\gamma,

where in the second equality we use the fact that
\underset{t\to 0}{\lim}
is compatible with external tensor product. The
K
-theory version can be argued similarly and we omit the details.

Remark 7.9
.

Specialization maps (
7.2
) and (
7.3
) are compatible with pushforward maps induced by the embedding of the zero locus into
X
. Namely the following diagrams are commutative:

\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X),}

\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X).}

In particular, if
\mathsf{f}=0
, then the specialization maps
\mathsf{sp}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{g}))\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(X)
and
\mathsf{sp}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{g}))\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(X)
equal to the pushforward maps induced by the closed immersion
Z(\mathsf{g})\hookrightarrow X
.

Remark 7.10
.

Specialization maps (
7.2
) and (
7.3
) are
functorial
with respect to
proper pushforward
and
lci pullback
. Namely, let
(Y,\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathbb{G}_{m})
be in Setting
7.1
, and assume that there exists a
(\mathsf{T}\times\mathbb{G}_{m})
-equivariant map
\pi\colon Y\to X
. Then we have commutative diagrams

\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(Z((\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g})\circ\pi))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f}\circ\pi))}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{!}}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces,}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{!}}

\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z((\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g})\circ\pi))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f}\circ\pi))}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{!}}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces,}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{!}}

where we use the fact that
X
and
Y
are smooth, so the above lci pullbacks
\pi^{!}
are well-defined.

If
\pi
is moreover proper, then we have commutative diagrams

\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(Z((\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g})\circ\pi))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{*}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f}\circ\pi))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{*}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f})),}

\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z((\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g})\circ\pi))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{*}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f}\circ\pi))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{*}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f})).}

Specialization maps are compatible with stable envelopes for zero loci.

Proposition 7.11
.

Fix
(X,\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathsf{f},\mathsf{g},\mathfrak{C},\mathsf{s})
as in Setting
7.2
, then the following diagrams are commutative:

\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g})^{\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}^{\mathsf{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f})^{\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f})),}

\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g})^{\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}^{\mathsf{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f})^{\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f})),}

where
\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}
and
\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}
are maps induced by the stable envelope correspondences as in Lemma
3.30
, and
\mathsf{sp}^{\mathsf{A}}
is the specialization maps (
7.2
) and (
7.3
) for the
\mathsf{A}
-fixed loci.

Proof.

We first prove the cohomology version. By definition, it is enough to show that every square in the following diagram is commutative:

\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}_{i}(Z(\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g})^{\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{pr}^{*}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}_{i+2}(Z(\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g})^{\mathsf{A}}\times\mathbb{C}^{*})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{a}_{*}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}_{i+2}((Z(\mathsf{w})^{*})^{\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\underset{t\to 0}{\lim}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}_{i}(Z(\mathsf{w})^{\mathsf{A}}\cap\{t=0\})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}_{i}(Z(\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{pr}^{*}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}_{i+2}(Z(\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g})\times\mathbb{C}^{*})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{a}_{*}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}_{i+2}(Z(\mathsf{w})^{*})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\underset{t\to 0}{\lim}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}_{i}(Z(\mathsf{w})\cap\{t=0\}),}

where the first and the fourth vertical arrows are induced by
[\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}]\in H^{\mathsf{T}}(X\times X^{\mathsf{A}})_{\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
, the second and the third ones are induced by
[\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}]\otimes[\Delta_{\mathbb{C}^{*}}]\in H^{\mathsf{T}}(X\times X^{\mathsf{A}}\times(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{2})_{\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}\times(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{2}}
, for the diagonal
\Delta_{\mathbb{C}^{*}}
in
(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{2}
. It is obvious that the first two squares commute. The commutativity of the third square follows from the fact that specialization commutes with the convolution
[
CG
, Prop. 2.7.23]
.

Similarly, in the
K
-theory version, we have

\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g})^{\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{pr}^{*}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g})^{\mathsf{A}}\times\mathbb{C}^{*})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{a}_{*}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}((Z(\mathsf{w})^{*})^{\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\underset{t\to 0}{\lim}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{w})^{\mathsf{A}}\cap\{t=0\})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{pr}^{*}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g})\times\mathbb{C}^{*})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{a}_{*}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{w})^{*})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\underset{t\to 0}{\lim}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{w})\cap\{t=0\}),}

where the first and the fourth vertical arrows are induced by
[\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}]\in K^{\mathsf{T}}(X\times X^{\mathsf{A}})_{\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
, the second and the third ones are induced by
[\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}]\otimes[\mathcal{O}_{\Delta_{\mathbb{C}^{*}}}]\in K^{\mathsf{T}}(X\times X^{\mathsf{A}}\times(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{2})_{\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}\times(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{2}}
. It is obvious that the first two squares
commute. The commutativity of the third square follows from
[
CG
, Thm. 5.3.9]
.
∎

The goal of the rest of this section is to adapt the above construction to critical cohomology and
K
-theory (in several settings) and to prove various compatibility properties.

7.2.
Specialization for critical cohomology

Assumption 7.12
.

In Setting
7.1
, we further assume the affinization map

\pi\colon X\to X_{0}=\operatorname{Spec}\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})

is proper and the induced
\mathbb{G}_{m}
action on
\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})
is nonpositive (equivalently, the induced
\mathbb{G}_{m}
action attracts
X_{0}
to the fixed locus
X_{0}^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}
).

Lemma 7.13
.

Under Assumption
7.12
, we have

X\times\{0\}=\bigcup_{F\in\operatorname{Fix}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\widetilde{X})}\operatorname{Attr}_{+}(F).

Proof.

Let
\widetilde{X}_{0}:=X_{0}\times\mathbb{C}
be the affinization of
\widetilde{X}
. By Setting
7.1
, the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
action on
\mathbb{C}
is repelling, so the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-fixed point locus
\widetilde{X}_{0}^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}
equals to
X_{0}^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}\times\{0\}
, and the attracting set
\operatorname{Attr}_{+}(\widetilde{X}_{0}^{\mathbb{G}_{m}})
equals to
X_{0}
by Assumption
7.12
. Denote the affinization map by
p:\widetilde{X}\to\widetilde{X}_{0}
, then
for any
F\in\operatorname{Fix}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\widetilde{X})
,

\operatorname{Attr}_{+}(F)\subseteq p^{-1}(\operatorname{Attr}_{+}(p(F)))\subseteq p^{-1}(\operatorname{Attr}_{+}(\widetilde{X}_{0}^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}))=X\times\{0\}.

This proves one direction of inclusion. For the other direction, take any point
x\in X\times\{0\}
, the limit

\lim_{u\to 0}p(u\cdot x)=\lim_{u\to 0}u\cdot p(x)

exists by Assumption
7.12
,
where
u\cdot
is the action of
u\in\mathbb{G}_{m}
. Since
p
is proper by Assumption
7.12
,
\lim_{u\to 0}u\cdot x
exists by valuative criterion of properness; thus
x\in\operatorname{Attr}_{+}(F)
for some
F\in\operatorname{Fix}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\widetilde{X})
. This concludes the proof.
∎

Definition 7.14
.

Under Assumption
7.12
, we define a
specialization map

(7.4)

\displaystyle\mathsf{sp}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g})\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{f}),

as the composition of the following maps

\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}_{i}(X,\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{id}\otimes\alpha\,\,}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}_{i+1}(X\times\mathbb{C}^{*},\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{a}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}_{i+1}(\widetilde{X}^{*},\mathsf{w}|_{\widetilde{X}^{*}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}_{i}(X\times\{0\},\mathsf{w}|_{\{t=0\}})=H_{i}^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{f}).}

Here
\alpha\in H_{1}(\mathbb{C}^{*})
is the class defined in Remark
7.6
,
\mathbf{a}
is given in (
7.1
), and
\delta
is the boundary map in the following excision long exact sequence (exactness follows from Proposition
2.4
and Lemma
7.13
):

\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}_{i+1}(\widetilde{X}^{*},\mathsf{w}|_{\widetilde{X}^{*}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}_{i}(X\times\{0\},\mathsf{w}|_{\{t=0\}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}_{i}(\widetilde{X},\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}_{i}(\widetilde{X}^{*},\mathsf{w}|_{\widetilde{X}^{*}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta}
.

Remark 7.15
.

If
\mathsf{g}=0
, then specialization map (
7.4
) is the identity map. This can be seen as follows. In this case,
\mathbf{a}_{*}
is the identity map because the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
action on
H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{f})
is trivial. Then for any
\gamma\in H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{f})
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{sp}(\gamma)=\delta(\gamma\otimes\alpha)=\gamma\otimes\delta(\alpha)=\gamma,

where by using Thom-Sebastiani isomorphism, the above equalities reduce to the calculation in Remark
7.6
.

Remark 7.16
.

Comparing Definition
7.14
with Remark
7.6
, and noticing that canonical maps commute with
(-)\otimes\alpha
,
\mathbf{a}_{*}
and boundary map
\delta
, we see that canonical maps commute with specialization maps (
7.2
) and (
7.4
), i.e. we have commutative diagram:

\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{can}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{can}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{f}).}

In particular, if
\mathsf{f}=0
, then
\operatorname{can}\circ\,\mathsf{sp}
equals to the pushforward induced by the inclusion
Z(\mathsf{g})\hookrightarrow X
.

Specialization map (
7.4
) is
functorial
with respect to
proper pushforward
and
lci pullback
, i.e.

Proposition 7.17
.

Let
(X,\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathbb{G}_{m},\mathsf{f},\mathsf{g})
be in Setting
7.1
and
suppose there is a
(\mathsf{T}\times\mathbb{G}_{m})
-equivariant map
\pi\colon Y\to X
, so that
(Y,\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathbb{G}_{m},\mathsf{f}\circ\pi,\mathsf{g}\circ\pi)
is also in Setting
7.1
.
Assume
X
,
Y
both satisfy Assumption
7.12
.

Then we have commutative diagram

\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(Y,(\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g})\circ\pi)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(Y,\mathsf{f}\circ\pi)}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{!}}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{f})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces,}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{!}}

where we use the fact that
X
and
Y
are smooth, so the above lci pullbacks
\pi^{!}
are well-defined.

If
\pi
is moreover proper, then we have commutative diagram

\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(Y,(\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g})\circ\pi)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{*}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(Y,\mathsf{f}\circ\pi)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{*}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{f}).}

Proof.

Lci pullback commutes with
(-)\otimes\alpha
,
\mathbf{a}_{*}
and boundary map
\delta
, so it commutes with specialization maps for critical cohomology, similarly for the proper pushforward case.
∎

Specialization map (
7.4
) is compatible with stable envelopes.

Proposition 7.18
.

Fix
(X,\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathbb{G}_{m},\mathsf{f},\mathsf{g},\mathfrak{C})
as in Setting
7.2
which satisfies Assumption
7.12
. Then the following diagram is commutative

\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{f}^{\mathsf{A}}+\mathsf{g}^{\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}^{\mathsf{A}}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{f}^{\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\,\,\,\,\mathsf{sp}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{f}).}

Proof.

It is easy to see that
\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}
commutes with
(-)\otimes\alpha
and
\mathbf{a}_{*}
. It remains to show that
\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}
commutes with the boundary map
\delta
. Note that
\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}
is induced from the stable envelope correspondence
[\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}]
via the critical convolution (see §
3.5
), which consists of a Gysin pullback
and a proper pushforward. Since they both commute with
\delta
,
\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}
commutes with
\delta
as well.
∎

We provide examples where Assumption
7.12
holds, so that there are specialization maps for critical cohomology.

Example 7.19
.

Let
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(Q,\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
be a quiver variety together with a flavor torus
\mathsf{T}
-action (see §
4.2
). Every regular function on
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(Q,\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
is a gauge group
G
-invariant function on the representation space
R(Q,\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
, which can be written as

\mathsf{w}=\operatorname{tr}(\mathsf{W})

where
\mathsf{W}
is a linear combination of cycles in the Crawley-Boevey quiver associated with
(Q,\underline{\mathbf{d}})

14
14

14

Crawley-Boevey quiver is obtained from
Q
by adding a new node
\infty
to
Q
and
\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in},i}
arrows from
\infty
to
i\in Q_{0}
and
\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out},i}
arrows from
i
to
\infty

[
CB
]
.
. Suppose that
\mathsf{W}
does not contain constant term,  i.e.  there is no trivial cycle, then
\mathsf{w}
has negative weights with respect to the
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-action which scale every arrow with weight
-1
. The data
X=\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(Q,\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
,
\mathsf{f}=0
,
\mathsf{g}=\mathsf{w}
, and the above
\mathbb{C}^{*}
action fit into
Setting
7.1
and
satisfy Assumption
7.12
.
In particular, there exists a specialization map for critical cohomology (Definition
7.14
):

\mathsf{sp}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(Q,\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w})\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(Q,\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})),

which makes the following diagram commutative (Remark
7.16
)

\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{w}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{can}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(Q,\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(Q,\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})).}

Moreover, in the case when
Q
is symmetric and
\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}}=\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}=\mathbf{d}
and
\mathsf{A}
-action is self-dual, the specialization maps are compatible with stable envelopes by Proposition
7.18
, i.e. the following diagram is commutative:

\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(Q,\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}^{\mathsf{A}}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(Q,\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(Q,\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\,\,\,\,\mathsf{sp}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(Q,\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})).}

The above construction also applies to Nakajima variety
\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})
, and for any potential
\mathsf{w}=\operatorname{tr}(\mathsf{W})
with negative weight under the
\mathbb{C}^{*}
action that scale every arrow with weight
-1
, we get a specialization

\mathsf{sp}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(Q,\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w})\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(Q,\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})),

which is compatible with canonical map and stable envelope.

7.3.
Specialization for critical
K
-theory

7.3.1.
Definition and properties

Contrary to the case of critical cohomology, the canonical map in critical
K
-theory is always surjective. Therefore it is convenient
to formulate its specalization map to be the unique one (if exists) which is compatible with the specialization map of the zero locus.

Definition 7.20
.

Under Setting
7.1
, a
specialization
for critical
K
-theory is a map
\mathsf{sp}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g})\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{f})
which makes the following diagram commutative:

\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{can}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{can}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{f}),}

where the upper horizontal line is given in (
7.3
).

Specialization map in Definition
7.20
is
functorial
with respect to
proper pushforward
and
lci pullback
, i.e.

Proposition 7.21
.

Let
(X,\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathbb{G}_{m},\mathsf{f},\mathsf{g})
be in Setting
7.1
and
suppose there is a
(\mathsf{T}\times\mathbb{G}_{m})
-equivariant map
\pi\colon Y\to X
, so that

(Y,\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathbb{G}_{m},\mathsf{f}\circ\pi,\mathsf{g}\circ\pi)
also fits into Setting
7.1
.
Assume that specialization maps
\mathsf{sp}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g})\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{f})
and
\mathsf{sp}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(Y,(\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g})\circ\pi)\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(Y,\mathsf{f}\circ\pi)
exist. Then we have a commutative diagram:

\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Y,(\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g})\circ\pi)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Y,\mathsf{f}\circ\pi)}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{!}}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{f})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces,}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{!}}

where we use the fact that
X
and
Y
are smooth, so the above lci pullbacks
\pi^{!}
are well-defined.

If
\pi
is moreover proper, then we have a commutative diagram:

\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Y,(\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g})\circ\pi)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{*}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Y,\mathsf{f}\circ\pi)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{*}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{f}).}

Proof.

Consider the following diagram:

\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z((\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g})\circ\pi))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{can}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f}\circ\pi))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{can}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Y,(\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g})\circ\pi)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Y,\mathsf{f}\circ\pi)}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{!}}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{f})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{!}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{can}}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{!}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{!}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{can}}

The outer square is commutative by Remark
7.10
. The left and right trapezoids are commutative by
[
VV1
, Lem. 2.4(a)]
. The top and bottom trapezoids are commutative by Definition
7.20
. Then it follows that

\displaystyle\pi^{!}\circ\,\mathsf{sp}\circ\operatorname{can}=\mathsf{sp}\circ\pi^{!}\circ\operatorname{can}.

Since
K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g}))\xrightarrow{\operatorname{can}}K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g})
is surjective, we get
\pi^{!}\circ\,\mathsf{sp}=\mathsf{sp}\circ\pi^{!}
.
The commutativity in the proper pushforward case can be proven similarly, with
[
VV1
, Lem. 2.4(a)]
replaced by
[
VV1
, Lem. 2.4(b)]
.
∎

Specialization map in Definition
7.20
is compatible with stable envelopes, i.e.

Proposition 7.22
.

Fix
(X,\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathbb{G}_{m},\mathsf{f},\mathsf{g},\mathfrak{C},\mathsf{s})
as in Setting
7.2
. Suppose that there exist specialization maps
\mathsf{sp}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g})\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{f})
and
\mathsf{sp}^{\mathsf{A}}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{f}^{\mathsf{A}}+\mathsf{g}^{\mathsf{A}})\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{f}^{\mathsf{A}})
. Then they are compatible with
K
-theoretic stable envelopes, namely the following diagram is commutative

\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{f}^{\mathsf{A}}+\mathsf{g}^{\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}^{\mathsf{A}}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{f}^{\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{f}).}

Proof.

The proof is similar as in above.
Consider the following diagram:

\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f}^{\mathsf{A}}+\mathsf{g}^{\mathsf{A}}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}^{\mathsf{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{can}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f}^{\mathsf{A}}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{can}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{f}^{\mathsf{A}}+\mathsf{g}^{\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}^{\mathsf{A}}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{f}^{\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{f})}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f}+\mathsf{g}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{can}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{can}}

The outer square is commutative by Proposition
7.11
. The left and right trapezoids are commutative by Lemma
3.30
. The top and bottom trapezoids are commutative by assumption (i.e. (
7.20
) commutes). Then it follows that

\displaystyle\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ\,\mathsf{sp}^{\mathsf{A}}\circ\operatorname{can}=\mathsf{sp}\circ\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ\operatorname{can}.

Since
K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{f}^{\mathsf{A}}+\mathsf{g}^{\mathsf{A}}))\xrightarrow{\operatorname{can}}K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{f}^{\mathsf{A}}+\mathsf{g}^{\mathsf{A}})
is surjective, we get
\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ\,\mathsf{sp}^{\mathsf{A}}=\mathsf{sp}\circ\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}
.
∎

In below, we provide two existence results for specialization maps in critical
K
-theory.

7.3.2.
Existence result I

The first existence is due to Pădurariu in his study of
K
-theoretic Hall algebras for quivers with potentials and relation to shuffle algebras.

Proposition 7.23

(
[
P
, Prop. 3.6]
)
.

Suppose that
\mathsf{f}=0
. Assume that

•

the
\mathsf{T}
-fixed locus of
X
lies in the zero locus of
\mathsf{g}
, i.e.
X^{\mathsf{T}}\subseteq Z(\mathsf{g})
,

•

K^{\mathsf{T}}(X)
is torsion free over
K_{\mathsf{T}}(\operatorname{pt})
.

Then the specialization map
\mathsf{sp}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{g})\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(X)
exists.

Remark 7.24
.

The argument in the proof of
[
P
, Prop. 3.6]
works for a reductive group
\mathsf{T}
, so is Proposition
7.23
.

7.3.3.
Existence result II

The second result is about how to induce specialization maps for GIT semistable loci from specialization maps of their ambient spaces.
This is motivated by
[
N
, Eqn. (104)]
where the induced specialization map is used.

Let
G
be a complex reductive group,
\mathsf{T}
be a torus such that

\mathsf{T}\times G
act on
\mathbb{P}^{n}\times\mathbb{A}^{m}
. Let
X\subset\mathbb{P}^{n}\times\mathbb{A}^{m}
be a smooth projective-over-affine variety invariant under the action of
\mathsf{T}\times G
,
\mathsf{g}\colon X\to\mathbb{C}
be a
(\mathsf{T}\times G)
-invariant regular function, which satisfies the weight conditions in Setting
7.1
with
\mathsf{T}
replaced with
\mathsf{T}\times G
. Let
\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{O}(1)
be a chosen linearization, and
X^{ss}
be the semistable locus of
X
with respect to the
G
action and polarization
\mathcal{L}
.

Proposition 7.25
.

Assume that
\mathsf{g}\colon X\to\mathbb{C}
is not a constant function. Suppose that there exists a specialization map
\mathsf{sp}_{X}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}\times G}(X,\mathsf{g})\to K^{\mathsf{T}\times G}(X)
. Then there exists a specialization map
\mathsf{sp}_{X^{ss}}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}\times G}(X^{ss},\mathsf{g})\to K^{\mathsf{T}\times G}(X^{ss})
, and the diagram

(7.9)

\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}\times G}(X,\mathsf{g})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}_{X}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}\times G}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}\times G}(X^{ss},\mathsf{g})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}_{X^{ss}}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}\times G}(X^{ss})}

is commutative, where the vertical arrows are the restriction to the semistable locus.

Moreover, if
\mathsf{sp}_{X}
is injective, then so is
\mathsf{sp}_{X^{ss}}
.

Proof.

We know that the unstable locus
X\setminus X^{ss}
admits a Kempf-Ness (KN) stratification
[
Kir
,
DH
]
, see §
4.7.2
for a description of KN stratification when
X
is a linear representation of
G
. By induction on the open strata, it suffices to prove the proposition replacing
X^{ss}
with
V:=X\setminus S
for a closed KN strata
S
. We note that there exists a cocharacter
\lambda:\mathbb{C}^{*}\to G
such that
Z:=X^{\lambda}
is a closed subvariety of
S
. Let

Y=\operatorname{Attr}_{\lambda}(Z):=\{x\in X,\lim_{t\to 0}\lambda(t)\cdot x\in Z\},

then
Y
is a closed subvariety in
S
and the action map
G\times_{P_{\lambda}}Y\to S
induces an isomorphism. Here
P_{\lambda}=\{g\in G:\lim_{t\to 0}\lambda(t)g\lambda(t)^{-1}\text{ exists}\}
is the parabolic subgroup associated to
\lambda
. We introduce notations

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.77083pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.77083pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.57538pt\raise 4.50694pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sigma}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.77083pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 30.77083pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.55421pt\raise-10.50694pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 6.77106pt\raise-3.53009pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 51.61195pt\raise
