5.99652pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.63542pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{j}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 68.7986pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 68.7986pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{S}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

where
\sigma
,
j
are natural closed immersions, and
\pi
is the attraction map
\pi(y)=\lim_{t\to 0}\lambda(t)\cdot y
. We also let

\mathfrak{X}:=[X/(\mathsf{T}\times G)],\quad\mathfrak{V}=[V/(\mathsf{T}\times G)],\quad\mathfrak{S}:=[S/(\mathsf{T}\times G)],

and we put a subscript
0
to denote the zero locus of
\mathsf{g}
, for example
\mathfrak{X}_{0}:=[Z(\mathsf{g})/(\mathsf{T}\times G)]
,
\mathfrak{V}_{0}=[(V\cap Z(\mathsf{g}))/(\mathsf{T}\times G)]
, and so on.

Fix
w\in\mathbb{Z}
, and recall that Halpern-Leistner constructed a semiorthogonal decomposition (SOD) of the bounded derived category
\operatorname{D}^{b}(\mathfrak{X})
of coherent sheaves on
\mathfrak{X}
in
[
Hal
, Thm. 3.35]
:

\displaystyle\operatorname{D}^{b}(\mathfrak{X})=\left\langle\operatorname{D}^{b}_{\mathfrak{S}}(\mathfrak{X})_{<w},\mathbf{G}_{w},\operatorname{D}^{b}_{\mathfrak{S}}(\mathfrak{X})_{\geq w}\right\rangle.

Moreover, the restriction from
\mathfrak{X}
to
\mathfrak{V}
induces an equivalence:

\displaystyle\mathbf{G}_{w}\cong\operatorname{D}^{b}(\mathfrak{V}).

On the other hand, since
\mathsf{g}
is
\mathsf{T}\times G
invariant, we have
Z_{0}=X_{0}^{\lambda}
, and
Y_{0}=\operatorname{Attr}_{\lambda}(Z_{0})
, and the action map
G\times_{P_{\lambda}}Y_{0}\to S_{0}
induces an isomorphism. We have the following induced maps

\textstyle{Z_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{0}}
\textstyle{Y_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{0}}
\scriptstyle{j}
\textstyle{S_{0}}

Notice that
Y_{0}\cong Z_{0}\times_{Z}Y
where
Z_{0}\to Z
is the inclusion and
Y\to Z
is
\pi
. In particular,
\pi_{0}\colon Y_{0}\to Z_{0}
is a locally trivial bundle of affine spaces, which is the condition (A) in
[
Hal
, pp. 7]
. We claim that the condition (L+) in
[
Hal
, pp. 7]
is also satisfied for the variety
X_{0}
with KN strata
S_{0}
, that is,
L\sigma_{0}^{*}\mathbf{L}_{S_{0}/X_{0}}
has nonnegative weights w.r.t.
\lambda
.

•

If
\mathsf{g}
is not constantly zero on
S
, then
\mathsf{g}|_{S}\colon S\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
is a flat morphism (since
\mathsf{g}|_{S}
can not be a nonzero constant function due to weight conditions in Setting
7.1
). In this case,
S_{0}
is isomorphic to the derived zero locus
S\times^{\mathbf{L}}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\{0\}
, and we have

\displaystyle L\sigma_{0}^{*}\mathbf{L}_{S_{0}/X_{0}}\cong L(Z_{0}\hookrightarrow Z)^{*}L\sigma^{*}\mathbf{L}_{S/X}.

Since
L\sigma^{*}\mathbf{L}_{S/X}
has nonnegative weights w.r.t.
\lambda
(see
[
Hal
, Lem. 2.7]
), so is
L\sigma_{0}^{*}\mathbf{L}_{S_{0}/X_{0}}
.

•

If
\mathsf{g}|_{S}=0
, then there is an exact triangle

\displaystyle L\sigma_{0}^{*}L(S_{0}\hookrightarrow X_{0})^{*}\mathbf{L}_{X_{0}/X}\to L\sigma_{0}^{*}\mathbf{L}_{S_{0}/X}\to L\sigma_{0}^{*}\mathbf{L}_{S_{0}/X_{0}}\to+1.

Since
S_{0}=S
and
\sigma_{0}=\sigma
,
L\sigma_{0}^{*}\mathbf{L}_{S_{0}/X}=L\sigma^{*}\mathbf{L}_{S/X}
has nonnegative weights w.r.t.
\lambda
(see
[
Hal
, Lem. 2.7]
).
Since
\mathsf{g}\colon X\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
is flat, we have
\mathbf{L}_{X_{0}/X}\cong\mathcal{O}_{X_{0}}[1]
. In particular,
L\sigma_{0}^{*}L(S_{0}\hookrightarrow X_{0})^{*}\mathbf{L}_{X_{0}/X}\cong\mathcal{O}_{Z_{0}}[1]
has zero
\lambda
weight. It follows that
L\sigma_{0}^{*}\mathbf{L}_{S_{0}/X_{0}}
has nonnegative weights w.r.t.
\lambda
.

In both cases, (L+) condition holds. Then by
[
Hal
, Thm. 3.35]
, we have an SOD

\displaystyle\operatorname{D}^{b}(\mathfrak{X}_{0})=\left\langle\operatorname{D}^{b}_{\mathfrak{S}_{0}}(\mathfrak{X}_{0})_{<w},\mathbf{G}_{0,w},\operatorname{D}^{b}_{\mathfrak{S}_{0}}(\mathfrak{X}_{0})_{\geqslant w}\right\rangle,

and the restriction from
\mathfrak{X}_{0}
to
\mathfrak{V}_{0}
induces an equivalence:

\displaystyle\mathbf{G}_{0,w}\cong\operatorname{D}^{b}(\mathfrak{V}_{0}).

The canonical functor

\operatorname{D}^{b}(\mathfrak{X}_{0})\to\operatorname{D}^{b}(\mathfrak{X}_{0})/\operatorname{Perf}(\mathfrak{X}_{0})\cong\mathrm{MF}(\mathfrak{X},\mathsf{g})

induces an SOD for the matrix factorization category (e.g.
[
P
, Prop. 2.1]
):

\displaystyle\mathrm{MF}(\mathfrak{X},\mathsf{g})=\left\langle\mathrm{MF}_{\mathfrak{S}}(\mathfrak{X},\mathsf{g})_{<w},\mathrm{MF}(\mathbf{G}_{w},\mathsf{g}),\mathrm{MF}_{\mathfrak{S}}(\mathfrak{X},\mathsf{g})_{\geqslant w}\right\rangle,

where
\mathrm{MF}_{\mathfrak{S}}(\mathfrak{X},\mathsf{g})_{<w}
,
\mathrm{MF}(\mathbf{G}_{w},\mathsf{g})
, and
\mathrm{MF}_{\mathfrak{S}}(\mathfrak{X},\mathsf{g})_{\geq w}
are the essential images of
\operatorname{D}^{b}_{\mathfrak{S}_{0}}(\mathfrak{X}_{0})_{<w}
,
\mathbf{G}_{0,w}
, and
\operatorname{D}^{b}_{\mathfrak{S}_{0}}(\mathfrak{X}_{0})_{\geqslant w}
respectively. Moreover, the restriction from
\mathfrak{X}
to
\mathfrak{V}
induces an equivalence:

\displaystyle\mathrm{MF}(\mathbf{G}_{w},\mathsf{g})\cong\mathrm{MF}(\mathfrak{V},\mathsf{g}).

Taking Grothendieck groups, and we obtain a commutative diagram:

(7.10)

\textstyle{K(\mathfrak{X}_{0})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{can}}
\textstyle{K(\mathfrak{X},\mathsf{g})}
\textstyle{K(\mathfrak{V}_{0})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s_{w}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{can}}
\textstyle{K(\mathfrak{V},\mathsf{g})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces,}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{w}}

where horizontal arrows are canonical maps, and vertical arrows are induced by the equivalences
\mathbf{G}_{0,w}\cong\operatorname{D}^{b}(\mathfrak{V}_{0})
and
\mathrm{MF}(\mathbf{G}_{w},\mathsf{g})\cong\mathrm{MF}(\mathfrak{V},\mathsf{g})
. Note that
(\mathfrak{V}_{0}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{X}_{0})^{*}s_{w}=\operatorname{id}
, and
(\mathfrak{V}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{X})^{*}\psi_{w}=\operatorname{id}
. Let
\mathsf{sp}_{\mathfrak{X}}=\mathsf{sp}_{X}\colon K(\mathfrak{X},\mathsf{g})\to K(\mathfrak{X})
denote the specialization map. Then the pushforward map
(\mathfrak{V}_{0}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{V})_{*}:K(\mathfrak{V}_{0})\to K(\mathfrak{V})
factors into

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{V}_{0}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{V})_{*}=(\mathfrak{V}_{0}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{V})_{*}(\mathfrak{V}_{0}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{X}_{0})^{*}s_{w}

\displaystyle=(\mathfrak{V}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{X})^{*}(\mathfrak{X}_{0}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{X})_{*}s_{w}

by def. of specialization

\displaystyle=(\mathfrak{V}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{X})^{*}\circ\mathsf{sp}_{\mathfrak{X}}\circ\operatorname{can}\circ s_{w}

by (
7.10
)

\displaystyle=(\mathfrak{V}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{X})^{*}\circ\mathsf{sp}_{\mathfrak{X}}\circ\psi_{w}\circ\operatorname{can}

Therefore, there exists a specialization map
\mathsf{sp}_{\mathfrak{V}}\colon K(\mathfrak{V},\mathsf{g})
to
K(\mathfrak{V})
and
\mathsf{sp}_{\mathfrak{V}}=(\mathfrak{V}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{X})^{*}\circ\mathsf{sp}_{\mathfrak{X}}\circ\psi_{w}
.

Finally, Let
\bar{\psi}_{w}\colon K(\mathfrak{V})\to K(\mathfrak{X})
be the map obtained by taking Grothendieck groups to the equivalence
\mathbf{G}_{w}\cong\operatorname{D}^{b}(\mathfrak{V})
. Then
\bar{\psi}_{w}
induces an isomorphism
K(\mathfrak{V})\cong K_{0}(\mathbf{G}_{w})
. The image of
\mathsf{sp}_{\mathfrak{X}}\circ\psi_{w}
, which equals to the image of
\mathsf{sp}_{\mathfrak{X}}\circ\psi_{w}\circ\operatorname{can}=\mathsf{sp}_{\mathfrak{X}}\circ\operatorname{can}\circ s_{w}=(\mathfrak{X}_{0}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{X})_{*}s_{w}
, is contained in the Grothendieck group of
(\mathfrak{X}_{0}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{X})_{*}(\mathbf{G}_{0,w})
. Notice that
(\mathfrak{X}_{0}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{X})_{*}(\mathbf{G}_{0,w})
is contained in
\mathbf{G}_{w}
; thus image of
\mathsf{sp}_{\mathfrak{X}}\circ\psi_{w}
is contained in the image of
\bar{\psi}_{w}
. In particular,
\bar{\psi}_{w}\circ(\mathfrak{V}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{X})^{*}
is identity on the image of
\mathsf{sp}_{\mathfrak{X}}\circ\psi_{w}
, and it follows that

\bar{\psi}_{w}\circ\mathsf{sp}_{\mathfrak{V}}=\bar{\psi}_{w}\circ(\mathfrak{V}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{X})^{*}\circ\mathsf{sp}_{\mathfrak{X}}\circ\psi_{w}=\mathsf{sp}_{\mathfrak{X}}\circ\psi_{w}.

Now assume that
\mathsf{sp}_{\mathfrak{X}}\colon K(\mathfrak{X},\mathsf{g})\to K(\mathfrak{X})
is injective, then
\mathsf{sp}_{\mathfrak{X}}\circ\psi_{w}
is also injective since
(\mathfrak{V}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{X})^{*}\psi_{w}=\operatorname{id}
, and the equation
\bar{\psi}_{w}\circ\mathsf{sp}_{\mathfrak{V}}=\mathsf{sp}_{\mathfrak{X}}\circ\psi_{w}
implies that
\mathsf{sp}_{\mathfrak{V}}
is injective.
∎

Combine Propositions
7.23
and
7.25
, we obtain the following.

Corollary 7.26
.

Let
X
be a linear representation of
\mathsf{T}\times G
,
\theta\colon G\to\mathbb{C}^{*}
be a character of
G
,
\mathsf{g}\colon X\to\mathbb{C}
be a
(\mathsf{T}\times G)
-invariant regular function, which satisfies the weight conditions in Setting
7.1
with
\mathsf{T}
replaced with
\mathsf{T}\times G
. Let
X^{ss}
be the
\theta
-semistable locus of
X
with respect to the
G
action. Assume
X^{\mathsf{T}\times G}\subset Z(\mathsf{g})
, then there exist specialization maps
\mathsf{sp}_{X}
and
\mathsf{sp}_{X^{ss}}
for
X
and
X^{ss}
respectively, and the diagram (
7.9
) is commutative. Moreover, if
\mathsf{sp}_{X}
is injective, then so is
\mathsf{sp}_{X^{ss}}
.

Example 7.27
.

Let
X=R(Q,\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
for a quiver
Q
with gauge dimension
\mathbf{v}
and framing dimension
\underline{\mathbf{d}}
,
G=\prod_{i\in Q_{0}}\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v}_{i})
be the gauge group,
\mathsf{T}
be a flavour torus (see §
4.2
). Suppose that every edge loop has onzero
\mathsf{T}
weight, then
X^{\mathsf{T}\times G}=\{0\}\subset Z(\mathsf{g})
for any
\mathsf{g}
that satisfies the weight conditions in Setting
7.1
with
\mathsf{T}
replaced with
\mathsf{T}\times G
. Then we have specializations

\mathsf{sp}_{\mathfrak{M}}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{g})\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})),\quad\mathsf{sp}_{\mathcal{M}}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{g})\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})),

where
\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})=[X/G]
and
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})=[X^{ss}/G]
. Moreover, the diagram

\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{g})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}_{\mathfrak{M}}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{g})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}_{\mathcal{M}}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}))}

is commutative, where the vertical arrows are the restriction to the semistable locus.

As an application, we give a proof of
[
N
, Conj. 1.9]
, which we thank Andrei Neguţ for helpful communications.

Let
(Q,\mathsf{W})
be the quiver with potential associated to a symmetrizable Kac-Moody algebra
\mathfrak{g}_{A,D}
in
[
COZZ1
, Def. A.1]
,
\mathsf{T}
be the torus
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
that scales the arrows in
loc. cit.
, and set
\mathsf{g}=\operatorname{tr}\mathsf{W}\colon R(Q,\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\to\mathbb{C}
. Then the argument of
[
COZZ2
, Lem. A.4]
can be applied to
K
-theory and the localized specialization map

\displaystyle\mathsf{sp}_{\mathfrak{M}}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{g})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar^{\pm}]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar)\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}))\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar^{\pm}]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar)

is injective. According to the above discussion, the localized specialization map

\displaystyle\mathsf{sp}_{\mathcal{M}}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{g})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar^{\pm}]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar)\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}))\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hbar^{\pm}]}\mathbb{C}(\hbar)

is also injective. This proves the injectivity statement in
[
N
, Conj. 1.9]
. The surjectivity statement in
[
N
, Conj. 1.9]
follows from the surjectivity of restriction-to-open map for critical
K
-theory (see §
2.3.5
) and torus localization (
2.22
).

7.3.4.
Existence result III

The third existence result is relevant to examples considered in
[
COZZ3
]
.
The idea of construction uses dimensional reduction to the case when there is no potential.

Construction 7.28
.

Let
G
be a linear reductive group,
\mathsf{T}
be a torus, with a direct sum of finite dimensional linear
(G\times\mathsf{T}\times\mathbb{G}_{m})
-representations:

W=V\oplus U.

Let
\pi\colon W\to V
be the projection with a pair of
(G\times\mathsf{T})
-equivariant sections:

s_{0},s_{\epsilon}\in\Gamma(V,\underline{U}^{\vee})

of the dual bundle
\underline{U}^{\vee}:=V\times U^{\vee}\to V
of
\pi
. Assume that
s_{0}
is
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant, and there exists a decomposition

s_{\epsilon}=\sum_{i=1}^{m}s_{\epsilon,i}

such that
\mathbb{G}_{m}
scales
s_{\epsilon,i}
with weight
-\mathsf{n}_{i}<0
, i.e.
u^{\mathsf{n}_{i}}s_{\epsilon,i}
is
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant (
i=1,\cdots,m
).
Define the section

s_{t}:=s_{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{m}t^{\mathsf{n}_{i}}s_{\epsilon,i},\quad t\in\mathbb{A}^{1}.

Let
Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s_{t})
be the derived zero locus of
s_{t}
,
which fits into the following diagram

\textstyle{\pi^{-1}(Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s_{t}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt\iota_{t}}
\scriptstyle{\Box}
\textstyle{W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s_{t})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{V.}

Let
e
be the coordinate of the fiber of
\pi
and define
(G\times\mathsf{T})
-invariant regular function:

\mathsf{w}_{t}:=\langle e,s_{t}\rangle\colon W\to\mathbb{C}.

Choose a stability condition such that the semistable locus equals the stable locus:

W^{ss}=W^{s}\neq\emptyset.

By abuse of notations, we write the descent of
\mathsf{w}_{t}
to be

\mathsf{w}_{t}\colon X:=W^{s}/G\to\mathbb{C}.

Then
(X,\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathsf{f}=\mathsf{w}_{0},\mathsf{g}=\mathsf{w}_{1}-\mathsf{w}_{0})
fits into Setting
7.1
.

By applying dimensional reduction (Proposition
D.2
, Remark
D.3
), we obtain

\displaystyle\iota_{t*}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}((\pi^{-1}(Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s_{t})))^{s}/G)\xrightarrow{\cong}K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w}_{t}),\,\,\,\forall\,\,t\in\mathbb{A}^{1}.

There is a standard specialization map for the left hand side:

\mathsf{sp}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}((\pi^{-1}(Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s_{1})))^{s}/G)\to K^{\mathsf{T}}((\pi^{-1}(Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s_{0})))^{s}/G),

defined by an extension to
K
-theory class of the family
\pi^{-1}(Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s_{t})))^{s}/G
(
t\in\mathbb{A}^{1}
), followed by the Gysin pullback to the special fiber
\pi^{-1}(Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s_{0})))^{s}/G
(e.g.
[
CG
, §5.3]
, Definition
7.7
).

Definition 7.29
.

Under the setting of Construction
7.28
,
we define
\nu
as the unique map which makes
the following diagram commute:

(7.11)

\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w}_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\nu}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w}_{0})}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}((\pi^{-1}(Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s_{1})))^{s}/G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{1*}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}((\pi^{-1}(Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s_{0})))^{s}/G).\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{0*}}

Proposition 7.30
.

The map
\nu
(
7.11
) is the specialization map for critical
K
-theory (Definition
7.20
).

Proof.

We have the following diagram

\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{w}_{1}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{can}}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}((\pi^{-1}(Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s_{1})))^{s}/G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{1*}}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447pt\,\,\iota_{1*}}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447pt\,\,\cong}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(W^{s}/G,\mathsf{w}_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\nu}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}((\pi^{-1}(Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s_{0})))^{s}/G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{0*}}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447pt\,\,\iota_{0*}}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447pt\,\,\cong}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(W^{s}/G,\mathsf{w}_{0})}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{w}_{0})).\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{can}}

Here the upper and lower triangles commute as
\pi^{-1}(Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s_{t}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i_{t}}}{{\hookrightarrow}}Z(\mathsf{w}_{t})
, and the canonical map is simply the pushforward map. The middle square commutes by Definition
7.29
.
The commutativity of

(7.12)

\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{w}_{1}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{can}\hskip 8.19447pt\,\,\,}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sp}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(W^{s}/G,\mathsf{w}_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\nu}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{w}_{0}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{can}\hskip 8.19447pt\,\,\,}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(W^{s}/G,\mathsf{w}_{0}),}

is equivalent to the equality of maps

\mathsf{sp}\circ(\iota_{1*}^{-1}\circ\operatorname{can})=(\iota_{0*}^{-1}\circ\operatorname{can})\circ\mathsf{sp}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{w}_{1}))\to K^{\mathsf{T}}((\pi^{-1}(Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s_{0})))^{s}/G).

Let
\mathrm{Kos}(\tau,s_{t})
denote the Koszul factorization (
D
). Then

(\iota_{t*}^{-1}\circ\operatorname{can})(-)=\left(i_{Z(\mathsf{w}_{t})}^{*}\mathrm{Kos}(\tau,s_{t})^{\vee}\right)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Z(\mathsf{w}_{t})}}(-)\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{w}_{t}))\to K^{\mathsf{T}}((\pi^{-1}(Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s_{t})))^{s}/G),

where
i_{Z(\mathsf{w}_{t})}\colon Z(\mathsf{w}_{t})\to W^{s}/G
is the inclusion.
Therefore, for any
[\mathcal{E}]\in K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{w}_{1}))
, we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{sp}\circ(\iota_{1*}^{-1}\circ\operatorname{can})[\mathcal{E}]

\displaystyle=\mathsf{sp}\left(i_{Z(\mathsf{w}_{1})}^{*}\mathrm{Kos}(\tau,s_{1})^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Z(\mathsf{w}_{1})}}[\mathcal{E}]\right)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{sp}\left(i_{Z(\mathsf{w}_{1})}^{*}\mathrm{Kos}(\tau,s_{1})^{\vee})\right)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Z(\mathsf{w}_{0})}}\mathsf{sp}([\mathcal{E}])

\displaystyle=\left(i_{Z(\mathsf{w}_{0})}^{*}\mathrm{Kos}(\tau,s_{0})^{\vee})\right)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Z(\mathsf{w}_{0})}}\mathsf{sp}([\mathcal{E}])

\displaystyle=(\iota_{0*}^{-1}\circ\operatorname{can})\circ\mathsf{sp}([\mathcal{E}]),

where we use the fact that the specialization of tensor products
is the tensor product of specializations.
∎

Example 7.31
.

be the gauge group and consider representation spaces

of the following quivers

0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}{{}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.44446pt\raise 23.59885pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{x}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}{{}{{}{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 30.86923pt\raise 7.49738pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\,\,.

We define
cyclic stability
on
W
, so the stable locus
W^{s}
are those representations which satisfy

\mathbb{C}\langle x,\xi,y\rangle A(\mathbb{C}^{N})=\mathbb{C}^{n}
,
and define the GIT quotient

X:=W^{s}/\mathrm{GL}_{n}.

We choose a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
W
by

\displaystyle u\cdot(\xi,x,y,A,B)=(u^{-2}\xi,ux,uy,uA,uB),

and choose a
\mathsf{T}
-action on
W
which commutes with
(\mathrm{GL}_{n}\times\mathbb{G}_{m})
-action, so that

W=V\oplus U

is a direct sum of
(G\times\mathsf{T}\times\mathbb{G}_{m})
-representations. For any
t\in\mathbb{A}^{1}
, we define a map

s_{t}\colon V\to U^{\vee},\quad(y,\xi,A)\mapsto([y,\xi],\,\xi A-t^{2}A\Xi),

where
\Xi
is a fixed
N
by
N
matrix. The potential function is determined by
s_{t}
via the trace pairing:

\mathsf{w}_{t}:=\operatorname{tr}(\xi[x,y]+\xi AB)-t^{2}\cdot\operatorname{tr}(A\Xi B)\colon W\to\mathbb{C}.

Then
\mathsf{w}_{0}
is
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-invariant and
\mathbb{G}_{m}
scales
\mathsf{w}_{1}-\mathsf{w}_{0}
with weight
-2
.
These data fit into the setting of Construction
7.28
. By Proposition
7.30
, the specialization map

\displaystyle\mathsf{sp}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w}_{1})\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w}_{0})

exists in critical
K
-theory. This map is useful to relate different modules of quantum toroidal algebra
U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_{1})
.

8.
Vector bundles and stable envelopes

Let
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A})
be as in Setting
2.1
, and take a
\mathsf{T}
-equivariant vector bundle
E
on
X
with projection

(8.1)

\pi\colon Y:=\mathrm{Tot}(E)\to X.

Define
i\colon X\hookrightarrow Y
as the zero section map. We note that
F\mapsto i^{-1}(F)
gives a bijection

\mathrm{Fix}_{\mathsf{A}}(Y)\cong\mathrm{Fix}_{\mathsf{A}}(X),

where the inverse map is given by
G\mapsto(\pi^{-1}(G))^{\mathsf{A}}
.

In this section, we prove several compatibility results for stable envelopes of
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A})
and
(Y,\widetilde{\mathsf{w}},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A})
,
when
\widetilde{\mathsf{w}}
is a
\mathsf{T}
-invariant function on
Y
such that
\widetilde{\mathsf{w}}|_{X}=\mathsf{w}
, and
E
is attracting or repelling.
We show that the closure of the attracting set of the diagonal gives stable envelope correspondence on
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A})
when
Y
is a symmetric quiver variety
and
E
has a decomposition into attracting and repelling subbundles (Theorem
8.9
). This explicit description is particularly useful in the manipulation of
stable envelopes.
Finally, we prove a triangle lemma for attracting closure correspondence without attracting or repelling condition on
E
(Proposition
8.10
),
which will be used to prove the triangle lemma of Hall envelopes in §
9.5
.

8.1.
Definitions and properties

Definition 8.1
.

An
\mathsf{A}
-equivariant vector bundle
E
on
X
is called
attracting
(resp.
repelling
) with respect to a chamber
\mathfrak{C}
if
\forall\,F\in\operatorname{Fix}_{\mathsf{A}}(X)
,
E|_{F}
has nonnegative (resp. nonpositive)
\mathsf{A}
-weights with respect to
\mathfrak{C}
.

Proposition 8.2
.

Assume that
E
is attracting with respect to
\mathfrak{C}
. Then cohomological stable envelope exists for
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C})
if and only if it exists for
(Y,\mathsf{w}\circ\pi,\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C})
. When they exist, the following diagram commutes

(8.2)

\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(Y^{\mathsf{A}},(\mathsf{w}\circ\pi)^{\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(Y,\mathsf{w}\circ\pi)}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\,\,\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{\mathsf{A}*}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{*}}

Similarly, let
\mathsf{s}\in\operatorname{Pic}_{\mathsf{A}}(X)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{R}
be a generic slope, then
K
-theoretic stable envelope exists for
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C},\mathsf{s})
if and only if it exists for
(Y,\mathsf{w}\circ\pi,\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C},\mathsf{s})
. When they exist, the following diagram commutes

(8.3)

\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Y^{\mathsf{A}},(\mathsf{w}\circ\pi)^{\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Y,\mathsf{w}\circ\pi)}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\,\,\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{\mathsf{A}*}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{*}}

Proof.

We prove the cohomology version. Assume that cohomological stable envelope exists for
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C})
. Define

\mathcal{S}=\pi^{*}\circ\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(Y,\mathsf{w}\circ\pi).

For an arbitrary
F\in\operatorname{Fix}_{\mathsf{A}}(Y)
, and take an arbitrary
\gamma\in H^{\mathsf{T}/\mathsf{A}}(i^{-1}(F),\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})
, we claim that
\mathcal{S}(\gamma)
satisfies the axioms (ii) and (iii) in Definition
3.4
and axiom (i’) in Remark
3.7
, namely

(1)

\mathcal{S}(\gamma)
is supported on
\operatorname{Attr}^{\leq}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F)
;

(2)

\mathcal{S}(\gamma)\big|_{F}=e^{\mathsf{T}}(N_{F/Y}^{-})\cdot\pi^{\mathsf{A}*}(\gamma)
;

(3)

For any
F^{\prime}\neq F
, the inequality
\deg_{\mathsf{A}}\mathcal{S}(\gamma)\big|_{F^{\prime}}<\deg_{\mathsf{A}}e^{\mathsf{T}}(N_{F^{\prime}/Y}^{-})
holds.

Here we choose the ample partial order
\leq
as in Remark
3.9
. Since
\pi^{\mathsf{A}*}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(Y^{\mathsf{A}},(\mathsf{w}\circ\pi)^{\mathsf{A}})
is an isomorphism with the inverse map given by
i^{\mathsf{A}*}
, the above three properties imply that
\mathcal{S}\circ i^{\mathsf{A}*}
is a cohomological stable envelope for
(Y,\mathsf{w}\circ\pi,\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C})
, moreover the diagram (
8.2
) commutes.

Since
E
is attracting, for all
G\in\operatorname{Fix}_{\mathsf{A}}(Y)
, we have

\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(G)=\pi^{-1}(\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(i^{-1}(G))).

Therefore
\mathcal{S}(\gamma)
is supported on

\pi^{-1}(\operatorname{Attr}^{\leq}_{\mathfrak{C}}(i^{-1}(F)))=\pi^{-1}\left(\bigcup_{i^{-1}(F)\leq i^{-1}(G)}\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(i^{-1}(F))\right)=\bigcup_{F\leq G}\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(G)=\operatorname{Attr}^{\leq}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F).

This proves (1). For (2), we notice that

\mathcal{S}(\gamma)\big|_{F}=\pi^{\mathsf{A}*}\left(\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\gamma)|_{i^{-1}(F)}\right)=\pi^{\mathsf{A}*}\left(e^{\mathsf{T}}\left(N^{-}_{i^{-1}(F)/X}\right)\cdot\gamma\right)=e^{\mathsf{T}}(N_{F/Y}^{-})\cdot\pi^{\mathsf{A}*}(\gamma),

where in the last equation we use the fact that
E
is attracting so that
N^{-}_{F/Y}\cong\pi^{\mathsf{A}*}\left(N^{-}_{i^{-1}(F)/X}\right)
.

For (3), we notice that
\mathcal{S}(\gamma)\big|_{F^{\prime}}=\pi^{\mathsf{A}*}\left(\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\gamma)|_{i^{-1}(F^{\prime})}\right)
. Since
\pi^{\mathsf{A}*}
preserves
\deg_{\mathsf{A}}
, we have

\deg_{\mathsf{A}}\mathcal{S}(\gamma)\big|_{F^{\prime}}=\deg_{\mathsf{A}}\left(\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\gamma)|_{i^{-1}(F^{\prime})}\right)<\deg_{\mathsf{A}}e^{\mathsf{T}}\left(N_{i^{-1}(F^{\prime})/X}^{-}\right)=\deg_{\mathsf{A}}e^{\mathsf{T}}\left(N_{F^{\prime}/Y}^{-}\right).

This proves one direction. For the other direction, assume that cohomological stable envelope exists for
(Y,\mathsf{w}\circ\pi,\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C})
, then a similar argument as above shows that
i^{*}\circ\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ\pi^{\mathsf{A}*}
is a cohomological stable envelope for
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C})
. This proves the cohomology version.

The
K
-theory version is proven similarly, and we omit the details.
∎

Proposition 8.3
.

Assume that
E
is repelling with respect to
\mathfrak{C}
. Let
\widetilde{\mathsf{w}}
be a
\mathsf{T}
-invariant function on
Y
such that
\widetilde{\mathsf{w}}|_{X}=\mathsf{w}
. Moreover, assume that cohomological stable envelopes exist for both
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C})
and
(Y,\widetilde{\mathsf{w}},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C})
. Then the following diagram is commutative

(8.4)

\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(Y^{\mathsf{A}},\widetilde{\mathsf{w}}^{\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(Y,\widetilde{\mathsf{w}})}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\,\,\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\scriptstyle{i^{\mathsf{A}}_{*}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{i_{*}}

Similarly, let
\mathsf{s}\in\operatorname{Pic}_{\mathsf{A}}(X)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{R}
be a generic slope, and assume that
K
-theoretic stable envelopes exist for both
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C},\mathsf{s}^{\prime})
and
(Y,\widetilde{\mathsf{w}},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C},\mathsf{s})
where
\mathsf{s}^{\prime}=\mathsf{s}\otimes(\det E)^{1/2}
. Then the following diagram is commutative

(8.5)

\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Y^{\mathsf{A}},\widetilde{\mathsf{w}}^{\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Y,\widetilde{\mathsf{w}})}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\,\,\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\scriptstyle{i^{\mathsf{A}}_{*}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{i_{*}}

Proof.

We prove the cohomology version (
K
-theory version can be proven similarly).
Define

\mathcal{S}=i_{*}\circ\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(Y,\widetilde{\mathsf{w}}).

For an arbitrary
F\in\operatorname{Fix}_{\mathsf{A}}(Y)
, and take an arbitrary
\gamma\in H^{\mathsf{T}/\mathsf{A}}(i^{-1}(F),\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})
, we need to show that
\mathcal{S}(\gamma)
satisfies the axioms (ii) and (iii) in Definition
3.4
and axiom (i’) in Remark
3.7
, namely

(1)

\mathcal{S}(\gamma)
is supported on
\operatorname{Attr}^{\leq}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F)
;

(2)

\mathcal{S}(\gamma)\big|_{F}=e^{\mathsf{T}}(N_{F/Y}^{-})\cdot i^{\mathsf{A}}_{*}(\gamma)
;

(3)

For any
F^{\prime}\neq F
, the inequality
\deg_{\mathsf{A}}\mathcal{S}(\gamma)\big|_{F^{\prime}}<\deg_{\mathsf{A}}e^{\mathsf{T}}(N_{F^{\prime}/Y}^{-})
holds.

Here we choose the ample partial order
\leq
as in Remark
3.9
.

Since
E
is repelling, we have
i^{-1}(\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(G))=\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(i^{-1}(G))
for all
G\in\operatorname{Fix}_{\mathsf{A}}(Y)
; therefore
\mathcal{S}(\gamma)
is supported on

i(\operatorname{Attr}^{\leq}_{\mathfrak{C}}(i^{-1}(F)))=i\left(\bigcup_{i^{-1}(F)\leq i^{-1}(G)}\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(i^{-1}(F))\right)=\bigcup_{F\leq G}X\cap\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(G)=X\cap\operatorname{Attr}^{\leq}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F)\subseteq\operatorname{Attr}^{\leq}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F).

This prove (1). For (2), we notice that

\mathcal{S}(\gamma)\big|_{F}=e^{\mathsf{T}}(E|_{F}^{\mathrm{mov}})\cdot i^{\mathsf{A}}_{*}\left(\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\gamma)|_{i^{-1}(F)}\right)=e^{\mathsf{T}}(E|_{F}^{\mathrm{mov}})\cdot i^{\mathsf{A}}_{*}\left(e^{\mathsf{T}}\left(N^{-}_{i^{-1}(F)/X}\right)\cdot\gamma\right)=e^{\mathsf{T}}(N_{F/Y}^{-})\cdot i^{\mathsf{A}}_{*}(\gamma),

where in the last equation we have used the fact that
E
is repelling so that

N^{-}_{F/Y}=\pi^{\mathsf{A}^{*}}\left(N^{-}_{i^{-1}(F)/X}\right)+E|_{F}^{\mathrm{mov}}\in K^{\mathsf{T}}(F).

For (3), we notice that
\mathcal{S}(\gamma)\big|_{F^{\prime}}=e^{\mathsf{T}}\left(E|_{F^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{mov}}\right)\cdot i^{\mathsf{A}}_{*}\left(\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\gamma)|_{i^{-1}(F^{\prime})}\right)
. Since
i^{\mathsf{A}}_{*}
preserves
\deg_{\mathsf{A}}
,
we have

\displaystyle\deg_{\mathsf{A}}\mathcal{S}(\gamma)\big|_{F^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\deg_{\mathsf{A}}e^{\mathsf{T}}(E|_{F^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{mov}})+\deg_{\mathsf{A}}\left(\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\gamma)|_{i^{-1}(F^{\prime})}\right)

\displaystyle<\deg_{\mathsf{A}}e^{\mathsf{T}}(E|_{F^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{mov}})+\deg_{\mathsf{A}}e^{\mathsf{T}}\left(N_{i^{-1}(F^{\prime})/X}^{-}\right)=\deg_{\mathsf{A}}e^{\mathsf{T}}(N_{F^{\prime}/Y}^{-}).\qed

The following lemma will be used in Appendix
E
.

Lemma 8.4
.

Assume that
E
is repelling with respect to
\mathfrak{C}
. Suppose that
F\in\operatorname{Fix}_{\mathsf{A}}(X)
is a fixed component in
X
such that
E|_{F}
is moving. Let
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}
act on
Y
by fixing
X
and scaling the fibers of
E
with weight
1
. Suppose that
K
-theoretic stable envelope exists for
(Y,\mathsf{w}\circ\pi,\mathsf{T}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C},\mathsf{s})
, then there exists a
K
-theoretic stable envelope map
15
15

15

This means a map satisfying axioms (i)-(iii) in Definition
3.10
for
component
F
.

\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{\mathfrak{C}}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(F,\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})
, where
\mathsf{s}^{\prime}=\mathsf{s}\otimes(\det E)^{1/2}
.

Proof.

By assumption, the projection
(\pi^{-1}(F))^{\mathsf{A}}\to F
is an isomorphism. Let
\sigma:\mathbb{C}^{*}\to\mathsf{A}
be a cocharacter in the chamber
\mathfrak{C}
, then we claim that

\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{\sigma}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(F,\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})
(in Definition
3.33
) satisfies axioms (i)-(iii) in Definition
3.10
with respect to the whole chamber
\mathfrak{C}
.

It is enough to check axiom (iii). We note that
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}
acts on
X
trivially, so
\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{\sigma}
extends to a
K
-theoretic stable envelope for
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u},\mathbb{C}^{*},+)
. Proposition
8.3
implies that

\displaystyle i_{*}\circ\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{\sigma}=\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ\,i^{\mathsf{A}}_{*}.

As
E|_{F}
is moving,
i^{\mathsf{A}}_{*}
is an isomorphism when restricted to
F
, we see that for all
\gamma\in K^{\mathsf{T}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}}(F,\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})
and for all
F^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Fix}_{\mathsf{A}}(X),F^{\prime}\neq F
, there is a strict inclusion of polytopes

\deg_{\mathsf{A}}e^{\mathsf{T}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}}_{K}(E|_{F^{\prime}})\cdot\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{\sigma}(\gamma)\big|_{F^{\prime}}\subsetneq\deg_{\mathsf{A}}e^{\mathsf{T}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}}_{K}\left(N_{\pi^{-1}(F^{\prime})^{\mathsf{A}}/Y}^{-}\right)+\mathrm{shift}_{F^{\prime}}-\mathrm{shift}_{F},

where
\mathrm{shift}_{F}:=\mathrm{weight}_{\mathsf{A}}\left(\det(N_{F/X}^{-})^{1/2}\otimes\mathsf{s}^{\prime}|_{F}\right)
. It follows that

\deg_{\mathsf{A}}\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{\sigma}(\gamma)\big|_{F^{\prime}}\subseteq\deg_{\mathsf{A}}e^{\mathsf{T}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}}_{K}(N_{F^{\prime}/X}^{-})+\mathrm{shift}_{F^{\prime}}-\mathrm{shift}_{F}.

Since
\mathsf{s}
is generic, the above inclusion must be strict by Remark (
3.12
). Evaluating at
u=1
,
\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{\sigma}
induces a
K
-theoretic stable envelope map
\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{\mathfrak{C}}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(F,\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})
.
∎

Remark 8.5
.

Under the assumption of Lemma
8.4
, suppose that
\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}
is a face of
\mathfrak{C}
and let
\mathsf{A}^{\prime}
be the subtorus of
\mathsf{A}
associated with
\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}
, and we also assume the existence of
K
-theoretic stable envelope for
(Y,\mathsf{w}\circ\pi,\mathsf{T}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u},\mathsf{A}^{\prime},\mathfrak{C}^{\prime},\mathsf{s})
. Assume moreover that
F
is an
\mathsf{A}^{\prime}
-fixed component and
E|_{F}
is moving with respect to
\mathsf{A}^{\prime}
-action. Then by Lemma
8.4
we have
K
-theoretic stable envelope maps
\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{\mathfrak{C}}
and
\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}

from
K^{\mathsf{T}}(F,\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})
to
K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})
, where
\mathsf{s}^{\prime}=\mathsf{s}\otimes(\det E)^{1/2}
. Moreover, there exists an obvious
K
-theoretic stable envelope map
\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime\prime}}_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(F,\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(F,\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})
for the
\mathsf{s}^{\prime\prime}
determined by Lemma
3.37
, which is the identity map. Therefore by Lemma
3.37
, we have
\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{\mathfrak{C}}=\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}
.

Combining Propositions
8.2
,
8.3
, we obtain the following.

Proposition 8.6
.

Assume that there exists a decomposition
E=E_{+}\oplus E_{-}
such that
E_{+}
is attracting and
E_{-}
is repelling with respect to
\mathfrak{C}
. Suppose that
\mathsf{w}^{\prime}
is a
\mathsf{T}
-invariant function on
\mathrm{Tot}(E_{-})
such that
\mathsf{w}^{\prime}|_{X}=\mathsf{w}
, and define
\widetilde{\mathsf{w}}:=(Y\to\mathrm{Tot}(E_{-}))^{*}(\mathsf{w}^{\prime})
. Moreover, assume that cohomological stable envelopes exist for both
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C})
and
(Y,\widetilde{\mathsf{w}},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C})
. Then the following diagram is commutative

(8.6)

\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(Y^{\mathsf{A}},\widetilde{\mathsf{w}}^{\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(Y,\widetilde{\mathsf{w}})}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\psi^{\mathsf{A}}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces,}
\scriptstyle{\psi}

where

(8.7)

\begin{split}\psi&=(Y\to\mathrm{Tot}(E_{-}))^{*}\circ(X\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Tot}(E_{-}))_{*}=(\mathrm{Tot}(E_{+})\hookrightarrow Y)_{*}\circ(\mathrm{Tot}(E_{+})\to X)^{*},\\
\psi^{\mathsf{A}}&=\left(Y^{\mathsf{A}}\to\mathrm{Tot}(E_{-})^{\mathsf{A}}\right)^{*}\circ\left(X^{\mathsf{A}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Tot}(E_{-})^{\mathsf{A}}\right)_{*}=\left(\mathrm{Tot}(E_{+})^{\mathsf{A}}\hookrightarrow Y^{\mathsf{A}}\right)_{*}\circ\left(\mathrm{Tot}(E_{+})^{\mathsf{A}}\to X^{\mathsf{A}}\right)^{*}.\end{split}

Similarly, let
\mathsf{s}\in\operatorname{Pic}_{\mathsf{A}}(X)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{R}
be a generic slope, and assume that
K
-theoretic stable envelopes exist for both
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C},\mathsf{s}^{\prime})
and
(Y,\widetilde{\mathsf{w}},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C},\mathsf{s})
, where
\mathsf{s}^{\prime}=\mathsf{s}\otimes(\det E_{-})^{1/2}
. Then the following diagram is commutative

(8.8)

\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Y^{\mathsf{A}},\widetilde{\mathsf{w}}^{\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(Y,\widetilde{\mathsf{w}})}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\psi^{\mathsf{A}}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces,}
\scriptstyle{\psi}

where
\psi
and
\psi^{\mathsf{A}}
are given by
K
-theoretic version of (
8.7
).

8.2.
Attracting closure as cohomological stable envelopes

The following explicit descriptions of cohomological stable envelopes will be useful.
In below, we omit the canonical map in Lemma
3.22
and simply write

[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}\left(\Delta_{(-)}\right)]:=\operatorname{can}\circ\,[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}\left(\Delta_{(-)}\right)].

Proposition 8.7
.

Assume that
E
is attracting with respect to
\mathfrak{C}
. If
[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})]
induces cohomological stable envelope for
(Y,\mathsf{w}\circ\pi,\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C})
,
then
[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}})]
induces cohomological stable envelope for
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C})
.

Proof.

Let
S\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})
be the map induced by
[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}})]
,

\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}
the cohomological stable envelope for
(Y,\mathsf{w}\circ\pi,\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C})
.
By Proposition
8.2
, it suffices to show that
S
satisfies

\pi^{*}\circ S=\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ\,\pi^{\mathsf{A}*}.

Let
W\subseteq Y\times X^{\mathsf{A}}
be the total space of vector bundle
\operatorname{pr}^{*}E
on
\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}})
,
where
\operatorname{pr}:\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}})\to X
is the projection, then

\pi^{*}\circ S
is induced by the correspondence
[W]
.

Note that
\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})\times(\operatorname{id}\times\pi^{\mathsf{A}})(Y^{\mathsf{A}})
intersects transversely with
Y\times\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}}\times X^{\mathsf{A}}

inside
(Y\times Y^{\mathsf{A}})\times(Y^{\mathsf{A}}\times X^{\mathsf{A}})
, and their intersection equals to
\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}((\Delta\times\pi^{\mathsf{A}})(Y^{\mathsf{A}}))\subseteq Y\times Y^{\mathsf{A}}\times X^{\mathsf{A}}
, where
\Delta\colon Y^{\mathsf{A}}\hookrightarrow Y^{\mathsf{A}}\times Y^{\mathsf{A}}
is the diagonal map. The intersection of
\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})\times(\operatorname{id}\times\pi^{\mathsf{A}})(Y^{\mathsf{A}})
with
Y\times\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}}\times X^{\mathsf{A}}
is contained in
\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}((\Delta\times\pi^{\mathsf{A}})(Y^{\mathsf{A}}))
, where the latter closure is taken inside
Y\times Y^{\mathsf{A}}\times X^{\mathsf{A}}
; thus in the equivariant Chow group, we have

\displaystyle\left[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})\times(\operatorname{id}\times\pi^{\mathsf{A}})(Y^{\mathsf{A}})\right]\,\bigcap\,\left[Y\times\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}}\times X^{\mathsf{A}}]=[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}((\Delta\times\pi^{\mathsf{A}})(Y^{\mathsf{A}}))\right]\in A^{\mathsf{T}}(Y\times Y^{\mathsf{A}}\times X^{\mathsf{A}}).

Then
\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ\,\pi^{\mathsf{A}*}
is induced by the correspondence
p_{13*}[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}((\Delta\times\pi^{\mathsf{A}})(Y^{\mathsf{A}}))]
, where
p_{13}\colon Y\times Y^{\mathsf{A}}\times X^{\mathsf{A}}\to Y\times X^{\mathsf{A}}
is the projection to the first and third components. Since
p_{13}
induces isomorphism
(\Delta\times\pi^{\mathsf{A}})(Y^{\mathsf{A}})\cong(\operatorname{id}\times\pi^{\mathsf{A}})(Y^{\mathsf{A}})
, we have

p_{13*}[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}((\Delta\times\pi^{\mathsf{A}})(Y^{\mathsf{A}}))]=[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}((\operatorname{id}\times\pi^{\mathsf{A}})(Y^{\mathsf{A}}))],

where the latter closure is taken in
Y\times X^{\mathsf{A}}
. Furthermore,
{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}((\operatorname{id}\times\pi^{\mathsf{A}})(Y^{\mathsf{A}}))
is the total space of vector bundle
\operatorname{pr}^{*}E
on
\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}})
where
\operatorname{pr}\colon\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}})\to X
is the natural projection. Therefore, we have
\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}((\operatorname{id}\times\pi^{\mathsf{A}})(Y^{\mathsf{A}}))=W
, and it follows that
\pi^{*}\circ S=\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ\,\pi^{\mathsf{A}*}
.
∎

Proposition 8.8
.

Assume that
E
is repelling with respect to
\mathfrak{C}
. Let
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}
act on
Y
by fixing
X
and scale the fibers of
E
with weight
1
, and let
\widetilde{\mathsf{w}}
be a
\mathsf{T}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}
-invariant function on
Y
such that
\widetilde{\mathsf{w}}|_{X}=\mathsf{w}
.

If
[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})]
induces cohomological stable envelope for
(Y,\widetilde{\mathsf{w}},\mathsf{T}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C})
, then
[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}})]
induces cohomological stable envelope for
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C})
.

Proof.

Step 1.
We show that cohomological stable envelope exists for
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C})
. Let
\sigma:\mathbb{C}^{*}\to\mathsf{A}
be a cocharacter that lies in
\mathfrak{C}
. Then cohomological stable envelope
\operatorname{Stab}_{\sigma}
exists for
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u},\mathbb{C}^{*},+)
by Proposition
3.23
. By Proposition
8.3
, we have

i_{*}\circ\operatorname{Stab}_{\sigma}=\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C},Y}\circ\,i^{\mathsf{A}}_{*}.

Here
\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C},Y}
is the stable envelope for
(Y,\widetilde{\mathsf{w}},\mathsf{T}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C})
and we use the fact that it is the same
as the stable envelope when replacing
\mathsf{A}
by above
\sigma
in chamber
\mathfrak{C}
(ref. Proposition
3.34
).

Let
\sigma^{\prime}:\mathbb{C}^{*}\to\mathsf{A}
be another cocharacter that lies in
\mathfrak{C}
, then we have

i_{*}\circ\operatorname{Stab}_{\sigma}=i_{*}\circ\operatorname{Stab}_{\sigma^{\prime}}.

Applying
i^{*}
to two sides of the above equation, we get

e^{\mathsf{T}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}}(E)\cdot(\operatorname{Stab}_{\sigma}-\operatorname{Stab}_{\sigma^{\prime}})=0.

We note that
H^{\mathsf{T}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}}(X,\mathsf{w})\cong H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})\otimes\mathbb{Q}[u]
, and multiplication by
e^{\mathsf{T}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}}(E)
is injective on
H^{\mathsf{T}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}}(X,\mathsf{w})
because
e^{\mathsf{T}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}}(E)=u^{\operatorname{rk}E}+\,
lower degree in
u
. Thus
\operatorname{Stab}_{\sigma}=\operatorname{Stab}_{\sigma^{\prime}}
, and it follows from Proposition
3.34
that cohomological stable envelope exists for
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C})
. Evaluating at
u=0
, we are done.

Step 2.
We show that
\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}
for
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C})
is induced by the correspondence
[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}})]
.

By Lemmata
8.11
,
8.12
,
{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})
is a vector bundle of rank
(\operatorname{rk}E|_{X^{\mathsf{A}}}^{\text{fixed}})
over
{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}})
, and

\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})\bigcap\,(X\times X^{\mathsf{A}})=\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}}),

this implies that

\displaystyle(i\times i^{\mathsf{A}})^{!}[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})]=u^{\operatorname{rk}E}\cdot[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}})]+\text{lower order terms in }u.

Let
\overline{p}_{1},\overline{p}_{2}
be the projections from
Y\times Y^{\mathsf{A}}
to
Y,Y^{\mathsf{A}}
respectively, and let
p_{1},p_{2}
be the projections from
X\times X^{\mathsf{A}}
to
X,X^{\mathsf{A}}
respectively. Then we have

(8.9)

\displaystyle i^{*}\circ\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C},Y}\circ\>i^{\mathsf{A}}_{*}(-)

\displaystyle=i^{*}\circ\overline{p}_{2*}\left([\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})]\otimes\overline{p}_{1}^{*}(i^{\mathsf{A}}_{*}(-))\right)

by base change

\displaystyle=p_{2*}\circ(i\times\operatorname{id})^{*}\left([\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})]\otimes\overline{p}_{1}^{*}(i^{\mathsf{A}}_{*}(-))\right)

by base change

\displaystyle=p_{2*}\left(((i\times i^{\mathsf{A}})^{!}[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})])\otimes p_{1}^{*}(-)\right)

\displaystyle=u^{\operatorname{rk}E}\cdot p_{2*}\left([\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}})]\otimes p_{1}^{*}(-)\right)+\text{lower order terms in }u.

By Proposition
8.3
, we also have

(8.10)

\displaystyle i^{*}\circ\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C},Y}\circ\>i^{\mathsf{A}}_{*}(-)=i^{*}\circ i_{*}\circ\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C},X}(-)=u^{\operatorname{rk}E}\cdot\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C},X}(-)+\text{lower order terms in }u.

Comparing the leading terms in (
8.9
) and in (
8.10
), we arrive at

\displaystyle\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C},X}(-)=p_{2*}\left([\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}})]\otimes p_{1}^{*}(-)\right),

i.e.
[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}})]
induces
\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C},X}
.
∎

8.3.
More existence results on cohomological stable envelopes

Cohomological stable envelopes are known to exist on (1) symmetric GIT quotients and symplectic varieties with general
\mathsf{A}
and on (2) smooth varieties with
\mathsf{A}=\mathbb{C}^{*}

(ref. Theorem
3.32
). As an application of results obtained in the previous section, we deduce another existence result for some

not necessarily symmetric GIT quotients
when
\mathsf{A}
is general.

Theorem 8.9
.

Suppose that in (
8.1
),
\mathrm{Tot}(E)=Y
is a symmetric GIT quotient with an action by tori
\mathsf{A}\subseteq\mathsf{T}
as Definition
4.1
.
Assume that there exists a decomposition of vector bundle

E=E_{+}\oplus E_{-}

into attracting (
E_{+}
) and repelling (
E_{-}
) subbundles with respect to
\mathfrak{C}
.
Then
[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}})]
induces cohomological stable envelope for
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C})
.

Moreover, let
\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}
be a face of
\mathfrak{C}
and let
\mathsf{A}^{\prime}
be the subtorus of
\mathsf{A}
associated with
\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}
. Then the cohomological stable envelopes
\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}
and
\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}
exist, and they fit into the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}}

Proof.

Let
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}
act on
Y=\mathrm{Tot}(E)
by fixing
X
and
E_{+}
, and scaling the fibers of
E_{-}
with weight
1
.
By Theorem
4.3
,
[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})]
is a cohomological stable envelope correspondence for
(Y,\mathsf{w}\circ\pi,\mathsf{T}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C})
.
By Propositions
8.7
,
8.8
,
[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}})]
is a cohomological stable envelope correspondence for
(X,\mathsf{w},\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C})
. The triangle lemma then follows from Proposition
8.10
below.
∎

8.4.
The triangle lemma for attracting closure correspondence

In this subsection, we do not impose any attracting or repelling condition on
E
. Let
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}
act on
Y=\mathrm{Tot}(E)
by fixing
X
and scaling the fibers of
E
with weight
1
. Let
\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}
be a face of
\mathfrak{C}
and let
\mathsf{A}^{\prime}
be the subtorus of
\mathsf{A}
associated with
\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}
.

Let
\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})
be the closure of
{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})
in
Y\times Y^{\mathsf{A}}
, similarly for
\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}})\subseteq X\times X^{\mathsf{A}}
.
We show that if the triangle lemma holds for attracting closure correspondences for
Y
, then it also holds for attracting closure correspondences for
X
.

Proposition 8.10
.

The equation

\displaystyle[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}})]\circ[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})]=[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})]\in\operatorname{Hom}(H^{\mathsf{T}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}}(Y^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}\circ\pi),H^{\mathsf{T}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}}(Y,\mathsf{w}\circ\pi))

implies that

\displaystyle[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}})]\circ[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}})]=[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}})]\in\operatorname{Hom}(H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}),H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})).

Here we use the correspondence to denote the induced map between critical cohomologies.

Proof.

Let
i\colon X\hookrightarrow Y
,
i^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}\colon X^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow Y^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}
,
i^{\mathsf{A}}\colon X^{\mathsf{A}}\hookrightarrow Y^{\mathsf{A}}
be zero sections.
By Lemmata
8.11
,
8.12
,
{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})
is a vector bundle of rank
(\operatorname{rk}E|_{X^{\mathsf{A}}}^{+}+\operatorname{rk}E|_{X^{\mathsf{A}}}^{\text{fixed}})
over
{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}})
, and
\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})\bigcap\,(X\times X^{\mathsf{A}})=\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}})
, then

\displaystyle(i\times i^{\mathsf{A}})^{!}[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})]=u^{\operatorname{rk}E-\operatorname{rk}E|_{X^{\mathsf{A}}}^{+}}\cdot[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}})]+\text{lower order terms in }u.

Therefore we have

(8.11)

\begin{split}&\quad\left((i\times i^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}})^{!}[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}})]\right)\circ\left((i^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}\times i^{\mathsf{A}})^{!}[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})]\right)\\
&=u^{\operatorname{rk}E-\operatorname{rk}E|_{X^{\mathsf{A}}}^{+}+\operatorname{rk}E|_{X^{\mathsf{A}}}^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}\text{-fixed}}}\cdot[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}})]\circ[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}})]+\text{lower order terms in }u.\end{split}

Let
\overline{p}_{ij}
be the projection from
Y\times Y^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}\times Y^{\mathsf{A}}
to the
ij
-th component, for example
\overline{p}_{13}\colon Y\times Y^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}\times Y^{\mathsf{A}}\to Y\times Y^{\mathsf{A}}
. Similarly, let
{p}_{ij}
be the projection from
X\times X^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}\times X^{\mathsf{A}}
to the
ij
-th component. Let
\widetilde{p}_{13}\colon X\times Y^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}\times X^{\mathsf{A}}\to X\times X^{\mathsf{A}}
be the natural projection. The LHS of (
8.11
) can be evaluated as:

\displaystyle\quad\,\,p_{13*}\left((i\times i^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}\times i^{\mathsf{A}})^{!}\left(\overline{p}_{12}^{!}[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}})]\otimes\overline{p}_{23}^{!}[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})]\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{p}_{13*}\left(e^{\mathsf{T}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}}(E|_{X^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}}^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}\text{-fixed}})\cdot(i\times\operatorname{id}\times i^{\mathsf{A}})^{!}\left(\overline{p}_{12}^{!}[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}})]\otimes\overline{p}_{23}^{!}[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})]\right)\right)

\displaystyle=u^{\operatorname{rk}E|_{X^{\mathsf{A}}}^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}\text{-fixed}}}\cdot\widetilde{p}_{13*}\left((i\times\operatorname{id}\times i^{\mathsf{A}})^{!}\left(\overline{p}_{12}^{!}[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}})]\otimes\overline{p}_{23}^{!}[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})]\right)\right)+\text{lower order terms in }u

by base change

\displaystyle=u^{\operatorname{rk}E|_{X^{\mathsf{A}}}^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}\text{-fixed}}}\cdot(i\times i^{\mathsf{A}})^{!}\overline{p}_{13*}\left(\overline{p}_{12}^{!}[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}})]\otimes\overline{p}_{23}^{!}[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})]\right)+\text{lower order terms in }u

\displaystyle=u^{\operatorname{rk}E|_{X^{\mathsf{A}}}^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}\text{-fixed}}}\cdot(i\times i^{\mathsf{A}})^{!}\left([\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}})]\circ[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})]\right)+\text{lower order terms in }u

by assumption

\displaystyle=u^{\operatorname{rk}E|_{X^{\mathsf{A}}}^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}\text{-fixed}}}\cdot(i\times i^{\mathsf{A}})^{!}[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})]+\text{lower order terms in }u

\displaystyle=u^{\operatorname{rk}E-\operatorname{rk}E|_{X^{\mathsf{A}}}^{+}+\operatorname{rk}E|_{X^{\mathsf{A}}}^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}\text{-fixed}}}\cdot[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}})]+\text{lower order terms in }u.

Compared with the leading term on the RHS of (
8.11
), and we obtain the desired equation

\displaystyle[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}})]\circ[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}})]=[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}})].

Here we used the fact that
H^{\mathsf{T}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{u}}(X,\mathsf{w})\cong H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})\otimes\mathbb{Q}[u]
is free over
\mathbb{Q}[u]
.
∎

In the above proof, we have used the following:

Lemma 8.11
.

{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})
is a vector bundle of rank
(\operatorname{rk}E|_{X^{\mathsf{A}}}^{+}+\operatorname{rk}E|_{X^{\mathsf{A}}}^{\text{fixed}})
over
{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}})
.

Proof.

The attraction map

a:{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})\to\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}}

is an affine fibration with
(\operatorname{rk}N_{X^{\mathsf{A}}/X}^{+}+\operatorname{rk}E|_{X^{\mathsf{A}}}^{+})
-dimensional fibers, and
\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}}
is the vector bundle
E|_{X^{\mathsf{A}}}^{\text{fixed}}
over
\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}}
, so
{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})
is smooth of dimension
(\dim{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}})+\operatorname{rk}E|_{X^{\mathsf{A}}}^{+}+\operatorname{rk}E|_{X^{\mathsf{A}}}^{\text{fixed}})
.

It is elementary to see that
{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})
is contained in
(\pi\times\pi^{\mathsf{A}})^{-1}({\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}}))
, and

{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})\bigcap\,(\pi\times\pi^{\mathsf{A}})^{-1}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}})
is isomorphic to the vector bundle
E|_{X^{\mathsf{A}}}^{+}\oplus E|_{X^{\mathsf{A}}}^{\text{fixed}}
, which implies that

(\pi\times\pi^{\mathsf{A}})|_{{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})}:{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})\to{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}})

is a smooth morphism in a Zariski open neighbourhood of
{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})\bigcap\,(\pi\times\pi^{\mathsf{A}})^{-1}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}})
. Since
(\pi\times\pi^{\mathsf{A}})|_{{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})}
is
\mathsf{A}
-equivariant and the
\mathsf{A}
-action (in the chamber
\mathfrak{C}
) is attracting,
(\pi\times\pi^{\mathsf{A}})|_{{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})}
is a smooth morphism.

Finally, suppose that
s_{1}
and
s_{2}
are two local sections of the map
(\pi\times\pi^{\mathsf{A}})|_{{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})}
on an open subset
U\subseteq{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}})
, then for all
a,b\in\mathcal{O}_{{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}})}(U)
,
as_{1}+bs_{2}
is also a section of
(\pi\times\pi^{\mathsf{A}})|_{{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})}
because
\lim_{\mathfrak{C}}(as_{1}+bs_{2})
exists and is equal to
a\lim_{\mathfrak{C}}(s_{1})+b\lim_{\mathfrak{C}}s_{2}
. It follows that
{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})
is a sub-bundle of
(\pi\times\pi^{\mathsf{A}})^{-1}({\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}}))
of rank
(\operatorname{rk}E|_{X^{\mathsf{A}}}^{+}+\operatorname{rk}E|_{X^{\mathsf{A}}}^{\text{fixed}})
.
∎

Lemma 8.12
.

\displaystyle\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})\bigcap\,(X\times X^{\mathsf{A}})=\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}}).

Proof.

It is easy to see that

{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})\bigcap\,(X\times X^{\mathsf{A}})={\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}}),

thus the inclusion “
\supseteq
” is obvious. For the opposite direction of inclusion, we notice that
(\pi\times\pi^{\mathsf{A}})^{-1}({\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}}))
contains
\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}}
and is closed under taking the attracting set, so we have

{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})\subseteq(\pi\times\pi^{\mathsf{A}})^{-1}({\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}})).

It follows that

\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})\subseteq(\pi\times\pi^{\mathsf{A}})^{-1}(\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}})),

therefore,

\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{Y^{\mathsf{A}}})\bigcap\,(X\times X^{\mathsf{A}})\subseteq(i\times i^{\mathsf{A}})^{-1}(\pi\times\pi^{\mathsf{A}})^{-1}(\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}}))=\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{X^{\mathsf{A}}}).\qed

9.
Hall envelopes and stable envelopes

The theory of stable envelopes on symplectic varieties
[
MO
,
Oko1
,
AO2
]
provides an effective way of producing
R
-
matrices of quantum groups
,
where the triangle lemma plays the key role. In the above constructions of stable envelopes
for critical loci, we prove the triangle lemma for symmetric quiver varieties (or more generally symmetric GIT quotients) with any potentials (Theorem
4.16
, Lemma
3.37
)
and also for certain asymmetric case (Theorem
8.9
).
In the symmetric cases, stable envelopes are compatible with Hall operations via nonabelian stable envelopes
(Theorems
4.22
,
5.5
). This motivates the definition
of
Hall envelopes
(Definition
9.4
) on any (not necessarily symmetric) quiver varieties with potentials as the following compositions:

\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi},\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi},\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{H}^{\phi}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{HallEnv}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w}),}

\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi},\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi},\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{K}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{HallEnv}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w}),}

where the downward arrows are restrictions to open substacks, and the upward arrows are called
interpolation maps
(Definition
9.1
),
defined by adding extra framings on quivers and using (abelian) stable envelopes
for one dimensional tori.
In the symmetric cases, we show that interpolation maps agree with nonabelian stable envelopes (Proposition
9.8
); therefore
Hall envelopes coincide with stable envelopes.
We also give a description of nonabelian stable envelopes using BPS cohomology in §
9.3
. For tripled quivers with canonical cubic potentials, we show that nonabelian stable envelopes constructed in this paper reproduce the nonabelian stable envelopes for Nakajima quiver varieties in
[
AO1
]
along the dimensional reductions (Proposition
9.13
).

We note that triangle lemma could fail for either Hall envelopes or stable envelopes in general (Example
9.25
, Proposition
9.26
).
For a class of asymmetric quiver varieties, where the asymmetric part comes only from the framing (Definition
9.19
),
we prove that triangle lemma of Hall envelopes holds
(Theorem
9.20
, Corollary
9.21
). This provides foundations for studying geometric
R
-matrices of shifted quantum groups.

9.1.
Definitions

Let
Q=(Q_{0},Q_{1})
be any quiver and
\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}},\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}\in\mathbb{N}^{Q_{0}}
. Denote

\displaystyle\underline{\mathbf{d}}=(\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}},\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}),\quad\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}}=(\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}}+\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}).

For simplicity, we consider cyclic stability condition
\theta\in\mathbb{Q}^{Q_{0}}
, i.e.

(9.1)

\theta_{i}<0,\,\,\,\;\forall\,i\in Q_{0}.

The space of framed representations (ref. (
4.1
)) is

R(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})=R(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\oplus\bigoplus_{i\in Q_{0}}\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{v}_{i}},\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{v}_{i}}),

which we visualize vertex-wise as

(9.2)

\textstyle{V_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{B_{i}}
\textstyle{V^{\prime}_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{I_{i}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in},i}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{A_{i}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out},i}}.}

In the above diagram
V^{\prime}_{i}\cong\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{v}_{i}}\cong V_{i}
.
Define GIT quotient and stack quotient by

\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}}):=R(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})/\!\!/_{\theta}G\;,\quad\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}}):=[R(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})/G].

The
\theta
-semistable locus
R(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})^{\theta\emph{-}ss}
contains the open subscheme

\mathring{R}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})
on which the map
I_{i}
are isomorphisms for all
i\in Q_{i}
.
\mathring{R}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})
is
G
-invariant and descends to an open subscheme

\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}}):=\mathring{R}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})/G\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}}).

One has an isomorphism

(9.3)

\displaystyle\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})\cong R(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),

under which, the flavour symmetry
G^{\prime}
which acts on
V^{\prime}_{i}
in (
9.2
) becomes the gauge group acting on
R(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
.

Consider a torus in the center of the flavour group:

(9.4)

\mathbb{C}^{*}\subseteq G^{\prime},

which acts on
V^{\prime}_{i}
in (
9.2
) with weight
-1
for all
i\in Q_{0}

16
16

16

For other generic stability condition
\zeta\in\mathbb{Q}^{Q_{0}}
, we take
\mathbb{C}^{*}\subseteq G^{\prime}
which acts on
V^{\prime}_{i}
in (
9.2
) with weight
\mathrm{sign}(\zeta_{i})
. Then we use this
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-action to construct
\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}
.
. Then
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
is naturally identified with a connected component in
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})^{\mathbb{C}^{*}}
.

Let
\mathsf{T}\subseteq\mathsf{F}
be a torus in the flavour group
\mathsf{F}=\operatorname{Aut}_{G}(R(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}))
. Then we fix a
(G\times\mathsf{T})
-invariant function

\mathsf{w}\colon R(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\to\mathbb{C}.

We denote the descent functions on
\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
and
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
still by
\mathsf{w}
.

Let
\mathsf{w}^{\prime}
be the pullback of
\mathsf{w}
along the projection
R(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})\to R(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
that forgets
\{I_{i}\}_{i\in Q_{0}}
.
\mathsf{w}^{\prime}
descends to a
(G^{\prime}\times\mathsf{T})
-invariant function

\mathsf{w}^{\prime}\colon\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})\to\mathbb{C}

By Proposition
3.23
and the discussions in §
3.10
, there exist a cohomological stable envelope
\operatorname{Stab}_{+}
for
(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}}),\mathsf{w}^{\prime},G^{\prime}\times\mathsf{T},\mathbb{C}^{*},+)
and a
K
-theoretic stable envelope
\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{+}
for
(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}}),\mathsf{w}^{\prime},G^{\prime}\times\mathsf{T},\mathbb{C}^{*},+,\mathsf{s})
with a slope
\mathsf{s}\in\mathrm{Char}(G)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{R}
.

Definition 9.1
.

We define
\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{H}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w})\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w})
as the composition of the following maps

\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{1\otimes\operatorname{id}}
\textstyle{H^{G^{\prime}\times\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}_{+}}
\textstyle{H^{G^{\prime}\times\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}}),\mathsf{w}^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j^{*}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w})}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces H^{G^{\prime}\times\mathsf{T}}(\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}}),\mathsf{w}^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\cong}

Here
1\in H_{G^{\prime}}(\operatorname{pt})
is the unit, and
j\colon\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})
is the open immersion.

Similarly, we define
\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w})\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w})
as the composition of the following maps

\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{1\otimes\operatorname{id}}
\textstyle{K^{G^{\prime}\times\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{+}}
\textstyle{K^{G^{\prime}\times\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}}),\mathsf{w}^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j^{*}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w})}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces K^{G^{\prime}\times\mathsf{T}}(\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}}),\mathsf{w}^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\cong}

Here in the first map
1\in K_{G^{\prime}}(\operatorname{pt})
is the unit.

We call
\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{H}
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}
cohomological and
K
-theoretic
interpolation maps
respectively.

Lemma 9.2
.

Let
\mathbf{k}:\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
be the open immersion of stable locus, then

\displaystyle\mathbf{k}^{*}\circ\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{H}=\operatorname{id},\quad\mathbf{k}^{*}\circ\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}=\operatorname{id}.

Proof.

Let
\mathsf{q}:\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})\to\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
be the quotient map. Then

\mathsf{q}^{-1}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}))\subseteq\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})

is the open subscheme of quiver representations which are
\theta
-semistable after forgetting
\{I_{i}\}_{i\in Q_{0}}
. Note that

\operatorname{Attr}_{+}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}))\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})

is exactly the locus where quiver representations are
\theta
-semistable after forgetting
\{I_{i}\}_{i\in Q_{0}}
. It follows that

(9.5)

\mathsf{q}^{-1}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}))=\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})\bigcap\operatorname{Attr}_{+}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})),

and the restriction of the quotient map to
\mathsf{q}^{-1}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}))
:

\mathsf{q}:\mathsf{q}^{-1}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}))\to\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})

is identified with the attraction map
a:\operatorname{Attr}_{+}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}))\to\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
. By a direct calculation, we have

\operatorname{Stab}_{+}(1\otimes\gamma)|_{\mathsf{q}^{-1}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}))}=\mathsf{q}^{*}(1\otimes\gamma),

for all
\gamma\in H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w})
. Since the isomorphism
H^{G^{\prime}\times\mathsf{T}}(\mathsf{q}^{-1}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})),\mathsf{w})\cong H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w})
maps
\mathsf{q}^{*}(1\otimes\gamma)
to
\gamma
, we see that
\mathbf{k}^{*}\circ\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{H}=\operatorname{id}
. The
K
-theoretic version is proven similarly and we omit the details.
∎

Hall operations are defined similarly as the symmetric case. Let
\mathsf{A}\subseteq\mathsf{T}
be a subtorus. Choose a homomorphism

\phi:\mathsf{A}\to G,

and denote
R(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}
the fixed locus of
R(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
under the action of
\mathsf{A}
via the homomorphism

(\phi,\operatorname{id}):\mathsf{A}\to G\times\mathsf{A}

and denote
G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}
the fixed subgroup of
G
under the conjugation action of the subgroup
\phi(\mathsf{A})
.

Note that

R(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}\cong R(Q_{\phi},\mathbf{v}_{\phi},\underline{\mathbf{d}_{\phi}}),\quad G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}\cong\prod_{i\in Q_{\phi,0}}\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v}_{\phi,i})

for some quiver
Q_{\phi}
with gauge dimension vector
\mathbf{v}_{\phi}
and in-coming and out-going framing dimension vectors
\mathbf{d}_{\phi,\operatorname{in}}
and
\mathbf{d}_{\phi,\operatorname{out}}
respectively. Define

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}:=\left[R(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}/G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}\right],\quad\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}:=R(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}/\!\!/_{\theta}G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}.

In the following discussions, we assume that
