\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}
is nonempty. Then the same argument as Lemma
4.7
shows that
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}
is a connected component of the
\mathsf{A}
-fixed locus
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A}}
, and we have

(9.6)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A}}=\bigsqcup_{\phi/\sim}\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi},

where the sum is taken for all homomorphisms
\phi:\mathsf{A}\to G
modulo the equivalence relation:
\phi_{1}\sim\phi_{2}
if and only if they give the isomorphic
(G\times\mathsf{A})
-module structures on
R(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
.

Let us fix a chamber
\mathfrak{C}\subseteq\operatorname{Lie}(\mathsf{A})_{\mathbb{R}}
, and define

\displaystyle L(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}:=\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(R(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi})\subseteq R(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}).

Then
L(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}
is a linear subspace of
R(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
, and there are maps

\textstyle{R(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces L(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{R(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),}

where
q
is the attraction map and
p
is the natural closed immersion.

Let
P^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}:=\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})
be the parabolic subgroup of
G
, which naturally acts on
L(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}
, and
p
and
q
in the above diagram are
P^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}
-equivariant, where
P^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}
-action on
R(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
is via
P^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\to G
and
P^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}
-action on
R(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}
is via attraction map
P^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\to G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}
. Passing to the quotient stack, we obtain the following diagram

\textstyle{\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathfrak{L}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{q}}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{p}}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),}

where
\mathfrak{L}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}:=[L(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}/P^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}]
. We note that
\mathfrak{q}
is a smooth morphism and
\mathfrak{p}
is a proper morphism.

Definition 9.3
.

We define the
Hall operations

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}:=\mathfrak{p}_{*}\circ\mathfrak{q}^{*}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi},\mathsf{w})\longrightarrow H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w}),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}:=\mathfrak{p}_{*}\circ\mathfrak{q}^{*}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi},\mathsf{w})\longrightarrow K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w}).

Definition 9.4
.

Let
\mathsf{s}\in\mathrm{Char}(G)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{R}
be a slope. We define the
Hall envelopes

\displaystyle\mathrm{HallEnv}_{\mathfrak{C}}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w})\longrightarrow H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w}),

\displaystyle\mathrm{HallEnv}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w})\longrightarrow K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w}),

by

\displaystyle\mathrm{HallEnv}_{\mathfrak{C}}\big|_{H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi},\mathsf{w})}

\displaystyle:=\mathbf{k}^{*}\circ\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{H}^{\phi},

\displaystyle\mathrm{HallEnv}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}\big|_{K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi},\mathsf{w})}

\displaystyle:=\mathbf{k}^{*}\circ\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{K}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}.

Here
\mathbf{k}:\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
is the open immersion of stable locus,
\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{H}^{\phi},\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{K}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}
are interpolation maps (Definition
9.1
) for the stack
\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}
with slope

(9.7)

\displaystyle\mathsf{s}^{\prime}=\mathsf{s}\otimes\det\left(T\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-repl}}\right)^{1/2}\in\mathrm{Char}(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{R},

and
T\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})=R(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})-\operatorname{Lie}(G)
.

Moreover, we say that
\mathrm{HallEnv}_{\mathfrak{C}}
(resp.
\mathrm{HallEnv}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}
) is
compatible with the Hall operation
if for all
\phi:\mathsf{A}\to G
that appears in (
9.6
) the left square (resp. right square) below is commutative

\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi},\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w})}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi},\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{H}^{\phi}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{HallEnv}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w}),\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{H}}

\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi},\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w})}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi},\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{K}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{HallEnv}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w}).\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}}

Remark 9.5
.

Hall envelope
\mathrm{HallEnv}_{\mathfrak{C}}
(resp.
\mathrm{HallEnv}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}
) satisfies axioms (i) and (ii) for stable envelopes in Definition
3.4
(resp. Definition
3.10
). Namely, for arbitrary
\beta\in H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi},\mathsf{w})
and
\gamma\in K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi},\mathsf{w})
, we have

(i)

\mathrm{HallEnv}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\beta)
and
\mathrm{HallEnv}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\gamma)
are supported on
\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi})
;

(ii)

\mathrm{HallEnv}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\beta)\big|_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}}=e^{\mathsf{T}}\left(N_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}/\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})}^{-}\right)\cdot\beta,\,\,\mathrm{HallEnv}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\gamma)\big|_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}}=e^{\mathsf{T}}_{K}\left(N_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}/\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})}^{-}\right)\cdot\gamma.

As a corollary,
\mathrm{HallEnv}_{\mathfrak{C}}
and
\mathrm{HallEnv}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}
are triangular with invertible diagonals after localization, in particular they are
invertible
after localization. In Section
9.6
, we will discuss situations when
\mathrm{HallEnv}_{\mathfrak{C}}
(resp.
\mathrm{HallEnv}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}
) satisfies the axiom (iii) in Definition
3.4
(resp. Definition
3.10
).

We show that triangle lemma of Hall envelopes follows from the associativity of Hall operations.

Lemma 9.6
.

Let
X=\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
,
\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}
be a face of
\mathfrak{C}
, and
\mathsf{A}^{\prime}
be the subtorus of
\mathsf{A}
associated with
\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}
. Suppose that
\mathrm{HallEnv}_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}
(resp.
\mathrm{HallEnv}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}
) is compatible with the Hall operation, then the left triangle (resp. right triangle):

(9.8)

\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{HallEnv}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{HallEnv}_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w}),}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\,\,\,\mathrm{HallEnv}_{\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}}

\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{HallEnv}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{HallEnv}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}},\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\,\,\,\mathrm{HallEnv}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}}

commutes, where
\mathsf{s}^{\prime}=\mathsf{s}|_{X^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}}\otimes\det\left(N^{-}_{X^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}/X}\right)^{1/2}
.

Proof.

Fix a homomorphism
\phi:\mathsf{A}\to G
, and use the same letter
\phi
to denote its restriction to subtorus
\mathsf{A}^{\prime}
. Then the restriction of
\mathsf{s}^{\prime}
on the component
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime},\phi}
can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\mathsf{s}^{\prime}=\mathsf{s}\otimes\det\left(T\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime},\phi\text{-repl}}\right)^{1/2}\in\mathrm{Char}(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A}^{\prime})})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{R}.

Let us define

\displaystyle\mathsf{s}^{\prime\prime}=\mathsf{s}\otimes\det\left(T\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-repl}}\right)^{1/2}\in\mathrm{Char}(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{R},

and consider the following diagram on a triangular prism:

\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi},\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{k}^{*}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime},\phi},\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi},\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{HallEnv}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}\hskip 16.38895pt\hskip 16.38895pt}

\scriptstyle{\mathrm{HallEnv}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{\mathfrak{C}/\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime\prime}}_{K}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w})}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A}^{\prime},\phi},\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\,\,\,\mathrm{HallEnv}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{K}}

The right and the backward squares are commutative by the definition of Hall envelope. The left square is commutative by assumption. The upper triangle is commutative because Hall operations are associative. These imply the commutativity of the lower triangle.

The cohomology version is proven similarly and we omit the details.
∎

We obtain an explicit formula for Hall envelopes when
\mathsf{w}=0
.

Proposition 9.7
.

Assume
\mathsf{w}=0
, then we have

(9.9)

\displaystyle\mathrm{HallEnv}_{\mathfrak{C}}([\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}])=\sum_{w\in W/W^{\phi}}w\left(e^{\mathsf{T}\times G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}}\left(T\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-repl}}\right)\right)\cdot[\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})].

Here
W
,
W^{\phi}
are Weyl groups of
G
,
G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}
respectively,

T\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})=R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})-\operatorname{Lie}(G)
and
(-)^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-repl}}
is the repelling part with respect to homomorphism
\phi\colon\mathsf{A}\to G
, and
w
acts on a weight
\mu
of
G
by
w(\mu)(g):=\mu(w^{-1}\cdot g\cdot w)
.

Let
\mathsf{s}\in\mathrm{char}(G)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{R}
be a generic slope and
\chi\in\mathrm{char}(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})
such that
\mathsf{s}\otimes\det\left(T\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-repl}}\right)^{1/2}
is in a sufficiently small neighbourhood of
\chi
, then

(9.10)

\displaystyle\mathrm{HallEnv}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}([\mathcal{L}_{\chi}])=\sum_{w\in W/W^{\phi}}w\left(\chi\cdot e^{\mathsf{T}\times G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}}_{K}\left(T\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-repl}}\right)\right)\cdot[\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})}].

Here
\mathcal{L}_{\chi}\in\operatorname{Pic}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi})
is the descent of the character
\chi
.

Proof.

Let
\operatorname{Stab}_{+}
be the same as in Definition
9.1
, then we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Stab}_{+}([\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})])=[\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})]

since the right-hand-side satisfies the axioms of stable envelope. Replacing
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
by
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}
, we get

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}^{\phi}_{H}([\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}])=[\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}].

Similar argument shows that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{K}([\mathcal{L}_{\chi}])=\chi\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}}.

Then (
9.9
) and (
9.10
) follow from explicit formulas of cohomological
[
KS
, Thm. 2]
and
K
-theoretic
[
P
, Prop. 3.4]
Hall operations respectively.
∎

9.2.
The case of symmetric quiver varieties

In this subsection, we discuss the case when
Q
is symmetric with a symmetric framing
\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}}=\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}
such that
(G\times\mathsf{A})
-action is self-dual, in other words,
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})=\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
is a symmetric quiver variety in Definition
4.5
, in particular, a symmetric GIT quotient in Definition
4.1
.

Proposition 9.8
.

Suppose that
Q
is symmetric with a symmetric framing
\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}}=\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}
such that
(G\times\mathsf{A})
-action is self-dual. Then
\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{H}=\mathbf{\Psi}_{H}
where the latter is the cohomological nonabelian stable envelope in Definition
4.10
.

For a generic slope
\mathsf{s}\in\mathrm{Char}(G)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{R}
, we have
\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}={\mathbf{\Psi}}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}
, where the latter is the
K
-theoretic nonabelian stable envelope in Definition
5.2
.

Proof.

It is easy to see that the symmetric quiver variety
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{d})
is isomorphic to the total space of a vector bundle
E
on
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})

17
17

17

This follows from the fact that
\theta_{i}<0
for all
i\in Q_{0}
. For other generic stability condition
\zeta\in\mathbb{Q}^{Q_{0}}
,
\mathrm{Tot}(E)
is isomorphic to an open subscheme of
\mathcal{M}_{\zeta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{d})
, where
E=\bigoplus_{\zeta_{i}<0}\operatorname{Hom}(V_{i},V^{\prime}_{i})\oplus\bigoplus_{\zeta_{i}>0}\operatorname{Hom}(V^{\prime}_{i},V_{i})
.
, where
E=\bigoplus_{i\in Q_{0}}\operatorname{Hom}(V_{i},V^{\prime}_{i})
.
E
is repelling with respect to the positive chamber. Then according to Theorem
8.9
,
\operatorname{Stab}_{+}
is induced by the correspondence

\left[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{+}\left(\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}\right)\right]\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})\times\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}).

So
\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{H}
is induced by the correspondence given by the irreducible closed substack

\left[\left(\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{+}\left(\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}\right)\bigcap\left(\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})\times\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})\right)\right)/G^{\prime}\right]\subseteq\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})\times\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}).

Using an equality similar as (
9.5
), we deduce that

(9.11)

\left[\left({\operatorname{Attr}}_{+}\left(\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}\right)\bigcap\left(\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})\times\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})\right)\right)/G^{\prime}\right]=\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})\times\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}).

Therefore
\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{H}
is induced by the correspondence given by their closure, i.e.

\overline{\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}}\subseteq\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})\times\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}).

This shows that
\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{H}=\mathbf{\Psi}_{H}
. The
K
-theoretic version
\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}={\mathbf{\Psi}}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}
follows from Theorem
5.3
and Proposition
9.9
below and a direct calculation showing that

\frac{1}{2}\deg_{\sigma_{i}}e^{G}_{K}(R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})-\operatorname{Lie}(G))+\operatorname{weight}_{\sigma_{i}}\mathsf{s}=\deg_{\sigma_{i}}e^{G}_{K}\left(N_{S_{i}/R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}\right)+\operatorname{weight}_{\sigma_{i}}\left(\mathsf{s}\otimes\left(\det N_{S_{i}/R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}\right)^{1/2}\right).\qed

Recall that the unstable locus

R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\theta\emph{-}u}:=R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})\setminus R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\theta\emph{-}ss}

admits equivariant Kempf-Ness (KN) stratification by connected locally-closed subvarieties
[
Kir
,
DH
]
.
The KN stratification is constructed iteratively by selecting a pair
(\sigma_{i}\in\operatorname{cochar}(G),Z_{i}:=R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\sigma_{i}})
which maximizes the numerical invariant

(9.12)

\displaystyle\mu(\sigma):=\frac{(\sigma,\theta)}{|\sigma|}

among those
(\sigma,Z)
for which
Z
is not contained in the union of the previously defined strata. Here
|\cdot|
is a fixed conjugation-invariant norm on the cocharacters of
G
. For our purpose, we fix the norm square
|\sigma|^{2}
to be

(9.13)

\displaystyle|\sigma|^{2}:=\sum_{i\in Q_{0}}|\theta_{i}|\operatorname{tr}(\sigma_{i}^{2}),

where
\sigma_{i}:\mathbb{C}^{*}\to\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v}_{i})
is the
i
-th component of
\sigma
, which is assumed without loss of generality to be diagonal, i.e.

\displaystyle\sigma_{i}(t)=\operatorname{diag}(t^{\sigma_{i,1}},\cdots,t^{\sigma_{i,\mathbf{v}_{i}}}),

and
\operatorname{tr}(\sigma_{i}^{2})
is a short-hand notation for
\sum_{j=1}^{\mathbf{v}_{i}}\sigma_{i,j}^{2}
. One defines the open subvariety
Z_{i}^{*}\subseteq Z_{i}
to consist of those points not lying on previously defined strata, and

Y_{i}:=\operatorname{Attr}_{\sigma_{i}}(Z_{i}^{*}).

Then define the new strata

S_{i}=G\cdot Y_{i}.

Proposition 9.9
.

Suppose that
Q
is symmetric with a symmetric framing
\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}}=\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}
such that
(G\times\mathsf{A})
-action is self-dual.
Then, for a generic
\mathsf{s}\in\mathrm{Char}(G)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{R}
,
\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}
is the unique
K_{\mathsf{T}}(\operatorname{pt})
-linear map from
K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\mathsf{w})
to
K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\mathsf{w})
which satisfies the following two properties

•

\mathbf{k}^{*}\circ\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}=\operatorname{id}
, where
\mathbf{k}:\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
is the open immersion of stable locus,

•

there is a strict inclusion of intervals

(9.14)

\displaystyle\deg_{\sigma_{i}}{\mathbf{i}}_{\sigma_{i}}^{*}\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}(\gamma)\subsetneq\deg_{\sigma_{i}}e^{G}_{K}\left(N_{S_{i}/R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}\right)+\operatorname{weight}_{\sigma_{i}}\left(\mathsf{s}\otimes\left(\det N_{S_{i}/R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}\right)^{1/2}\right)

for all cocharacters
\sigma_{i}:\mathbb{C}^{*}\to G
that appear in the KN stratification, and all
\gamma\in K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\mathsf{w})
. Here
\mathbf{i}_{\sigma_{i}}:[Z^{*}_{i}/G^{\sigma_{i}}]\to\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})
is the natural map between quotient stacks.

The proof of this proposition is postponed to the Appendix
E
.

Corollary 9.10
.

Suppose that
Q
is symmetric with a symmetric framing
\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}}=\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}
such that
(G\times\mathsf{A})
-action is self-dual. Then for a generic slope
\mathsf{s}
, we have

\mathrm{HallEnv}_{\mathfrak{C}}=\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}},\quad\mathrm{HallEnv}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}=\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}.

Moreover,
\mathrm{HallEnv}_{\mathfrak{C}}
and
\mathrm{HallEnv}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}
are compatible with Hall operations.

Proof.

This follows from Proposition
9.8
, Theorem
4.22
and Theorem
5.5
.
∎

In the below, we simplify the notations by setting
\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{H}=\mathbf{\Psi}_{H}
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}={\mathbf{\Psi}}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}
for symmetric quiver
Q
with a symmetric framing
\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}}=\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}
such that
(G\times\mathsf{A})
-action is self-dual.

9.3.
Connection to BPS cohomology

In this section, we give a description of cohomological nonabelian stable envelopes on symmetric quiver varieties
via BPS cohomology of Davison-Meinhardt
[
DM
]
.
This will be used to confirm the prediction of Botta-Davison on ‘critical stable envelopes’
[
BD
, §1.2]
(ref. Remark
9.12
).

Consider the Jordan-Hölder morphism

\mathsf{JH}:\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})\to\mathcal{M}_{0}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})

sending a representation to the direct sum of the subquotients (simple modules) appearing in its Jordan–Hölder filtration. Let

\operatorname{IC}_{\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}:=\mathbb{Q}_{\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}[\dim\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})]

be the constant perverse sheaf on
\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})
. It is shown in
[
DM
, Thm. A]
that the negative degree perverse cohomology of
\mathsf{JH}_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\operatorname{IC}_{\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}
vanishes, i.e.

\displaystyle\mathsf{JH}_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\operatorname{IC}_{\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}\in\prescript{p}{}{\operatorname{D}}_{c}^{\geqslant 0}(\mathcal{M}_{0}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})).

We note that the framed quiver in this paper is related to the unframed quiver considered in
[
DM
]
by Crawley-Boevey’s procedure
[
CB
]
, which results in a
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-gerbe

\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})\to[\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})/\mathbb{C}^{*}],

and the original statement of
[
DM
, Thm. A]
is that

\mathsf{JH}_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\operatorname{IC}_{[\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})/\mathbb{C}^{*}]}\in\prescript{p}{}{\operatorname{D}}_{c}^{\geqslant 1}(\mathcal{M}_{0}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})).

For a
\mathsf{T}
-invariant function
\mathsf{w}
on
\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})
, one defines the
BPS sheaf
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{BPS}_{\mathsf{w}}:=\prescript{p}{}{\mathcal{H}}^{0}\left(\mathsf{JH}_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\operatorname{IC}_{\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}\right)\in\operatorname{Perv}(\mathcal{M}_{0}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})).

Note that
\mathcal{BPS}_{\mathsf{w}}
is contructed
\mathsf{T}
-equivariantly, and we define the
BPS cohomology
:

\displaystyle H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\mathsf{w})_{\mathrm{BPS}}:=H_{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{0}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\mathcal{BPS}_{\mathsf{w}}[\dim\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})]).

We note that
\omega_{\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}\cong\operatorname{IC}_{\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}[\dim\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})]
, so there exists a natural
H_{\mathsf{T}}(\operatorname{pt})
-linear map

(9.15)

\displaystyle\iota\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\mathsf{w})_{\mathrm{BPS}}\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\mathsf{w}).

By
[
DM
, Thm. C]
, the map (
9.15
) is injective.

Consider the Jordan-Hölder morphism

\mathsf{JH}^{\theta}:\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})\to\mathcal{M}_{0}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})

sending a
\theta
-semistable representation to the direct sum of the subquotients (simple modules) appearing in its Jordan–Hölder filtration. It is well-known that
\mathsf{JH}^{\theta}
is a proper morphism, and fits into the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{JH}^{\theta}}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{JH}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{M}_{0}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),}

where
j
is the open immersion of
\theta
-semistable locus. Let

\operatorname{IC}_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}:=\mathbb{Q}_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}[\dim\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})]

be the constant perverse sheaf on
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})
. Since
\mathsf{JH}^{\theta}
is proper and semismall
[
Toda2
, Lem. 4.4]

18
18

18

It can be shown that
\mathsf{JH}^{\theta}
is small, see
[
COZZ2
, Thm. 1.1]
.
,
\mathsf{JH}^{\theta}_{*}\operatorname{IC}_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}
is a perverse sheaf on
\mathcal{M}_{0}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})
; thus

\mathsf{JH}^{\theta}_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\operatorname{IC}_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}\cong\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\mathsf{JH}^{\theta}_{*}\operatorname{IC}_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}

is also a perverse sheaf. The natural map
\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\operatorname{IC}_{\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}\to j_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\operatorname{IC}_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}
induces a map
\mathsf{JH}_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\operatorname{IC}_{\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}\to\mathsf{JH}^{\theta}_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\operatorname{IC}_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}
. Truncation of the above map to the perverse degree
\leqslant 0
part gives a map:

(9.16)

\displaystyle\Theta\colon\mathcal{BPS}_{\mathsf{w}}\to\mathsf{JH}^{\theta}_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\operatorname{IC}_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}.

According to
[
Toda2
, Lem. 4.7]
, the map
\Theta
in (
9.16
) is an isomorphism. We still use
\Theta
to denote the map induced by taking hypercohomologies on domain and codomain of (
9.16
). Define

(9.17)

\displaystyle\jmath:=\iota\circ\Theta^{-1}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\mathsf{w})\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\mathsf{w}).

Proposition 9.11
.

We have
\jmath=\mathbf{\Psi}_{H}
. In particular,
\mathbf{\Psi}_{H}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\mathsf{w})\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\mathsf{w})
induces an isomorphism
H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\mathsf{w})\cong H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\mathsf{w})_{\mathrm{BPS}}
.

Proof.

According to the proof of Proposition
9.8
,
\mathbf{\Psi}_{H}
is induced by the correspondence
[\overline{\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}}]
,
where

\overline{\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}}\subseteq\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})\times\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})

is the closure of the diagonal of the
\theta
-semistable locus
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})\times\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})
. Precisely, we regard

\displaystyle[\overline{\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}}]

\displaystyle\in H^{\mathsf{T}}_{2\dim\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})\times\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\mathsf{w}\boxminus\mathsf{w})_{\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})\underset{\mathcal{M}_{0}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}{\times}\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}\text{ \tiny via the canonical map \eqref{can with supp_coh}}

Thom-Sebastiani Thm.

\displaystyle=H_{\mathsf{T}}^{-2\dim\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}\left(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})\underset{\mathcal{M}_{0}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}{\times}\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),i^{!}(\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\omega_{\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}\boxtimes\varphi_{-\mathsf{w}}\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})})\right),

where
i:\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})\times_{\mathcal{M}_{0}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})\times\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})
is the natural closed immersion.

According to
[
CG
, (8.6.4)]
, we have an isomorphism

\displaystyle H_{\mathsf{T}}^{\bullet}\left(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})\underset{\mathcal{M}_{0}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}{\times}\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),i^{!}(\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\omega_{\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}\boxtimes\varphi_{-\mathsf{w}}\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})})\right)

\displaystyle\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{\bullet}_{\operatorname{D}^{b}_{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{0}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}))}\left(\mathsf{JH}^{\theta}_{*}\varphi_{-\mathsf{w}}\mathbb{Q}_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})},\mathsf{JH}_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\omega_{\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}\right)

\displaystyle\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{\bullet}_{\operatorname{D}^{b}_{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{0}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}))}\left(\mathsf{JH}^{\theta}_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\mathbb{Q}_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})},\mathsf{JH}_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\omega_{\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}\right),

so
[\overline{\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}}]
induces a morphism in
\operatorname{D}^{b}_{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{0}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}))
:

\displaystyle[\overline{\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}}]\colon\mathsf{JH}^{\theta}_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}\to\mathsf{JH}_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\omega_{\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}.

Then according to
[
VV1
, Prop. 2.11]
, critical convolution map
\mathbf{\Psi}_{H}
is obtained by taking hypercohomologies of the above morphism. As we have seen previously,

\mathsf{JH}^{\theta}_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}\in\operatorname{Perv}(\mathcal{M}_{0}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}))[\dim\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})],\quad\mathsf{JH}_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\omega_{\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}\in\prescript{p}{}{\operatorname{D}}_{c}^{\geqslant-\dim\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}(\mathcal{M}_{0}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})),

therefore
[\overline{\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}}]
factors as

[\overline{\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}}]\colon\mathsf{JH}^{\theta}_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}\to\mathcal{BPS}_{\mathsf{w}}[\dim\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})]\xrightarrow{[\overline{\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}}]^{\prime}}\mathsf{JH}_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\omega_{\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})},

where
\mathcal{BPS}_{\mathsf{w}}[\dim\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})]=\prescript{p}{}{\tau}^{\leqslant-\dim\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}\mathsf{JH}_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\omega_{\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}
.

Composing
[\overline{\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}}]
with the natural map

\mathsf{JH}_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\omega_{\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}\to\mathsf{JH}_{*}j_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}\cong\mathsf{JH}^{\theta}_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})},

we get the critical convolution map
[\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}]:\mathsf{JH}^{\theta}_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\omega_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}
induced by the diagonal of
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})\times\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})
, which is identity map. It follows that
\Theta\circ[\overline{\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}}]^{\prime}=\operatorname{id}
. Then by the definition of
\jmath
, we have
\jmath=\mathbf{\Psi}_{H}
.
∎

Remark 9.12
.

Combining Corollary
9.10
with Proposition
9.11
, we obtain the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\mathsf{A},\phi},\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\mathsf{w})}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\mathsf{A},\phi},\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\jmath^{\phi}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\textstyle{H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\mathsf{w}),\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\jmath}

where
\jmath^{\phi}
is the map (
9.17
) for the stack
\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}
. Suppose that
\mathsf{A}\cong(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{l}
is the framing torus which acts on the framing vector space

by setting

such that

agrees with the “critical stable envelope”
\mathrm{CStab}
defined in
[

, §1.2]
.

9.4.
The case of tripled quivers with canonical cubic potentials

When
Q
is the tripled quiver of another quiver
Q^{\prime}
with cubic potential
\mathsf{w}=\sum_{i\in Q_{0}}\operatorname{tr}(\varepsilon_{i}\mu_{i})
,
where
\varepsilon_{i}
is the loop at
i
-th node and
\mu_{i}
is the
i
-th component of the moment map
\mu\colon R(\overline{Q^{\prime}},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})\to\operatorname{Lie}(G)^{\vee}
, dimensional reduction gives isomorphisms
(Example
6.13
, Remark
6.14
):

(9.18)

\begin{split}&\delta_{H}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}))\cong H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\mathsf{w}),\quad\delta_{H}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{N}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}))\cong H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\mathsf{w}),\\
&\delta_{K}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}))\cong K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\mathsf{w}),\quad\delta_{K}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{N}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}))\cong K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\mathsf{w}).\end{split}

We define the following maps:

(9.19)

\begin{split}&\Psi_{H}:=\delta_{H}^{-1}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}_{H}\circ\delta_{H}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}))\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{N}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})),\\
&\Psi^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}:=\delta_{K}^{-1}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}\circ\delta_{K}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}))\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{N}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})).\end{split}

Here the Nakajima quiver variety
\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})
, the preprojective stack
\mathfrak{N}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})
fits into open and closed immersion:

\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})=\mu^{-1}(0)/\!\!/_{\theta}G\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{N}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})=[\mu^{-1}(0)/G]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\iota}}}{{\hookrightarrow}}\mathfrak{R}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}):=[R(\overline{Q^{\prime}},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})/G].

In
[
AO1
, §2.1.8]
, a nonabelian stable envelope
19
19

19

What we call
\overline{\Psi}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}(\alpha)
here is denoted by
\mathbf{s}_{\alpha}
in
[
AO1
, §2.1.8]
.

\overline{\Psi}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}))\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{R}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}))

is constructed as the composition of the following maps

\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{1\otimes\operatorname{id}}
\textstyle{K^{G^{\prime}\times\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{+}}
\textstyle{K^{G^{\prime}\times\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{d}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j^{*}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{R}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}))}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces K^{G^{\prime}\times\mathsf{T}}(\mathring{\mathcal{N}}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{d})),}
\scriptstyle{\cong}

where
\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{d})
is the Nakajima quiver variety with the quiver data visualized vertex-wise as

\textstyle{V_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{J_{i}}
\scriptstyle{B_{i}}
\textstyle{V^{\prime}_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{I_{i}}
\textstyle{D_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces,}
\scriptstyle{A_{i}}

and
j:\mathring{\mathcal{N}}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{d})\subseteq\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{d})
is the open immersion of the locus on which the maps
\{I_{i}\}_{i\in Q_{0}}
are isomorphisms,
\mathsf{s}^{\prime}
is related to
\mathsf{s}
by

(9.20)

\displaystyle\mathsf{s}^{\prime}=\mathsf{s}\otimes\det\left(T^{1/2}_{\mathfrak{N}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{d})}\right)^{-1/2},\quad\text{where }T^{1/2}_{\mathfrak{N}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{d})}=T^{1/2}_{\mathfrak{N}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}+\sum_{i\in Q_{0}}\hbar^{-1}\operatorname{Hom}(V_{i},V^{\prime}_{i})\in K_{G}(\operatorname{pt}).

The shift in slope comes from the difference between degree conditions in Definition
3.10
and in
[
Oko1
, §9.1]
(see Remark
3.15
). We fix an orientation of
Q^{\prime}
so that

\displaystyle T^{1/2}_{\mathfrak{N}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}=\sum_{a\in Q^{\prime}_{1}}\operatorname{Hom}(V_{t(a)},V_{h(a)})+\sum_{i\in Q_{0}}(\operatorname{Hom}(D_{i},V_{i})-\operatorname{End}(V_{i}))\in K_{G}(\operatorname{pt}).

Repeating the above procedure with
K
-theory replaced by Borel-Moore homology, we obtain a cohomological nonabelian stable envelope

\overline{\Psi}_{H}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}))\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{R}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})).

Proposition 9.13
.

We have the following equations of maps:

(9.21)

\displaystyle\overline{\Psi}_{H}=\boldsymbol{\iota}_{*}\circ\Psi_{H},\quad\overline{\Psi}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}=\boldsymbol{\iota}_{*}\circ\Psi^{\mathsf{s}\otimes\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{K},\,\,\,\mathrm{where}\,\,\boldsymbol{\delta}=\det\left(T^{1/2}_{\mathfrak{N}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}\right)^{-1/2}.

Proof.

Notice that
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{d})
is the total space of the vector bundle
E=\bigoplus_{i\in Q_{0}}\operatorname{Hom}(V_{i},V^{\prime}_{i})
over
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{d},\mathbf{d})
, and
E
is repelling with respect to the
+
chamber. Let
\widetilde{\mathsf{w}}=\sum_{i\in Q_{0}}\operatorname{tr}(\varepsilon_{i}\widetilde{\mu}_{i})
be a function on
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{d})
, where
\widetilde{\mu}_{i}\colon R(\overline{Q^{\prime}},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{d})\to\operatorname{Lie}(G)^{\vee}
is the moment map. Then
\widetilde{\mathsf{w}}
is
(\mathsf{T}\times G^{\prime})
-invariant and
\widetilde{\mathsf{w}}\big|_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{d})}=\mathsf{w}^{\prime}
. By Proposition
8.3
, the pushforward along zero section map gives the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{K^{G^{\prime}\times\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{+}}
\textstyle{K^{G^{\prime}\times\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{d}),\widetilde{\mathsf{w}})}
\textstyle{K^{G^{\prime}\times\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{id}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime\prime}}_{+}}
\textstyle{K^{G^{\prime}\times\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}}),\mathsf{w}^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces,}
\scriptstyle{\overline{\boldsymbol{\iota}}_{*}}

where
\mathsf{s}^{\prime\prime}=\mathsf{s}^{\prime}\otimes(\det E)^{1/2}
and
\overline{\boldsymbol{\iota}}:\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{d})
is the zero section map. Using (
9.20
), we can rewrite
\mathsf{s}^{\prime\prime}=\mathsf{s}\otimes\boldsymbol{\delta}
. The pushforward map is compatible with dimensional reduction, i.e. the following diagram is commutative

\textstyle{K^{G^{\prime}\times\mathsf{T}}(\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}}),\mathsf{w}^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{\boldsymbol{\iota}}_{*}}
\textstyle{K^{G^{\prime}\times\mathsf{T}}(\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{d}),\widetilde{\mathsf{w}})}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{N}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{\iota}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{K}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{R}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{K}}

By Theorem
6.11
, we have the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{K^{G^{\prime}\times\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}),\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{+}}
\textstyle{K^{G^{\prime}\times\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{d}),\widetilde{\mathsf{w}})}
\textstyle{K^{G^{\prime}\times\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{+}}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{K}}
\textstyle{K^{G^{\prime}\times\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{d}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{K}}

Here we set normalizer
\mathcal{E}
equal to the structure sheaf. Combining the above three commutative diagrams, we obtain the equation
\overline{\Psi}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}=\boldsymbol{\iota}_{*}\circ\Psi^{\mathsf{s}\otimes\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{K}
. The other equation can be proven similarly and we omit the details.
∎

In the coming remark, we explain that the above results reproduce previous works
on comparing stable envelopes and Hall operations on Nakajima quiver varieties.

Remark 9.14
.

20

\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\mathsf{A},\phi})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\scriptstyle{\mathbf{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})),\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{\Psi}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}}

where
\overline{\Psi}_{H},\overline{\Psi}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}
are constructed in
[

]
.

When
\mathsf{A}
is a framing torus, the left square in (
9.26
) reproduces
a result of Botta
[
Bot
, Thm. 5.6]
, where his proof uses the method of abelianizations
[
Sh
]
. Botta-Davison
[
BD
]
has another proof of Botta’s result by showing that
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\phi}_{\Pi,\mathfrak{C}}\circ{\Psi}_{H}^{\phi}
and
{\Psi}_{H}\circ\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}
are induced by the same correspondence.

Moreover, the left square in (
9.26
) can be used to deduce an inductive formula of stable envelopes for Nakajima quiver varieties
[
Bot
, Thm. 5.12]
, which reduces the computations of stable envelopes to the computations of
\overline{\Psi}_{H}
in the framing rank one cases.

As an application, we deduce explicit formulas of stable envelopes for the Nakajima quiver variety
\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})
using Remark
9.14
under certain assumptions on the fixed loci. Note that

(9.27)

R(\overline{Q^{\prime}},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}=R(\overline{Q^{\prime}_{\phi}},\mathbf{v}_{\phi},\mathbf{d}_{\phi})

for a certain quiver
Q^{\prime}_{\phi}
(possibly disconnected) with gauge dimension vector
\mathbf{v}_{\phi}
and framing dimension vector
\mathbf{d}_{\phi}
.

The following result, in the case when
Q^{\prime}
is an affine type A quiver and
\mathsf{A}=
framing torus
\times

\mathbb{C}^{*}
that resolves the loop (i.e. (
9.27
) is a finite type A quiver variety),
recovers cohomological and
K
-theoretic limits of
[
Din
, Thm. 1]
and also
[
AO1
, Prop. 7]
.

Corollary 9.15
.

Assume that every connected component of
(Q^{\prime}_{\phi},\mathbf{v}_{\phi},\mathbf{d}_{\phi})
is a Dynkin quiver with minuscule framing, i.e. there is a node
i_{0}
that corresponds to a minuscule fundamental weight of the Lie algebra associated with the Dynkin quiver, such that
\mathbf{d}_{\phi,i_{0}}=1
,
\mathbf{d}_{\phi,i}=0
if
i\neq i_{0}
.

Then
in
\mathsf{T}
-localized homology
H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{N}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}))_{\mathrm{loc}}:=H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{N}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}))\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}[\operatorname{Lie}(\mathsf{T})]}\mathrm{Frac}(\mathbb{Q}[\operatorname{Lie}(\mathsf{T})])
, we have

(9.28)

\displaystyle\Psi_{H}\circ\mathbf{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}([\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}])=\sum_{w\in W/W^{\phi}}w\left(e^{\mathsf{T}\times G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}}\left((T^{1/2})^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-repl}}+\hbar(T^{1/2})^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-attr}}\right)\right)\cdot[\mathfrak{N}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})]^{\mathrm{vir}}\>,

where
\mathfrak{N}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})
(
9.23
) has a quasi-smooth stack structure and
[\mathfrak{N}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})]^{\mathrm{vir}}
denotes its virtual class. In particular,

\displaystyle\mathbf{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}([\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}])=\sum_{w\in W/W^{\phi}}w\left(e^{\mathsf{T}\times G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}}\left((T^{1/2})^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-repl}}+\hbar(T^{1/2})^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-attr}}\right)\right)\cdot[\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})]\>.

Let
\mathsf{s}
be a generic slope and
\chi\in\mathrm{char}(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})
such that
\mathsf{s}\otimes\boldsymbol{\delta}_{\phi}
is in a sufficiently small neighbourhood of
\chi
, then
in
\mathsf{T}
-localized
K
-theory
K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{N}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}))_{\mathrm{loc}}:=K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{N}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}))\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}[\mathsf{T}]}\mathrm{Frac}(\mathbb{Q}[\mathsf{T}])
, we have

(9.29)

\displaystyle\Psi^{\mathsf{s}\otimes\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{K}\circ\mathbf{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}([\mathcal{L}_{\chi}])=\sqrt{\hbar}^{\operatorname{rk}(T^{1/2})^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-attr}}}\cdot\sum_{w\in W/W^{\phi}}w\left(\chi\cdot e^{\mathsf{T}\times G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}}_{K}\left((T^{1/2})^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-repl}}+\hbar(T^{1/2})^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-attr}}\right)\right)\cdot[\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\mathfrak{N}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}]\>,

where
\mathcal{L}_{\chi}\in\operatorname{Pic}(\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\mathsf{A},\phi})
is the descent of the character
\chi
, and

[\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\mathfrak{N}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}]
denotes the virtual structure sheaf.

In particular,

\displaystyle\mathbf{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}([\mathcal{L}_{\chi}])=\sqrt{\hbar}^{\operatorname{rk}(T^{1/2})^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-attr}}}\cdot\sum_{w\in W/W^{\phi}}w\left(\chi\cdot e^{\mathsf{T}\times G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}}_{K}\left((T^{1/2})^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-repl}}+\hbar(T^{1/2})^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-attr}}\right)\right)\cdot[\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}]\>.

Proof.

By Lemma
9.16
below, we have

\displaystyle\Psi_{H}([\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}])=[\mathfrak{N}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}]^{\mathrm{vir}},\quad\Psi^{\phi,\mathsf{s}\otimes\boldsymbol{\delta}_{\phi}}_{K}([\mathcal{L}_{\chi}])=[\chi\otimes\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\mathfrak{N}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}}].

By the commutativity of diagrams (
9.22
), we get

\displaystyle\Psi_{H}\circ\mathbf{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}([\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}])=\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\Pi,\mathfrak{C}}\left(\epsilon\cdot[\mathfrak{N}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}]^{\mathrm{vir}}\right),\quad\Psi^{\mathsf{s}\otimes\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{K}\circ\mathbf{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}([\mathcal{L}_{\chi}])=\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\Pi,\mathfrak{C}}\left(\mathcal{E}\cdot[\chi\otimes\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\mathfrak{N}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}}]\right).

Since

R(\overline{Q^{\prime}},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})=M\oplus\hbar^{-1}M^{\vee},\,\,\mathrm{where}\,\,\,M=\bigoplus_{a\in Q^{\prime}_{1}}\operatorname{Hom}(V_{t(a)},V_{h(a)})\oplus\bigoplus_{i\in Q^{\prime}_{0}}\operatorname{Hom}(D_{i},V_{i}),

we have inclusion
R(\overline{Q^{\prime}},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\mathsf{T}}\subseteq M
. Since
M\subseteq\mu^{-1}(0)
, we see that
R(\overline{Q^{\prime}},\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\mathsf{T}}\subseteq\mu^{-1}(0)
. Therefore the pushforward maps

\boldsymbol{\iota}_{*}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{N}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}))\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{R}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})),\quad\boldsymbol{\iota}_{*}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{N}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}))\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{R}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}))

are isomorphisms after
\mathsf{T}
-localization. Note that

\boldsymbol{\iota}_{*}([\mathfrak{N}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})]^{\mathrm{vir}})=e^{\mathsf{T}\times G}(\hbar^{-1}\operatorname{Lie}(G)^{\vee})\cdot[\mathfrak{R}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})],\quad\boldsymbol{\iota}_{*}([\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\mathfrak{N}}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})])=e^{\mathsf{T}\times G}_{K}(\hbar^{-1}\operatorname{Lie}(G)^{\vee})\cdot[\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{R}}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})].

Then it follows from the compatibility (
9.24
) that

\displaystyle\quad\,\Psi_{H}\circ\mathbf{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}([\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}])=\boldsymbol{\iota}_{*}^{-1}\circ\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\left(\epsilon\cdot e^{\mathsf{T}\times G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}}(\hbar^{-1}\operatorname{Lie}(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{\vee})\cdot[\mathfrak{R}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}]\right)

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\iota}_{*}^{-1}\sum_{w\in W/W^{\phi}}w\left(\epsilon\cdot e^{\mathsf{T}\times G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}}\left(\hbar^{-1}\operatorname{Lie}(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{\vee}+\hbar^{-1}\operatorname{Lie}(G)^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-attr}}+T\mathfrak{R}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-repl}}\right)\right)\cdot[\mathfrak{R}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})]

\displaystyle=\sum_{w\in W/W^{\phi}}w\left(\epsilon\cdot e^{\mathsf{T}\times G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}}\left(\hbar^{-1}\operatorname{Lie}(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{\vee}+\hbar^{-1}\operatorname{Lie}(G)^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-attr}}+T\mathfrak{R}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-repl}}\right)\right)\cdot\frac{[\mathfrak{N}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})]^{\mathrm{vir}}}{e^{\mathsf{T}\times G}(\hbar^{-1}\operatorname{Lie}(G)^{\vee})}

\displaystyle=\sum_{w\in W/W^{\phi}}w\left(e^{\mathsf{T}\times G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}}\left((T^{1/2})^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-repl}}+\hbar(T^{1/2})^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-attr}}\right)\right)\cdot[\mathfrak{N}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})]^{\mathrm{vir}}.

Here in the second equality, we use (
9.25
) and the explicit formula of Hall operation as (
4.26
).

Similarly in the
K
-theory, we have

\displaystyle\quad\,\Psi^{\mathsf{s}\otimes\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{K}\circ\mathbf{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}([\mathcal{L}_{\chi}])=\boldsymbol{\iota}_{*}^{-1}\circ\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\left(\epsilon\cdot e^{\mathsf{T}\times G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}}_{K}(\hbar^{-1}\operatorname{Lie}(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{\vee})\cdot[\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{R}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}}]\right)

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\iota}_{*}^{-1}\sum_{w\in W/W^{\phi}}w\left(\chi\cdot\mathcal{E}\cdot e^{\mathsf{T}\times G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}}_{K}\left(\hbar^{-1}\operatorname{Lie}(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{\vee}+\hbar^{-1}\operatorname{Lie}(G)^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-attr}}+T\mathfrak{R}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-repl}}\right)\right)\cdot[\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{R}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{w\in W/W^{\phi}}w\left(\chi\cdot\mathcal{E}\cdot e^{\mathsf{T}\times G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}}_{K}\left(\hbar^{-1}\operatorname{Lie}(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{\vee}+\hbar^{-1}\operatorname{Lie}(G)^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-attr}}+T\mathfrak{R}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-repl}}\right)\right)\cdot\frac{[\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\mathfrak{N}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}]}{e^{\mathsf{T}\times G}_{K}(\hbar^{-1}\operatorname{Lie}(G)^{\vee})}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\hbar}^{\operatorname{rk}(T^{1/2})^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-attr}}}\cdot\sum_{w\in W/W^{\phi}}w\left(\chi\cdot e^{\mathsf{T}\times G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}}_{K}\left((T^{1/2})^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-repl}}+\hbar(T^{1/2})^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-attr}}\right)\right)\cdot[\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\mathfrak{N}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}].\qed

In the above proof, we use the following lemma, whose proof will be given in Appendix
F
.

Lemma 9.16
.

Let
\Gamma
be a Dynkin quiver with gauge dimension vector
\mathbf{r}
and framing dimension vector
\mathbf{e}
such that
\mathbf{e}
is minuscule. Assume that
\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(\mathbf{r},\mathbf{e})
is nonempty. Then

(9.30)

\displaystyle\Psi_{H}([\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(\mathbf{r},\mathbf{e})])=[\mathfrak{N}(\mathbf{r},\mathbf{e})]^{\mathrm{vir}}.

Let
G=\prod_{i\in\Gamma_{0}}\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{r}_{i})
denote the gauge group, and
\mathsf{s}\in\mathrm{char}(G)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{R}
be a generic slope taken in a sufficiently small neighbourhood of
\chi\in\mathrm{char}(G)
, then

(9.31)

\displaystyle\Psi^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}([\mathcal{L}_{\chi}])=[\chi\otimes\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\mathfrak{N}(\mathbf{r},\mathbf{e})}],

where
\mathcal{L}_{\chi}\in\operatorname{Pic}(\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(\mathbf{r},\mathbf{e}))
is the descent of the character
\chi
.

Remark 9.17
.

We note that
\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(\mathbf{r},\mathbf{e})
is a point for minuscule
\mathbf{e}
, so
\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}
in Corollary
9.15
is also a point. Then (
9.28
) and (
9.29
) completely determine
\mathbf{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}
and
\mathbf{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}
in this situation.

9.5.
Hall operation compatible Hall envelopes

We show for a class of asymmetric quiver varieties, where the asymmetric part comes only from the framing, their Hall envelopes
are compatible with Hall operations.

Definition 9.18
.

Let
Q
be a symmetric quiver and
\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}},\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}\in\mathbb{N}^{Q_{0}}
be dimension vectors. The
symmetrization
of
R(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
is defined to be
R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{c})
(
4.5
) where
\mathbf{c}_{i}=\max\{\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in},i},\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out},i}\}
. We identify
R(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
as a
G
-subrepresentation of

\displaystyle R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{c})=R(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\oplus\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in Q_{0}\\
\mathrm{s.t.}\,\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in},i}>\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out},i}\end{subarray}}\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{v}_{i}},\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in},i}-\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out},i}})\oplus\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in Q_{0}\\
\mathrm{s.t.}\,\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in},i}<\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out},i}\end{subarray}}\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out},i}-\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in},i}},\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{v}_{i}}).

Definition 9.19
.

Let
Q
be a symmetric quiver and
\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}},\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}\in\mathbb{N}^{Q_{0}}
be dimension vectors and
\mathsf{A}
be a subtorus of flavour group
\operatorname{Aut}_{G}R(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
. We say that the
\mathsf{A}
-action on
R(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
is
pseudo-self-dual
if it extends to a self-dual
\mathsf{A}
-action on the symmetrization
R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{c})
.
We say that the
\mathsf{A}
-action on
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
is
pseudo-self-dual
if it is induced from a pseudo-self-dual action on
R(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
.

Here is the main theorem of this section.

Theorem 9.20
.

Let
Q
be a symmetric quiver and
\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}},\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out}}\in\mathbb{N}^{Q_{0}}
be dimension vectors such that
\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in},i}\geqslant\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out},i}
for all
i\in Q_{0}
, choose cyclic stability
\theta

(
9.1
). Let
\mathsf{T}\subseteq\operatorname{Aut}_{G}R(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
be a torus in the flavour group
and
\mathsf{A}
be a pseudo-self-dual subtorus of
\mathsf{T}
,
\mathfrak{C}
be a chamber associated with
\mathsf{A}
action on
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
,
and
\mathsf{s}
be a generic slope.

Then Hall envelopes
\mathrm{HallEnv}_{\mathfrak{C}}
and
\mathrm{HallEnv}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}
are compatible with Hall operations.

By Theorem
9.20
and Lemma
9.6
, we get the triangle lemma for the above asymmetric
quiver varieties.

Corollary 9.21
.

In the setting of Theorem
9.20
and let
X=\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
, then both diagrams in (
9.8
) commute.

9.5.1.
Proof of Theorem
9.20
, cohomology case

We begin with the following lemma.
In below, we omit the canonical map in Lemma
3.22
and simply write

[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}\left(\Delta_{(-)}\right)]:=\operatorname{can}\circ\,[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}\left(\Delta_{(-)}\right)].

Lemma 9.22
.

In the setting of Theorem
9.20
, the Hall envelope

\mathrm{HallEnv}_{\mathfrak{C}}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w})\longrightarrow H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w})

is induced by the correspondence

\displaystyle\left[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A}}})\right]\in H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\times\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A}},\mathsf{w}\boxminus\mathsf{w})_{\operatorname{Attr}^{f}_{\mathfrak{C}}}\;.

Proof.

Consider the vector bundle
E=E_{+}\oplus E_{-}
on
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})
, where the fibers of
E_{+}
and
E_{-}
are

(9.32)

\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in Q_{0}\\
\mathrm{s.t.}\,\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in},i}>\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out},i}\end{subarray}}\operatorname{Hom}(V_{i},\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in},i}-\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out},i}})\;,\quad\bigoplus_{i\in Q_{0}}\operatorname{Hom}(V_{i},V^{\prime}_{i})\;.

Then
\mathrm{Tot}(E)
is isomorphic to the symmetric quiver variety
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}})
.
Note that

E_{+}
(resp.
E_{-}
) is attracting (resp. repelling) with respect to the positive chamber under the
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-action in (
9.4
). Then by
Propositions
8.7
,
8.8
, the
\operatorname{Stab}_{+}
(in Definition
9.1
) is induced by the correspondence

\left[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{+}\left(\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})}\right)\right]\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})\times\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),

where the closure is taken inside
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})\times\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
.

So
\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{H}
(in Definition
9.1
) is induced by the correspondence given by the irreducible closed substack

\left[\left(\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{+}\left(\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})}\right)\bigcap\left(\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})\times\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\right)\right)/G^{\prime}\right]\subseteq\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\times\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}).

As in (
9.11
), we have

\left[\left(\operatorname{Attr}_{+}\left(\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})}\right)\bigcap\left(\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})\times\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\right)\right)/G^{\prime}\right]=\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})}\subseteq\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\times\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}).

Therefore
\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{H}
is induced by the correspondence given by the closure of the diagonal of stable locus
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
inside the stack
\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\times\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
, i.e.

\overline{\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})}}\subseteq\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\times\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}).

Let
\phi:\mathsf{A}\to G
be a homomorphism that appears in (
9.6
), then
\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{H}^{\phi}
is similarly induced by the correspondence

\displaystyle[\overline{\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}}}].

The same argument as Proposition
9.8
shows that
\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{H}^{\phi}
is induced by the correspondence

[Z]\in H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\times\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi},\mathsf{w}\boxminus\mathsf{w})_{Z},

where
Z=\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}\left(\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}}\right)
is the closure of
\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}\left(\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}}\right)
inside the stack
\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\times\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}
(here attracting set is taken inside the stable locus

\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\times\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}
).

It follows that
\mathrm{HallEnv}_{\mathfrak{C}}=\mathbf{k}^{*}\circ\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{H}^{\phi}
is induced by the correspondence
\left[Z\big|_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\times\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}}\right]
, which is exactly
\left[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}})\right]
,
where the closure is taken in
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\times\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}

∎

Let
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{t}\subseteq G^{\prime}
be a torus in the center of the flavour group which acts on
V^{\prime}_{i}
in (
9.2
) with weight
-1
for all
i\in Q_{0}
, then
\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}:=\mathsf{A}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{t}
naturally acts on
R(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})
. Let

K_{\mathbb{C}^{*}_{t}}(\operatorname{pt})=\mathbb{Q}[t^{\pm}],\quad K_{\mathsf{A}}(\operatorname{pt})=\mathbb{Q}[a_{i}^{\pm}]_{i=1}^{r},

and consider the chamber
\widetilde{\mathfrak{C}}
in
\operatorname{Lie}(\widetilde{\mathsf{A}})_{\mathbb{R}}
such that
\mathfrak{C}_{+}=\{a_{i}=0\}_{i=1}^{r}\times\{t>0\}
is a face of
\widetilde{\mathfrak{C}}
and
\widetilde{\mathfrak{C}}/\mathfrak{C}_{+}=\mathfrak{C}
. Combining Lemma
9.22
with Proposition
8.10
and Theorem
4.16
, we see that the composition

\displaystyle\operatorname{Stab}_{+}\circ\mathrm{HallEnv}_{\mathfrak{C}}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}\times G^{\prime}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi},\mathsf{w})\to H^{\mathsf{T}\times G^{\prime}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}}),\mathsf{w})

is induced by the correspondence

\left[\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{C}}}\left(\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}}\right)\right],

which is the closure of the attracting set
\operatorname{Attr}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{C}}}\left(\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}}\right)
inside the variety
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})\times\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}
.

Therefore the map
\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{H}\circ\mathrm{HallEnv}_{\mathfrak{C}}
is induced by the correspondence

[S]\in H^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\times\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi},\mathsf{w}\boxminus\mathsf{w})_{S},

where

\displaystyle S=\left[\frac{\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{C}}}\left(\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}}\right)\bigcap\left(\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})\times\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}\right)}{G^{\prime}}\right]

is an irreducible closed substack of
\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\times\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}
. We have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Attr}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{C}}}\left(\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}}\right)\bigcap\left(\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})\times\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}\right)=\left(\mathsf{q}\times\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}}\right)^{-1}\left(\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}\left(\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}}\right)\right),

where
\mathsf{q}:\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})\to\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})
is the quotient map and
\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}\left(\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}}\right)
is the attracting set of
\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}}
in
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\times\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}
, regarded as a locally closed substack in
\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})\times\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}
. It follows that
S
contains
\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}\left(\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}}\right)
as an open substack.
By the closedness and irreducibility of
S
,

S=\overline{\operatorname{Attr}}_{\mathfrak{C}}\left(\Delta_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}}\right)=Z.

This is exactly the correspondence that induces
\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{H}^{\phi}
(by the proof of Lemma
9.22
),
and we are done.

9.5.2.
Proof of Theorem
9.20
, K-theory case

By Lemma
9.2
,
\mathbf{k}^{*}\circ\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}=\operatorname{id}
, it suffices to show that

\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{K}(\gamma)\in\operatorname{im}(\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K})

for all
\gamma\in K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi},\mathsf{w})
.

Recall the vector bundle
E_{+}
(
9.32
) on
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})
. Denote

\displaystyle\underline{\mathbf{d}}_{\operatorname{in}}:=(\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}},\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}}),\quad\underline{\mathbf{d}}_{\operatorname{in}}^{\mathbf{v}}:=(\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}},\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}}).

\mathrm{Tot}(E_{+})
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}_{\operatorname{in}}^{\mathbf{v}})
which is used to define nonabelian stable envelope
\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}
for symmetric quiver variety
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}})
.
Restricting to the open locus (
9.3
) (where
\mathbf{d}=\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}}
) and (
9.3
),
and taking quotient by
G
, the bundle projection becomes

\pi:\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}})\to\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}).

By Proposition
8.2
, we have commutative diagrams:

\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w}\circ\pi)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}}),\mathsf{w}\circ\pi)}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}),\mathsf{w}\circ\pi)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}}),\mathsf{w}\circ\pi)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces,}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K,\text{sym}}}

\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi},\mathsf{w}\circ\pi)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{\mathsf{A}*}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi},\mathsf{w}\circ\pi)}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi},\mathsf{w}\circ\pi)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{K,\text{sym}}}
\scriptstyle{i^{\mathsf{A}*}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi},\mathsf{w}\circ\pi)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces,}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{K}}

where
i^{\mathsf{A}}:\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}
is the zero section of the fixed locus
\pi^{\mathsf{A}}
. The right diagram follows from
the torus fixed version of the left diagram and
i^{\mathsf{A}*}=(\pi^{\mathsf{A}*})^{-1}
.
Here “sym” in the subscript means the nonabelian stable envelopes for symmetric quiver varieties. Then it remains to show that for all
\gamma\in K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi},\mathsf{w})
, we have

\pi^{*}\circ\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ i^{\mathsf{A}*}\circ\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{K,\text{sym}}(\gamma)\in\operatorname{im}(\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K,\text{sym}}).

By Proposition
9.9
, it is enough to prove the following degree condition

•

there is a strict inclusion of intervals

\displaystyle\deg_{\sigma_{i}}{\mathbf{i}}_{\sigma_{i}}^{*}\circ(\pi^{*}\circ\mathfrak{m}^{\phi}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ i^{\mathsf{A}*}\circ\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{K,\text{sym}}(\gamma))\subsetneq\deg_{\sigma_{i}}\hat{e}^{G}_{K}\left(N_{S_{i}/R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}})}\right)+\operatorname{weight}_{\sigma_{i}}\left(\mathsf{s}\right)

for all cocharacters
\sigma_{i}:\mathbb{C}^{*}\to G
that appear in the KN stratification, and all
\gamma\in K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi},\mathsf{w})
,
where
\hat{e}^{G}_{K}
is defined in (
5.3
).

Similarly as the proof of Theorem
5.5
,
it remains to show the following analogy of (
5.5
):

(9.33)

\begin{split}&\lim_{t\to 0,\infty}\frac{\mathbf{j}^{\phi*}_{w^{-1}(\sigma)}\circ\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{K}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}(\gamma)}{\sqrt{\hat{e}^{(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}}_{K}\left(R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}-\operatorname{Lie}(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})\right)}\cdot\hat{e}^{(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}}_{K}\left(E_{+}^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-repl}}\right)\cdot w^{-1}(\sigma)^{*}(\mathsf{s}^{\prime})}\\
&\text{ exist for all $w\in W^{\sigma}\backslash W/W^{\phi}$.}\end{split}

Note that by the attracting condition, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to 0,\infty}\frac{1}{\hat{e}^{(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\sigma)}}_{K}\left(E_{+}^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-repl}}\right)}\;\text{ exist for all $w\in W^{\sigma}\backslash W/W^{\phi}$},

and by Theorem
5.3
, we have

\displaystyle\deg_{w^{-1}(\sigma)}\mathbf{j}^{\phi*}_{w^{-1}(\sigma)}\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}_{K}(\gamma)\subsetneq\frac{1}{2}\deg_{w^{-1}(\sigma)}e^{G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}}_{K}(R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}-\operatorname{Lie}(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})}))+\operatorname{weight}_{w^{-1}(\sigma)}\mathsf{s}^{\prime},

then (
9.33
) follows from the above two degree bounds.

This finishes the proof of Theorem
9.20
in the
K
-theory case.

9.6.
Hall v.s. stable envelopes

In this section we compare Hall envelopes with stable envelopes.

For cohomology, the following result is a consequence of Lemma
9.22
and Theorem
8.9
.

Proposition 9.23
.

In the setting of Theorem
9.20
, and assume that the bundle

\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in Q_{0}\\
\mathrm{s.t.}\,\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in},i}>\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out},i}\end{subarray}}\operatorname{Hom}(V_{i},\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in},i}-\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out},i}})

in (
9.32
) can be written as a direct sum of attracting and repelling subbundles in chamber
\mathfrak{C}
(as in Theorem
8.9
),
then cohomological stable envelope exists and

\displaystyle\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}=\mathrm{HallEnv}_{\mathfrak{C}}.

The
K
-theory counterpart of Proposition
9.23
is more complicated, as we do not have a
K
-theory analogy of Theorem
8.9
.

Proposition 9.24
.

In the setting of Theorem
9.20
, consider one of the following situations

(1)

the bundle
\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in Q_{0}\\
\mathrm{s.t.}\,\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in},i}>\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out},i}\end{subarray}}\operatorname{Hom}(V_{i},\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in},i}-\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out},i}})

in (
9.32
) is attracting in chamber
\mathfrak{C}
,

(2)

\mathsf{A}
is a minuscule framing torus, i.e.
\mathsf{A}\cong\mathbb{C}^{*}
which acts on framing vector space with weight
0
and
1
.

then for a generic slope
\mathsf{s}
,
K
-theoretic stable envelope exists and

\displaystyle\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}=\mathrm{HallEnv}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}.

Proof.

It is enough to show that
