\mathrm{HallEnv}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}
satisfies three axioms of stable envelopes. The support axiom (Definition
3.10
(i)) and the normalization axiom (Definition
3.10
(ii)) are checked in the same way as Theorem
5.5
and we shall not repeat here. As in (
9.32
), we denote

\displaystyle F=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in Q_{0}\\
\mathrm{s.t.}\,\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in},i}>\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out},i}\end{subarray}}\operatorname{Hom}(V_{i},\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in},i}-\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{out},i}}).

Similar as the proof of Theorem
5.5
, the degree axiom is examined by showing the following analogy of (
5.7
)

(9.34)

\begin{split}&\lim\frac{\phi^{*}(\mathsf{s}^{\prime})\cdot\mathbf{j}^{\phi*}_{\mathsf{A},w^{-1}({\phi^{\prime}})}\circ\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{K}^{\phi,\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}(\gamma)}{w^{-1}({\phi^{\prime}})^{*}(\mathsf{s}^{\prime})\cdot\sqrt{\hat{e}^{\mathsf{T}\times(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\phi^{\prime}(\mathsf{A}))}}_{K}\left(R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}_{\operatorname{in}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}-\operatorname{Lie}(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})\right)}}\times\frac{\hat{e}^{\mathsf{T}\times(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\phi^{\prime}(\mathsf{A}))}}_{K}\left(F^{\mathsf{A},w^{-1}(\phi^{\prime})\text{-repl}}\right)}{\hat{e}^{\mathsf{T}\times(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\phi^{\prime}(\mathsf{A}))}}_{K}\left(F^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-repl}}\right)}\\
&\text{exist for all $w\in W^{\phi^{\prime}}\backslash W/W^{\phi}$ and all directions of $\mathsf{a}\to\infty$,}\end{split}

where
\mathsf{s}^{\prime}
is as (
9.7
),
\phi^{\prime}\colon\mathsf{A}\to G
is a homomorphism such that
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi^{\prime}}\bigcap\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}^{f}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi})
is nonempty.

In situation (1), the repelling part of
F
is trivial by assumption, and (
9.34
) is the same as (
5.7
) which has been proven in Theorem
5.5
.

In situation (2), let us write decomposition of framing vector spaces into
\mathsf{A}
-weight spaces:

\displaystyle\underline{\mathbf{d}}=\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{(0)}\oplus\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{(1)},

where the superscripts denote the
\mathsf{A}
-weight
\in\{0,1\}
. Since
\mathsf{A}
-action is pseudo-symmetric by assumption, we have
\mathbf{d}^{(0)}_{\operatorname{in},i}\geqslant\mathbf{d}^{(0)}_{\operatorname{out},i}
and
\mathbf{d}^{(1)}_{\operatorname{in},i}\geqslant\mathbf{d}^{(1)}_{\operatorname{out},i}
for all
i\in Q_{0}
. We can write
\phi
and
\phi^{\prime}
fixed components as

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}=\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v}_{\phi}^{(0)},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{(0)})\times\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v}_{\phi}^{(1)},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{(1)}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi^{\prime}}=\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v}_{\phi^{\prime}}^{(0)},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{(0)})\times\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v}_{\phi^{\prime}}^{(1)},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{(1)}),

for certain decompositions
\mathbf{v}=\mathbf{v}^{(0)}_{\phi}+\mathbf{v}^{(1)}_{\phi}=\mathbf{v}^{(0)}_{\phi^{\prime}}+\mathbf{v}^{(1)}_{\phi^{\prime}}
. Then we have

\displaystyle F^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-repl}}=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in Q_{0}\\
\mathrm{s.t.}\,\mathbf{d}^{(0)}_{\operatorname{in},i}>\mathbf{d}^{(0)}_{\operatorname{out},i}\end{subarray}}\operatorname{Hom}(V^{(1)}_{\phi,i},\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{d}^{(0)}_{\operatorname{in},i}-\mathbf{d}^{(0)}_{\operatorname{out},i}}),\quad F^{\mathsf{A},\phi^{\prime}\text{-repl}}=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in Q_{0}\\
\mathrm{s.t.}\,\mathbf{d}^{(1)}_{\operatorname{in},i}>\mathbf{d}^{(1)}_{\operatorname{out},i}\end{subarray}}\operatorname{Hom}(V^{(1)}_{\phi^{\prime},i},\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{d}^{(1)}_{\operatorname{in},i}-\mathbf{d}^{(1)}_{\operatorname{out},i}}).

It follows that

\displaystyle\hat{e}^{\mathsf{T}\times(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\phi^{\prime}(\mathsf{A}))}}_{K}\left(F^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-repl}}\right)=O(\mathsf{a}^{\pm\frac{n}{2}}),\quad\hat{e}^{\mathsf{T}\times(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\phi^{\prime}(\mathsf{A}))}}_{K}\left(F^{\mathsf{A},w^{-1}(\phi^{\prime})\text{-repl}}\right)=O(\mathsf{a}^{\pm\frac{n^{\prime}}{2}})

as
\mathsf{a}^{\pm}\to\infty
, where

\displaystyle n=\sum_{i\in Q_{0}}\mathbf{v}_{\phi}^{(1)}\cdot(\mathbf{d}^{(0)}_{\operatorname{in},i}-\mathbf{d}^{(0)}_{\operatorname{out},i}),\quad n^{\prime}=\sum_{i\in Q_{0}}\mathbf{v}_{\phi^{\prime}}^{(1)}\cdot(\mathbf{d}^{(0)}_{\operatorname{in},i}-\mathbf{d}^{(0)}_{\operatorname{out},i}).

Note that
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi^{\prime}}\bigcap\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}^{f}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi})\neq\emptyset
implies
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi}\leq\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}})^{\mathsf{A},\phi^{\prime}}
in the ample partial order (Remark
3.9
), and the latter is equivalent to

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}_{\phi,i}^{(1)}\geqslant\mathbf{v}_{\phi^{\prime},i}^{(1)},\,\,\,\forall\,i\in Q_{0},

In particular,
n\geqslant n^{\prime}
, and it follows that

\displaystyle\lim_{\mathsf{a}^{\pm}\to\infty}\frac{\hat{e}^{\mathsf{T}\times(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\phi^{\prime}(\mathsf{A}))}}_{K}\left(F^{\mathsf{A},w^{-1}(\phi^{\prime})\text{-repl}}\right)}{\hat{e}^{\mathsf{T}\times(G^{\phi(\mathsf{A})})^{w^{-1}(\phi^{\prime}(\mathsf{A}))}}_{K}\left(F^{\mathsf{A},\phi\text{-repl}}\right)}\;\text{exist}.

Then (
9.34
) follows from (
5.7
).
∎

There are examples where Hall envelopes are not equal to stable envelopes and do not satisfy the triangle lemma.

Example 9.25
.

Consider the quiver
Q
consisting of a single node with no arrows, and take
\underline{\mathbf{d}}=(1,2)
. Let

\mathcal{M}(n\underline{\mathbf{d}})=\bigsqcup_{k}\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(k,n\underline{\mathbf{d}})

be the disjoint union of quiver varieties, where we fix
\theta=-1
. Note that
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(k,n\underline{\mathbf{d}})
is a vector bundle on
\operatorname{Gr}(k,n)
. Let
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
act on

\mathcal{M}(n\underline{\mathbf{d}})
by scaling the out-going framing with weight
\hbar^{-1}
, and
\mathsf{A}=\mathbb{C}^{*}_{a_{1}}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{a_{2}}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{a_{3}}
act on

X=\mathcal{M}(3\underline{\mathbf{d}}),

by assigning weights to the framings:
a_{1}\underline{\mathbf{d}}+a_{2}\underline{\mathbf{d}}+a_{3}\underline{\mathbf{d}}
. Set
\mathsf{T}=\mathsf{A}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\hbar}
. Then the equivariant
K
-theory of the
\mathsf{A}
-fixed loci is generated by the tensor product of structure sheaves:

\displaystyle K^{\mathsf{T}}(X^{\mathsf{A}})=\bigoplus_{(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})\in\{0,1\}^{3}}K_{\mathsf{T}}(\operatorname{pt})\cdot[\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(k_{1},\underline{\mathbf{d}})}\boxtimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(k_{2},\underline{\mathbf{d}})}\boxtimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(k_{3},\underline{\mathbf{d}})}]\>.

Fix a generic slope
\mathsf{s}\in\mathbb{R}\cong\operatorname{Pic}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(k,3\underline{\mathbf{d}}))\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{R}
.

Proposition 9.26
.

For
(X,\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A})
in the above example, we have:

(1)

There exists a chamber
\mathfrak{C}
of
\mathsf{A}
with a face
\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}
such that the triangle lemma (
9.8
) fails for the
K
-theoretic Hall envelope.

(2)

There exists a chamber
\mathfrak{C}
of
\mathsf{A}
such that
K
-theoretic stable envelope
\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}^{\mathsf{s}}
does not exist.

(3)

There exists a one-dimensional torus
\sigma:\mathbb{C}^{*}\to\mathsf{A}
such that
\operatorname{Stab}_{\sigma}^{\mathsf{s}}\neq\mathrm{HallEnv}_{\sigma}^{\mathsf{s}}
, where
\mathrm{HallEnv}_{\sigma}^{\mathsf{s}}
is the Hall envelope defined using the image of
\sigma
in the positive chamber.

Proof.

Suppose that the triangle lemma (
9.8
) always holds for any chamber
\mathfrak{C}
and face
\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}
, then by
[
COZZ3
]
, the following Yang-Baxter equation

(9.35)

\displaystyle R^{\mathsf{s}+\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(3)}_{+}}_{12}(a_{1}/a_{2})R^{\mathsf{s}+\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(2)}_{-}}_{13}(a_{1}/a_{3})R^{\mathsf{s}+\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(1)}_{+}}_{23}(a_{2}/a_{3})=R^{\mathsf{s}+\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(1)}_{-}}_{23}(a_{2}/a_{3})R^{\mathsf{s}+\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(2)}_{+}}_{13}(a_{1}/a_{3})R^{\mathsf{s}+\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(3)}_{-}}_{12}(a_{1}/a_{2})

should hold, where
\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(i)}_{+}=\frac{1-k_{i}}{2}
,
\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(i)}_{-}=\frac{k_{i}-2}{2}
, with
R
-matrix:

\displaystyle R^{\mathsf{s}}_{ij}(a_{i}/a_{j})=(\mathrm{HallEnv}_{a_{i}<a_{j}}^{\mathsf{s}})^{-1}\mathrm{HallEnv}_{a_{i}>a_{j}}^{\mathsf{s}}.

Denote
[ab]:=[\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(a,\underline{\mathbf{d}})}\boxtimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(b,\underline{\mathbf{d}})}]
. In the basis
[00],[01],[10],[11]
, the Hall envelopes can be explicitly computed using (
9.10
) and we get

(9.36)

\begin{split}\mathrm{HallEnv}_{a_{1}>a_{2}}^{\mathsf{s}}=\begin{pmatrix}1&&&\\
&1-a_{1}/a_{2}&(a_{2}/a_{1})^{\lfloor\mathsf{s}-1/2\rfloor}(1-\hbar)^{2}&\\
&0&(1-\hbar a_{1}/a_{2})^{2}&\\
&&&(1-\hbar a_{1}/a_{2})^{2}\end{pmatrix},\\
\mathrm{HallEnv}_{a_{1}<a_{2}}^{\mathsf{s}}=\begin{pmatrix}1&&&\\
&(1-\hbar a_{2}/a_{1})^{2}&0&\\
&(a_{1}/a_{2})^{\lfloor\mathsf{s}-1/2\rfloor}(1-\hbar)^{2}&1-a_{2}/a_{1}&\\
&&&(1-\hbar a_{2}/a_{1})^{2}\end{pmatrix}.\end{split}

It is a straightforward to check that
R^{\mathsf{s}}_{ij}
does not satisfy the Yang-Baxter equation (
9.35
), and this proves (1).

We notice that the Hall envelopes (
9.36
) satisfy the axioms in Definition
3.10
therefore we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{HallEnv}_{a_{1}\gtrless a_{2}}^{\mathsf{s}}=\operatorname{Stab}_{a_{1}\gtrless a_{2}}^{\mathsf{s}}.

In particular,
R
-matrix is given by
R^{\mathsf{s}}_{ij}(a_{i}/a_{j})=(\operatorname{Stab}_{a_{i}<a_{j}}^{\mathsf{s}})^{-1}\operatorname{Stab}_{a_{i}>a_{j}}^{\mathsf{s}}
. The failure of Yang-Baxter equation implies that there exists a chamber
\mathfrak{C}
of
\mathsf{A}
such that
\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}^{\mathsf{s}}
does not exist (otherwise the triangle lemma automatically holds by Lemma
3.37
and YBE should also hold). This proves (2).

Finally, suppose that
\operatorname{Stab}_{\sigma}^{\mathsf{s}}=\mathrm{HallEnv}_{\sigma}^{\mathsf{s}}
for every one-dimensional torus
\sigma:\mathbb{C}^{*}\to\mathsf{A}
. Suppose that
\mathfrak{C}=\{a_{i}>a_{j}>a_{k}\}
and
\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}=\{a_{i}=a_{j}>a_{k}\}
are a pair of chambers and faces such that triangle lemma fails for the
K
-theoretic Hall envelope as in (1). According to the proof of Lemma
3.37
, there exists an one-dimensional torus
\xi:\mathbb{C}^{*}\to\mathsf{A}
in the chamber
\mathfrak{C}
, such that

\displaystyle\operatorname{Stab}_{\xi}^{\mathsf{s}}=\operatorname{Stab}_{a_{i}=a_{j}>a_{k}}^{\mathsf{s}}\circ\operatorname{Stab}_{a_{i}>a_{j}}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}.

We have
\operatorname{Stab}_{a_{i}=a_{j}>a_{k}}^{\mathsf{s}}=\mathrm{HallEnv}_{a_{i}=a_{j}>a_{k}}^{\mathsf{s}}
since they are given by one-dimensional torus.
We also have

\operatorname{Stab}_{a_{i}>a_{j}}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}=\mathrm{HallEnv}_{a_{i}>a_{j}}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}

by the above explicit computation. Note that the construction of Hall envelope can be replaced by an arbitrary one-dimensional torus in the chamber
\mathfrak{C}
, and result remains the same, in particular
\mathrm{HallEnv}_{\mathfrak{C}}^{\mathsf{s}}=\mathrm{HallEnv}_{\xi}^{\mathsf{s}}
. Thus we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{HallEnv}_{\mathfrak{C}}^{\mathsf{s}}=\mathrm{HallEnv}_{\xi}^{\mathsf{s}}=\operatorname{Stab}_{\xi}^{\mathsf{s}}=\mathrm{HallEnv}_{a_{i}=a_{j}>a_{k}}^{\mathsf{s}}\circ\mathrm{HallEnv}_{a_{i}>a_{j}}^{\mathsf{s}^{\prime}}.

This contracts with the choice of
\mathfrak{C}
and
\mathfrak{C}^{\prime}
. This prove (3).
∎

Appendix A
Pullback isomorphisms along attraction maps

In this section, we take
(Y,\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathsf{w})
as in Setting
2.1
. Assume moreover that
F=Y^{\mathsf{A}}
is connected, and
X=\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F)
. Consider the attraction map

\displaystyle f\colon X\to F,\quad x\mapsto\lim_{t\to 0}\sigma(t)\cdot x\quad\mathrm{for}\,\,\mathrm{a}\,\,\sigma\in\mathfrak{C}.

By
[
BB
]
,
f
is an affine fibration and does not depend on the choice of
\sigma
. Since
\mathsf{w}
is
\mathsf{A}
-invariant, we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{w}=f^{*}(\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}}),

so there are smooth pullbacks

\displaystyle f^{*}\colon H^{\mathsf{T}}(F,\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\to H^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w}),\quad f^{*}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(F,\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w}).

By (
2.12
), the first map is an isomorphism. As we do not find a reference on the comparison of the second map, we show below the following.

Proposition A.1
.

In the above situation,
f^{*}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(F,\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

By
[
Hal
, Amplification 3.18]
, we have a SOD for the derived category of coherent sheaves:

\displaystyle\operatorname{D}^{b}([X/\mathsf{T}])=\left\langle\cdots,f^{*}\operatorname{D}^{b}([F/\mathsf{T}])_{-1},f^{*}\operatorname{D}^{b}([F/\mathsf{T}])_{0},f^{*}\operatorname{D}^{b}([F/\mathsf{T}])_{1},\cdots\right\rangle,

where

\left\langle\cdots,\operatorname{D}^{b}([F/\mathsf{T}])_{-1},\operatorname{D}^{b}([F/\mathsf{T}])_{0},\operatorname{D}^{b}([F/\mathsf{T}])_{1},\cdots\right\rangle

is an SOD of
\operatorname{D}^{b}([F/\mathsf{T}])
, and
f^{*}
maps
\operatorname{D}^{b}([F/\mathsf{T}])_{v}
fully faithfully onto its image in
\operatorname{D}^{b}([X/\mathsf{T}])
for all
v\in\mathbb{Z}
. Here
\operatorname{D}^{b}([F/\mathsf{T}])_{v}
is the full subcategory of
\operatorname{D}^{b}([F/\mathsf{T}])
whose objects are complexes with
\sigma
-weight equals to
v
, where
\sigma
is a fixed cocharacter in
\mathfrak{C}
.

This induces an SOD for the matrix factorization category (e.g.
[
P
, Prop. 2.1]
):

\displaystyle\mathrm{MF}([X/\mathsf{T}],\mathsf{w})=\left\langle\cdots,f^{*}\mathrm{MF}\left([F/\mathsf{T}],\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}}\right)_{-1},f^{*}\mathrm{MF}\left([F/\mathsf{T}],\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}}\right)_{0},f^{*}\mathrm{MF}\left([F/\mathsf{T}],\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}}\right)_{1},\cdots\right\rangle,

where

\left\langle\cdots,\mathrm{MF}\left([F/\mathsf{T}],\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}}\right)_{-1},\mathrm{MF}\left([F/\mathsf{T}],\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}}\right)_{0},\mathrm{MF}\left([F/\mathsf{T}],\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}}\right)_{1},\cdots\right\rangle

is an SOD of
\mathrm{MF}\left([F/\mathsf{T}],\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}}\right)
, and
f^{*}
maps
\mathrm{MF}\left([F/\mathsf{T}],\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}}\right)_{v}
fully faithfully onto its image in
\mathrm{MF}\left([X/\mathsf{T}],\mathsf{w}\right)
for all
v\in\mathbb{Z}
. By passing to the Grothendieck group, we get isomorphism
f^{*}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(F,\mathsf{w}^{\mathsf{A}})\cong K^{\mathsf{T}}(X,\mathsf{w})
.
∎

Appendix B
Excess intersection formula of critical cohomology and
K
-theory

Consider the following Cartesian diagram of smooth and connected varieties:

(B.1)

\textstyle{X^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{h}
\scriptstyle{\Box}
\textstyle{Y^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{Y,}

where
f
is a closed immersion.
The natural map
N_{f^{\prime}}\to h^{*}N_{f}
between normal bundles is an embedding of vector bundles. We define the
excess bundle
:

E:=h^{*}N_{f}/N_{f^{\prime}}.

Suppose that
\mathsf{w}\colon Y\to\mathbb{C}
is a regular function on
Y
. We have proper pushforward

f_{*}\colon H(X,\varphi_{\mathsf{w}\circ f}\omega_{X})\to H(Y,\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\omega_{Y}),\quad f^{\prime}_{*}\colon H(X^{\prime},\varphi_{\mathsf{w}\circ f\circ h}\omega_{X^{\prime}})\to H(Y^{\prime},\varphi_{\mathsf{w}\circ g}\omega_{Y^{\prime}}),

and l.c.i. pullback

g^{*}\colon H(Y,\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\omega_{Y})\to H(Y^{\prime},\varphi_{\mathsf{w}\circ g}\omega_{Y^{\prime}}[-2d_{g}]),\quad h^{*}\colon H(X,\varphi_{\mathsf{w}\circ f}\omega_{X})\to H(X^{\prime},\varphi_{\mathsf{w}\circ f\circ h}\omega_{X^{\prime}}[-2d_{h}]).

Here
d_{g}=\dim Y^{\prime}-\dim Y
and
d_{h}=\dim X^{\prime}-\dim X
.

Proposition B.1

(Excess intersection formula for critical cohomology)
.

Notations as above, we have

\displaystyle g^{*}\circ f_{*}=f^{\prime}_{*}\circ e(E)\cdot h^{*},

where
e(E)
is the Euler class of the excess bundle
E
.

Proof.

Note that
g=\operatorname{pr}_{Y}\circ\Gamma_{g}
, where
\Gamma_{g}\colon Y^{\prime}\to Y^{\prime}\times Y
is the graph of
g
and
\operatorname{pr}_{Y}\colon Y^{\prime}\times Y\to Y
is the projection to the second component.
They fit into the following Cartesian diagram:

(B.2)

\textstyle{X^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}\times h}
\scriptstyle{\Box}
\textstyle{Y^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Gamma_{g}}
\textstyle{Y^{\prime}\times X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{1_{Y^{\prime}}\times f}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{pr}_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\Box}
\textstyle{Y^{\prime}\times Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{pr}_{Y}}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{Y.}

In particular, we have compatibility of proper pushforward and flat pullback:

\displaystyle\operatorname{pr}_{Y}^{*}\circ f_{*}=(1_{Y^{\prime}}\times f)_{*}\circ\operatorname{pr}_{X}^{*}.

Thom-Sebastiani isomorphism gives

H(Y^{\prime}\times Y,\varphi_{\mathsf{w}\circ\operatorname{pr}_{Y}}\omega_{Y^{\prime}\times Y})\cong H(Y^{\prime},\omega_{Y^{\prime}})\otimes H(Y,\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\omega_{Y}).

Note that
\operatorname{pr}_{Y}^{*}\colon H(Y,\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\omega_{Y})\to H(Y^{\prime}\times Y,\varphi_{\mathsf{w}\circ\operatorname{pr}_{Y}}\omega_{Y^{\prime}\times Y})
equals to
\gamma\mapsto[Y^{\prime}]\otimes\gamma
, where
[Y^{\prime}]\in H^{-2\dim_{Y^{\prime}}}(Y^{\prime},\omega_{Y^{\prime}})
is the fundamental cycle, and similarly
\operatorname{pr}_{X}^{*}(-)=[Y^{\prime}]\otimes(-)
.

Suppose that the proposition holds for the upper square of the above diagram, i.e.

\Gamma_{g}^{*}\circ(1_{Y^{\prime}}\times f)_{*}=f^{\prime}_{*}\circ e(E)\cdot(f^{\prime}\times h)^{*},

then we have

\displaystyle g^{*}\circ f_{*}=\Gamma_{g}^{*}\circ\operatorname{pr}_{Y}^{*}\circ f_{*}=\Gamma_{g}^{*}\circ(1_{Y^{\prime}}\times f)_{*}\circ\operatorname{pr}_{X}^{*}=f^{\prime}_{*}\circ e(E)\cdot(f^{\prime}\times h)^{*}\circ\operatorname{pr}_{X}^{*}=f^{\prime}_{*}\circ e(E)\cdot h^{*},

where we use
N_{1_{Y^{\prime}}\times f}\cong\operatorname{pr}_{X}^{*}N_{f}
in the third equality.
So it is enough to show the claim for the upper square. As
\Gamma_{g}
is a closed immersion, so we are left to show the proposition holds for (
B.1
)
when
g
is a closed immersion.

Applying the composition of adjunctions
f_{*}f^{!}\to\mathrm{id}
,
\mathrm{id}\to g_{*}g^{*}
to
\omega_{Y}
(here these are operations on complexes of sheaves), we obtain
a morphism in the derived category
D^{b}_{c}(Y)
of constructible complexes:

(B.3)

f_{*}\omega_{X}\to g_{*}\omega_{Y^{\prime}}[-2d_{g}],

where we use
g^{*}\omega_{Y}\cong\omega_{Y^{\prime}}[-2d_{g}]
. Since
f_{*}\omega_{X}
is supported on
X
, the map (
B.3
) factors as

\displaystyle f_{*}\omega_{X}\to f_{*}f^{!}g_{*}\omega_{Y^{\prime}}[-2d_{g}]\to g_{*}\omega_{Y^{\prime}}[-2d_{g}],

where
f_{*}\omega_{X}\to f_{*}f^{!}g_{*}\omega_{Y^{\prime}}[-2d_{g}]
is obtained by applying
f_{*}
to map

c:\omega_{X}\to h_{*}\omega_{X^{\prime}}[-2d_{g}]\cong h_{*}f^{\prime!}\omega_{Y^{\prime}}[-2d_{g}]\cong f^{!}g_{*}\omega_{Y^{\prime}}[-2d_{g}].

Using the adjoint pair
(h^{*},h_{*})
, the map
c
factors as

\omega_{X}\to h_{*}h^{*}\omega_{X}\xrightarrow{h_{*}(\tilde{c})}h_{*}\omega_{X^{\prime}}[-2d_{g}],

where
\tilde{c}:h^{*}\omega_{X}\to\omega_{X^{\prime}}[-2d_{g}]
is given by applying the natural transformation

(B.4)

h^{*}f^{!}\to f^{\prime!}g^{*}

to
\omega_{Y}
. Here the transformation is obtained by the adjunction of the map
f^{!}\to f^{!}g_{*}g^{*}\cong h_{*}f^{\prime!}g^{*}
.

Using smoothness, we have
\omega_{X}=\mathbb{Q}_{X}[2\dim X]
,
\omega_{X^{\prime}}=\mathbb{Q}_{X^{\prime}}[2\dim X^{\prime}]
, so
the map
\tilde{c}
can be identified with multiplication by an element
\alpha\in H^{2d_{h}-2d_{g}}(X^{\prime})
.

In summary, we obtain the following commutative diagram in
D^{b}_{c}(Y)
:

(B.5)

\textstyle{\omega_{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{f_{*}\omega_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{f_{*}f^{!}g_{*}\omega_{Y^{\prime}}[-2d_{g}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{g_{*}\omega_{Y^{\prime}}[-2d_{g}]}
\textstyle{f_{*}h_{*}h^{*}\omega_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{*}h_{*}\alpha\cdot\hskip 8.19447pt\,\,}
\textstyle{f_{*}h_{*}\omega_{X^{\prime}}[-2d_{g}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{g_{*}f^{\prime}_{*}\omega_{X^{\prime}}[-2d_{g}].\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

We note that
g^{*}\circ f_{*}
(here
g^{*}
denotes the Gysin pullback) is induced by applying
\mathbf{R}\Gamma(Y,-)
to the path

\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}f_{*}\omega_{X}\to\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\omega_{Y}\to\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}g_{*}\omega_{Y^{\prime}}[-2d_{g}],

followed by natural transformation

(B.6)

\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}g_{*}\omega_{Y^{\prime}}[-2d_{g}]\xrightarrow{\cong}g_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}\circ g}\omega_{Y^{\prime}}[-2d_{g}],

and
f^{\prime}_{*}\circ\alpha\cdot h^{*}
(here
h^{*}
denotes the Gysin pullback) is induced by applying
\mathbf{R}\Gamma(Y,-)
to the path

\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}f_{*}\omega_{X}\to\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}f_{*}h_{*}h^{*}\omega_{X}\to\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}f_{*}h_{*}\omega_{X^{\prime}}[-2d_{g}]\xrightarrow{\cong}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}g_{*}f^{\prime}_{*}\omega_{X^{\prime}}[-2d_{g}]\to\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}g_{*}\omega_{Y^{\prime}}[-2d_{g}],

followed by the same transformation (
B.6
).
Therefore we get the equation

\displaystyle g^{*}\circ f_{*}=f^{\prime}_{*}\circ\alpha\cdot h^{*}.

It remains to show that
\alpha=e(E)
.
Recall that the ‘restriction with supports’ (ref.
[
CG
, §8.3.21]
):

f^{!}\colon H^{\mathrm{BM}}_{*}(Y^{\prime})\to H^{\mathrm{BM}}_{*+2d_{f}}(X^{\prime}),

which is defined by applying
\mathbf{R}\Gamma(Y^{\prime},-)
to the map

\omega_{Y^{\prime}}\cong g^{!}\omega_{Y}\to g^{!}f_{*}f^{*}\omega_{Y}\cong f^{\prime}_{*}h^{!}\omega_{X}[-2d_{f}]\cong f^{\prime}_{*}\omega_{X^{\prime}}[-2d_{f}].

The map
\omega_{Y^{\prime}}\to f^{\prime}_{*}\omega_{X^{\prime}}[-2d_{f}]
factors into

\omega_{Y^{\prime}}\to f^{\prime}_{*}f^{\prime*}\omega_{Y^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{f^{\prime}_{*}(c^{\prime})}f^{\prime}_{*}\omega_{X^{\prime}}[-2d_{f}],

where
c^{\prime}
is the natural transformation
f^{\prime*}g^{!}\omega_{Y}\to h^{!}f^{*}\omega_{Y}
, which is given by applying
(
B.4
) to
\mathbb{Q}_{Y}
and then applying Verdier duality functor
\mathbb{D}_{X^{\prime}}
.
Since
c^{\prime}
is obtained by
\widetilde{c}
by applying Verdier duality functor
\mathbb{D}_{X^{\prime}}
,
c^{\prime}
can also be identified with multiplication by
\alpha
.
Then we get an equation on maps
between Borel-Moore homologies:

(B.7)

\displaystyle f^{!}=\alpha\cdot f^{\prime*}\colon H^{\mathrm{BM}}_{*}(Y^{\prime})\to H^{\mathrm{BM}}_{*+2d_{f}}(X^{\prime}),

where
f^{\prime*}\colon H^{\mathrm{BM}}_{*}(Y^{\prime})\to H^{\mathrm{BM}}_{*+2d_{f^{\prime}}}(X^{\prime})
is the Gysin pullback.

Next we determine
\alpha
by using the excess intersection formula in Chow homology and the cycle map.
In Chow homology, we have
[
Ful
, Thm. 6.3]
:

(B.8)

\displaystyle f^{!}=e(E)\cap f^{\prime*}\colon A_{*}(Y^{\prime})\to A_{*+d_{f}}(X^{\prime}),

where
A_{*}(-)
is the Chow homology,
f^{!}
is the refined Gysin pullback, and
f^{\prime*}
is the Gysin pullback. It is known that the ‘restriction with supports’ is compatible with refined Gysin map
[
CG
, §8.3.21, §2.6.21]
, i.e.

\displaystyle f^{!}_{\mathrm{BM}}\circ\mathrm{cyc}=\mathrm{cyc}\circ f^{!}_{\mathrm{CH}}\colon A_{*}(Y^{\prime})\to H^{\mathrm{BM}}_{2*+2d_{f}}(X^{\prime}),

where
\operatorname{cyc}\colon A_{*}\to H^{\mathrm{BM}}_{2*}
is the cycle map, and
f^{!}_{\mathrm{BM}},f^{!}_{\mathrm{CH}}
denote Gysin pullbacks in BM and Chow homologies.

Since Gysin pullback is a special case of ‘restriction with supports’, we also have

(B.9)

f^{\prime!}_{\mathrm{BM}}\circ\mathrm{cyc}=\mathrm{cyc}\circ f^{\prime!}_{\mathrm{CH}}.

Let
[Y^{\prime}]_{\mathrm{CH}}
(
[Y^{\prime}]_{\mathrm{BM}}
) be the fundamental cycle in BM homology (Chow homology) respectively, then

(B.10)

\operatorname{cyc}([Y^{\prime}]_{\mathrm{CH}})=[Y^{\prime}]_{\mathrm{BM}}.

Combining (
B.7
), (
B.8
), (
B.9
), (
B.10
), we obtain

\displaystyle\alpha\cdot[X^{\prime}]_{\mathrm{BM}}=\alpha\cdot f^{\prime!}_{\mathrm{BM}}([Y^{\prime}]_{\mathrm{BM}})=f^{!}_{\mathrm{BM}}\circ\operatorname{cyc}([Y^{\prime}]_{\mathrm{CH}})=\operatorname{cyc}\circ f^{!}_{\mathrm{CH}}([Y^{\prime}]_{\mathrm{CH}})=\operatorname{cyc}(e(E)\cap[X^{\prime}]_{\mathrm{CH}})=e(E)\cdot[X^{\prime}]_{\mathrm{BM}}.

By Poincaré duality, the map
H^{*}(X^{\prime})\ni\beta\mapsto\beta\cdot[X^{\prime}]_{\mathrm{BM}}\in H^{\mathrm{BM}}_{2\dim X^{\prime}-*}(X^{\prime})
is an isomorphism; thus
\alpha=e(E)
.
∎

Remark B.2

(Equivariant version of Proposition
B.1
)
.

Suppose that the maps in the diagram (
B.1
) are equivariant morphisms between
G
-varieties, where
G
is an algebraic group, and assume that
\mathsf{w}
is
G
-invariant. Then we claim that the equivariant version of Proposition
B.1
holds, i.e.

(B.11)

\displaystyle g^{*}\circ f_{*}=f^{\prime}_{*}\circ e^{G}(E)\cdot h^{*},

where
e^{G}(E)
is the
G
-equivariant Euler class of excess bundle
E
. To prove this claim, we consider the
n
-acyclic free
G
-variety
M_{n}
as in the
[
BL
, §3.1]
(see also
[
Dav1
, §2.4]
), and for every
G
-variety
V
we define

V_{n}:=(V\times M_{n})/G.

Then we get the following Cartesian diagram of smooth connected varieties by applying
(-\times M_{n})/G
to (
B.1
)

\textstyle{X^{\prime}_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{n}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{h_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\Box}
\textstyle{Y_{n}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{n}}
\textstyle{X_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{n}}
\textstyle{Y_{n}}

By Proposition
B.1
we have

(B.12)

\displaystyle g_{n}^{*}\circ f_{n*}=f^{\prime}_{n*}\circ e(E_{n})\cdot h_{n}^{*},

where

E_{n}=h_{n}^{*}N_{f_{n}}/N_{f^{\prime}_{n}}\cong(E\times M_{n})/G

is the excess bundle. It is known that there exists
G
-equivariant closed embedding
M_{n}\hookrightarrow M_{n+1}
. As in
[
Dav1
, §2.4]
, the equivariant critical cohomology is isomorphic to the inductive limit, i.e.

\displaystyle H_{G}(Y,\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\,\omega_{Y})\cong\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}\longrightarrow\\
n\to\infty\end{subarray}}H(Y_{n},\varphi_{\mathsf{w}_{n}}\omega_{Y_{n}}),

where
\mathsf{w}_{n}
is the descent of
\mathsf{w}\circ\operatorname{pr}_{Y}\colon Y\times M_{n}\to\mathbb{C}
from
Y\times M_{n}
to
Y_{n}
since
\mathsf{w}
is
G
-invariant. And the functors between equivariant critical
cohomologies are inductive limit of their finite counterparts, e.g.

\displaystyle f_{*}=\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}\longrightarrow\\
n\to\infty\end{subarray}}f_{n*},\quad g^{*}=\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}\longrightarrow\\
n\to\infty\end{subarray}}g_{n}^{*},\quad e^{G}(E)\cdot=\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}\longrightarrow\\
n\to\infty\end{subarray}}e(E_{n})\cdot.

Then (
B.11
) follows from the inductive limit of (
B.12
).

There is also a
K
-theoretic counterpart of Proposition
B.1
. Given diagram (
B.1
), we have proper pushforward

f_{*}\colon K(X,\mathsf{w}\circ f)\to K(Y,\mathsf{w}),\quad f^{\prime}_{*}\colon K(X^{\prime},\mathsf{w}\circ f\circ h)\to K(Y^{\prime},\mathsf{w}\circ g),

and l.c.i. pullback

g^{*}\colon K(Y,\mathsf{w})\to K(Y^{\prime},\mathsf{w}\circ g),\quad h^{*}\colon K(X,\mathsf{w}\circ f)\to K(X^{\prime},\mathsf{w}\circ f\circ h).

Proposition B.3

(Excess intersection formula for critical
K
-theory)
.

Notations as above, we have

\displaystyle g^{*}\circ f_{*}=f^{\prime}_{*}\circ e_{K}(E)\cdot h^{*},

where
e_{K}(E):=\bigwedge^{*}E^{\vee}
is the
K
-theoretic Euler class of the excess bundle
E
.

Proof.

As in the proof of Proposition
B.1
, by the graph construction (
B.2
), we may assume
g
is a closed immersion.

We have a commutative diagram of derived schemes

(B.13)

\textstyle{X^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i\,\,\,}
\scriptstyle{h}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}}
\textstyle{X^{\prime\mathrm{der}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\,\,\,\,f^{\prime\mathrm{der}}}
\scriptstyle{h^{\mathrm{der}}}
\textstyle{Y^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{Y,}

where the middle square is the homotopy pullback diagram.
We denote
Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{Y}:=Z^{\mathrm{der}}(\mathsf{w})\hookrightarrow Y
to be the derived zero locus of
\mathsf{w}
and similarly
Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{X}:=Z^{\mathrm{der}}(\mathsf{w}\circ f)\hookrightarrow X
,
Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{Y^{\prime}}
,
Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{X^{\prime}}
for the derived zero loci of pullback functions. They fit into commutative diagram of derived schemes (here we use same notations for maps as in the previous diagram):

(B.14)

\textstyle{Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{X^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i\,\,\,}
\scriptstyle{h}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}}
\textstyle{Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{X^{\prime\mathrm{der}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\,\,\,\,f^{\prime\mathrm{der}}}
\scriptstyle{h^{\mathrm{der}}}
\textstyle{Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{Y^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{Y}.}

As the canonical map commutes with proper pushforward and Gysin pullback, we have commutative diagrams

\textstyle{K(Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g^{*}\circ f_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{can}}
\textstyle{K(Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{Y^{\prime}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{can}}
\textstyle{K(X,\mathsf{w}\circ f)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g^{*}\circ f_{*}}
\textstyle{K(Y^{\prime},\mathsf{w}\circ g),}

\textstyle{K(Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}_{*}\circ e_{K}(E)\cdot h^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{can}}
\textstyle{K(Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{Y^{\prime}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{can}}
\textstyle{K(X,\mathsf{w}\circ f)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}_{*}\circ e_{K}(E)\cdot h^{*}}
\textstyle{K(Y^{\prime},\mathsf{w}\circ g).}

Then the claimed equality follows from Lemma
B.4
below.
∎

Lemma B.4
.

When
g\colon Y^{\prime}\to Y
above is a closed immersion, we have an equality of maps

g^{*}\circ f_{*}=f^{\prime}_{*}\circ e_{K}(E)\cdot h^{*}\colon K(Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{X})\to K(Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{Y^{\prime}}).

Proof.

For a Noetherian derived scheme
Z^{\mathrm{der}}
with inclusion of its classical truncation

i\colon Z:=t_{0}(Z^{\mathrm{der}})\to Z^{\mathrm{der}},

and
\mathcal{F}\in\operatorname{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{coh}}(Z^{\mathrm{der}})
, we have

\sum_{p}(-1)^{p}i_{*}[\mathcal{H}^{p}(\mathcal{F})]=[\mathcal{F}]\in K(Z^{\mathrm{der}}).

Here by the nil-invariance of
G
-theory
[
Kh
, Cor. 3.4]
, we have

(B.15)

i_{*}\colon K(Z)\cong K(Z^{\mathrm{der}}).

By base change in diagram (
B.14
), for any
\mathcal{F}\in\operatorname{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{coh}}(Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{X})
, we have equalities in
K
-theory:

(B.16)

\displaystyle[g^{*}f_{*}\mathcal{F}]=[\textbf{L}g^{*}\circ\mathbf{R}f_{*}(\mathcal{F})]=[\mathbf{R}f^{\prime\mathrm{der}}_{*}\circ\textbf{L}h^{\mathrm{der}*}(\mathcal{F})]=\sum_{n}(-1)^{n}[(t_{0}(f^{\prime}))_{*}\mathcal{H}^{n}(\textbf{L}h^{\mathrm{der}*}\mathcal{F})]\in K(Z_{Y^{\prime}}).

Here
t_{0}(f^{\prime})\colon Z_{X^{\prime}}:=t_{0}\left(Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{X^{\prime}}\right)\to Z_{Y^{\prime}}:=t_{0}\left(Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{Y^{\prime}}\right)
denotes the classical truncation of
f^{\prime}
,
and we have used the fact that classical truncation of
i
is an isomorphism, and we identify the
G
-theory of
Z_{Y^{\prime}}
and
Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{Y^{\prime}}
by (
B.15
).

Using diagrams (
B.13
), (
B.14
), we have

(B.17)

\displaystyle\textbf{L}h^{\mathrm{der}*}\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}\otimes^{\textbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{Z_{X}^{\mathrm{der}}}}\mathcal{O}_{Z_{X^{\prime\mathrm{der}}}^{\mathrm{der}}}=\mathcal{F}\otimes^{\textbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime\mathrm{der}}}.

Here in the first equality we omit
h^{\mathrm{der}}_{*}
since
h^{\mathrm{der}}
is an affine morphism so we can identify
\mathcal{O}_{Z_{X^{\prime\mathrm{der}}}^{\mathrm{der}}}
with its direct image in
Z_{X}^{\mathrm{der}}
and identify
\operatorname{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{coh}}\left(Z_{X^{\prime\mathrm{der}}}^{\mathrm{der}}\right)
with
\operatorname{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{coh}}\left(h^{\mathrm{der}}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Z_{X^{\prime\mathrm{der}}}^{\mathrm{der}}}\right)
. In the second equality, we use the identification

\mathcal{O}_{Z_{X}^{\mathrm{der}}}\otimes^{\textbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime\mathrm{der}}}\cong\mathcal{O}_{Z_{X^{\prime\mathrm{der}}}^{\mathrm{der}}},

and we regard
\mathcal{F}\otimes^{\textbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime\mathrm{der}}}
as an object in

\operatorname{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{coh}}\left(h^{\mathrm{der}}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Z_{X^{\prime\mathrm{der}}}^{\mathrm{der}}}\right)
.

By a direct calculation, cohomology sheaves of
\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime\mathrm{der}}}
(viewed as
\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}
-modules) are given by

(B.18)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{i}(\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime\mathrm{der}}})=\begin{cases}{\bigwedge}_{\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}}^{-i}(E^{\vee}),&\text{if}-\operatorname{rk}E\leqslant i\leqslant 0,\\
\quad\quad 0,&\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

Note also the spectral sequence

(B.19)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{q}(\mathcal{F}\otimes^{\textbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{H}^{p}(\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime\mathrm{der}}}))\Longrightarrow\mathcal{H}^{p+q}(\mathcal{F}\otimes^{\textbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime\mathrm{der}}}),

where

(B.20)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\otimes^{\textbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{H}^{p}(\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime\mathrm{der}}})

\displaystyle\cong\mathcal{F}\otimes^{\textbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\wedge_{\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}}^{-p}E^{\vee}

\displaystyle\cong\mathcal{F}\otimes^{\textbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}\otimes^{\textbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}}\wedge_{\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}}^{-p}E^{\vee}

\displaystyle\cong\textbf{L}h^{*}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{\textbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}}\wedge_{\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}}^{-p}E^{\vee}

\displaystyle\cong\textbf{L}h^{*}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{\textbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{X^{\prime}}}}\wedge_{\mathcal{O}_{Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{X^{\prime}}}}^{-p}E^{\vee}.

Combining (
B.16
), (
B.17
), (
B.18
), (
B.19
), (
B.20
), we obtain

\displaystyle[g^{*}f_{*}\mathcal{F}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{p,q\in\mathbb{Z}}(-1)^{p+q}\left[t_{0}(f^{\prime})_{*}\mathcal{H}^{q}\left(\textbf{L}h^{*}\mathcal{F}\otimes^{\textbf{L}}_{\mathcal{O}_{Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{X^{\prime}}}}\wedge_{\mathcal{O}_{Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{X^{\prime}}}}^{-p}E^{\vee}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{p,q\in\mathbb{Z}}(-1)^{p+q}\left[t_{0}(f^{\prime})_{*}\left(\mathcal{H}^{q}\left(\textbf{L}h^{*}\mathcal{F}\right)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{X^{\prime}}}}\wedge_{\mathcal{O}_{Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{X^{\prime}}}}^{-p}E^{\vee}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\left[\mathbf{R}f^{\prime}_{*}\left(\left(\textbf{L}h^{*}\mathcal{F}\right)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{X^{\prime}}}}\wedge_{\mathcal{O}_{Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{X^{\prime}}}}^{\bullet}E^{\vee}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=f^{\prime}_{*}(e_{K}(E)\cdot h^{*}(\mathcal{F})),

where the third equality uses (
B.15
).
∎

Remark B.5

(Equivariant version of Proposition
B.3
)
.

Suppose that the maps in the diagram (
B.1
) are equivariant morphisms between
G
-varieties, where
G
is an algebraic group, and assume that
\mathsf{w}
is
G
-invariant. Then the equivariant version of Proposition
B.3
holds, i.e.

(B.21)

\displaystyle g^{*}\circ f_{*}=f^{\prime}_{*}\circ e^{G}_{K}(E)\cdot h^{*},

where
e^{G}_{K}(E):=\bigwedge^{*}E^{\vee}
is the
G
-equivariant
K
-theoretic Euler class of excess bundle
E
. This is because the spectral sequence in the proof of Proposition
B.3
is
G
-equivariant if
\mathcal{F}
is
G
-equivariant.

Remark B.6
.

In the case when
X^{\prime}
is disconnected, the Propositions
B.1
and
B.3
and Remarks
B.2
and
B.5
still hold. In this case,
e(E)\cdot
is the component-wise multiplication.

Appendix C
A deformed dimensional reduction for critical cohomology

Let
Y
be a smooth and connected variety and
f_{1},\ldots,f_{n},\phi
are regular functions on
Y
. Consider the product and a function on it:

X:=Y\times\mathbb{A}^{n},\quad\mathsf{w}:=\phi+\sum_{i=1}^{n}f_{i}x_{i},

where we omit the pullback of functions from
Y
to
X
,

\{x_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
are linear coordinates of
\mathbb{A}^{n}
. Denote the common zero locus of
f_{1},\ldots,f_{n}
by

Z=Z(\{f_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}).

Let
\pi\colon X\to Y
be the projection, and
j\colon S:=\pi^{-1}(Z)\hookrightarrow X
the inclusion. We have natural morphism in
\operatorname{D}^{b}_{c}(Y)
:

(C.1)

\displaystyle\pi_{*}j_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}|_{S}}j^{!}\omega_{X}\longrightarrow\pi_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\omega_{X}\>.

Theorem C.1
.

Assume that
Z
is smooth of codimension
n
in
Y
, then (
C.1
) is an isomorphism.

We first prove the case when
n=1
.

Lemma C.2
.

Theorem
C.1
is true when
n=1
.

Proof.

We write
f:=f_{1}
,
\mathsf{w}=\phi+fx
. Since
\pi_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\omega_{X}
is supported on
\pi(\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}))\subseteq Z
and
\pi_{*}j_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}|_{S}}j^{!}\omega_{X}
is obviously supported on
Z
, it is enough to show that for an arbitrary
p\in Z
, and an arbitrary analytic open neighbourhood
W
of
p
in
Y
, there exists an analytic open neighbourhood
V
of
p
in
W
such that the induced map between cohomologies

\displaystyle H^{*}\left(V,(\pi_{*}j_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}|_{S}}j^{!}\omega_{X})\big|_{V}\right)\longrightarrow H^{*}\left(V,(\pi_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\omega_{X})\big|_{V}\right)

is an isomorphism. Equivalently by base change, we need to find
V
such that the pushforward map

\displaystyle H^{*}\left(\pi^{-1}(V)\cap S,\mathsf{w}\big|_{\pi^{-1}(V)\cap S}\right)\longrightarrow H^{*}\left(\pi^{-1}(V),\mathsf{w}\big|_{\pi^{-1}(V)}\right)

of critical cohomology is isomorphism.

By assumption,
f\colon Y\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
is smooth in a Zariski open neighbourhood of
Z
. For any
p\in Z
, we can take an analytic open neighbourhood
V
of
p
in
Y

such that
f|_{V}\colon V\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
is of form

V=V_{0}\times B\to B,\quad(v,z)\mapsto z,

where
B
is the open disk
\{|z|<\epsilon\}\subseteq\mathbb{A}^{1}
for some
0<\epsilon\ll 1
.
Then we have

V\cap Z=V_{0}\times\{0\},

and

\mathsf{w}|_{\pi^{-1}(V)}=\phi|_{V}+zx.

Let
\phi_{0}:=\phi|_{V\cap Z}=\phi|_{V_{0}\times\{0\}}
and we regard it as a holomorphic function on
V
:

\phi_{0}\colon V\to\mathbb{C},

by pulling it back along the projection
V\to V_{0}
. Let

\delta\phi:=\phi|_{V}-\phi_{0},

then we have
\delta\phi|_{z=0}=0
by definition. Therefore
\frac{\delta\phi}{z}\colon V\to\mathbb{C}
is a well-defined holomorphic function.

Consider an isomorphism between complex manifolds:

\pi^{-1}(V)=V_{0}\times B\times\mathbb{C}_{x}\cong V_{0}\times B\times\mathbb{C}_{\tilde{x}},\quad(v,z,x)\mapsto\left(v,z,\tilde{x}:=x+\frac{\delta\phi}{z}\right).

In this new coordinate system, we have

\pi^{-1}(V)\cap S\cong V_{0}\times\{0\}\times\mathbb{C}_{\tilde{x}},\quad\mathrm{and}\quad\mathsf{w}|_{\pi^{-1}(V)}=\phi_{0}+z\tilde{x},

where we have separated the variables, where
\phi_{0}
depends only on coordinate
v
.

Combining Thom-Sebastiani isomorphism and a direct computation of
H^{*}(B\times\mathbb{C}_{\tilde{x}},z\tilde{x})
, we get

\displaystyle H^{*}\left(\pi^{-1}(V),\mathsf{w}\big|_{\pi^{-1}(V)}\right)

\displaystyle\cong H^{*}(V_{0}\times B\times\mathbb{C}_{\tilde{x}},\phi_{0}+z\tilde{x})

\displaystyle\cong H^{*}(V_{0},\phi_{0})\otimes H^{*}(B\times\mathbb{C}_{\tilde{x}},z\tilde{x})

\displaystyle\cong H^{*}(V_{0},\phi_{0})\otimes H^{\mathrm{BM}}_{-*}(\{z=0\}\times\mathbb{C}_{\tilde{x}})

\displaystyle\cong H^{*}(V_{0}\times\{z=0\}\times\mathbb{C}_{\tilde{x}},\phi_{0})

\displaystyle\cong H^{*}\left(\pi^{-1}(V)\cap S,\mathsf{w}\big|_{\pi^{-1}(V)\cap S}\right).

Note that the isomorphism is induced by the pushforward map for critical cohomology.
Therefore we are done.
∎

Proof of Theorem
C.1
.

By assumption, the map

(f_{1},\ldots,f_{n})\colon Y\to\mathbb{A}^{n}

is smooth in a Zariski open neighbourhood of
Z
. Since
\pi_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\omega_{X}
is supported on
\pi(\mathrm{Crit}(\mathsf{w}))\subseteq Z
, we can replace
Y
by an open neighbourhood
U
of
Z
. By shrinking
U
, we may assume
(f_{1},\ldots,f_{n})\colon U\to\mathbb{A}^{n}
is a smooth map.

Let
Z_{\leqslant k}:=Z(\{f_{i}\}_{i=1}^{k})
be the zero locus, then we have inclusions of smooth varieties:

Z=Z_{\leqslant n}\subseteq Z_{\leqslant n-1}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq Z_{\leqslant 1}\subseteq U.

Let
S_{\leqslant k}:=\pi^{-1}(Z_{\leqslant k})
(where
\pi\colon U\times\mathbb{A}^{n}\to U
is projection) and set
S_{\leqslant 0}:=U\times\mathbb{A}^{n}
. Denote the closed immersions:

j_{k}\colon S_{\leqslant k}\hookrightarrow S_{\leqslant k-1},\quad j_{\leqslant k}\colon S_{\leqslant k}\hookrightarrow U\times\mathbb{A}^{n}.

Then the natural morphism (
C.1
) factors into a sequence of natural morphisms:

\displaystyle\pi_{*}j_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}|_{S}}j^{!}\omega_{X}=\pi_{*}j_{\leqslant n*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}|_{S_{\leqslant n}}}j_{\leqslant n}^{!}\omega_{X}\to\pi_{*}j_{\leqslant n-1*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}|_{S_{\leqslant n-1}}}j_{\leqslant n-1}^{!}\omega_{X}\to\cdots\to\pi_{*}j_{\leqslant 1*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}|_{S_{\leqslant 1}}}j_{\leqslant 1}^{!}\omega_{X}\to\pi_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}}\omega_{X}\>,

where the intermediate arrow

\pi_{*}j_{\leqslant k*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}|_{S_{\leqslant k}}}j_{\leqslant k}^{!}\omega_{X}\to\pi_{*}j_{\leqslant k-1*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}|_{S_{\leqslant k-1}}}j_{\leqslant k-1}^{!}\omega_{X}

is induced by applying
(Z_{\leqslant k-1}\hookrightarrow U)_{*}
to the natural map

\displaystyle(S_{\leqslant k-1}\to Z_{\leqslant k-1})_{*}j_{k*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}|_{S_{\leqslant k}}}j_{k}^{!}\omega_{S_{\leqslant k-1}}\to(S_{\leqslant k-1}\to Z_{\leqslant k-1})_{*}\varphi_{\mathsf{w}|_{S_{\leqslant k-1}}}\omega_{S_{\leqslant k-1}}\>,

which is an isomorphism by Lemma
C.2
. Thus (
C.1
) is an isomorphism.
∎

Appendix D
A dimensional reduction for critical
K
-theory

In the appendix, we prove a dimensional reduction for critical
K
-theories.

Setting D.1
.

Let
G
be a complex linear reductive group with a direct sum of finite dimensional linear
G
-representations

W=V\oplus U.

Let
\pi\colon W\to V
be the projection with a
G
-equivariant section
s\in\Gamma(V,\underline{U}^{\vee})
of the dual bundle
\underline{U}^{\vee}:=V\times U^{\vee}\to V
of
\pi
. Let
Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s)
be the derived zero locus of
s
,
which fits into the following diagram

(D.1)

\textstyle{\pi^{-1}(Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt\iota}
\scriptstyle{\Box}
\textstyle{W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{V.}

We define a
G
-invariant regular function

(D.2)

\mathsf{w}:=\langle e,s\rangle\colon W\to\mathbb{C},

where
e
is the coordinate of the fiber of
\pi
.

Choose a stability condition such that the semistable locus equals the stable locus:

W^{ss}=W^{s}\neq\emptyset,

with unstable locus denoted by
W^{u}:=W\setminus W^{ss}
.

By
[
Hal
, Thm. 2.10]
, there is a semi-orthogonal decomposition (SOD):

\langle\operatorname{D}^{b}_{[W^{u}/G]}([W/G])_{<0},\,\mathbb{G},\,\operatorname{D}^{b}_{[W^{u}/G]}([W/G])_{\geqslant 0}\rangle=\operatorname{D}^{b}([W/G]).

Moreover, under the restriction to the open locus:

\operatorname{D}^{b}([W/G])\twoheadrightarrow\operatorname{D}^{b}(W^{s}/G),

we have an equivalence of categories:

\mathbb{G}\cong\operatorname{D}^{b}(W^{s}/G).

This induces an SOD for matrix factorization categories of (
D.2
) (e.g.
[
P
, Prop. 2.1]
):

(D.3)

\langle\mathrm{MF}_{[W^{u}/G]}([W/G],\mathsf{w})_{<0},\mathbb{G}_{\mathsf{w}},\mathrm{MF}_{[W^{u}/G]}([W/G],\mathsf{w})_{\geqslant 0}\rangle=\mathrm{MF}([W/G],\mathsf{w})\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{MF}(W^{s}/G,\mathsf{w}),

and an equivalence of categories:

\Phi\colon\mathbb{G}_{\mathsf{w}}\cong\mathrm{MF}(W^{s}/G,\mathsf{w}).

Proposition D.2
.

The natural inclusion
\iota
in (
D.1
) induces an isomorphism of
K
-groups:

(D.4)

\iota_{*}\colon K((\pi^{-1}(Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s)))^{s}/G)\xrightarrow{\cong}K(W^{s}/G,\mathsf{w}).

Proof.

We have the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{K(\mathbb{G}_{\mathsf{w}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{K([W/G],\mathsf{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K(W^{s}/G,\mathsf{w})}
\textstyle{K([\pi^{-1}Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s)/G])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{*}}
\textstyle{K((\pi^{-1}Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s))^{s}/G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{*}}
\textstyle{K([Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s)/G])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces,}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\cong}

where
\iota_{*}\circ\pi^{*}
is an isomorphism by the dimensional reduction result
[
Toda1
, Prop. 2.5 & Cor. 3.13]
.
The commutativity of the diagram implies that the map (
D.4
) is surjective, and the composition

K(\mathbb{G}_{\mathsf{w}})\hookrightarrow K([W/G],\mathsf{w})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\iota_{*})^{-1}}}{{\cong}}K([\pi^{-1}Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s)/G])\twoheadrightarrow K((\pi^{-1}Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s))^{s}/G)\xrightarrow{\iota_{*}}K(W^{s}/G,\mathsf{w})

is the isomorphism given by
\Phi
. We are left to check the injectivity of the map (
D.4
), which is further reduced to
check the surjectivity of the composition

K(\mathbb{G}_{\mathsf{w}})\hookrightarrow K([W/G],\mathsf{w})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\iota_{*})^{-1}}}{{\cong}}K([\pi^{-1}Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s)/G])\twoheadrightarrow K((\pi^{-1}Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s))^{s}/G).

By the SOD (
D.3
), it is enough to check that any
\mathcal{P}^{\bullet}\in\mathrm{MF}_{[W^{u}/G]}([W/G],\mathsf{w})

goes to zero under the composition

(D.5)

K([W/G],\mathsf{w})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\iota_{*})^{-1}}}{{\cong}}K([\pi^{-1}Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s)/G])\twoheadrightarrow K((\pi^{-1}Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s))^{s}/G).

Here that the first map
(\iota_{*})^{-1}
of (
D.5
) is given by the tensor product

[\mathcal{P}^{\bullet}\otimes\mathrm{Kos}(\tau,s)^{\vee}]\in K([\pi^{-1}Z^{\mathrm{der}}(s)/G]),

with the Koszul factorization

\displaystyle\mathrm{Kos}(\tau,s):=\left(\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern where

is the tautological section of

. By definition,
\mathcal{P}^{\bullet}
is acyclic when restricted to the stable locus; therefore
\mathcal{P}^{\bullet}
and
