Remark D.3
.

One can extend the above result to the equivariant setting in the presence of a torus action.

Appendix E
Proof of Proposition
9.9

Our strategy of proving Proposition
9.9
is to deduce it from the axiom (iii) of the abelian stable envelope (Definition
3.10
). The difficulty is that the nonabelian stable envelope is constructed from an abelian stable envelope with respect to a
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-action which is in general different from the
\sigma_{i}
that appears in the KN stratification. We resolve this issue by showing that the
\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{+}
that appears in the construction of
\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}
is essentially the same as the stable envelope with respect to the two dimensional torus
\mathbb{C}^{*}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\sigma_{i}}
(see Lemma
E.6
). The main geometric fact that we use in the proof is to identify the KN strata
S_{i}
with an open subset in a
\mathbb{C}^{*}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\sigma_{i}}
-attracting set (Lemma
E.5
). We deduce it from a preliminary result that identifies
S_{i}
with an open subset in a
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-attracting set (Proposition
E.3
).

E.1.
KN strata of the unstable locus

We give an explicit description of the KN strata. We first define

(E.1)

\displaystyle\Sigma:=\{\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{N}^{Q_{0}}\colon\mathbf{u}_{i}\leqslant\mathbf{v}_{i},\,\forall\,i\in Q_{0}\},

together with the partial order
\leq
on
\Sigma
given by
\mathbf{u}\leq\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
if and only if
\mathbf{u}_{i}\leqslant\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{i}
for all
i\in Q_{0}
. For
\mathbf{u}\in\Sigma
, we define cocharacter
\sigma(\mathbf{u})
of
G
by

(E.2)

\displaystyle\sigma(\mathbf{u})=(-\omega_{i,\mathbf{u}_{i}})_{i\in Q_{0}}\,,

where
\omega_{i,\mathbf{u}_{i}}
is the
\mathbf{u}_{i}
-th fundamental cocharacter of
\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v}_{i})
. It is straightforward to see that if
\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\Sigma
then
\mathbf{u}\leq\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
implies
\mu(\sigma(\mathbf{u}))\leq\mu(\sigma(\mathbf{u}^{\prime}))
.
We keep using the notation

\displaystyle\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}}=(\mathbf{d}+\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d}).

Remark E.1
.

For
\mathbf{u}\in\Sigma
, we associate to it the following connected component in
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})^{\mathbb{C}^{*}}
:

(E.3)

\displaystyle F_{\mathbf{u}}:=\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{u},\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{v}})\times\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{u},\mathbf{d}).

Here
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{u},\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{v}})
is the quiver variety given by the GIT quotient

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{u},\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{v}})=\left(\bigoplus_{a\in Q_{1}}\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{u}_{t(a)}},\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{u}_{h(a)}})\oplus\bigoplus_{i\in Q_{0}}\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{v}_{i}},\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{u}_{i}})\right)/\!\!/_{\theta}\prod_{i\in Q_{0}}\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{u}_{i})

We note that
F_{\mathbf{u}}
is nonempty if and only if
R(\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{u},\mathbf{d})^{\theta\emph{-}ss}
is nonempty, because
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{u},\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{v}})
is always nonempty.

Let us also define

(E.4)

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\theta}:=\{\mathbf{u}\in\Sigma\colon R(\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{u},\mathbf{d})^{\theta\emph{-}ss}\neq\emptyset\},

then
F_{\mathbf{u}}\neq\emptyset\Longleftrightarrow\mathbf{u}\in\Sigma_{\theta}
. Consider the ample line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\theta}:=\bigotimes_{i\in Q_{0}}(\det\mathcal{V}_{i})^{-\theta_{i}}
on
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})
, where
\mathcal{V}_{i}
is the tautological vector bundle corresponding to
i
-th node.
\mathcal{L}_{\theta}
induces the ample partial order
\leq
on
\operatorname{Fix}_{\mathbb{C}^{*}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}}))
as in the Remark
3.9
. Then for
\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\Sigma_{\theta}
, we have the following implication

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}\leq\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\Longrightarrow F_{\mathbf{u}}\leq F_{\mathbf{u}^{\prime}}.

Let us refine
\leq
to a total order on
\Sigma
such that its restriction to
\Sigma_{\theta}
refines the ample partial order.

Lemma E.2
.

We have the decomposition

\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})=\bigcup_{\mathbf{u}\in\Sigma_{\theta}}\operatorname{Attr}_{+}(F_{\mathbf{u}}).

Proof.

Since the
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-weights in the ring
\mathbb{C}[R(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})]^{G}
of invariant functions are nonpositive, the
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-action on the affine quotient
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{0}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})
is attracting. Since the canonical map
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})\to\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{0}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})
is proper, we obtain the decomposition

\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})=\bigcup_{F\in\operatorname{Fix}_{\mathbb{C}^{*}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}}))}\operatorname{Attr}_{+}(F).

We note that any
F\in\operatorname{Fix}_{\mathbb{C}^{*}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}}))
is of form
F_{\mathbf{u}}
for some
\mathbf{u}\in\Sigma_{\theta}
and vice versa, then the lemma follows.
∎

Proposition E.3
.

Up to
G
-conjugation and a positive scalar multiple, the cocharacters that appear in KN stratification of
R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\theta\emph{-}u}
are given by cocharacters in the set

(E.5)

\displaystyle\{\sigma(\mathbf{u}):\mathbf{u}\in\Sigma_{\theta},\;\mathbf{u}\neq\mathbf{0}\}.

For the above
\sigma(\mathbf{u})
, the associated strata
S_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}
is given by

S_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}=\operatorname{Attr}_{+}(F_{\mathbf{u}})\cap\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}}),

where
F_{\mathbf{u}}
is the connected component in
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})^{\mathbb{C}^{*}}
given by (
E.3
).

Moreover, if
\mathbf{u}\in\Sigma_{\theta}
, then we have

\displaystyle\overline{S_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}}\setminus S_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}\subseteq\bigcup_{\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\Sigma_{\theta},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}>\mathbf{u}}S_{\sigma(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})},

if
\mathbf{u}\notin\Sigma_{\theta}
, then we have

\displaystyle R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}\subseteq\bigcup_{\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\Sigma_{\theta},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}>\mathbf{u}}S_{\sigma(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})}.

Proof.

Using Cauchy–Schwarz inequality
21
21

21

Namely, we have
\left(\sum_{i\in Q_{0}}|\theta_{i}|\operatorname{tr}(\sigma_{i}^{2})\right)\left(\sum_{i\in Q_{0}}|\theta_{i}|\right)\geqslant\left(\sum_{i\in Q_{0}}|\theta_{i}|\sum_{j=1}^{\mathbf{v}_{i}}|\sigma_{i,j}|\right)^{2}\geqslant(\sigma,\theta)^{2}
, which implies that
|\mu(\sigma)|\leqslant\sqrt{\sum_{i\in Q_{0}}|\theta_{i}|}
. Moreover the equality holds if and only if
\sigma_{i,j}=\sigma_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}
for all possible
i,i^{\prime},j,j^{\prime}
. Taking sign into consideration, we see that the maximal value of
\mu(\sigma)
is achieved if and only if
\sigma_{i,j}=-1
for all possible
i,j
, up to
G
-conjugation and a positive scalar multiple.
, one can see that the invariant
\mu(\sigma)
is maximized by taking
\sigma=\sigma(\mathbf{v})
, up to
G
-conjugation and a positive scalar multiple. Note that
\sigma(\mathbf{v})
is nothing but the
\mathbb{C}^{*}\subseteq G
that we previously considered to construct
\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}
.
In particular, the image of
\sigma(\mathbf{v})
is in the center of
G
, so
\operatorname{Attr}_{\sigma(\mathbf{v})}(R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\sigma(\mathbf{v})})
is invariant under the
G
-action. Moreover
R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\sigma(\mathbf{v})}\cong R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{0})\times R(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{d})
, and obviously we have
R(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{d})=R(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{d})^{\theta\emph{-}ss}=\operatorname{pt}
. It follows from Lemma
E.4
below that the minimal KN stratum is given by

S_{\sigma(\mathbf{v})}=\operatorname{Attr}_{\sigma(\mathbf{v})}\left(R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\sigma(\mathbf{v})}\right)=\operatorname{Attr}_{+}(F_{\mathbf{v}})\cap\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}}).

By minimality, we have
\overline{S_{\sigma(\mathbf{v})}}=S_{\sigma(\mathbf{v})}
.

To proceed, we introduce a conjugation-invariant map:

\mathsf{Supp}:\operatorname{cochar}(G)\to\Sigma,\quad\mathsf{Supp}(\lambda)_{i}=\operatorname{rk}\lambda^{\prime}_{i},

where
\lambda^{\prime}
is
G
-conjugate to
\lambda
and
\lambda^{\prime}_{i}=\operatorname{diag}(\lambda^{\prime}_{i,j})_{j=1}^{\mathbf{v}_{i}}
is a cocharacter of
\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v}_{i})
in the standard basis. Then it is easy to see that if
\lambda\in\operatorname{cochar}(G)
and
\mathbf{u}=\mathsf{Supp}(\lambda)
, then

(E.6)

\displaystyle R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\lambda}\subseteq G\cdot R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}.

Moreover, the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality shows that the numerical invariant

\displaystyle\mu(\lambda)=\frac{(\lambda,\theta)}{|\lambda|}\bigg|_{\mathsf{Supp}(\lambda)=\mathbf{u}}

is maximized by
\lambda=\sigma(\mathbf{u})
up to a positive scalar multiple and
G
-conjugation. Then we claim that any
\sigma_{i}
that appears in the KN stratification, up to a positive scalar multiple and
G
-conjugation, belongs to the set

\displaystyle\{\sigma(\mathbf{u}):\mathbf{u}\in\Sigma,\;\mathbf{u}\neq\mathbf{0}\}.

In fact,
R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\sigma_{i}}
is not contained in previously defined strata by the construction of KN strata. Let
\mathbf{u}_{i}:=\mathsf{Supp}(\sigma_{i})
, then (
E.6
) implies that
R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\sigma(\mathbf{u}_{i})}
is not contained in previously defined strata. Since
\sigma_{i}
maximize
\mu(\sigma)
among those
\sigma
such that
R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\sigma}
is not contained in previously defined strata, we must have
\sigma_{i}=\sigma(\mathbf{u}_{i})
up to a positive scalar multiple and
G
-conjugation.

It remains to show that

(1)

\sigma(\mathbf{u})
appears in KN stratification if and only if
\mathbf{u}\in\Sigma_{\theta}
,

(2)

if
\mathbf{u}\in\Sigma_{\theta}
, then
S_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}=\operatorname{Attr}_{+}(F_{\mathbf{u}})\cap\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})
,

(3)

if
\mathbf{u}\in\Sigma_{\theta}
, then we have

\displaystyle\overline{S_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}}\setminus S_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}\subseteq\bigcup_{\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\Sigma_{\theta},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}>\mathbf{u}}S_{\sigma(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})},

(4)

if
\mathbf{u}\notin\Sigma_{\theta}
, then we have

\displaystyle R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}\subseteq\bigcup_{\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\Sigma_{\theta},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}>\mathbf{u}}S_{\sigma(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})}.

We prove the above four statements by induction downwards along the total order
\leq
on
\Sigma
defined in Remark
E.1
. The maximal case
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{v}
has been proven previously. Let
\mathbf{u}\in\Sigma,\mathbf{u}\neq\mathbf{0}
. Suppose that (1)-(4) have been proven for all
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\Sigma
such that
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}>\mathbf{u}
. Consider the following union of KN strata

\displaystyle X_{\mathbf{u}}:=\bigcup_{\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\Sigma_{\theta},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}>\mathbf{u}}S_{\sigma(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})}.

We claim that
X_{\mathbf{u}}
is closed. In fact, by the induction hypothesis, we have
\overline{S_{\sigma(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})}}\subseteq X_{\mathbf{u}}
for all
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\Sigma_{\theta},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}>\mathbf{u}
. Then
X_{\mathbf{u}}=\bigcup_{\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\Sigma_{\theta},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}>\mathbf{u}}\overline{S_{\sigma(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})}}
is closed.

Suppose that
\mathbf{u}\notin\Sigma_{\theta}
, then according to Lemma
E.4
we have the inclusion

R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}\subseteq\bigcup_{\mathbf{u}^{\prime}>\mathbf{u}}G\cdot\operatorname{Attr}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})}\left(R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\sigma(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})}\right).

By induction hypothesis, we have

\displaystyle\bigcup_{\mathbf{u}^{\prime}>\mathbf{u}}G\cdot\operatorname{Attr}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})}\left(R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\sigma(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})}\right)\subseteq\bigcup_{\mathbf{u}^{\prime\prime}\in\Sigma_{\theta},\mathbf{u}^{\prime\prime}>\mathbf{u}}S_{\sigma(\mathbf{u}^{\prime\prime})}=X_{\mathbf{u}}.

Here we have used the fact that
S_{\sigma(\mathbf{u}^{\prime\prime})}
is
G
-invariant so it is invariant under taking attracting set and
G
-action. It follows that
R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}\subseteq X_{\mathbf{u}}
. Thus
\sigma(\mathbf{u})
does not appear in KN stratification. This proves (4) and part of (1).

Suppose that
\mathbf{u}\in\Sigma_{\theta}
, then according to Lemma
E.4
we have

\displaystyle G\cdot\operatorname{Attr}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}\left(R(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{0})\times R(\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{u},\mathbf{d})^{\theta\emph{-}ss}\right)=\operatorname{Attr}_{+}(F_{\mathbf{u}})\cap\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}}).

In particular,
R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}\cap\left(\operatorname{Attr}_{+}(F_{\mathbf{u}})\cap\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})\right)\neq\emptyset
. We note that by induction hypothesis, we have

\displaystyle X_{\mathbf{u}}=\left(\bigcup_{\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\Sigma_{\theta},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}>\mathbf{u}}\operatorname{Attr}_{+}(F_{\mathbf{u}^{\prime}})\right)\cap\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}}).

Since
F_{\mathbf{u}}\cap F_{\mathbf{u}^{\prime}}=\emptyset
if
\mathbf{u}\neq\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
, we deduce that
R(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{0})\times R(\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{u},\mathbf{d})^{\theta\emph{-}ss}
is an nonempty open subset in
R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}
, and
(R(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{0})\times R(\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{u},\mathbf{d})^{\theta\emph{-}ss})\cap X_{\mathbf{u}}=\emptyset
. Then
\sigma(\mathbf{u})
appears in KN stratification because
\sigma(\mathbf{u})
maximizes
\mu(\sigma)
among those
\sigma\in\{\sigma(\mathbf{u}^{\prime}):\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\Sigma,\;\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\leq\mathbf{u}\}
. This finishes the proof of (1).

Denote
S^{\prime}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}:=\operatorname{Attr}_{+}(F_{\mathbf{u}})\cap\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})
, then we have

(E.7)

\displaystyle S_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}=G\cdot\operatorname{Attr}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}\left(R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}\setminus X_{\mathbf{u}}\right)\supseteq G\cdot\operatorname{Attr}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}\left(R(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{0})\times R(\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{u},\mathbf{d})^{\theta\emph{-}ss}\right)=S^{\prime}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}

S^{\prime}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}
is dense in
S_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}
because
R(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{0})\times R(\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{u},\mathbf{d})^{\theta\emph{-}ss}
is open and dense in
R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}
. Therefore we have

\displaystyle\overline{S_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}}\setminus S^{\prime}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}=\overline{S^{\prime}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}}\setminus S^{\prime}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}

\displaystyle\subseteq\left(\overline{\operatorname{Attr}_{+}(F_{\mathbf{u}})}\cap\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})\right)\setminus\left(\operatorname{Attr}_{+}(F_{\mathbf{u}})\cap\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})\right)

by definition of
\preceq

\displaystyle\subseteq\left(\bigcup_{F^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Fix}_{\mathbb{C}^{*}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})),F_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}\prec F^{\prime}}\operatorname{Attr}_{+}(F^{\prime})\right)\bigcap\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})

since
\leq
refines
\preceq

\displaystyle\subseteq\left(\bigcup_{F^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Fix}_{\mathbb{C}^{*}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})),F_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}<F^{\prime}}\operatorname{Attr}_{+}(F^{\prime})\right)\bigcap\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})

by Remark
E.1

\displaystyle=\left(\bigcup_{\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\Sigma_{\theta},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}>\mathbf{u}}\operatorname{Attr}_{+}(F_{\mathbf{u}^{\prime}})\right)\bigcap\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})

\displaystyle=X_{\mathbf{u}}.

It follows that

(E.8)

\displaystyle S_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}\subseteq\overline{S_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}}\setminus X_{\mathbf{u}}\subseteq\overline{S_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}}\setminus(\overline{S_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}}\setminus S^{\prime}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})})=S^{\prime}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}.

The inclusions (
E.7
) and (
E.8
) imply that
S_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}=S^{\prime}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}
. This proves (2). Finally (3) follows from (2) because we just show that
\overline{S_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}}\setminus S^{\prime}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}\subseteq X_{\mathbf{u}}
. This finishes the induction step.
∎

Let
\mathbf{u}\in\Sigma
be a nonzero element. Note that

R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}\cong R(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{0})\times R(\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{u},\mathbf{d}).

In view of the isomorphism (
9.3
),
R(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{0})\times R(\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{u},\mathbf{d})^{\theta\emph{-}ss}
is naturally identified with a (possibly empty) locally-closed subvariety of
\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})
.

Lemma E.4
.

Let
\mathbf{u}\in\Sigma
be a nonzero element.

•

If
\mathbf{u}\notin\Sigma_{\theta}
, then we have

\displaystyle R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}\subseteq\bigcup_{\mathbf{u}^{\prime}>\mathbf{u}}G\cdot\operatorname{Attr}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})}\left(R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\sigma(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})}\right).

•

If
\mathbf{u}\in\Sigma_{\theta}
, then we have

\displaystyle G\cdot\operatorname{Attr}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}\left(R(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{0})\times R(\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{u},\mathbf{d})^{\theta\emph{-}ss}\right)=\operatorname{Attr}_{+}(F_{\mathbf{u}})\cap\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}}),

in particular, the right-hand-side is nonempty.

Proof.

If
\mathbf{u}\notin\Sigma_{\theta}
, then by definition of
\Sigma_{\theta}
every quiver representation in
R(\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{u},\mathbf{d})
is
\theta
-unstable. Let
(H_{i})_{i\in Q_{0}}\in R(\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{u},\mathbf{d})
be a quiver representation, and take its Harder-Narasimhan filtration

0=H^{(0)}\subseteq H^{(1)}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq H^{(n)}=H

with respect to the
\theta
-stability, i.e.
H^{(i+1)}/H^{(i)}
is
\theta
-semistable for all possible
i
and its
\theta
-slope decreases strictly as
i
increases. By assumption
H^{(1)}\neq H
, so the
\theta
-slope of
H^{(1)}
is greater than the the
\theta
-slope of
H
, and the latter is zero. Since we assume
\theta_{i}<0
for all
i\in Q_{0}
, this implies that
H^{(1)}
contains the framing node
\infty
in the Crawley-Boevey quiver associated with
(Q,\mathbf{d})
, i.e.
H^{(1)}\in R(\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{u}^{\prime},\mathbf{d})^{\theta\emph{-}ss}
for some
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}>\mathbf{u}
. It follows that
H/H^{(1)}\in R(\mathbf{u}^{\prime}-\mathbf{u},\mathbf{0})
. By decomposing
H
into an extension of
H/H^{(1)}
by
H^{(1)}
, we see that

\displaystyle H\in G_{\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{u}}\cdot\operatorname{Attr}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u}^{\prime}-\mathbf{u})}\left(R(\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{u},\mathbf{d})^{\sigma(\mathbf{u}^{\prime}-\mathbf{u})}\right),

where
G_{\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{u}}=\prod_{i\in Q_{0}}\mathrm{GL}(\mathbf{v}_{i}-\mathbf{u}_{i})
is the gauge group. Since
H
is arbitrary, we have

\displaystyle R(\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{u},\mathbf{d})\subseteq\bigcup_{\mathbf{u}^{\prime}>\mathbf{u}}G_{\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{u}}\cdot\operatorname{Attr}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u}^{\prime}-\mathbf{u})}\left(R(\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{u},\mathbf{d})^{\sigma(\mathbf{u}^{\prime}-\mathbf{u})}\right).

Since
R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}\cong R(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{0})\times R(\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{u},\mathbf{d})
, it follows that

\displaystyle R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}\subseteq\bigcup_{\mathbf{u}^{\prime}>\mathbf{u}}G\cdot\operatorname{Attr}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})}\left(R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})^{\sigma(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})}\right).

If
\mathbf{u}\in\Sigma_{\theta}
, then it is enough to show that

(E.9)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Attr}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}\left(R(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{0})\times R(\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{u},\mathbf{d})^{\theta\emph{-}ss}\right)\subseteq\operatorname{Attr}_{+}(F_{\mathbf{u}})

and

(E.10)

\displaystyle G\cdot\operatorname{Attr}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}\left(R(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{0})\times R(\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{u},\mathbf{d})^{\theta\emph{-}ss}\right)\supseteq\operatorname{Attr}_{+}(F_{\mathbf{u}})\cap\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}}).

In view of the isomorphism (
9.3
),
R(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{0})\times R(\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{u},\mathbf{d})^{\theta\emph{-}ss}
is isomorphic to the moduli space of representations of the following quiver:

(E.11)

\textstyle{M_{i}}
\textstyle{N_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{B_{i}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{u}_{i}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{I^{\prime}_{i}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{v}_{i}-\mathbf{u}_{i}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{I^{\prime\prime}_{i}}
\textstyle{D_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{A_{i}}

where it is required that
I^{\prime}_{i}
and
I^{\prime\prime}_{i}
are isomorphisms for all
i\in Q_{0}
, and the RHS part is
\theta
-semistable after forgetting
I^{\prime\prime}
. Taking attracting set with respect to
\sigma(\mathbf{u})
action amounts to taking an extension of the LHS of (
E.11
) by the RHS of (
E.11
), i.e.
\operatorname{Attr}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}(R(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{0})\times R(\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{u},\mathbf{d})^{\theta\emph{-}ss})
is the moduli space of the following quiver

(E.12)

\textstyle{M_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{N_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{B_{i}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{u}_{i}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{I^{\prime}_{i}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{v}_{i}-\mathbf{u}_{i}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{I^{\prime\prime}_{i}}
\textstyle{D_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{A_{i}}

with the same conditions as above. Here dotted lines are new linear maps that appear in extensions between representations. Similar argument shows that
\operatorname{Attr}_{+}(F_{\mathbf{u}})
is the moduli space of the quiver

(E.13)

\textstyle{M_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{N_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{B_{i}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{v}_{i}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{D_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{A_{i}}

with the condition that rigid lines parts are
\theta
-semistable. Writing
\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{v}_{i}}=\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{u}_{i}}\oplus\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{v}_{i}-\mathbf{u}_{i}}
and comparing stability conditions between (
E.12
) and (
E.13
), we obtain (
E.9
).

To prove (
E.10
), we notice that
\operatorname{Attr}_{+}(F_{\mathbf{u}})\cap\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})
is the moduli space of the quiver (
E.13
) with the condition that rigid lines parts are
\theta
-semistable, and the

\textstyle{M_{i}}
\textstyle{N_{i}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{v}_{i}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

part induces isomorphisms
\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{v}_{i}}\cong M_{i}\oplus N_{i}
. Given a representation of the quiver (
E.13
) with the above conditions satisfied, let
K_{i}:=\ker(\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{v}_{i}}\to M_{i})
and
P_{i}:=\ker(\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{v}_{i}}\to N_{i})
. Then by the assumption we have
\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{v}_{i}}=P_{i}\oplus K_{i}
, and the induced maps
P_{i}\to M_{i},K_{i}\to N_{i}
are isomorphisms. We rewrite the quiver diagram (
E.13
) with
\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{v}_{i}}
replaced by
P_{i}\oplus K_{i}
, and obtain the following quiver

(E.14)

\textstyle{M_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{N_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{B_{i}}
\textstyle{P_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{I^{\prime}_{i}}
\textstyle{K_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{I^{\prime\prime}_{i}}
\textstyle{D_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{A_{i}}

The stability conditions in (
E.14
) are such that
I^{\prime}_{i},I^{\prime\prime}_{i}
are isomorphisms and the part formed by
N_{i},D_{i}
and arrows among them is
\theta
-semistable. Up to a choice of isomorphisms
P_{i}\cong\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{u}_{i}},K_{i}\cong\mathbb{C}^{\mathbf{v}_{i}-\mathbf{u}_{i}}
(which can be accomplished by a
G^{\prime}
action), (
E.14
) is identified with a special case of (
E.12
). This proves (
E.10
).
∎

E.2.
Finish the proof

We have given an explicit description of the KN stratification in terms of attracting sets of
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})
in Proposition
E.3
. For our purpose, we shall need the following enhancement of Proposition
E.3
.

Let
\mathsf{A}=(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{2}
act on
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})
via
(\sigma(\mathbf{u}),\sigma(\mathbf{v})):\mathbb{C}^{*}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}\to G^{\prime}
. Let
(t_{1},t_{2})\in\operatorname{Lie}(\mathsf{A})_{\mathbb{R}}
be the coordinate, then it is easy to see that the root hyperplanes are
t_{1}=0
,
t_{2}=0
, and
t_{1}+t_{2}=0
. Define
\mathfrak{C}
to be the chamber
t_{1}>0,t_{2}>0
. Write the
\sigma(\mathbf{u})
-fixed components of
F_{\mathbf{u}}
as

\displaystyle F_{\mathbf{u}}^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}=\bigsqcup_{\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\Sigma,\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\leq\mathbf{u}}F_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}},\;\text{ where }\;F_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}}=\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime},\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{u}})\times\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{u}-\mathbf{u}^{\prime},\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{u}})\times\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{u},\mathbf{d}).

Then it is straightforward to see that

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})^{\mathsf{A}}=\bigsqcup_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\Sigma,\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\leq\mathbf{u}}F_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}}.

Lemma E.5
.

If
\mathbf{u}\in\Sigma_{\theta}
, then we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Attr}_{+}(F_{\mathbf{u}})\cap\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})=\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u}})\cap\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}}).

In particular,
S_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}=\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u}})\cap\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})
.

Proof.

The “+” chamber is given by
t_{1}=0,t_{2}>0
, which is on the boundary of
\mathfrak{C}
. Let
\mathfrak{C}/+
be the chamber
t_{1}>0
of the torus
\mathsf{A}/\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\sigma(\mathbf{v})}
action on
F_{\mathbf{u}}
, then the same argument as Lemma
E.2
shows that
F_{\mathbf{u}}=\bigcup_{\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\Sigma,\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\leq\mathbf{u}}\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}/+}(F_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}})
. Since for any
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\Sigma,\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\leq\mathbf{u}
we have
\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}})=\operatorname{Attr}_{+}(\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}/+}(F_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}}))
, it follows that we have decomposition

\displaystyle\operatorname{Attr}_{+}(F_{\mathbf{u}})=\bigcup_{\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\Sigma,\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\leq\mathbf{u}}\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}}).

Then the lemma follows from the following claim: if
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}<\mathbf{u}
then
\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}})\cap\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})=\emptyset
. In fact,
\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}})
is the moduli space of quiver representations
V\in\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}})
which admits filtration by subrepresentations
V^{(1)}\subseteq V^{(2)}\subseteq V
such that

\displaystyle V/V^{(2)}\in\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{u}^{\prime},\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{u}}),\quad V^{(2)}/V^{(1)}\in\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{u}-\mathbf{u}^{\prime},\underline{\mathbf{0}}^{\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{u}}),\quad V^{(1)}\in\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{u},\mathbf{d}).

The linear maps
\{I_{i}\}_{i\in Q_{0}}
in (
9.2
) respect the filtration, in particular it is isomorphism if and only if it is isomorphism after passing to associated graded vector spaces. If
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}<\mathbf{u}
, then there exists
i\in Q_{0}
such that the source and the target of
I_{i}
have different ranks, so
I_{i}
can not be isomorphism. This proves the claim.
∎

Lemma E.6
.

There exists
K
-theoretic stable envelope
\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}
for
(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\underline{\mathbf{d}}^{\mathbf{v}}),\mathsf{w}^{\prime},G^{\prime\sigma(\mathbf{u})}\times\mathsf{T},\mathsf{A},\mathfrak{C},\mathsf{s})
, and we have

(E.15)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}\circ\,\mathrm{res}^{G^{\prime}}_{G^{\prime\sigma(\mathbf{u})}}=\mathrm{res}^{G^{\prime}}_{G^{\prime\sigma(\mathbf{u})}}\circ\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{+}.

Proof.

Consider the vector bundle
E=\bigoplus_{i\in Q_{0}}\operatorname{Hom}(\mathcal{V}_{i},V^{\prime}_{i})
on
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{d})
. It is easy to see that
E
is repelling with respect to the chamber
\mathfrak{C}
, and
\mathrm{Tot}(E)\cong\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{d})
is a symmetric quiver variety. We note that the
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-fixed component
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})
is fixed by bigger torus
\mathsf{A}
. Moreover, the restriction of
E
to the
\mathsf{A}
-fixed component
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})
is moving. Then
the existence of
\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}
follows from Lemma
8.4
. The equation (
E.15
) follows from Remark
8.5
and the commutative diagram (
3.14
).
∎

Now we have all the ingredients of proving Proposition
9.9
.

By Lemma
E.6
, Proposition
E.3
and Lemma
E.5
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{i}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}^{*}\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}(\gamma)=\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(1\otimes\gamma)|_{\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u}})\cap\mathring{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{d})}.

By the degree axiom of stable envelope, we have

\displaystyle\deg_{\mathsf{A}}\operatorname{Stab}^{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathfrak{C}}(1\otimes\gamma)|_{\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u}})}

\displaystyle\subsetneq\deg_{\mathsf{A}}e^{G^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}}_{K}\left(N^{-}_{F_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u}}/\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{d})}\right)+\operatorname{weight}_{\mathsf{A}}\left(\mathsf{s}\otimes\left(\det N^{-}_{F_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u}}/\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{d})}\right)^{1/2}\right)

\displaystyle=\deg_{\mathsf{A}}e^{G}_{K}\left(N_{S_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}/R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}\right)+\operatorname{weight}_{\mathsf{A}}\left(\mathsf{s}\otimes\left(\det N_{S_{i}/R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}\right)^{1/2}\right).

Projection of the above inclusion of polytopes to the cocharacter lattice of subtorus
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}
gives rise to the desired degree bound:

\displaystyle\deg_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}\mathbf{i}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}^{*}\widetilde{\mathbf{\Psi}}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}(\gamma)

\displaystyle\subseteq\deg_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}\operatorname{Stab}_{\mathfrak{C}}(1\otimes\gamma)|_{\operatorname{Attr}_{\mathfrak{C}}(F_{\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u}})}

\displaystyle\subseteq\deg_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}e^{G}_{K}\left(N_{S_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}/R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}\right)+\operatorname{weight}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}\left(\mathsf{s}\otimes\left(\det N_{S_{i}/R(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{d})}\right)^{1/2}\right).

Since
\mathsf{s}
is generic, the above inclusion must be strict by Remark (
3.12
). Finally, the uniqueness can be proven in the same way as Proposition
3.16
, and we omit the details.

Appendix F
Proof of Lemma
9.16

We prove here the
K
-theory version (
9.31
) (the proof of the cohomology version (
9.30
) is similar and we leave readers to check details).
Recall that
\Psi^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}
is by definition the dimensional reduction of the nonabelian stable envelope
\mathbf{\Psi}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}
for the symmetric quiver variety
\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{r},\mathbf{e})
associated with the tripled quiver
\widetilde{\Gamma}
with potential
\mathsf{w}=\sum_{i\in\Gamma_{0}}\operatorname{tr}(\varepsilon_{i}\mu_{i})
, where
\{\varepsilon_{i}\}_{i\in\Gamma_{0}}
is the set of edge loops and
\mu\colon R(\overline{\Gamma},\mathbf{r},\mathbf{e})\to\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}
is the moment map.

We claim that it is enough to show that

\displaystyle\operatorname{can}([\chi\otimes\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{Z(\mu)}])\in K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{r},\mathbf{e}),\mathsf{w})

satisfies the degree condition

(F.1)

\displaystyle\deg_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}{\mathbf{i}}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}^{*}\operatorname{can}([\chi\otimes\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{Z(\mu)}])\subseteq\frac{1}{2}\deg_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}e^{G}_{K}\left(R(\overline{\Gamma},\mathbf{r},\mathbf{e})-\mathfrak{g}\right)+\operatorname{weight}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}\left(\mathsf{s}\right),

for all cocharacters in
\{\sigma(\mathbf{u}):\mathbf{u}\in\Sigma_{\theta},\>\mathbf{u}\neq\mathbf{0}\}
with
\sigma(\mathbf{u})
defined in (
E.2
) and
\Sigma_{\theta}
the following (see (
E.1
) and (
E.4
)):

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\theta}:=\{\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{N}^{\Gamma_{0}}\colon\mathbf{u}_{i}\leqslant\mathbf{r}_{i},\,\forall\,i\in\Gamma_{0},\,\text{ and }\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{u},\mathbf{e})\neq\emptyset\}.

In (
F.1
),
{\mathbf{i}}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}:[Z^{*}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}/G^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}]\to\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{r},\mathbf{e})=[R(\widetilde{\Gamma},\mathbf{r},\mathbf{e})/G]
is the natural map (
4.23
), and we define
Z(\mu)\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{r},\mathbf{e})
by the following Cartesian diagrams

(F.2)

\textstyle{Z(\mu)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Box}
\textstyle{[R(\widetilde{\Gamma},\mathbf{r},\mathbf{e})/G]=\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{r},\mathbf{e})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{N}(\mathbf{r},\mathbf{e})=[\mu^{-1}(0)/G]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Box}
\textstyle{[R(\overline{\Gamma},\mathbf{r},\mathbf{e})/G]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu}
\textstyle{[0/G]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0}
\textstyle{[\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}/G],}

where
0
is the inclusion of zero,
\mu
is the moment map of double quiver representation and the right vertical map in the upper square is the
vector bundle map by forgetting loops.
We endow
Z(\mu)
with virtual structure sheaf

\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{Z(\mu)}:=0^{!}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{r},\mathbf{e})},

where
0^{!}
is the refined Gysin pullback.
The canonical map (
2.18
)
\operatorname{can}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}}(Z(\mathsf{w}))\to K^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{r},\mathbf{e}),\mathsf{w})
is the proper pushforward and note that
Z(\mu)\subseteq Z(\mathsf{w})
.

Let
\delta_{K}
be the dimensional reduction map given by pullback followed by the pushforward (see (
6.7
), also (
9.18
) in the stacky case).
By base change on (
F.2
), we know

(F.3)

\delta_{K}([\chi\otimes\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\mathfrak{N}(\mathbf{r},\mathbf{e})}])=\operatorname{can}([\chi\otimes\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{Z(\mu)}]).

Restricting to the stable locus, we have

\mathbf{k}^{*}\operatorname{can}([\chi\otimes\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{Z(\mu)}])=\operatorname{can}([\chi\otimes\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{Z(\mu)}\big|_{\mathcal{M}_{\theta}(\mathbf{r},\mathbf{e})}])=\delta_{K}([\mathcal{L}_{\chi}]).

Since
\mathsf{s}
is generic, the inclusion in (
F.1
) must be strict if it holds. Combining with the characterization of nonabelian stable envelopes (Proposition
9.9
), (
F.1
) implies that

(F.4)

\operatorname{can}([\chi\otimes\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{Z(\mu)}])=\mathbf{\Psi}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}\circ\delta_{K}([\mathcal{L}_{\chi}]).

Eqn. (
9.19
), (
F.3
) and (
F.4
) imply that

\displaystyle\Psi^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}([\mathcal{L}_{\chi}])=\delta_{K}^{-1}\circ\mathbf{\Psi}^{\mathsf{s}}_{K}\circ\delta_{K}([\mathcal{L}_{\chi}])=[\chi\otimes\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\mathfrak{N}(\mathbf{r},\mathbf{e})}],

which is (
9.31
). This proves the claim. We are therefore left to show (
F.1
).

Now suppose that
\mathbf{u}\in\Sigma_{\theta}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}
. Since the canonical map commutes with Gysin pullbacks, we have

{\mathbf{i}}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}^{*}\operatorname{can}([\chi\otimes\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{Z(\mu)}])=\operatorname{can}({\mathbf{i}}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}^{*}[\chi\otimes\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{Z(\mu)}])=\operatorname{can}([\chi\otimes{\mathbf{i}}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}^{*}\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{Z(\mu)}]).

The naturality of refined Gysin pullbacks implies that

\displaystyle{\mathbf{i}}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}^{*}\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{Z(\mu)}={\mathbf{i}}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}^{*}0^{!}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{r},\mathbf{e})}=0^{!}{\mathbf{i}}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{M}(\mathbf{r},\mathbf{e})}=0^{!}\mathcal{O}_{[Z^{*}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}/G^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}]}.

Note that there is a commutative diagram

\textstyle{Z^{*}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu}
\textstyle{R(\widetilde{\Gamma},\mathbf{r},\mathbf{e})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu}
\textstyle{(\mathfrak{g}^{\vee})^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{g}^{\vee},}

with horizontal arrows being natural closed immersion and vertical arrows being the composition of the right vertical maps
in (
F.2
) (by abuse of notations, we denote it also as
\mu
). Then we have

\displaystyle 0^{!}\mathcal{O}_{[Z^{*}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}/G^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}]}

\displaystyle=\left([0/G^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}]\hookrightarrow[(\mathfrak{g}^{\vee})^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}/G^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}]\right)^{!}\circ\left([(\mathfrak{g}^{\vee})^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}/G^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}]\hookrightarrow[\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}/G^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}]\right)^{!}\mathcal{O}_{[Z^{*}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}/G^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}]}

\displaystyle=e^{G^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}}_{K}\left((\mathfrak{g}^{\vee})^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})\text{-moving}}\right)\cdot\left([0/G^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}]\hookrightarrow[(\mathfrak{g}^{\vee})^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}/G^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}]\right)^{!}\mathcal{O}_{[Z^{*}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}/G^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}]}.

To summarize, we arrive at

\displaystyle{\mathbf{i}}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}^{*}\operatorname{can}([\chi\otimes\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{Z(\mu)}])=\chi\cdot e^{G^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}}_{K}\left((\mathfrak{g}^{\vee})^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})\text{-moving}}\right)\cdot\operatorname{can}\left(\left([0/G^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}]\hookrightarrow[(\mathfrak{g}^{\vee})^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}/G^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}]\right)^{!}\mathcal{O}_{[Z^{*}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}/G^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}]}\right).

Note that

\deg_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}\left([0/G^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}]\hookrightarrow[(\mathfrak{g}^{\vee})^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}/G^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}]\right)^{!}\mathcal{O}_{[Z^{*}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}/G^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}]}=0.

Since the map
\operatorname{can}\colon K^{\mathsf{T}\times G^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}}(Z(\mathsf{w})\cap Z^{*}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})})\to K^{\mathsf{T}\times G^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}}(Z^{*}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})},\mathsf{w})
does not enlarge the
\sigma(\mathbf{u})
degree, we have

\displaystyle\deg_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}{\mathbf{i}}_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}^{*}\operatorname{can}([\chi\otimes\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{Z(\mu)}])\subseteq\deg_{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}\left(\chi\cdot e^{G^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}}_{K}\left((\mathfrak{g}^{\vee})^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})\text{-moving}}\right)\right).

Then it is enough to show that

(F.5)

\lim_{t\to 0,\infty}\frac{\chi\cdot e^{G^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}}_{K}((\mathfrak{g}^{\vee})^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})\text{-moving}})}{\mathsf{s}\cdot\sqrt{\hat{e}^{G^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}}_{K}\left((R(\mathbf{r},\mathbf{e})-\mathfrak{g})^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})\text{-moving}}\right)}}\,\,\text{ exist},

where
t
is the equivariant parameter in
K_{\mathbb{C}^{*}}(\operatorname{pt})=\mathbb{Q}[t^{\pm}]
.

By the assumption on
\mathsf{s}
, we have
\chi/\mathsf{s}=t^{\epsilon}
for
0<|\epsilon|\ll 1
. Straightforward computation leads to

\displaystyle\frac{\chi\cdot e^{G^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}}_{K}((\mathfrak{g}^{\vee})^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})\text{-moving}})}{\mathsf{s}\cdot\sqrt{\hat{e}^{G^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})}}_{K}\left((R(\mathbf{r},\mathbf{e})-\mathfrak{g})^{\sigma(\mathbf{u})\text{-moving}}\right)}}\sim O\left(t^{\epsilon\pm\mathsf{d}}\right)\>\text{ as }\>t^{\pm 1}\to\infty,\>\text{ where }\>\mathsf{d}=\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{u}\cdot(\mathsf{C}\mathbf{r}-\mathsf{C}\mathbf{u}-\mathbf{e}).

In the above,
\mathsf{C}
is the Cartan matrix of
\Gamma
, and
\mathbf{u}\cdot\mathbf{v}:=\sum_{i\in\Gamma_{0}}\mathbf{u}_{i}\mathbf{v}_{i}
is the standard inner product. Then (
F.5
) is equivalent to the following claim:

•

if
\mathbf{u}\in\Sigma_{\theta}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}
, then
\mathbf{u}\cdot(\mathbf{e}-\mathsf{C}\mathbf{r}+\mathsf{C}\mathbf{u})>0
.

According to
[
Nak2
, Thm. 10.2]
, the nonemptyness of
\mathcal{N}_{\theta}(\mathbf{r},\mathbf{e})
implies that
\lambda_{\mathbf{e}}-\alpha_{\mathbf{r}}
is a weight that appears in the irreducible module
V(\lambda_{\mathbf{e}})
of
\mathfrak{g}_{\Gamma}
with highest weight
\lambda_{\mathbf{e}}
. Here
\mathfrak{g}_{\Gamma}
is the Lie algebra whose Dynkin diagram is
\Gamma
(not to be confused with
\mathfrak{g}
which is the Lie algebra of the gauge group). Let
\{\alpha_{i}\}_{i\in\Gamma_{0}}
be simple roots of
\mathfrak{g}_{\Gamma}
and
\{\lambda_{i}\}_{i\in\Gamma_{0}}
be fundamental weights of
\mathfrak{g}_{\Gamma}
, then

\lambda_{\mathbf{e}}:=\sum_{i\in\Gamma_{0}}\mathbf{e}_{i}\lambda_{i},\quad\alpha_{\mathbf{r}}:=\sum_{i\in\Gamma_{0}}\mathbf{r}_{i}\alpha_{i}.

Note that
\lambda_{\mathbf{e}}
is a minuscule dominant weight of
\mathfrak{g}_{\Gamma}
, so every weight
\lambda
that appears in
V(\lambda_{\mathbf{e}})
satisfies the minuscule condition:

\displaystyle(\lambda,\alpha)\in\{0,\pm 1\},\;\forall\>\text{root }\alpha.

In particular, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}\cdot(\mathbf{e}-\mathsf{C}\mathbf{r}+\mathsf{C}\mathbf{u})=(\lambda_{\mathbf{e}}-\alpha_{\mathbf{r}}+\alpha_{\mathbf{u}},\alpha_{\mathbf{u}})=(\lambda_{\mathbf{e}}-\alpha_{\mathbf{r}},\alpha_{\mathbf{u}})+2\geqslant 1.

This finishes the proof.
